# Decisions record (v2)

**Status:** plan of record, 2026-10-11. It replaces the v1 record (the PSF-oriented plan, v1 D-01 to v1 D-136), which stays in git history (the repository's first commit) and is **not binding**. Binding input: the owner's direction of 2026-10-11 (community showcase, Android and iOS only, Material 3 only and identical on both platforms, not a 1:1 port, Zen of Python, phases rather than deadlines). Plan: [`PLAN.md`](../PLAN.md). Scope per feature id: [`feature-matrix.md`](feature-matrix.md).

**How this record works**
- Every decision is binding for code, docs and agents.
- Numbering restarts at D-01 for v2. v1 decisions are cited only as "v1 D-nn"; no live doc cites a v1 number against this file. From now on, numbers are never reused or renumbered. Numbers have gaps, and rows are grouped by topic rather than in number order: numbers are handed out ahead of the work, and one that ends up unused stays unused. `tests/test_decisions_doc.py` checks that every number cited has its row, and that a row saying "amends D-x" is named in D-x's row.
- To change a decision, open an issue labelled `needs-decision` with the options and a recommendation, and settle it as D-101 describes (reversible choices don't wait for the owner). Then, in a PR merged before or together with the code that depends on it, add a new D-number and mark the old one "superseded by D-nn", or, when only part of it changes, "amended by D-nn" with the changed words struck through and the rest still binding.
- Decisions marked **prov. O-n** are provisional: they apply until open decision O-n is settled under D-101 (end of this file).
- `F`-numbers refer to the verified Flet facts in section N. Spikes `S1`..`S9` are defined in PLAN §8.

---

## A. Purpose and scope

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-01 | We rebuild the PyCon US mobile app ([PyCon/pycon-us-mobile](https://github.com/PyCon/pycon-us-mobile): Ionic 8, Angular 17, Capacitor 7) in Python with Flet, as a **community showcase** for the Flet and Python communities. The official app's quality is our bar. The PSF is not a dependency and is not contacted now. | Owner's direction. |
| D-02 | Presenting the app at **PyCon US 2027** (Long Beach, CA, 2027-05-12..18) is the objective, not a deadline. Work is ordered in phases P0..P4 with exit criteria and soft markers. Quality is never traded for a date. | Owner's direction: "not a blocking constraint". |
| D-03 | **Not a 1:1 port.** The Ionic app is the functional reference. We keep its data semantics and improve flows, information architecture, visuals, accessibility and offline behaviour wherever we can. | Owner's direction. |
| D-04 | Each of the 263 catalog features gets a v2 disposition and a phase in `docs/feature-matrix.md`, which is the source of truth for scope. Dispositions are `keep`, `improve`, `redesign`, `mock`, `interim`, `later` and `drop`, plus `new` for our additions, and they can combine (e.g. `improve+interim`). Ids are an inventory for traceability, not a contract: there is no "one test per id" rule and no parity gate. | Parity gating served the PSF plan; a test per id is weak evidence of quality (both external reviews). |
| D-05 | The only real backend is the public feed `https://us.pycon.org/{year}/schedule/conference.json`. The active year is one constant, `config.YEAR`; the feed URL and all other year facts are data (`data/<year>/conference.toml`), never code. We use 2026 until the 2027 feed is published. | Public data needs no permission. |
| D-06 | Features that need the private PSF `/api/v1` (sign-in, favourites sync, role flags, the four staff tools) are built faithfully against our **local mock** (section F). The app never calls private PSF endpoints. | No permission to use them. |
| D-07 | The staff tools (lead retrieval, door check, swag pickup, Code of Conduct incident capture) stay in scope, in P3. They are the best showcase for the barcode-scanner extension and offline queueing. Attendee features never wait for them. | Showcase value, attendee-first ordering. |
| D-08 | Non-goals: web, PWA and desktop targets; Cupertino or adaptive UI; push notifications; analytics; a crash-reporting service; remote config, kill switches and OTA; public store listings; multi-event support; in-app chat or session feedback. | Each is PSF-only, off-platform, or scope the showcase does not need. |

## B. Platforms and UI

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-09 | Android and iOS only, phones first. Tablets get a proper layout: centred maximum widths (720 dp for reading, 840 dp for lists) and, from P2, a `NavigationRail` at 600 dp and wider. A desktop window is only a developer and test host. | Owner's direction; the UI is designed for phones and works on tablets. |
| D-10 | *(The back arrow's node amended by D-234.)* **Material 3 only, identical on both platforms.** Never `adaptive=True` and no Cupertino controls. We pin the Material defaults that change on iOS: the bundled font (D-26), left-aligned titles (`center_title=False`) and the Material back arrow. Flet has no theme-level back icon (no `Theme.platform`, F8), so `AppScaffold` sets `AppBar.leading=IconButton(ft.Icons.ARROW_BACK, tooltip="Back", on_click=<navigate to parent>)` on pushed views. Residual platform adaptations that Flet cannot pin today (overscroll physics: bounce on iOS, stretch on Android; the text-selection toolbar and handles in selectable Markdown and text fields) are accepted and listed in `ux.md`; pinning them is upstream candidate U19. | Otherwise Flutter Material adapts typography and app-bar details on iOS. No Flet scrollable exposes scroll physics. |
| D-11 | *(O-2 settled by D-148.)* The one intended platform difference is gesture behaviour (the accepted residual adaptations are in D-10): `Theme.page_transitions = PageTransitionsTheme(android=PREDICTIVE, ios=CUPERTINO)`. iOS keeps edge-swipe Back and Android 14+ keeps predictive Back. Visuals stay identical. | All three UX studies recommend it. iOS swipe-back exists only with the Cupertino transition, and the build template already enables predictive back (F9). |
| D-12 | *(Values recorded by D-106; overlay values amended by D-113.)* Device floors are Flet's template defaults: iOS 15 from Flet 1.1, and Flutter's default Android `minSdk` (confirmed in S5: 24). We target the current SDK with edge-to-edge, in portrait and landscape. System bars are never hidden, and content respects safe areas. Each colour scheme sets `Theme.system_overlay_style` (status- and navigation-bar icon brightness, ~~enforced navigation-bar contrast~~ transparent bars with navigation-bar contrast not enforced, D-113). Both are checked with 3-button and gesture navigation, in light and dark (#311). | Widest reasonable reach: v1's API 34 floor was a PSF policy, and Ionic #307 asked for older Android. Ionic's edge-to-edge layout left the Android navigation-bar icons the same colour as the background, so they were invisible (#311). |
| D-148 | **Per-platform transitions are confirmed** (settles O-2 as recommended; S4). `Theme.page_transitions = PageTransitionsTheme(android=PREDICTIVE, ios=CUPERTINO)` in every scheme. The iOS edge swipe is checked with a finger on the iPhone (#121); if it fails, this reopens. | On Android 14 (with the developer flag) and 16 (by default) an edge swipe on a detail ran Flutter's predictive page animation, a cancel kept the route and a commit popped through our handler. On iOS the pushed page slides in and out (screen recording); simctl cannot drag, so the swipe itself waits for a finger. |

## C. Information architecture and navigation

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-13 | *(Settled by D-454.)* A bottom `NavigationBar` with five fixed destinations: **Now · Schedule · My schedule · Map · More**. Labels are always shown, and the bar is identical for every user: it never changes with sign-in or role. Speakers live in More › Programme and in search. | M3 allows 3-5 destinations. This gives the two hero features (Now & Next for me, My schedule) a tab each, and a stable bar fixes Ionic's shape-shifting tab bar. It resolves the UX studies' disagreement over a Speakers tab versus a My schedule tab. |
| D-14 | *(Settled by D-454.)* *(Now's phases and queries recorded by D-179; the screen by D-190.)* The app opens on **Now**, a home dashboard. Before the conference it shows a countdown and key dates; during it, your next session, what is happening now and what is up next; afterwards, a wrap-up. First run shows Welcome once (P2). | It answers "what should I do next?" in every season. |
| D-454 | **The bottom bar stays Now · Schedule · My schedule · Map · More, and the app opens on Now; this is the recommendation, unproven by use** (#819, #18; settles O-1 under D-101 as recommended; D-13 and D-14 are no longer provisional).<br>• **No dogfooding happened:** O-1 and #18 asked for a week with the P1 build first. Nobody ran that week, so no use speaks for or against these tabs.<br>• **Reversible:** a later change can reopen it under D-101, with its reason, and the owner can override it. | Agents may settle it: O-1 carries no owner-only mark, though #347's update of 2026-10-31 listed #18 as the owner's. #18 had no comments when this was recorded, and "dogfood" appeared in the docs only as the plan to do it, so saying the week happened would be false. Waiting has no end: the tabs are built, and no week is scheduled. |
| D-15 | No navigation drawer. **More** is one flat, sectioned list: Staff tools (staff only, P3), Programme, Expo & sponsors, At the venue, About, You (settings and About this app). | M3 Expressive deprecates the modal drawer; flat is better than nested. |
| D-16 | Staff tools have no tab. They are the first More section and a card on Now, both shown from `staff_entries(flags)`. P3 adds the setting "Open the app on: Now / Lead scanner". It defaults to Lead scanner for lead-retrieval accounts and is applied only after the stored sign-in has loaded. | The bar stays stable, and sponsors keep landing on the scanner (#310) without Ionic's race. |
| D-17 | *(Bar amended by D-144; the Tracks hub's pages of their own sit under the hub, D-207.)* **Real back stack.** Every list → detail move and every cross-link pushes exactly one View through a recursive drill route, so Back pops one step (fixes #279). Switching tabs shows that tab's root~~, and tapping the active tab returns to its root~~; the bar is on tab roots only, and re-tapping the active tab scrolls its root to the top (D-144). Back on any root other than Now goes to Now; Back on Now exits. Non-Now root Views set `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that navigates to `/now` and awaits `e.control.confirm_pop(False)` (F1, F10); S4 verifies it with predictive back. | Predictable Back. Router semantics are verified in source (F6); S4 confirms on devices. |
| D-18 | *(Lifetime amended by D-143; how often the Schedule jumps by D-204; a filter change keeps the rows in view by D-371; a text-size change keeps each day list's place by D-331.)* State that must survive navigation lives outside screen state: filters in the `Prefs` store (persisted); the selected day, scroll offsets, the search query, scope and base, and the map sub-tab and zoom in `ViewMemory`, a plain, non-observable holder for the ~~app session~~ app process (D-143), so remembering a position re-renders nothing (architecture §6.3). Screens never move by themselves on return. The Schedule scrolls to the current slot once per ~~app session~~ ~~app process (D-143)~~ conference day in each app process (D-204) and when "Jump to now" is tapped, never on Back, refresh or a filter change (#313). | Fixes #313. `scroll_to` needs explicit triggers. |
| D-19 | *(Where `ViewMemory` is created amended by D-143; Search's back arrow amended by D-165; the screen recorded by D-193; the P2 scopes by D-351.)* **One search**: a global modal route `/search` over all days, opened from Now, Schedule, Map and More with a preset scope. Scopes are All, Sessions, Speakers and Rooms in P1; ~~Sponsors and Places join in P2~~ in P2 they are All, Sessions, Speakers, Places (which replaces Rooms) and Sponsors (D-351). Results push details on top of search, so Back returns to the results. The stock Router pops every view of a modal chain to the modal's base (F6), so `main()` sets our own `page.on_view_pop` before `page.render_views(App)` (the Router then hands every pop to it). It navigates to `parent_of(e.route)`, one pure, unit-tested function in `routes.py` that drops the last drill pair or page segment; for `/search` it returns the remembered base location, query included (`ViewMemory.search_base`, recorded by the opener; ~~`main()` creates the `ViewMemory`~~ `main.py` creates the `ViewMemory` once per process (D-143), and `main()` passes it on with `page.render_views(App, memory)`). The pushed views' back arrow (D-10) uses the same function, ~~and `AppScaffold(back_to=...)` points Search's arrow at the same base~~ which already returns that base for `/search`, so Search's arrow needs no parameter (D-165). The handler goes once U18 lands; S4 verifies search → result → Back. | One obvious way. It replaces four per-page searches and "Search all days". |
| D-20 | *(Where the summary sits at the largest text sizes amended by D-345.)* Only Schedule has filters: a bottom sheet of grouped filter chips that apply immediately, with a live "Show N sessions". Whenever a track is hidden, a summary under the day tabs says so, including the default: Open spaces are hidden by default, and that shows as a filter state. The filter button's dot appears only when filters differ from the default. Filters persist. My schedule, Now and Search ignore them. | Ionic leaked filters between screens and hid its default. |
| D-21 | *(Unknown ids amended by D-186; the deep-linking tables amended by D-269.)* Deep links (P2) use a custom URL scheme and host, named with the app identity (O-4); links have the form `<scheme>://<host>/<route path>`, mirroring internal routes. ~~Each deep-linking table sets~~ The one common deep-linking table sets (D-269) both `scheme` and `host`: the build template registers nothing when either is missing. A link opens its root with the target pushed, so Back never exits. ~~Unknown ids land on the root with a SnackBar~~ An unknown id opens its detail's not-found state, pushed on the root like any target (D-186), and reminder taps use the same paths. Shared links use the public us.pycon.org page. ~~Both `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` and `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]` are configured, so links work with or without `flet-dev/flet#6953` (F16).~~ One common `[tool.flet.deep_linking]` table serves both platforms (D-269). | Without the PSF domain there are no universal links or App Links. |
| D-269 | *(The placeholders became `confcompanion` and `app` by D-452. At the pin `a9ef26fdb` (#1005) the resolver is `get_deep_linking()`, `build_base.py:2801-2855`, the template registers once a scheme resolves (`AndroidManifest.xml:40-48`, `Info.plist:53-67`), and the Flutter flags are gone; we still set both keys. The function, lines and "unless both resolve" below are as of the older pin.)* **One common `[tool.flet.deep_linking]` table, with the placeholders `pyconus` and `app`** (amends D-21's per-platform tables and F16; #384, P2-24).<br>• **Where:** `pyproject.toml` sets `scheme` and `host` once, in `[tool.flet.deep_linking]`, and has no `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` or `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]`. At the pin, `flet build` resolves each key from the platform's table, else from the common one (`get_deep_linking_setting()`, `flet_cli/commands/build_base.py:2960-2982`, from `flet-dev/flet#6953`, in the pin since `68a4b326c`), so Android and iOS builds both get them. A platform table comes back only if one platform ever needs a different value.<br>• **Values:** links are `pyconus://app/<route path>`. Both are placeholders until O-4 (#15), which changes them in that one table; the comment on #15 lists every other place the swap touches (docs and examples). The scheme stays lowercase (Android matches an intent filter's scheme case-sensitively) and is never `http`, `https` or `file`.<br>• **What each platform registers** (the template registers nothing unless both resolve): Android an intent filter for the scheme and host (`AndroidManifest.xml:47-56`); iOS one `CFBundleURLTypes` entry whose `CFBundleURLSchemes` is the scheme and whose `CFBundleURLName` is the host (`Info.plist:53-69`), so on iOS the scheme alone opens the app: `pyconus://elsewhere/more` opened More there. The app cannot check the host either: Flet's `FletRouteInformationProvider.normalize()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/routing/route_information_provider.dart:10-20`, for cold and warm links) keeps only the path, query and fragment, so `page.route` is `/more` for any host. This is accepted, since links only show screens (architecture §5.6).<br>• **The guard:** `tests/test_deep_linking.py` runs flet-cli's resolver over `pyproject.toml` for Android and iOS and fails when the scheme or the host is missing, uppercase or malformed, or the scheme is a web or file one. | D-21 asked for both per-platform tables because the pin then ignored the common one on mobile (F16). `flet-dev/flet#6953` merged into `flet-1.1.0` on 2026-10-11 and is in our pin, so one table is simpler and leaves #15 one place to change. Checked at `15854a0ac` from this table alone: the generated `AndroidManifest.xml` has the `pyconus`/`app` intent filter and `Info.plist` the `CFBundleURLTypes` entry (scheme `pyconus`, name `app`) with `FlutterDeepLinkingEnabled`; a release arm64 APK on `flet_probe_api34` (Android 14) opened cold from `am start -W -a android.intent.action.VIEW -d pyconus://app/now` on Now and from `pyconus://app/more` on More, and resolved neither `pyconus://other/now` nor `PYCONUS://app/now`; an `ios-simulator` build on an iPhone 17 (iOS 26.5) opened from `simctl openurl` on Now, More and Schedule. Only the first `openurl` on that simulator showed the "Open in …?" prompt; every later one, after a terminate and after an uninstall and reinstall, opened the app directly (amends F31). |
| D-141 | *(How the Back key reaches our handler amended by D-208; a sixth kind, `changes/<year>`, added by D-255.)* **The drill route shape stands, and every pop goes through our `page.on_view_pop`** (S4; confirms section M, architecture §5.2 and D-19).<br>• **Shape:** ~~`ft.Route(path=":kind(session\|speaker\|room\|sponsor\|plan)/:ident", recursive=True, component=Drill)`~~ `ft.Route(path=":kind(session\|speaker\|room\|sponsor\|plan\|changes)/:ident", recursive=True, component=Drill)` (`changes` joins `DRILL_KINDS` with the Schedule changes screen, #435; D-255) is the last child of every tab root, More page and `/search`, and `Drill` returns the detail component. Each View level reads its own `kind` and `ident` (`use_route_params()`) and its own `use_view_path()`. `drill_href()` quotes ids with `quote(ident, safe="")` and `Drill` unquotes them. The `s~id` fallback is not needed.<br>• **Back:** `main()` sets `page.on_view_pop` before `page.render_views`; it receives the Android Back key and committed predictive gestures and navigates to `parent_of(e.route, memory.search_base)`, so search → result → detail → Back → Back lands on the results with the query from `ViewMemory`, and Back again on `search_base`. | On the Android 14 and 16 emulators and the iOS 26.5 simulator, a five-level stack gave each level its own params, and ids with `:`, `+`, a space, `~` and `ç` reached their level intact by tap, by deep entry and by `--route` (`docs/spikes/04-router-back.md`); the Router escapes each consumed prefix before matching the next level. Each Back was one `view-pop` and one level; without our handler the Router pops a whole modal chain to its base (F6). |
| D-142 | **Roots: D-17's Back rule works as written, and every root View shares one route key** (S4; ux.md §2.4).<br>• Non-Now roots set `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that calls `page.navigate("/now")` and awaits `confirm_pop(False)`; Back on Now is left to the system.<br>• Every tab root's `View.route` is `"/"`; pushed Views keep their resolved path (`use_view_path()`). | On Android 14 and 16, the Back key and the predictive gesture on Schedule, My schedule, Map and More each fired `confirm_pop` and landed on Now (the gesture shows no page animation there); on Now the system's back-to-home animation ran and the activity finished (F29). With a key per root, a tab switch ran the page transition (about 400 ms of slide and fade), as for a detail; with one key it swaps without one. Both pop handlers work from the route tree or from `e.route` of pushed Views, so the shared key changes nothing else. |
| D-143 | *(Welcome first until its Get started, D-287.)* **`ViewMemory` lives per process, and the start route returns a new session to where the user was** (amends D-18 and D-19; S4). `main.py` creates one `ViewMemory` at import and every `main()` passes that one to `App`. `AppScaffold` records the top View's `page.route` in `ViewMemory.last_location`; `Start` (`/`) navigates there when it is set, else to `/now` (`/welcome` on first run, P2). A last location under `/search` restores its `search_base` instead: a fresh Router would put the global modal over `/` (its last non-modal location), not over the opener. A deep link or a `--route` start still opens its own route. | On Android a new session starts in the same Python process after an activity recreation (F26) and after Back on Now and a relaunch (F29). With memory per session, a recreation on `/now/session/42/speaker/17` dropped the user on Now; with memory per process it rebuilt the three Views and Back popped from there. After Back on Now the last location is `/now`, so a relaunch looks like a fresh start with positions kept. |
| D-287 | **Welcome comes before the start route's every other target until its Get started, and only a plain start reaches it; it is a one-View root without a bar or the drill, in the boot purple in every scheme** (records D-14's "First run shows Welcome once", ux.md §6.8 and §2.6, architecture §5.2 and §5.3 for SHELL-02, SHELL-03 and STORE-07; amends D-143's start rule and D-198's landing after Reset app; adds `/welcome` to section M's routes; records F105 and U85; #423, P2-63; cited by P2-79 and P2-90).<br>• **The rule:** `locations.start_target(last_location, search_base, *, onboarded)` returns `/welcome` while `Prefs.onboarded` is false, whatever `ViewMemory` holds; otherwise D-143's rule as before. `Start` passes `prefs.onboarded`, which the boot gate has already read (D-161). Only a plain start comes to `/`: a deep link, a reminder tap (P2-90) or a `--route` start opens its own target with nothing to dismiss first, and Welcome waits for the next plain start, as a new session in the process after an activity recreation (F26) does too: rare, and Get started is one tap. Reset app clears `onboarded` and navigates to `start_target()`, so it lands on Welcome with its "App reset" (D-198).<br>• **Get started** (`welcome.start`) awaits `Prefs.save(onboarded=True)`, then `page.navigate("/now")`: a root replaces a root, so Back on Now leaves the app, never returning to Welcome. A failed save keeps the value for the session (D-120), is logged, and goes on to Now all the same; Welcome shows again at the next start, the one cost of the failure.<br>• **The route:** `ft.Route(path="welcome", component=Welcome)`, after `Start` and with no `DRILL`: nothing opens over it, so `/welcome/<kind>/<id>` is `NotFound` (D-270). `/welcome` is in `PAGES` (`locations.WELCOME`), the one page without the drill. The View shares the roots' key `"/"`, so Start's purple gives way to it, and it to Now, without a transition (D-142); Back there goes to Now, as on the other non-Now roots (D-321; it left the app until then).<br>• **The page** (ux.md §6.8; `screens/welcome.py`): full-bleed `#680579` in every scheme, high contrast included (white text 11.1:1, which passes HC's 7:1); "Welcome to {name}" (level 1, D-211), "{city} · {dates}" and, after the last day, "Thanks for coming. See you next year." from `conference.toml` through a pure `welcome_copy(info, day, *, reminders)`; two value rows, a third ("Get reminders…") only when `capabilities.REMINDERS`, so the page never promises what the build lacks; the D-36 footnote; Get started pinned under the scrolling list, white with the purple's ink. No icon until O-4, no permission prompt (D-61).<br>• **The status bar (U85 interim):** a View without an `AppBar` gives the status bar the theme's style (F105), whose icons are dark in a light scheme, 1.9:1 on the purple. Welcome's first control is a toolbar-less `AppBar` in the purple, inside a `Container` whose theme holds only a white style; Flutter takes the status bar's style from the frontmost region at its middle, so it wins, and the `SafeArea` under it leaves out the top inset. The navigation bar keeps the theme's (D-151). It goes when U85 (#509) is in the pinned Flet.<br>• **UI flows:** `tests/ui/helpers.py`'s `start_app()` waits for Welcome or Now and taps Get started until Now's title shows; every flow and `fresh_start()` start with it, and the shell flow checks Welcome first. #348's Maestro flows take the same step when they land. | The issue's choice, settled here: a plain start is the moment someone meets the app, and a link or a reminder tap is a promise to show one thing, which a screen in front of it would break; with the link's target opened, Welcome loses nothing by waiting. On my Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): a fresh install opened Welcome; Get started landed on Now, and Back left the app (the launcher resumed); a relaunch in the same process and a cold start after `force-stop` opened Now; after `pm clear`, a start with `--es route /more/app` opened About this app, and the next plain start opened Welcome; Reset app (a Maestro flow through About's seven taps and the dialog) landed on Welcome with "App reset". `uiautomator dump` showed one node per line and row, the footnote said as two sentences, and Get started one `Button` with `resource-id` `welcome.start`, 126 px tall (48 dp), with no node for the status-bar `AppBar`. At 200 % text and in landscape the lines wrapped and scrolled above the pinned button with nothing clipped. On an iPhone 17 simulator of #423's own (iOS 26.5, `flet build ios-simulator` checked against `build/python-app`), a Maestro flow tapped Get started, saw Now and no Welcome, relaunched the app and saw Now again; at the largest accessibility text size, portrait and landscape, the page scrolled to its footnote with the button whole. Before the interim the status-bar icons were dark grey on the purple on Android and the clock black on iOS; with it, white on both, in light and dark. With TalkBack on (the same emulator, its speech read from its verbose log and its next item pressed as Alt+Right on the `qwerty2` keyboard, testing-and-quality §7.3), a fresh start said "Welcome to PyCon US 2026", "Heading. 1 of 12. In list", then, one stop each, "Long Beach, CA. May 13 to 19", "Thanks for coming. See you next year.", the two rows, "Built with Python and Flet by the community. Not an official PSF app" and "Get started", "Button. Out of list"; the list's count includes its gaps, so the stops are 1, 3, 5, 7, 9 and 12 of 12. VoiceOver waits for the iPhone (#121). |
| D-321 | **Back on Welcome goes to Now, as on the other non-Now roots, whether a link or a plain start opened it** (amends D-287's route; applies D-142; #545, the issue's option 2, settled under D-101).<br>• `Welcome`'s View sets `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that calls `page.navigate("/now")` and awaits `confirm_pop(False)`, D-142's pair: Now replaces it under the shared key `"/"`, without a transition, and Back on Now leaves the app.<br>• Back is not Get started: it saves nothing, so `Prefs.onboarded` stays false and the next plain start shows Welcome again (D-287).<br>• `tests/test_deep_links.py` walks a cold `/welcome` link as Now, then out; `tests/test_welcome.py` checks the pop is declined, Now is the target and nothing is saved. | Consistency with D-142: a link to `/welcome` (D-291) would otherwise drop someone out of the app with one Back, where every other root's link lands them on Now. The View can't tell a link from a first run (both are `/welcome` under the key `"/"`), and nothing in the code asks for a difference, so one rule covers both; a first-run Back to Now loses nothing, since Welcome comes back at the next plain start. The pop path is the one D-142 checked on Android 14 and 16 for the other roots (F29). On an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5, a `flet build ios-simulator` of #551), the link `pyconus://app/welcome` opened Welcome, a left-edge swipe left it there (iOS has no Back on a lone root), and a plain relaunch showed Welcome again. Android's Back from a link-opened Welcome is still unchecked on a device: the run for #551 stalled on an overloaded host. |

| D-164 | *(`NotFound`'s route amended by D-270.)* **Location helpers live in `locations.py`; the route table in `routes.py`** (amends section M's `routes.py`; #74). `routes.py` imports every screen for `ROUTES`, and screens build links, so the helpers they use cannot live there without an import cycle.<br>• `locations.py`: `TAB_ROOTS`, `DRILL_KINDS`, `PAGES` (every page path without a parameter, the places Back walks up to; a test keeps it equal to the table's), `drill_href()`, `parent_of()`, `start_target(last_location, search_base)` (D-143's rule for `Start`), `query_of()`, `is_top_view(location, view_path)` and the `use_query()` hook. All but the hook are pure and unit-tested.<br>• `routes.py`: `ROUTES`, `DRILL`, `Drill`, `Start` and `NotFound`; ~~`App` passes `not_found=NotFound`, which the Router wraps in a View of its own (F6), so it brings a `SafeArea` and a "Go to Now" button~~ `ROUTES` ends with `UNKNOWN`, a catch-all whose component is `NotFound`, a View of its own (D-270).<br>• The top View is the one `page.route` addresses: `is_top_view(page.route, use_view_path())` compares the route's path with the View's. Not `use_route_location()`: under a global modal the Router renders the Views below at the opener's location, so the opener's View would match too, and so would `Start` under a `/search` entered directly. `use_query()` reads the query once, when its View mounts, and only for the top View: a deep entry also builds the Views below, a modal keeps its opener's mounted, and the query is theirs in neither case. `stub_view()` (then `AppScaffold`) records only the top View in `last_location`.<br>• A page with a parameter nests under the page Back returns to: `/more/tracks/<slug>` is a child of `/more/tracks`, so the stack the Router builds is the one `parent_of()` walks (as siblings, the track's View would sit on More while Back went to Tracks).<br>• `Start` shows the boot purple and shares the tab roots' View key `"/"` (D-142), so Now replaces it without a transition or a flash. It navigates only when it is the top View: a direct `/search` entry builds the modal over `/`, and `Start` below it leaves the entry alone (Back from search goes to `search_base`). | One obvious place for each: screens import `locations` and never the table. Deriving `PAGES` from `ROUTES` at import would put the cycle back; a test is enough to keep two short lists equal. Reading the query only on the top View keeps a deep entry such as `/map/room/hall-a?highlight=42` from handing `highlight` to the Map root below it. |
| D-165 | *(Strip during a refresh amended by D-181; the strip's module by D-200; the tablet rail recorded by D-277; the 720/840 dp widths by D-305.)* **`AppScaffold` and the status strip** (records architecture §5.5 and D-32's strip; #76). In `ui/scaffold.py` (`StatusStrip` and `strip_line()`: `ui/status_strip.py` since D-200):<br>• **`AppScaffold(*, title, body, actions=None)`** builds every screen's View: a surface `AppBar` whose title is left-aligned (`center_title=False`) and stays a header for screen readers (Flutter's `exclude_header_semantics=False`), the screen's actions, `automatically_imply_leading=False` with the Material back arrow on pushed Views to `parent_of(view_path, memory.search_base)` (D-10, D-19), D-142's shared root key and D-17's Back to Now on non-Now roots, and `body` (one control, usually an `expand=True` `ListView`) in a `SafeArea`. No `back_to`: `parent_of()` already sends `/search` to its opener. The status- and navigation-bar style is the theme's (D-113, D-151): Flet's `AppBar` applies `Theme.system_overlay_style`, so no screen sets one. The bottom bar joins in #75 (roots only, D-144); the tablet rail and the 720/840 dp widths (D-09) wait for #30: Flet has no max-width constraint, so both need the page width and its changes.<br>• **D-112's content line moves to `AppScaffold`:** it logs `Content shown: <source> feed, version <n>` once per feed version per session, from the first screen that renders it (`Services.shown_versions`), after a SharedPreferences round trip; so a session that starts on another route (D-143, `--route`) logs it too, and a tab switch does not log it again. A newer version re-runs the effect, and Flet cancels the run in progress (`hook.cancel()`; an unmount does not, as the effect has no cleanup), so the round trip and the line run under `asyncio.shield`: otherwise a network version published within the round trip dropped the cached version's line, and the cold-start mark moved to the network version (once in three launches on a deep route on the iOS 26.5 simulator; with the shield both lines were logged in every launch). `AppScaffold` places the body in `Shown(body)`, a leaf component that reads `Conference`, logs the line and returns the same `body`: a refresh re-renders that leaf only, so screens that do not show the feed (More, its pages, the stubs) do not re-render with it (Flet keeps a retained child as is, `object_patch.py` `src is dst`).<br>• **`StatusStrip`** is its own component, placed by the screens that show the feed's state (Now, Schedule, My schedule): hidden when `fresh`; otherwise an icon, one line from the pure `strip_line()` and a Refresh `TextButton` (disabled while ~~refreshing~~ a refresh the user asked for runs, D-181), with an indeterminate 2 dp `ProgressBar` while ~~refreshing~~ any refresh runs (`Conference.refreshing`, D-181), on `surface_container_high`, as one live region with the identifier `app.status`. Lines: "Updating the schedule…" (only for a refresh the user asked for, D-181), "Updated 9 hours ago" (`timefmt.ago_label()`, whole units in words, so screen readers say "hours", not "h"), "Offline · schedule from Tue, May 12, 10:42 AM" and, after an answer we could not use (D-146), "Couldn't update · schedule from …", in conference time and the device's 12/24-hour format. Without data (rare, NEW-03) a stale strip says "No schedule yet", and a failed refresh keeps its reason: "Offline · no schedule yet", "Couldn't update · no schedule yet". #82 adds the refresh triggers and re-renders the age every 60 s.<br>• `SectionHeader(text)` is a `TITLE_SMALL` heading in `primary` inside `Semantics(header=True)`; `search_button(memory, scope)` moves here from the stubs. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), gesture and 3-button navigation in light and dark and in landscape, the title was left-aligned, pushed Views showed the Material arrow, rows stopped above the navigation bar and clear of the side bar in landscape, the bars' icons contrasted, and a real offline start showed the offline strip; on the iOS 26.5 simulator the same held around the notch and the home indicator. One log line per version keeps D-112's cold-start mark wherever a session starts, without a duplicate on every tab switch. A per-strip component keeps the strip's own hooks out of the screens, and `body` as one control lets a screen choose a `ListView` or a `Column` with tabs (P1's Schedule) without a wrapper. |
| D-166 | *(Why the identifier merges corrected by F95; the rail's destinations, without one, by D-277.)* **The bottom bar: its module, identifiers and re-tap** (records D-13, D-27 and D-144 for the bar; amends section M's `ui/` list; #75).<br>• **`ui/navigation.py`** holds `TABS` (`Tab(root, label, icon, selected_icon)` in `TAB_ROOTS`' order, with `Tab.identifier`), `navigation_bar(root, on_change)` and `use_scroll_to_top()`. `AppScaffold` puts the bar on tab roots only: a Material `NavigationBar` (never adaptive), labels `ALWAYS_SHOW`, `selected_index` the View's own root.<br>• **Identifiers:** `NavigationBarDestination` has no `Semantics` or identifier of its own, so its `icon` and `selected_icon` are each an unlabelled `Semantics(identifier="app.nav.<tab>", content=Icon(...))` (`app.nav.now`, `app.nav.schedule`, `app.nav.my_schedule`, `app.nav.map`, `app.nav.more`; section M). Flutter merges a child's identifier into the enclosing node when that node has none (`SemanticsConfiguration.absorb`), so the destination's one node carries it, beside its label. Flet's `Semantics.identifier` docstring says an identifier forces a dedicated node (like `container=True`); the Dart control does not (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/semantics.dart` reads only `container`), and the merge relies on that, so each pin bump re-checks it (development.md §2.2) and #163 tracks the upstream fix.<br>• **Taps:** `AppScaffold(on_retap=...)`. On a root, another destination navigates to its root (one shared View key, D-142); the active one awaits the root's `on_retap`, and without one does nothing. `use_scroll_to_top()` gives a root one list ref (`use_memo`) and an action that awaits `scroll_to(offset=0, duration=300)`, a jump (`duration=0`) under `Prefs.reduce_motion` (D-34), and nothing before the list is built. Each render's `ListView(ref=...)` points the ref at the new control, which takes over the mounted list's identity (F38), so the ref stays live although `Ref.current` is weak; keeping one ref also keeps a list that a memoised child (F34) did not rebuild reachable. A re-tap is the user's own request, so D-18's "screens never move by themselves" stands. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), `uiautomator dump` showed each destination as one clickable node with `resource-id` `app.nav.<tab>`, named "Schedule, Tab 2 of 5" (TalkBack's text), the active one `selected`, each 82 × 80 dp; adb journeys in gesture navigation passed for tab switches, the re-tap scroll, a push (no bar) and Back, the Back key and the predictive gesture (cancelled and committed) on details and roots, and Back on Now leaving the app; the bar and a barless push were checked again in 3-button navigation and in dark mode. A recording showed a tab switch as a one-frame swap and the root's one bar fading under a pushed page. On the iOS 26.5 simulator an in-app journey (15 checks, light and dark) saw the same switches, re-tap and push. At 115 % to 200 % OS text (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS), "My schedule" wraps to two lines without clipping, because Flutter clamps the bar's labels at 1.3x and the label does not fit 82 dp; whether to keep it is #164's decision. Merging the identifier needs no change in Flet; an identifier field on the destination would be the cleaner upstream fix (#163). |

| D-277 | *(The store's room for the content widths added by D-305; phone landscape's sign that the rail scrolls by D-309.)* **From 600 dp the tab roots show a `NavigationRail` beside the body, from a `Layout` store that notifies only when the window crosses 600 dp; the body keeps its place in the tree, so a rotation keeps its state; the rail's destinations carry no identifier until Flet can give them one** (records D-09's rail and ux.md §8 for SHELL-14; amends section M's top-level modules, stores and `ui/navigation.py`, D-165's "the tablet rail waits for #30", D-166's identifiers for the rail, and D-216's probe; #387, P2-27).<br>• **The store:** `layout.py`'s `Layout`, an `@ft.observable` with one field, `wide` (`is_wide(width)`: `width >= WIDE_DP`, 600 dp, M3's medium window class; no width yet counts as a phone), in its own context `LayoutCtx` with `use_layout()` (D-70 rule 5). `App` builds it from `page.width`, which Flet has before the session starts (`flet_backend.dart` registers the client only after the first size), and an effect of `App`'s calls `follow_width(page, layout)`, the app's one `page.on_resize`, which also reads `page.width` once more for a resize before it. `set_width()` assigns the class; the same value notifies nothing (F5), so a resize within a class re-renders nothing and a crossing re-renders the screens that read it (`AppScaffold`), never `App`, which only provides it. No `reset()`: a device fact, as `Prefs`' device fields, which Reset app keeps (D-198). Flutter sends a resize after a rotation, a Split View or multi-window change, at most every 100 ms (`PageMedia`'s debouncer).<br>• **The rail:** `ui/navigation.py`'s `navigation_rail(root, on_select, *, visible)` from `TABS`: `label_type=ALL`, `group_alignment=-1`, `min_width=RAIL_WIDTH` (80), `scrollable=True`, the root's destination selected; a `NavigationRail` has no adaptive form (D-10). The bar and the rail take the same `on_select(index)`, so `AppScaffold` has one tap rule: another destination navigates to its root (one View key, D-142), the active one awaits `on_retap` (D-144, D-166); Flutter reports the active destination on the rail too (F95).<br>• **Where it goes:** on a tab root `AppScaffold` puts the body in a `Row` inside the `SafeArea` (D-12), beside the rail (`beside_rail()`: spacing 0, children stretched to the row's height, which the rail needs bounded), and the rail is `visible` only when `wide`, when the View has no `navigation_bar`. Pushed Views do not change: the back arrow and no bar or rail (D-144). The tree is the same in both classes, so a crossing patches `visible` and `navigation_bar` and leaves the body, the screen's components and Flutter's state under them as they were (D-18). A hidden control is no widget at all (Flet's Dart side builds only visible children), and every root body is an `expand=True` control that fills the row as it filled the `SafeArea`, so under 600 dp the roots look as before, pixel for pixel.<br>• **No identifier on the rail's destinations** (amends D-166 for the rail; #163): at the pin's Flutter a `Semantics` with an identifier is a node of its own (F95), which the bar's destinations fold back in through their `MergeSemantics`; the rail's have none, and Flet's `Semantics` always states `enabled` (U34, F42), which makes that node focusable, so each destination would hold a second, empty TalkBack stop. One stop per destination wins over a test hook: UI flows find a rail destination by its name ("Map, Tab 4 of 5" on Android, the label on iOS), and `app.nav.<tab>` stays on the bar's. #163 asks Flet for an identifier on both destinations; `tagged_icon()` goes to the rail with it.<br>• **Phone landscape** keeps the rail (about 870 to 915 dp wide). Beside the body it has about 295 dp of height there, and five destinations with their labels need about 330 (64 dp each), so the rail scrolls: More shows its icon at the bottom edge and comes in with a scroll, at the default text size and at 200 %. The labels are not clamped (unlike the bar's, D-167): at 200 % the rail widens to about 160 dp with "My schedule" on one line. Kept over the bar in landscape, which took 80 dp of the 330 the body has, and over `label_type=SELECTED`, which ux.md §8 rules out; this is the fallback the issue asked to record (revisited in #515: a fade over a short rail's bottom edge now shows that it scrolls, D-309).<br>• **The time gutter's probe** (amends D-216): `GutterProbe` measures the View's width, and from 600 dp a root's slots have the rail's width less; `time_gutter.slot_width()` takes `RAIL_WIDTH` off from 600 dp, for every View, so one answer errs towards the time above. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, my own on port 5584, release arm64 APKs whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`, offline): the five roots of `main` and of this change, screenshot in portrait at a cold start, were identical below the status bar in light and in dark (a pixel diff, 0 pixels). In landscape (2400 × 1080, 914 dp) the roots showed the rail and no bar; a Schedule scrolled in portrait kept its offset through the rotation to landscape and back; a tap on Map opened the Map, a re-tap scrolled its list to the top, and the Back key on the Map landed on Now. `uiautomator dump` read each destination as one clickable node, "Map / Tab 4 of 5", the active one `selected`, 80 × 64 dp; with the bar's tagged icons, each destination had a second node, an empty focusable `View` with `resource-id` `app.nav.<tab>` (24 dp), on which TalkBack, moved with Alt+Right on the emulator's keyboard and read from its log (D-241), stopped and said nothing; without them it said "Schedule. Heading", "Search", "Filter sessions", "Now", "selected. Schedule", "My schedule", "Map", "More", each once, and Alt+Enter on Map opened the Map. At 200 % font scale in landscape the rail widened, wrapped nothing and scrolled to More. With the display at 1600 × 2560 and 320 dpi (800 dp, a tablet) the rail showed in light and dark. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (an `ios-simulator` build whose bundle matched `build/python-app`, an iPhone 17 and an iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) of #387's own): the iPad (834 dp) showed the rail on the Schedule and Now in light and dark; on the iPhone, driven by the free Maestro CLI, the Schedule scrolled to its third tutorial in portrait kept that offset through `setOrientation: LANDSCAPE_LEFT` (the rail, all five destinations on screen beside the body, no bar) and back to `PORTRAIT` (the bar); `tapOn: "Map.*Tab 4 of 5"` opened the Map (`map.title`), `"Schedule.*Tab 2 of 5"` the Schedule, and in portrait `app.nav.map` was on screen again. |
| D-305 | **Content widths: each screen asks `use_margin()` for its width class, reading 720 dp, list 840 dp or full, and gets side margins it puts in its list's own padding, so a list scrolls from its margins** (records D-09's widths and ux.md §8 for SHELL-14; amends D-277's store, which now holds the body's room too, D-216's probe and section M's `ui/`; #440, P2-80).<br>• **The classes** (`ui/widths.py`): `READING` (720 dp: session, speaker and sponsor detail, the info pages through `InfoBody`, About this app, Developer options), `LIST` (840 dp: every other screen, the not-found page included) and `FULL` (the floor viewer, which needs no call). A screen calls the hook `use_margin(cap)` once per render, with its hooks, and hands the margin to its list's padding through `sides(margin, top=, bottom=)` and to its fixed chrome through `capped(control, margin)`: the Schedule's day tabs, filter summary, status strip and zone note, the day tabs of a room and of Open spaces, and the Speakers and Sprints filters. The memoised day lists of the Schedule and Open spaces call it themselves; a room's `day_list()` takes it. The Schedule's Now button takes it as its right `margin`, so it sits on the capped list: a FAB is a `LayoutControl`, and its transparent margin passes taps through. The margins are the page's `surface`.<br>• **The store** (amends D-277): `Layout.room` is the window's width less the left and right safe-area insets (`page.media.padding`, the `SafeArea`'s), recorded only when it is over `READING_DP`, else 0: under 720 dp no cap applies, so a phone in portrait still re-renders on nothing, and a tablet's screens re-render when its room changes (a rotation, Split View). `follow_width()` takes the insets on each resize and on each `page.on_media_change`, which Flutter sends apart; `layout_for(page)` seeds both fields for the first screen. `use_margin()` takes `RAIL_WIDTH` off on a tab root from 600 dp (`ft.use_view_path()` in `TAB_ROOTS`); the rail is wider at large text, so the list there is a little narrower than its cap, never wider.<br>• **The gutter's probe** (amends D-216): `time_gutter.slot_width()` caps the slot's width at `LIST_DP`, since every list with slots is a list: on a 1,366 dp tablet a 170 dp gutter, under a fifth of the 1,286 dp beside the rail, is over a fifth of the 840 dp list, and the time goes above.<br>• **Not chosen:** a fixed-width box in a centred parent, which is one line in `AppScaffold` but leaves the margins dead to a drag (the issue asks that a list scrolls from them); a context that `AppScaffold` provides around the body, which cannot reach the lists: a screen builds its body, the controls and the components in it, before it calls `AppScaffold`, and Flet takes a component's contexts when it is created, from the providers open at that moment (`component.py`'s `_render_component()`, `c._contexts = self._snapshot_contexts()`), so the class has to be the screen's own call; `AppScaffold` editing the body's padding, which is implicit and misses the lists inside a column or a memoised component.<br>• **Out:** sheets and dialogs (Material caps them), two panes (ux.md §8). | D-09 asks for readable lines on a tablet: a line of 1,280 dp is no line. Padding in the list keeps the whole width a drag surface, as Material's own centred lists are, and needs no Flet change: Flet has no max-width (D-165). The class lives in the screen because only the screen builds its list. Checked by `tests/test_widths.py` at 400, 700, 1,000 and 1,300 dp, by `tests/test_time_gutter.py` against the capped column, and on the devices the PR lists. |
| D-309 | **When the rail is shorter than its five labelled destinations, as on a phone in landscape, it keeps its labels and its scroll, and a 40 dp fade over its bottom edge shows that it scrolls** (amends D-277's phone landscape bullet and ux.md §8; SHELL-14; #515).<br>• **When:** `AppScaffold` places `Rail(root, on_select, visible)`, a component in `ui/navigation.py` that measures its own height (`on_size_change` on the rail's `Stack`, F51: at mount, after a rotation and after a text-size change) and keeps `short` in a `use_state`: true under `RAIL_FIT_DP`, 320 dp. Flutter's rail needs 8 + 5 × 64 = 328 dp for five labelled destinations at the default text size (an 8 dp spacer, then 12 + 32 + 4 + 16 dp each, `navigation_rail.dart`), but the lower half of More's 16 dp label line holds no ink, so from 320 dp all five show whole. About 303 dp on the Android 14 emulator in landscape: short. The iPhone 17 in landscape (402 pt less the 56 pt app bar and the 21 pt home-indicator inset, about 325) and tablets: no fade and no extra space, so their last label is drawn as before; a first build that faded under 328 dp dimmed More's whole label on the iPhone. P2's `map.toml` gives the rooms their pins; these stay the places without one.<br>• **Rows:** a `LinkTile` (`PLACE_OUTLINED`, the room's name, `CHEVRON_RIGHT`) whose subtitle is the count, "26 sessions" ("1 session"), and, only while something is on, a line of its own under it, "Now: <title>" in `Tokens.live`, cut at three lines as a session row's title is (ux.md §3.6), and when several are on a further line "and 1 more", kept out of the cut text so an ellipsis never hides it. Each has the header words of ux.md §5.1 and its own sections: key dates and "Build your schedule"; your next session, Happening now (phase `day` only), Up next and the Schedule link; a Sprints row (the last evening too); "Look back at the schedule". "Day N of M" counts the feed's days with sessions up to today (`data.days`: 5 in 2026). The header is one ~~level-1~~ level-2 (D-211) heading node read as one phrase; "Times are Pacific Time (PDT)" (D-35) only in the conference season, the one that shows times; the brand indigo `#3B3EA9` in both schemes (light `primary`, dark `primary_container`), its season icon in ux.md §3.1's gold.<br>• **Your next session:** `next_for_me()` in a `Card(FILLED)`: the `SessionRow` with its star (identifiers `now.next.*`) and, once it is on, its elapsed bar, counted like its status from `display_start` (D-186: the Job Fair's from 9:30, though its slot is 10:00); under it session detail's `StatusLine` and "Show room" (`room_href()`, only for a session with a room) in a wrapping row, so at large text the button moves under the status whole; the status is a stop of its own (row, status, star, button: four). The card's session is not repeated in Happening now or in Up next, and a section it leaves empty is left out: when it is all that is on (the card says "Happening now"; an empty section would say nothing is) or all of the next slot (the card says "Starts in 20 min"), which is usual, since the next saved talk is mostly in the next slot. Not on `primary_container` as §5.1 sketched: the dark one is the header's indigo, and the row's `on_surface_variant` lines read 3.7:1 on it; M3's filled colour, `surface_container_highest`, is a surface the token and badge tests already cover. With no answer on a conference day, a `LinkTile` says why: nothing saved (to the Schedule), or nothing in the next 3 h (to My schedule; in the evening after a day, phase `after_day`, "Nothing more saved for today").<br>• **Elapsed bars:** `SessionRow(elapsed=)` takes `agenda.elapsed_percent(session, now)`, whole percent from `display_start` to `end`, rounded down by integer division (a float share made 29 % into 28), and draws a 3 dp `ProgressBar` in `Tokens.live` among the row's excluded texts, M3's current bar (`year_2023=False`): a gap after the fill, a track of `on_surface` at 16 % (about 1.4:1 on `surface` and on the card's `surface_container_highest`, where `surface_container_highest` itself vanished; the fill reads 3.7:1 or more on it) and a stop dot in `on_surface_variant` (4.5:1 or more) at the end, which marks 100 % as WCAG 1.4.11 asks; a track at 3:1 on the surface (`outline`) would have left the fill at about 1.4:1 on it in light, since `live` is a dark green; the row's phrase ends with "60 percent elapsed." (`elapsed_phrase()`). ux.md §5.1's `semantics_label="Elapsed"` and `semantics_value="60 percent"` cannot say that at the pin: Flet types `semantics_value` as a number and sends it as a double ("60.0"), and without it Flutter gives the bar the value "60" (`progress_indicator.dart`, material_ui 1.6.0), which a merged row reads bare (#210). Whole numbers keep the memo: a minute re-renders only the rows whose bar moved, which a test checks and which fails with `ft.memo` removed.<br>• **Time:** `use_minute()` in `Now` (D-188): every 60 s while it is mounted and in the foreground, at once on a resume (`Services.run_every`), from one `clock.now()` per render, so the fake clock drives it (D-187); a test pauses the loop, moves the clock and sees the resume recompute. The strip keeps `auto_refresh=True` (D-175).<br>• **"See the rest of today in Schedule"** ("Open the schedule" when the next slot is on a later day) is a navigation to `/schedule` and nothing more, on every conference day until the conference's last end (gated on the phase, not on a next slot, so the day's last slot keeps it): the Schedule (#86) opens on today during the conference and owns its jump (D-155); a hint for it, such as a slot key in `ViewMemory` with D-185's `current_slot_key()` fallback, is #89's.<br>• **`SectionHeader(leading=, spoken=)`:** a decorative mark before the text, Happening now's 8 dp `live` dot, the text expanding beside it so a long heading wraps; the heading still reads its text, or `spoken`, which `now_header.spoken()` makes of it ("·" a pause, a range's dash "to"), as for the header phrase, the key dates and the Sprints row.<br>• **Identifiers on buttons and notes are merged** (`MergeSemantics(Semantics(identifier=...))`): alone, the identifier's `Semantics` is a node of its own, an empty extra TalkBack stop around the button. | A screen that changes with the minute is only worth its place at the front door if it reads right at every moment of the year, so each season and phase is tested at fixed times of the 2026 feed (`tests/test_now.py`), and the bars' semantics avoid a field Flet cannot fill. It stays where it is when tapped, so focus stays on it.<br>• **Its state:** `Now`'s `use_state` of opened `fold_key()`s, a section's name and the ids of its whole set, the card's session included (`folded(whole=)`): a minute keeps it open, and so does a star that moves a session into the card or out of it, a new slot folds it again, and leaving Now folds it.<br>• **Not on the first screen:** #222 asked for Up next's heading on an iPhone 17's first screen at 11:10 AM on Friday; amended on #222 to "after Happening now's saved rows, three others and the button". When nothing is ahead, a compact notice heads the list, start-aligned above the "Earlier days" tile rather than the full-screen empty state, which pushed the tile to the middle of the screen: "All your saved sessions are over" (a level-2 heading in a live region: `header=True` alone is no heading, F40), with "See what's on today" (to Now) while today, in the conference zone, is on or before the feed's last day, and after it "<name> is over. Your saved sessions stay here." (the year data's name) with no button, since Now has nothing on today.<br>• **No scroll to today:** with the past folded, today's header is the second item, so ux.md §5.6's once-per-process `scroll_to(scroll_key="day-<iso>")` and D-154's `cache_extent` for this list would move the list by one tile; the list stays lazy and opens at its top. This is only the scroll to today: restoring the tab's offset from `ViewMemory` (ux.md §2.4) is a separate item, still open for this root (D-192 restores the Schedule's day lists).<br>• **A clash in words:** a session ahead that overlaps another gets a last line: `SessionRow(..., overlaps=(names…))` shows `WARNING_AMBER` beside "Overlaps with “<first>”", cut at three lines like a title, and, with more clashes, "and 2 more" as a line of its own, never cut (D-188's "Now:" line: an ellipsis inside one two-line text hid the second name and the count at default size and the first name at 200 %), both in `Tokens.warning`; `spoken_phrase()` ends by naming every clash, "Overlaps with “A”, “B” and “C”.", so the clash is heard in full (D-33); each row of a clash shows it. Only a clash that can still change a plan shows: `fold_ended()`'s `overlaps` keeps, for each session ahead, the clashes with sessions that have not ended, and folded rows get none, so the history carries no warning colour and "and N more" counts only what matters.<br>• **A removal goes at once, with Undo** (ux.md §4.4, §5.6): the star's tap removes the row, and "Removed from My schedule · Undo" (`use_favorite_toggle`, D-184) brings it back where it was; a clash line on the other row goes and comes back with it. Not "keep the row until the SnackBar ends": that would be a second removal rule beside every other list's, and a row that stays after its star went hollow says the opposite of the star.<br>• **States** (ux.md §4.6): `Favorites.loaded` False after the gate shows "Couldn't open My schedule" with no button (D-172), before anything else; no data shows the Schedule's skeleton or "Can't load the schedule" (`ui/states.py`'s new `NoDataState`, which the Schedule now uses too); saved ids that the feed no longer lists at all (D-172 keeps them) show "None of your saved sessions is in the schedule", not "Nothing saved yet", which would be untrue; nothing saved shows "Nothing saved yet" with "Browse the schedule" (to Schedule). Icons: `STAR_BORDER`, `EVENT_BUSY_OUTLINED`, `ERROR_OUTLINE`, `EVENT_AVAILABLE_OUTLINED`.<br>• **Memo** (D-153): rows keyed by session id get views from `use_session_views()` over *every* session of the feed, so a star tap that changes the saved ids leaves every view the same object; each day (or part of today) is a memoised `_Day` keyed by its date and part, given its `AgendaDay` (equal by value until a session in it changes) and only its own overlap lines. A removal re-renders only its own day's section and, in it, only a row whose overlap line changed; tests fail with either `ft.memo` removed. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), with a check-only build (never committed) whose clock started at Thursday, May 14, 10:45 AM: the earlier days and the 9:30 workshop folded into their tiles, the 9:00 tutorial and summit stayed with their clash lines, light and dark; on the shared simulator two overlapping tutorials starred on the Schedule stub showed both lines, a star tap removed one row and its partner's line with "Removed from My schedule · Undo", and Undo brought both back. After the review (Claude Fable for D-100 and a UI and accessibility review), on the same devices: the tutorial and the summit name each other with "and 1 more" on its own line, the ended 9:30 workshop no longer counted, and the folded rows carry no clash line; at 200 % (Android) and at iOS's accessibility-extra-large the name and the count both show whole, and at the largest Larger Text the name is cut at three lines but the count still shows; the compact all-over notice with "See what's on today" on Sunday evening and without it on June 1, and the dropped state; the 16 dp warning icon keeps its size beside 200 % text (Flutter scales text, not icons). Without the folds, scrolling to today would have been the first thing the screen did on every conference day; with them, today is on screen at once. |
| D-329 | **My schedule says when some saved sessions are no longer in the schedule: a compact notice under the status strip that counts them, links to Schedule changes when the change log holds one's removal, and forgets them with Undo** (settles #215's design under D-101; extends D-172, D-191 and ux.md §5.6's states).<br>• **When:** some saved ids, not all, are missing from the current feed (`favorites.ids` minus the feed's sessions, kept by D-172). When all are missing, #209's "None of your saved sessions is in the schedule" state stays as it is.<br>• **Where and what:** right under the `StatusStrip`, above the agenda and above "All your saved sessions are over", start-aligned on the fold tile's text column as that notice (`screens/my_schedule_extras.py`'s `dropped_notice()`): `EVENT_BUSY_OUTLINED` (the change log's removal icon), "1 saved session is no longer in the schedule" / "3 saved sessions are no longer in the schedule" (`TITLE_SMALL`, a level-2 heading), then "If the schedule lists it again, it comes back here." / "…them again, they come back here." One live-region node (`my_schedule.dropped_notice`), a level-2 heading whose explicit label is the title, a newline, then the body (so it changes with the count), with its texts excluded (`exclude_semantics`), the buttons outside it. Not a merge of the texts, which TalkBack read body first (#564): Flet's `Semantics` always states `enabled` (F42), and two configurations that both state a tristate flag conflict (`Tristate.hasConflict`, `dart:ui` `semantics.dart:1133`, Flutter 3.47.7), so a heading `Semantics` inside the live region is marked a conflict (`_marksConflictsInMergeGroup`, `rendering/object.dart:6557`) and forms a child node; the live region absorbs only the body's text, and `getSemanticsData()` puts a merged node's own label before its descendants' (`semantics.dart:3861`). The list always has it, quiet (1 dp, no words, `hidden`) while nothing is dropped, so the node exists before its label changes and TalkBack says it when a refresh drops one (F61, as D-241's note). `hidden` because empty is not quiet: Flet's `Semantics` always states `enabled` (F42), a focusable flag on Android (F95), so the empty node would be a blank stop. The engine keeps a hidden node (`updateWith()` runs and records its empty label) but leaves it out of its parent's children and the hit test (`AccessibilityBridge.java:900`, `:2682`); the update that shows it changes its label too, so `updateSemantics()` (`:1564`) counts it as updated and sends the live region's change (`:1705`).<br>• **See what changed** (`my_schedule.see_changes`) pushes `…/changes/<year>` on My schedule's View (D-255), where each removed saved session already shows its last time and room, "Removed from the latest schedule" and its star (#435). Only when the change log holds the removal of one of them (`logged()`): the log keeps 14 days and is deleted with a feed URL change, and the screen would otherwise not mention them.<br>• **Forget it / Forget them** (`my_schedule.forget`) unsaves only the dropped ids at once (`Favorites.remove_many()`, through `ui/favorite.py`'s `use_forget()`), with the star's "Removed from My schedule" and Undo, which saves them all again; no dialog, since it is reversible (D-29). It is what lets the notice go when the log has no record. | Rejected: a second list of the dropped sessions on My schedule, which would duplicate Schedule changes' removed items; a link alone, which leads nowhere once the log has trimmed the removal, and leaves the notice for good; forgetting dropped ids automatically, which D-172 rules out (a later feed may list them again). Tested in `tests/test_my_schedule.py` with saved ids partly absent from the 2026 feed. |
| D-193 | *(Each type's first rows and its "Show all" row added by D-218; the field's text, Clear and count line are `ui/fields.py`'s, D-226; Focus on Back (S4) replaced by D-330; the Rooms scope, its hint and its results became Places by D-351; the debounce's no-op cleanup dropped by D-387.)* **Global search: a pinned pill, a debounce per keystroke, a fresh search per opening, and the results' memoised path** (records D-19 and D-46 for NEW-02, SCHED-05 and SPKR-02, and ux.md §4.3; amends §4.3's `AppBar` field and section M's `core/`, `ui/` and `screens/` lists; settles S4's focus question on #97 under D-101).<br>• **Modules:** `screens/search.py` (`Search`, `scope_of()`) and `screens/search_results.py` (`ResultList`, `DayGroup`, `room_row()`, `summary()`), so each stays under about 400 lines (D-66). `ui/fields.py`'s `SearchField` is the pill both search and the Speakers filter use (D-189's field, moved; its hint now wraps to two lines only while it shows). The room count line, `session_count()`, moves from `screens/map.py` to `core/rooms.py`, since the Map and search both show it.<br>• **The field:** under an `AppBar` titled "Search" (back arrow to `search_base`, D-19, D-165), a `SearchField` pinned above the results: controlled (D-157), `autofocus`, the default text keyboard (D-450; `KeyboardType.WEB_SEARCH` before), autocorrect and suggestions off, named by its hint while empty and "Search" once it holds text, the hint by scope ("Search sessions, speakers, rooms" in All, "Search sessions", "Search speakers", "Search rooms"), so it agrees with the idle title and, shorter, is no longer cut at the largest text, a Clear button ("Clear search") that empties it at once and puts the focus back in it. Not ux.md's `AppBar(title=TextField)`: a field in the 56 dp toolbar cannot grow with the text (Flet cannot read the scale, U16), while the pill reflows, as the Speakers filter did at the largest text (D-189).<br>• **Debounce:** the field's text is state set at every keystroke; the searched query is a second state that an effect on the text sets after `DEBOUNCE_SECONDS` (0.25 s, D-46), so each keystroke restarts the wait (Flet cancels the running setup, F2), with a sync `cleanup=` so an unmount cancels it too (F2, F39). The keyboard's action key, a scope chip and Clear search at once. The action key reads Search: `SearchField` passes `text_input_action=TextInputAction.SEARCH` whenever it has an `on_submit` (U30, landed as `flet-dev/flet#7021` at the pin `22a6dcb`, adopted by #807, D-450; it read Done before). Results are `use_memo` on (index, query, scope); the index is built once per feed version (D-180).<br>• **Scopes:** All, Sessions, Speakers and Rooms as `Chip`s with their check (not colour alone, D-33), one row that scrolls sideways when wider than the screen; a chip keeps the query, searches at once and gives `selection_click()` (ux.md §4.5). One scope is always on, so the chips use `on_click` with `selected` set by the screen: `on_select` toggles `selected` on the Dart side and sends it before the event (`chip.dart`), so a tap on the selected chip, which changes no state and so patches nothing back, left no chip selected on screen. The start scope is `scope_of(?scope=, ViewMemory.search_scope)`, decided and recorded once in the `use_state` initialiser: the opener's, All for an unknown one (D-180), the remembered one for a View that mounts without a query (a deep entry below a result).<br>• **Query and Back:** a result pushes `/search/<kind>/<id>` (D-141); the Search View stays mounted below it (the Router keeps the stack), so Back shows the same field, results and scroll, with no new search. `ViewMemory` gains `search_query` and `search_scope` (D-18; architecture §6.3), written by the handlers, for a View rebuilt below a result. `search_button()` clears `search_query`: each opening starts an empty search, scoped to the opener; recent queries stay a *(could)*.<br>• **Results:** sessions by day (`DaySessions`, D-185) as `SessionRow`s with the star, notices as `NoticeRow`s (D-184), then `SpeakerRow`s (D-189), then rooms as `LinkTile`s with their count (the Map's row without "Now:", D-188), in `core.search`'s order. Headings carry a level (F40): when the results hold more than one type, "Sessions", "Speakers" and "Rooms" at level 2, without counts (the count line says them), and the days at 3; with one type, no type heading (the count line already says "7 sessions") and the days at 2. A type heading is a step above a day's on screen too: `SectionHeader(theme_style=TITLE_MEDIUM, color=ON_SURFACE)` against the days' `TITLE_SMALL` in `primary`, a `Divider` before every type but the first, and the first day right under it (`top=0`). `ResultList` is memoised and each day is a memoised item keyed by its date given only its saved ids (D-153), so a keystroke before the pause re-renders no row and a star one row. A polite live region under the chips says what was found ("12 sessions, 3 speakers, 1 room", `search.count`).<br>• **States:** before a word, "Find sessions, speakers and rooms" (by scope) with "Search covers all days and ignores your filters." (`search.idle`); a miss, "No results for “…”" with the same sentence (ux.md §4.6), and, in a narrower scope that All would answer, "Show all results" (`search.no_match`), which goes away when used and so, as Clear, puts the focus back in the field; without data, the strip and the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again (D-182).<br>• **Focus on Back (S4):** Flutter's route focus restoration stands. The field gets the focus back only if it had it when the result opened; after the keyboard's action key (which unfocuses) nothing comes back. No `blur()` exists (the Dart `TextField` reads a `blur` property the Python control lacks), and moving the focus elsewhere would be a hack for a standard behaviour: the first Android Back then closes the keyboard, as for any focused field. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps): Search opened from the Schedule with Sessions selected and the field focused; typing "typing" showed the idle hint until the pause, then "7 sessions" by day; a result pushed its detail and the back arrow returned to the same results with the query and the field's focus; All kept the query; "chen" in All showed sessions by day and speakers under their type headings; Speakers showed the two rows under "2 speakers" with no type heading; Rooms gave the miss with "Show all results", which switched to All; a speaker result pushed `/search/speaker/120` and Back returned; Back from search landed on the Schedule; Clear emptied the field and kept the focus in it; light and dark. At the largest Larger Text the first build's wrapped chips took three rows and, with the keyboard up, the header overflowed by 10 px; with one sideways row nothing overflowed, and with the hint wrapping only while it shows the field shrank to one line once it held text. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): the field took the focus and the keyboard on open; `uiautomator dump` showed `search.field` as the `EditText`, each chip a `Button` named by its label with All `selected`, `search.count` read "2 sessions, 2 speakers", each session row one `Button` read as its phrase (`search.session.15`) with its star a `Switch` (`search.favorite.15`), and each speaker row a `Button`; after the keyboard's action key a result opened and the Back key returned to the results with "chen" kept, the field unfocused and no keyboard (`mInputShown=false`); dark at 200 % reflowed with the chips scrolling sideways. A keystroke, measured with a throwaway build that logged the debounced search and its patch (D-159's method; the host was loaded, load average 14 to 30 and `Feed processed in 128 ms` against S2's 66): one search per pause, never per keystroke; `core.search` a median 1.0 ms (0.3 to 6.2) over 17 queries and the index 3.3 ms once per version; handler to patch a median 95 ms, from 7 to 57 ms for queries that find a few rows up to 240 to 720 ms for one or two letters that find most of the feed ("p": 326 sessions), the first render of their rows (#216). A real Flet session under the real Router (`tests/test_search_screen.py`) sees no search before the pause, one after it, no row re-rendered by a keystroke before it, and the same View, query and results after Back; the debounce test fails with the search taken from the typed text. After the review (a rebuilt build on both devices, checked against `build/python-app`): "chen" and "grand" in All show "Sessions" (`TITLE_MEDIUM`, `on_surface`) with "Thursday, May 14" close under it in `primary`, and a divider before "Speakers" or "Rooms"; the Rooms scope lists "Grand Ballroom A" and "B" with "26 sessions"; a tap on the selected chip keeps it selected (with `on_select` it showed none); "Show all results" left the cursor in the field; the Sessions scope opens with "Search sessions" over "Find sessions". At the largest Larger Text even "Search sessions" takes two lines, now whole rather than cut ("Search sessions, s…"), and the Speakers filter looks as before. |
| D-330 | *(Amended by D-438 for the scan screen only: its badge field never loses `can_request_focus`.)* **A `SearchField` can take the focus only while its View is the top one, so Back from a result never brings the keyboard back** (settles #344; replaces D-193's "Focus on Back (S4)"; amends D-226's hook and ux.md §4.3).<br>• **The cause:** on Android the Back key closes the keyboard but leaves the field focused, as in any Flutter app. Opening a result pushes a route; when Back pops it, Flutter gives the route below the focus it last had (`_ModalScopeState` calls `setFirstFocus` on the route's `FocusScopeNode`, which re-focuses the last entry of its focus history, `routes.dart:1136-1138, 1165-1168`, `focus_manager.dart:1452-1469, 1503-1524` at Flutter 3.47.7), and the focused field opens the keyboard again over the results. `autofocus` played no part: Flet keeps the Search View mounted (D-193), so the field is not built again.<br>• **The fix:** `use_field_query()` returns `focusable`, true only while `is_top_view(page.route, use_view_path())` (or outside a Router), and `SearchField(focusable=)` passes it as the `TextField`'s `can_request_focus`. Flet's `TextField` hands it to Flutter's (`textfield.dart:327` at the pin), which sets it on the field's focus node at each update (`text_field.dart:1299, 1318`), and the node's `canRequestFocus` setter unfocuses a focused node and takes it out of its scope's focus history (`focus_manager.dart:549-559, 949-959`), and a scope skips an entry that cannot take the focus (`:1506-1509`). So the field lets the focus go as a result opens and Back returns with nothing to restore; a tap focuses it again. A Search View mounted below a deep-linked result keeps `autofocus`, which takes nothing there (`_doRequestFocus` returns for a node that cannot take the focus, `:1178-1183`), so it does not open with the keyboard on Back either. Every `SearchField` gets it from the shared hook: Speakers, Sprints and Jobs had the same path through a speaker, a sprint or a sponsor.<br>• Not chosen: moving the focus to another control before a result opens (a hack around one screen, D-193); a `blur()`, which Flet's Python `TextField` lacks although the Dart control reads a `blur` property (`textfield.dart:265-273`). | In real Flet sessions under the real Router (`tests/test_search_screen.py`): the field is focusable on `/search`, not while a result covers it, and focusable again after Back, with no focus request on Back; a Search View mounted below a deep-linked result keeps `autofocus` but cannot take the focus. Both tests fail with `focusable` always true. On an iOS 26.5 simulator of #344's own, driven by a Maestro flow (Search from Now, "pathlib" typed, the result tapped with the keyboard up, the app bar's Back): a build of `main` showed the keyboard over the results after Back in 4 runs out of 4 (Maestro's hierarchy held the keyboard's keys); the fix's build in 4 out of 4 returned to the results with the query and no keyboard, and a tap on the field brought the keyboard back. Each app bundle was checked for the change (`fields.pyc`). Android (the issue's Back-key path) was not checked on a device: the Android 16 emulator's system server died again and again under the host's memory pressure (swap 26 of 27 GB), during the install and after it; the cause in Flutter is the same on both platforms. |

It reuses the Schedule's `schedule_days.day_tab()` (which gains `screen=`, for its identifier) and `TAB_MS` (moved there from `screens/schedule.py`, so no screen imports another), and `ui/scrolling`'s `jump_to_key()`, `FULL_LIST_EXTENT`, `JUMP_MS` and `SCROLL_EVENT_MS` (D-192), and My schedule's `TimeSlot` (#209).<br>• **Rows without the room:** `session_view()`, `session_views()` and `use_session_views()` take `in_room`, the room's slug: a place whose slug is the room's is left out of `SessionView.place`, so the meta shows the time alone and `spoken_phrase()` / `notice_phrase()` say no place, both from one field.
