In 2026 the rooms list 348 rows: 308 lose the room's name, 16 keep a place elsewhere, and 24 generic breaks had none.<br>• **Tabs:** one per day the room has sessions (`by_day()`, conference-local), the Schedule's tab ("Fri 15", read "Friday, May 15", today's with its dot and ", today", 56 dp), `room.day_tab.<n>`; the room opens on the highlighted session's day, else today (ROOM-02's "today preselected"), else its first day. The picked day is component state set by `on_change`, so a re-render never moves the tab back. All days render at once: a room's day holds a few dozen rows at most (D-156's deferral is the Schedule's).<br>• **Now in this room:** `rooms.room_now(data, slug, now)`, the room's `RoomEntry.on_now` (Now & Next's rule, D-179: the Map, room detail and Now agree), shown by `NowInRoom`, a leaf component on `use_minute()` (D-175), so the fake clock drives it and the screen does not re-render each minute. A ~~level-2~~ level-3 (D-211) `SectionHeader` (F40) with Now's `live` dot (D-190) over one `LinkTile` to the session that started last: its name in a `Text` cut at three lines (`LinkTile.title` now takes a `Text`), its time in `Tokens.live`, its place only when given elsewhere (time and place two texts in a wrapping row, as a row's meta, D-184), "and 1 more" on a line of its own (D-188's form), then a divider that closes the section (only while something is on, so none sits under the tab bar otherwise); `room.now`. It heads **today's list**, not the page above the tabs as ux.md §5.9 drew it: there, at the largest Larger Text, the header and a three-line title filled half the screen and left the slots a quarter; in the list it scrolls away with them, and "now" is always today. It is a pointer, not a `SessionRow`: the session's row, with its star, is below it.<br>• **Highlight:** `highlight_of()` takes `?highlight=` from `use_query()` (already unquoted) and keeps it only when it is one of `Room.session_ids`, compared as a string; another room's id or a hand-made one is ignored. The lit box is `Container(~~key=ScrollKey("session-<id>"),~~ bgcolor=...)` (its `ScrollKey` on an empty anchor inside it, D-242) around the whole time slot, its gutter's time too and edge to edge as an M3 selected list item, its divider above the box, as the Schedule's slots (§5.2), when the row has the slot to itself, and around the row alone when it shares it (Room 102C's two 2:00 PM sessions): a row-only tint started at the gutter's edge and left the slot's time unlit, beside the inset Break card; lit with `primary` at 16 % over the surface, not `primary_container`: dark `primary_container` (`#3B3EA9`) puts `on_surface_variant` at 3.7:1, while the tint keeps it at 5.0:1 (light, `#E0E0F1`) and 5.9:1 (dark, `#2E2E38`), close to `primary_container` in light (a test). After a round trip (D-156), the shown day's list, built in full (`cache_extent` 100,000, D-154; `build_controls_on_demand=False` would not register a far key, F32), scrolls the row to its top with `jump_to_key()`, 400 ms or a jump under reduced motion, confirmed by the scroll's end and asked up to 3 times (D-155). It stays lit 2 s once in place (after the scroll's end, or from the start when the list stayed still), then fades in 250 ms, or simply goes under reduced motion (D-173).<br>• **Once per View:** the effect runs at mount; Back finds the View mounted and the query dropped (architecture §5.2), so nothing re-runs. `ViewMemory.room_highlights` maps the View's path to the session it lit; the effect's cleanup removes it when the View leaves (Back), so the next room tile highlights again, while a View rebuilt in a new session (D-143; the old one never unmounts, F30) finds it and neither scrolls nor lights up, still opening on the session's day. | A room's rows under its own name said it twice and doubled a row's height at 200 % (#101's comment); one field for display and phrase keeps them in step. Tachyon: … 3:15 to 3:45 PM. Pablo Galindo Salgado, Laszlo Kiss Kollar." (no room), `room.session.173` with "5:45 to 6:45 PM, Pacific Ballroom - Arena", and `room.now` as one button "Python for Humans - … / 10:30 to 11:00 AM"; dark at 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped. |
| D-346 | **Room detail's `LocationCard` heads each day's list, before today's "Now in this room", and its pin pulses once per room View** (records ux.md §5.9's place for the card, ROOM-04, #389; amends D-194's list heads; adds `ui/location_card.py` to section M).<br>• **Where:** at the head of every day's list, as D-194 put "Now in this room" (`room.list_head()`), and above a sprint-only room's state; a room without a pin ("Elsewhere", "Lobby") shows nothing in its place and no gap. Above the day tabs, the first build's place, the card at iOS's largest text size (AX5, iPhone 17) was about 520 of the 820 dp under the app bar, leaving the slots about a quarter of the screen (#389's screenshots); in the list it scrolls away with the slots, which then keep the screen.<br>• **The pulse:** each list builds its own card (a control has one parent), so the room keeps a set of the pins already pulsed on its View (`LocationCard(seen=)`, a `use_memo` set): the shown day's card (`LocationCard(active=)`; ~~the first card to mount~~, #602: all days mount at once, so a hidden Friday card claimed the pin first) pulses twice and adds its pin before any await, and the cards of the other days, hidden or shown later by a tab change, or one rebuilt as its list scrolls back, stay still. Back from the floor viewer finds the room mounted, so a return never pulses (D-18); reduced motion leaves the pin still (D-34, D-173).<br>• **The card** (ux.md §3.9): one button node, "Show Grand Ballroom A on the Upper level floor plan", `room.location`, the thumbnail and words excluded; at large text (`Prefs.time_above`, D-216) the words go under the thumbnail. A tap pushes `…/plan/<plan>?pin=<key>` (#388). | Reversible. Ionic put its map card above the room's sessions; the issue (#389) asked for this check and this fallback. A pin pulsing again on each tab would be repeating motion (D-34). |
| D-199 | *(The rows of tracks with a page of their own amended by D-205 and D-207; the Keynotes row's count by D-217; the track page's title at large text by D-328.)* **The Tracks hub and the track pages: slugs, legends and "New track" are year data; the hub lists session types with their counts; a track's name heads its page** (records SCHED-21, SCHED-22, EVT-04, DATA-29 and SHELL-10, and D-15's Programme for tracks; amends ux.md §3.4's New track, §5.10 and §4.6, architecture §8.1's schema and section M's `screens/` and `ui/` lists; #102).<br>• **Year data:** each `[[tracks]]` entry of `conference.toml` gains `slug` (the page's segment, written out: `talks`, `ai`, `charlas`, `lightning-talks`, `sponsor-presentations`, `open-spaces`, …, never derived from the key as Ionic's `ais` and `lightning-talkss` were), `legend` (one line on what its sessions are, the hub's and the page's legend: EVT-04's Session Types page as data) and an optional `new` (2026: AI and Security, Ionic's "New track!"). Both years have them, and every track has an icon of its own (Events `CELEBRATION_OUTLINED`, no longer Summits' `GROUPS_OUTLINED`; a test checks), since the hub shows them side by side. The loader refuses a slug that is not lower-case words joined by single `-`, a duplicate slug and an empty legend; `ConferenceInfo.track_for_slug()` finds a track by its slug, and a track the registry lacks gets no slug, so no page.<br>• **Hub** (`screens/tracks.py`, `/more/tracks`): the registry's tracks under level-2 headings for its groups, Presentations (`talks`), Hands-on and Community; the `logistics` group (breaks, notices) is not a kind of session to choose and is left out, as is a track without sessions in the current feed, which would open an empty page. A row is built like `SpeakerRow` (D-189), one phrase and a button ("Charlas. Talks given in Spanish. Charla, 16 sessions.", `tracks.track.<slug>`): a 40 dp circle in the track's badge colours with its registry icon, so the hub is also the key to the badges' colours; the plural name; the legend; then the track's badge, "New track" and the count, in one wrapping row; a badge that repeats the name is not read twice ("Lightning talks. Five-minute talks, back to back. 4 sessions."). "New track" (`ui/badges.py`'s `NEW_TRACK`) is outlined in `primary` (`Tokens.new_track`, contrast-tested on every neutral surface), as Registration required is in `error`: a status mark, not one more tonal category. The count is what the page lists (`track_sessions()`: 25 posters, not the Posters row; every open space, whatever the filter). Every row pushes `/more/tracks/<slug>`, Keynotes, Lightning talks and Open spaces included: ~~their own pages (#104, #105) point their rows at themselves when they land~~ the route table opens their own pages at those slugs (D-207), and #106 adds a Sprints row; until then a row that opened a stub page would be a dead end. Without data the skeleton ("Loading tracks"), then "Can't load the tracks" with Try again; a feed without sessions, "No sessions announced yet".<br>• **Track page** (`screens/track.py`, `/more/tracks/<slug>`; `TrackPage` reads the route, `TrackDetail(slug)` is the screen): the intro holds the badges every row would repeat, the track's and, where every session is in Spanish (Charlas), "En Español" (`shared_badges()`), then "New track"; then the plural name as the page's level-1 heading in `HEADLINE_SMALL` (`track.title`), then the legend; the app bar's title stays empty, as session detail's (D-186). ux.md §5.10 put the name in the app bar, where at the largest Larger Text it read "Trailblazing Python Se…". Then a lazy list of days, each one memoised item under a level-2 day heading (F40) with `SessionRow`s and their stars (`track.session.<key>`, `track.favorite.<key>`), no paging, which leave out the intro's badges (`DayGroup`/`SessionRow`/`badge_specs()`'s `omit`) and keep "Registration required"; the Schedule's filter never applies (D-20). An unknown slug shows "This track isn't in the current schedule" (`LABEL_OFF_OUTLINED`, `track.not_found`) under the back arrow with "All tracks", never a redirect (D-186); a registry track without sessions in the feed says "No sessions in this track yet". "Add all to My schedule" is #103's.<br>• **One day item for search and the track pages:** search's memoised `DayGroup` moves to `ui/day_groups.py` with a `screen` argument for its rows' identifiers (D-153, D-193), so both lists re-render one day and one row on a star tap. | Slugs, legends and the "New" flag change with the year, so they are data (D-45), and a slug written out is the one obvious way to a clean URL (DATA-29). The review of #232 measured `secondary_container`, ux.md §3.4's first choice for "New track", at a CIE76 distance of 6.8 from the En Español fill in light (the palette keeps 10) and at a CIELAB chroma of 66 in dark against the tracks' 22, the loudest thing on the hub; an outline in `primary` reads as a mark on a category instead. Rows that repeated the page's badge spent a pill line, and a screen reader's sentence, per row on what the intro said, which D-186 already avoids on session detail. One list that names each kind, shows its badge and counts its sessions answers both "what is a charla?" and "show me the charlas", which Ionic split between a static legend and nine menu links. In a real Flet session (`tests/test_tracks.py`) a star tap re-renders one day body and one row, and fails with `ft.memo` removed from `DayGroup`. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`, an iPhone 17 of #102's own, light and dark): the hub listed 13 tracks under three headings with their circles, badges, "New track" on AI and Security only, and counts (Posters 25, Open spaces 123); `/more/tracks/ai` showed the badges, the name, the legend and Friday's four AI talks with stars; `/more/tracks/posters` the 25 posters one by one; `/more/tracks/ais` the not-found state. At the largest Larger Text (about 310 %) every row reflowed with nothing clipped; the page's name wraps where the app bar cut it, though at that size only "Trailblazing" breaks inside the word, as session detail's long words do (D-186). On the shared simulator, driven by taps: More › Tracks & session types › Tutorials › a tutorial pushed its detail, Back returned to the track page, a star filled and unfilled, Back returned to the hub at the same scroll and Back again to More. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read each hub row as one clickable `Button`, `resource-id` `tracks.track.<slug>`, described by its phrase ("Future of AI with Python. A dedicated track on AI: machine learning, LLMs and more. AI, New track, 8 sessions."), and the group heading as a node of its own; a tap on Charlas pushed its page (`track.title` "Charlas", each row a `Button` `track.session.<id>` with its star a `Switch` "Save to My schedule"); a row pushed its session, and the Back key returned to the track page, then to the hub. Dark at 200 % text wrapped the legends, badges, counts and the track's two-line name with nothing clipped. After #232's review (debug and release builds checked against `build/python-app` again): "New track" read as an outlined mark in light and dark on both platforms; the Charlas page showed "Charla" and "En Español" once, in the intro, and rows without badges; the tutorials kept "Registration required"; `uiautomator dump` read the AI rows' phrases without the "AI." sentence, "Lightning talks. Five-minute talks, back to back. 4 sessions." and Events with its own icon under Community. |
| D-202 | *(The hub's Sprints row pushes `/more/tracks/sprints`, D-207; the filter's text, Clear and count line are `ui/fields.py`'s, D-226; the logo tile is `ui/logos.py`'s `logo_tile()`, D-262.)* **The Sprints page: projects by folded name, a filter over text built in processing, cards that cut a long description with "More", the sprint pages as year data, and a Sprints row on the Tracks hub** (records EVT-05..08 and ux.md §5.11's Sprints; amends D-140's sprint text, ux.md §5.10's Sprints row and section M's `core/` and `screens/` lists; #106).<br>• **Data:** `feed.process()` gives each `Sprint` its `description_text` (`html_md.to_text()`, the card's flat preview) and a `search_text`, `fold(name + submitter + description_text)`, once per feed version, as sessions have theirs (D-130, D-140): the filter never sees markup, and no screen converts. D-140 had left the sprints' text for P2's search; the page's filter needs it now.<br>• **Query** (`core/sprints.py`, pure): `projects(data)` by folded name, then id (D-46's order for names: the feed puts `aio-libs`, `conda` and `hatch` after `Tiny Hub Energy`); `filter_projects(listed, query)` keeps a project when every folded word of the query is a substring of its `search_text` (EVT-06's AND, D-180's matching), and returns `listed` itself for a filter with no word, so the screen knows no filter applies; `cut_short(sprint)`.<br>• **Year data:** `conference.toml` gains the optional `sprints_url` (the site's Development Sprints page) and `sprint_submit_url` (the project form), checked as https URLs like `registration_url`; 2026 has both (Ionic's two pages, both answering on us.pycon.org on 2026-10-13), 2025 neither. `ConferenceInfo.first_sprint_day` is the day after `last_talk_day`, or None when `last_day` is the last talk day: the sprints' days are year data (the feed has no sessions for them, D-126), and Now's key dates use it too. `timefmt.day_range_label()` ("Mon, May 18 – Tue, May 19") moves there from `now_header.py`, which used it privately.<br>• **Screen** (`screens/sprints.py`; the card `screens/sprint_card.py`, so each stays under D-66's 400 lines): an overflow (`sprints.menu`, merged into its button by `named()`, D-190) with "Sprint info on us.pycon.org" and "Submit a sprint project", links read "…, Opens in browser", as session detail's (D-186), each only when the year has its URL; the Speakers list's pinned `SearchField` ("Filter projects") and live count (D-189), the field only when there are projects to filter; then the list: the intro and the days (`sprints.dates`), a level-2 "Projects" heading and a `Card(OUTLINED)` per project, a memoised component keyed by id (D-153), whose name is a level-3 heading (`sprints.project.<id>`, F40) so a screen reader goes card by card.<br>• **The logo** sits on a white 56 dp tile with a hairline `outline_variant` edge, in both schemes: logos are drawn for a white page (dark marks on a transparent ground vanish on the dark surface), as the sponsor spotlight's tile (ux.md §5.1). Without a logo, or when it cannot load (offline), the initials stand in, in the brand indigo (8.6:1 on white), inside a `CircleAvatar`, which does not scale its text (`MediaQuery.withNoTextScaling`), so two letters fit the tile at any text size; a `Text` grew past it.<br>• **"More":** Flet cannot say whether a text overflows its lines, so `cut_short()` decides by the text: a description over `PREVIEW_CHARS` (160, about four lines on a phone at 100 %), or whose Markdown has a line break or a link, shows its text cut at four lines with "More"; a shorter one is never cut, so it shows whole at any size. "More" (`sprints.more.<id>`, read "More about <name>" or "Less about <name>", the visible word first so voice control finds it (WCAG 2.5.3), `Semantics(expanded=)`) swaps the preview for the description's selectable Markdown (`feed_markdown(inset=0)`), whose links open by D-197's rule, and becomes "Less"; the preview takes the Markdown body's size and line height (`BODY_LARGE`, 1.5), so expanding continues the same text. 5 of 2026's 24 projects show whole.<br>• **"Project page"** (`sprints.project_page.<id>`, a link read "<name> project page, Opens in browser") opens `feed_link(url, site_url)` in the in-app browser: three 2026 projects write their site without a scheme (`documentdb.io`), which D-197's rule completes; a URL that names no web page leaves the card without the button.<br>• **States:** no projects: the intro, then "No sprint projects yet" with "Submit a project" (the form, when the year has one) and no field; a miss: "No projects match “…”" with "Clear filter", which puts the focus back in the field; without data, the skeleton ("Loading sprint projects"), then "Can't load the sprints" with Try again. A refresh is the `Conference` store's, which never wipes the cache (EVT-07, D-145); Ionic's "keep the old list when a new emission is empty" has nothing to guard here, since the store never publishes an empty interim.<br>• **Entry points:** the Tracks hub's Community section ends with a Sprints row built like a track's (`tracks.sprints`, ux.md §5.10; it pushes the page under the hub, `/more/tracks/sprints`, D-207): `ROCKET_LAUNCH_OUTLINED` in a neutral circle (the sprints have no badge colours to be the key to), "Sprints", a legend, and "May 18–19 · 24 projects"; a Community heading heads it even in a year whose community tracks have no sessions. `screens/tracks.py`'s rows share `hub_tile()`. The Schedule's sprint-day "See the sprints", Now's Sprints row and More's Sprints row already push `/more/sprints`, which now shows the page. | One query module per list (D-185, D-189), with the text built where every other searchable text is, keeps the screen to drawing. A rule by length is the one honest test Flet allows, and leaving short texts uncut means no text is ever lost at 200 %; the only cost is a "More" on a text that would have fitted, which still adds its links. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`, an iPhone 17 of #106's own, light and dark): the page listed the 24 projects by name with their logos on the white tiles (in dark too, where a transparent logo stays legible), the days and the cut descriptions; at the largest Larger Text the field and the intro reflowed with nothing clipped (the cards at large text were checked on Android at 200 %, below). On the shared simulator, driven by taps: "More" on aio-libs showed its Markdown list, code span and links in the preview's size, a tap on "sprint board" opened GitHub in the in-app Safari view and its close returned to the page; "python web" kept five projects with "5 projects" under the field and the list where it was; "zzz" gave the no-match state, whose Clear filter brought every card back; the menu opened us.pycon.org's Development Sprints page in the in-app browser; the Tracks hub ended Community with the Sprints row, which pushed the page. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read the field as `sprints.filter`, `sprints.dates` "Sprints: Monday, May 18 to Tuesday, May 19", each name `sprints.project.<id>`, "More" a 48 dp `Button` "More about …", "Project page" a `Button` "… project page, Opens in browser", the hub row a `Button` `tracks.sprints` read as its phrase, and, once `named()` wrapped it (D-190; session detail's `session.overflow`, still bare, dumped as a `View` "More options" over an empty `Button`: #252), the menu one `Button` `sprints.menu` "More options" whose two items read "…, Opens in browser"; as one card node it had read "by Nick" and the text before the name, hence `semantic_container=False`; dark at 200 % text reflowed the cards, "More" and "Project page" on one line, and CUDA Python showed "CP" on its tile. Back from the page opened from the hub lands on More, not the hub (a sibling page, not its child): #245. |
| D-262 | *(`fill=`, a plate as wide as its parent, and the list's plate sizes, D-279.)* **The white logo tile is `ui/logos.py`'s `logo_tile(name, logo_url, *, size, width=None)`, for the Sprints cards and every sponsor screen** (settles #374; amends D-202's logo, which the card built itself, and section M's `ui/` list).<br>• **The tile, as D-202 drew it:** white in both schemes with a 1 dp `outline_variant` edge (`EDGE`) and 12 dp corners (`CORNER`); the logo `BoxFit.CONTAIN` inside a 6 dp inset (`INSET`); the initials (`core/speakers.py`'s `initials()`) in `INK`, the brand indigo (8.6:1 on white, pinned by a test), in a `CircleAvatar` of radius half the height less the inset and the edge (`EDGE`), since Flutter's `Container` adds its border to its padding: 42 dp across on the 56 dp tile, 82 dp on the 96 dp plate (D-202's card asked for 44 and was held to 42, so nothing moved), when the URL is missing or empty and as the image's `error_content` when it cannot load (offline, a broken URL). Decorative, as every screen says the name beside it: the image sets `exclude_from_semantics=True` and the initials' `Text` `semantics_label=""`, and no `Semantics` wraps the tile, since one with `exclude_semantics=True` was an empty, focusable node on Android (F81; D-202's card had it).<br>• **The caller sizes it:** the Sprints card passes 56 (`sprint_card.LOGO`), the Sponsors list's tiles will pass 48, and sponsor detail's plate `size=96, width=240` (ux.md §6.5), whose initials keep to the height. It takes a name and a URL, not a model, so a `Sprint` and a sponsor use it alike. | One obvious way: four P2 screens (#395, #396, #398, #399) need the tile the Sprints card drew, so it is built once and its white ground, edge, ink and offline fallback cannot drift apart. A `width` serves the one wide plate ux.md names without a second builder. On the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator, Sprints was the same before and after, pixel for pixel below the status bar, in light and dark, logos and initials alike; on Android with the network off every card showed its initials; `uiautomator dump` showed no focusable node on the tile (#477). |
| D-203 | *(The bar's title without data added by D-211; the title at large text amended by D-328.)* **Speaker detail: the name heads the page, the sessions come by day before the bio, their rows name only the co-speakers, and a placeholder bio is no bio** (records ux.md §5.7's detail for SPKR-03 and SESS-12; amends §5.7's track badges and app-bar title, D-184's speaker rows and section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists; settles #99's comment under D-101).<br>• **Intro** (`screens/speaker.py`'s `intro()`): centred, the photo over the initials (`ui/rows.py`'s `speaker_avatar()` gains `radius=` and `initials_style=`: 48, a 96 dp circle, with `HEADLINE_MEDIUM` initials, named in `on_primary_container` again since a theme style brings the text theme's colour; `CircleAvatar` draws them without the text scale, so they always fit), decorative (`Semantics(exclude_semantics=True)`: the initials would be read before the name), then the name in `HEADLINE_SMALL`, centred, `selectable`, the page's level-1 heading (`speaker.title`, F40), with the app bar's title empty, as session detail's and the track pages' (D-186, D-199). No track badges: each row shows its own, and a speaker's two sessions can be of two tracks.<br>• **Sessions before the bio:** `core/speakers.py`'s pure `speaker_sessions(data, speaker_id)` gives `DaySessions` (D-185), every session that links the speaker (DATA-15), poster details included, in `directory()`'s order (the day, then `schedule_order`), so the page's first session is the Speakers list's (D-189). Each day is `ui/day_groups.py`'s memoised `DayGroup` (D-199) at level 2, `screen="speaker"` (`speaker.session.<key>`, `speaker.favorite.<key>`), so a star tap re-renders one day and one row (D-153). No "Sessions" heading above them: the day headings say what follows, and in both feeds a speaker has one or two sessions, so a heading over a day heading over one row would cost a line and a screen-reader stop.<br>• **Rows name only the co-speakers** (#99's comment): `session_view()`, `session_views()` and `use_session_views()` take `of_speaker`, the speaker's name, as `in_room` takes a room (D-194): `SessionView.speakers` becomes "With Robert Masson" (the others, in the feed's order), or empty when the speaker is alone, and `spoken_phrase()` reads that one field, so the row and its phrase never disagree. "With", so the line does not read as the session's whole cast with the page's speaker missing. Names are compared, not ids: feed processing links authors by name (D-125), and in both feeds every linked speaker's name is among its session's authors (177 links in 2025, 182 in 2026).<br>• **The bio** under `SectionHeader("About", level=2)`, through `feed_markdown()` (`speaker.bio`; D-116, D-197), and only when `has_bio()`: a letter or a digit in its text (`html_md.to_text()`). The feed writes a lone "-" for a bio nobody wrote (2025: 12 speakers; 2026: 7, among them five panellists of "Beyond the Hype"), which showed as "About" over a dash; a word such as "TBD" stays, since the app cannot tell it from a bio.<br>• **States:** a directory speaker the feed links to no session (neither recorded feed has one), "No sessions in the current schedule" (`EVENT_BUSY_OUTLINED`, `speaker.empty`) where the days would be, with no button (the app refreshes on its own, D-175) and the bio still below, as a track page without sessions (D-199); an unknown id, "This speaker isn't in the current schedule" (`PERSON_OFF_OUTLINED`) with "All speakers", under the back arrow titled "Not found" (D-186, `speaker.not_found`); without data, `NoDataState` (the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again, `speaker.no_data`) under the bar titled "Speaker" (D-211).<br>• **Back** needs nothing new: a row pushes `drill_href(view_path, "session", id)` and session detail's `SpeakerTile` the speaker (D-197), so session → speaker → session → room is five Views that `parent_of()` pops one at a time, from every root and from search (D-17, D-141; a test walks it). | The page answers "who is this and when do they speak" in the order an attendee asks it (ux.md §5.7), with the rows, star and day groups every other list uses, and nothing it repeats: the name once, not on every row (#190's review: a repeated speakers line added noise and, at 200 % text, doubled the row's height), and no section that says "-". On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; an iPhone 17 of #99's own, deep entries with `--route`): Catherine Nelson (`/now/session/110/speaker/112`) showed her photo and name, Thursday's tutorial "With Robert Masson" and Friday's panel "With Carol Willing, Jelle Zijlstra, Jodie Burchell, Laís Carvalho, Maike Scherer", then About; Justin Lee's one talk had no speakers line; Jelle Zijlstra's initials "JZ" on `primary_container`, and, once `has_bio()` was in, no "About -"; an unknown id the not-found state under "Not found"; light, dark and the largest Larger Text (about 310 %), where the name wrapped by words and the badges, titles and meta reflowed with nothing clipped apart from the titles' 3-line ellipsis (D-184), whose full text the row's phrase says; at the largest text the panel's 13-word title stopped at its third line too. On the shared simulator, driven by taps: Now › session 110 › Catherine Nelson › the tutorial › Room 101A pushed one View each; three left-edge swipes (the Cupertino slide, the page below showing through mid-swipe) and the back arrow popped room → session → speaker → session → Now, one step each; Schedule › "Python Performance Lab" › Arthur Pastel showed "With Adrien Cacciaguerra", a star tap filled the star and a second showed "Removed from My schedule" with Undo, and two back arrows returned to the Schedule at the same place. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`, gesture navigation with predictive back on): `uiautomator dump` read the name as `speaker.title`, each day heading as a node of its own, each row a `Button` (`speaker.session.106`: "Tutorial, Registration required. Going from Notebooks to Production Code. 9:00 AM to 12:30 PM, Room 101A. With Robert Masson."), each star a `Switch` and `speaker.bio` the bio, the photo no node; the same chain popped one step per Back key and per left-edge gesture, and Back on Now left the app. Dark at 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped apart from the panel's title, cut at its third line by the rows' 3-line ellipsis (D-184), whose full text the row's phrase says. |
| D-205 | *(The hub's rows amended by D-207; their list's module by D-228; the rows' thumbnail settled by D-332.)* **The Open spaces and Lightning talks pages: day tabs from the feed, the pages' copy and links as year data, and the hub's rows open them** (records EVT-01, EVT-02 and EVT-03, D-15's Programme for both and D-31 for the open spaces' slots; amends ux.md §5.10's hub rows, §5.11, §4.6 and §3.6's list tiles, architecture §8.1's schema, and section M's `screens/`, `core/`, `ui/` `year_schema.py` and `year_pages.py` entries; #105).<br>• **Year data:** `content.toml` gains two optional tables. `[open_spaces]`: `intro` and `propose_url`; `[lightning_talks]`: `heading`, `intro` (paragraphs), `signup_url`, `info_url` and `[[lightning_talks.doubts]]` (`doubt`, `answer`). 2026's are said in our own words from the site's public pages (the sign-ups `/2026/signup/open-space/` and `/2026/signup/lightning-talk/`, which ask the attendee to sign in; the info page `/2026/events/lightning-talks/`), with no exclamation marks (ux.md §4.9). The loader (`year_schema.build()`) learns a table of its own, an `X \| None` field built as its dataclass, and refuses a URL that is not `https://` with a host and an empty text. The two tables' dataclasses and these checks (`check_pages()`) are in `year_pages.py`, which `config.py` imports: with them `config.py` passed D-66's ~400 lines (428), without them it has 359. A year without them (2025) shows the pages without the action, the intro, the card and the fold.<br>• **Open spaces** (`screens/open_spaces.py`, `/more/open-spaces`): the app bar's `ADD` "Propose an open space", in the in-app browser (the link rule, INFO-14), a link read "Propose an open space, Opens in browser" with its tooltip out of the semantics (D-184); `ui/links.py`'s `external()` marks such a button as a link and names it (`open_spaces.propose`, `lightning_talks.sign_up`). A tab per day the feed has open spaces on (`track_sessions()`), the Schedule's `day_tab()` (`open_spaces.day_tab.<n>`), so the labels are dates and never Ionic's "Sunday"; the page opens on today when it has open spaces, else the first day (`opening_day()`), and keeps the picked tab across renders. Each day is a memoised lazy list (D-153) given only its own saved ids: the year's intro line, then gutter slots (`TimeSlot`) of `SessionRow`s with stars that push the session, without the "Open space" badge (D-199). The slots come from `row_views.by_slot()`, room detail's grouping by time group start moved into `core` so both share it; it returns its slots by start whatever order the views come in, since the open spaces come by the start a row shows (`schedule_order`), which a display-time override can put before an earlier slot. The intro heads each day's list rather than sitting above the tabs, where at large text it would take the room the slots need (D-194's reason). No thumbnail yet: #242 decides. The Schedule's filter never applies (D-20).<br>• **Lightning talks** (`screens/lightning_talks.py`, `/more/lightning-talks`): a `Card(FILLED)` with the copy (its heading a level-2 heading, the paragraphs in `BODY_LARGE`) and `FilledButton("Sign up for a lightning talk", icon=OPEN_IN_NEW)`, its text read "…, Opens in browser" (D-186: on the text, as an icon's label is read first); "Think you can't do one?", a `FoldTile` that starts collapsed and is controlled, so a star tap below never folds it (D-191), each doubt one node; "More about lightning talks", an external `LinkTile` with the host as its subtitle; then the talks by day (`DayGroup`, D-199) with stars, without the "Lightning talks" badge. The info comes before the talks, so the page reads as what, how to sign up, then when; ux.md had the link last.<br>• **One entry each:** More's Programme row opens these pages at `/more/lightning-talks` and `/more/open-spaces`; the Tracks hub's row (~~`tracks.OWN_PAGES`, by track key, the feed's kinds~~ ~~`config.HUB_PAGES`~~ `year_tracks.HUB_PAGES`, D-207, D-228) opens the same pages at `/more/tracks/lightning-talks` and `/more/tracks/open-spaces`, routes listed under the hub before its `:slug` route, so the View stack holds the hub and Back returns to it (D-17), and the generic track page never shows for these two slugs. Lightning talks appear once in More (a test).<br>• **Polish:** the theme's `ListTileTheme(title_alignment=TITLE_HEIGHT)` keeps a tall tile's icons beside its title's first line, the rule `LinkTile` follows (D-188), for every `ListTile` without its own alignment and for fold headers, which have no setting of their own: at 200 % the wrapped "Think you can't do one?" had its icons beside the middle of its title. The fold's title is a row's title, as the link row under it, not `TITLE_MEDIUM`. The sign-up has 10 dp above and below its label (M3's 16/24 dp at the sides), so a label wrapped at 200 % keeps clear of the pill's edge; one line stays 40 dp. The open spaces' intro is one sentence that names the action ("… or tap + to host one."). The loader also refuses an empty `doubts`.<br>• **States:** without data the open spaces show the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" (`NoDataState`), with the action still there; the lightning copy stays above the same states; a feed with no open spaces says "No open spaces yet" with "Propose one", the same sign-up as `+` and so an external link like it (`OPEN_IN_NEW`, "Propose one, Opens in browser", `open_spaces.propose_one`; `EmptyState(link_id=...)`), one with no lightning talks "No lightning talks in the schedule yet". | A tab per day the feed has is the one way to never mislabel a day (Ionic's "Sunday"), and the Schedule's tab keeps the two pages alike. The pages' words and links change with the year and come from the site, so they are year data (D-45), in our own words rather than the site's copy. One entry per page, from More and from the hub, is what SHELL-10 asked of Ionic's two lightning-talks targets. In a real Flet session (`tests/test_open_spaces.py`) a star tap re-renders one day list and one row, and fails with `ft.memo` taken off the day list. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds, the bundle checked against `build/python-app`; an iPhone 17 of #105's own, light and dark): Open spaces showed "Fri 15", "Sat 16", "Sun 17", the intro, then gutter slots of rows with stars and no badge; Lightning talks the card, the folded tile, the link and the four talks under their days. At the largest Larger Text the one-sentence intro left the first slot on the first screen, the tabs scrolled sideways, and the card's heading and text reflowed with nothing clipped. On the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps: the hub's Lightning talks row opened the page; "Think you can't do one?" opened, stayed open through a scroll, a star tap below it and a session pushed and popped; a star filled and a second tap showed "Removed from My schedule" with Undo; "More about lightning talks" and "+" opened us.pycon.org in the in-app Safari view (the open-space sign-up asks to sign in: "You must be registered for PyCon US to host an Open Space"), whose close returned to the page; "Sun 17" showed Sunday's open spaces, a row pushed its session and Back returned to Sunday. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read `open_spaces.propose` as a `Button` "Propose an open space / Opens in browser" (a link is a button to TalkBack, F41), each tab "Tab 1 of 3 / Friday, May 15", each row a `Button` read as its phrase ("Web Scraping. 10:00 to 11:00 AM, S-3.") with its star a `Switch`; a row pushed the session and the Back key returned; dark at 200 % text wrapped the card, the sign-up button's label (clear of the pill's edge), the fold's title (its icons by its first line, as the link row's) and the rows with nothing clipped. After the reviews (#248): on the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", the hub's Lightning talks row opened the page and its back arrow returned to the hub, as did the back arrow of `/more/tracks/open-spaces`; on the emulator, the Developer feed URL pointed at a local copy of the feed without open spaces showed "No open spaces yet" with "Propose one" (`OPEN_IN_NEW`), which `uiautomator` read as `open_spaces.propose_one`, a `Button` "Propose one / Opens in browser". |
| D-332 | **No thumbnail on open-space rows: the image stays on the session's detail** (settles EVT-01's optional thumbnail and ux.md §5.11's "optional 56 dp thumbnail"; amends feature-matrix EVT-01; #242). The Open spaces page keeps the Schedule's `SessionRow` (D-184), memoised as it is (D-153), with the Changed badge (D-315); no leading or trailing tile, no `OpenSpaceRow` variant. An open space's image shows only on its detail, in the 16:9 frame with its own loading and error states (SESS-11, D-206). Reversible: a row thumbnail can return as a new issue if the feed ships small thumbnails and the app caches images on disk. | The 2026 feed's 67 images (of 123 open spaces) weigh 55.8 MB: median 116 KB, 12 over 1 MB, the largest 11 MB, and a 56 dp tile downloads the whole file (`cache_width` cuts only the decoded size). A URL `ft.Image` is Flutter's `Image.network`, cached in memory only, so after a restart or offline each such row would show a blank or error tile, at the venue, where the rows are used (offline first). The pictures are mostly screenshots, flyers with text and generated covers, unreadable at 56 dp and undescribed by the feed, so decorative for screen readers. And the tile and its gap take about a quarter of the text's width beside the gutter (D-216), on every row, for 54 % of them. The original's trailing image (a third of the card) has the same costs. |
| D-207 | *(The list's module amended by D-228.)* **The Tracks hub's pages of their own also live under the hub, so Back from any page it opens returns to it** (settles #245 as option 2, D-101; amends D-199's and D-205's hub rows, D-202's Sprints row, D-17's note, ux.md §5.10 and §5.11, architecture §5.2 and §5.3, and section M's More pages; #245).<br>• **Rule:** a More page that a hub row opens is also a static child of the hub, `/more/tracks/<segment>`, listed before `:slug`, which would match it too: `/more/tracks/sprints` (Sprints), `/more/tracks/lightning-talks` and `/more/tracks/open-spaces`; #104 adds `/more/tracks/keynotes`. The Router builds the stack from the location (More, Tracks, the page), and `parent_of()` walks it, so one Back returns to the hub (D-17, D-164). Each page keeps its More entry at its own location (`/more/sprints`, …), whose Back returns to More; the other cross-links (Now's Sprints row, the Schedule's "See the sprints") switch to the More tab and keep `/more/sprints`.<br>• **One list:** ~~`config.HUB_PAGES`~~ `year_tracks.HUB_PAGES` (D-228) maps each segment to the key of the track whose page it is (the feed's kinds, DATA-14), or `None` for the sprints, which are not a track (D-126). It sits beside the registry it constrains, so the year loader stays free of Flet and dependencies keep pointing down (architecture §1): `locations.py` imports it from ~~`config.py`~~ `year_tracks.py` (D-228). `PAGES` takes the pages' locations from it, and a test keeps both equal to the route table. Every hub row pushes `/more/tracks/<slug>` and the Sprints row `/more/tracks/sprints`; `tracks.OWN_PAGES` and `hub_target()` go, since the route table picks the screen.<br>• **Loader:** `load_year()` refuses a track slug that names one of these pages unless the page is that track's (`tracks[6].slug: 'sprints' is the segment of another page`), and a track with a page of its own whose slug is not the page's segment (`expected 'lightning-talks'`): its row pushes its slug, which must be its page. | One rule for every row of the hub, decided once, rather than three behaviours: from the hub, Back from Sprints showed More (#243 on the iOS 26.5 simulator), a sibling page, not the hub's child. Two locations per page cost one line each in the table and `HUB_PAGES`; option 3 (the track page linking on to the dedicated page) would add a step to every visit. On the shared iOS 26.5 "PyCon iPhone 17" (debug build checked against `build/python-app`), driven by taps: More › Tracks & session types › Sprints, then the back arrow, showed the hub at the same scroll, and so did an edge swipe; Open spaces (edge swipe) and Lightning talks (back arrow) returned to the hub too; More › Sprints › back arrow showed More. A `--route=/more/tracks/sprints` entry on a simulator of #245's own built More, Tracks, Sprints. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` after each Back key: the hub's `tracks.*` rows after Sprints, Open spaces and Lightning talks, and `more.title` after More › Sprints. |
| D-208 | *(Its TalkBack note amended by D-225: the check, not the app.)* **Back with a dialog open closes the dialog only: every pushed View declares an `on_confirm_pop`, so Flet leaves Back to Flutter's Navigator** (fixes #235; amends how D-141's Back key reaches our handler; F46, U33).<br>• **The cause is in Flet:** at the pin, Flet takes the Android Back (key or committed gesture, and VoiceOver's escape on iOS, which Flutter also sends as `popRoute`) before Flutter's Navigator and, when the top View can pop and has no `on_confirm_pop`, pops that View itself, without looking for a dialog, picker, sheet or menu open over it; the open route goes with the View's page (F46). Our `page.on_view_pop` only followed a `view_pop` Flet had already acted on, and ignoring it there would have left Flutter without the View and Python with it.<br>• **Interim:** `AppScaffold` gives every pushed View, `can_pop` still true, an async `on_confirm_pop` that awaits `confirm_pop(True)` (`let_pop`). With a handler, Flet hands Back to `Navigator.maybePop()`, which pops the topmost route: the open dialog, picker, sheet or menu, else the View, whose pop reaches `on_view_pop` through `onDidRemovePage`, the path a committed predictive gesture and the iOS edge swipe already take (D-141, D-148). Flet calls the handler only for a pop that `can_pop` refuses, which a pushed View never does, so it lets any such pop through. Roots keep D-142: non-Now roots already have a handler, and Now is a one-View stack, where Flet always leaves Back to the Navigator. One rule for every View the app builds, and every modal route at once, Flutter's own menus included.<br>• **Not `can_pop=False` while a dialog is open:** every opener would have to report opening and closing (a `PopupMenuButton`'s menu, a picker's dismiss), a missed close would leave Back doing nothing on that View, and `can_pop=False` turns off the iOS edge swipe and the predictive animation (D-148).<br>• **Upstream (U33, #259):** #259 has the full analysis and the minimal repro (also in [flet-dev/flet#6964](https://github.com/flet-dev/flet/issues/6964)); the suggested fix: return `null` from Flet's system-pop handler while the Navigator's top route is not a page. When the pin has it, `let_pop` goes in its own PR (D-64). | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APKs, gesture navigation), a build of `main` reproduced #235: one Back key press with Developer options' "Clear the cached schedule?", the fake clock's date picker, its time picker or the Sprints page's menu open landed on More. With this change, `uiautomator dump` showed Back closing only the confirmation (the cache kept), the date picker, the time picker (the field left empty), "Reset the app?" and the menu, the View staying; a second Back popped one level; the Back gesture did the same as the key; two quick Back presses on Developer went to More and then Now, as two gestures do; on the Schedule root, Back with the filter sheet open closed the sheet alone, as on `main` (D-142's handler). A minimal app without the Router (two Views, an `AlertDialog` or a `DatePicker`) showed the same `view_pop` with the dialog's dismiss, which #259 carries (and flet-dev/flet#6964). On the shared iOS 26.5 simulator (the build checked against `build/python-app`), the edge swipe with the confirmation open closed neither it nor the View (the dialog's barrier takes the touch), and without a dialog it still popped Developer options to More. With TalkBack on (the same emulator), the Back key with the confirmation open closed only the confirmation, and TalkBack's focus went to the app bar's Back arrow rather than back to the row; closing with Cancel does no better, so that is #262, not this change (~~a defect~~ an artifact of the check, which opened the dialog with an `adb` tap: D-225). VoiceOver's escape is read from the engine source, not seen: the Simulator has no VoiceOver, so it waits for the iPhone (#261). Keeping the handler on the Views and off the openers is the one place that needs no bookkeeping, and the pin's own Navigator does the rest. |
| D-270 | **An unknown location is a catch-all route's own View, a one-View stack whose Back goes to Now; it says "This page isn't in the app" without naming the path, and a new session never returns to it** (settles #385 under D-101; amends D-164's `NotFound`, ux.md §2.4, §2.6 and §4.6, and architecture §4.1, §5.1 and §5.3; records F89).<br>• **The route:** `routes.UNKNOWN = ft.Route(path=":unknown*", component=NotFound)` is the last entry of `ROUTES`. A splat takes any number of segments, and the Router tries routes in order, so only a location no page or drill matches gets here, at any depth: `/nope`, `/now/nope`, `/now/session/42/nope`, `/search/nope` (F89). `App` passes no `not_found`: the Router's own wraps the component in `View(route=pathname, controls=[...])` (`router.py:731-741`), which sets neither `can_pop` nor `on_confirm_pop`, so after a cold link the Back key closed the app. A test keeps the catch-all last and checks that every `PAGES` path, and a drill of every kind on every page, still opens its own screens.<br>• **Back:** `NotFound` builds its View with `AppScaffold(unknown=True)`, which treats it as D-142 treats a non-Now root: `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that navigates to `/now` and awaits `confirm_pop(False)`, and the roots' shared View key `"/"`, so Now takes its place as it takes a root's (D-142). Unlike a root it has no bar (D-144) and has the Material back arrow (D-10), which goes to Now as well, not to `parent_of()`'s `/`, whose `Start` would send the user to `last_location`. On iOS the arrow is the visible Back: a one-View stack has no edge swipe (F89), as on the other roots. VoiceOver's escape is a `popRoute`, which reaches `on_confirm_pop` on a one-View stack (F46); read from the source, not heard (#261).<br>• **Never recorded:** `AppScaffold` leaves `ViewMemory.last_location` alone for it, so a new session in the process (F26, F29) returns to the last real page (D-143).<br>• **Content**, as the details' not-found Views (D-186, D-203): "Not found" in the bar, the page's level-1 heading (D-211), then `EmptyState` "This page isn't in the app" / "The link may be old or mistyped." (`LINK_OFF_OUTLINED`, `app.not_found`) with "Go to Now". Like the details' titles, the state's title says something the bar does not: a screen reader reads "Not found", then a sentence, not the same words twice (#486's review). The path is never shown: it means nothing to the person who tapped the link, and the old "Nothing in the app is at /x." put any text a link carried on screen. Back target: Now, D-142's rule (#385's comment). | On `main` (`2ae7957`), on my Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) a cold start with `--es route /nope` showed the bare page with no app bar, "Nothing in the app is at /nope.", and one Back key press left the app (the launcher resumed); on an iPhone 17 simulator of #385's own (iOS 26.5, debug build, `--route=/nope`) the same page had no app bar and no arrow, and a left-edge swipe (Maestro) did nothing. With this change (the APK's code checked by its file list and `routes.pyc`; the iOS bundle by `diff` against `build/python-app`): on Android `/nope`, `/now/session/42/nope` and `/search/nope` showed "Not found" over the state, `uiautomator dump` read the arrow as a `Button` "Back", the title "Not found", `app.not_found` with the state's title a node of its own inside it and "Go to Now" a `Button`; the Back key, the left-edge predictive gesture (gesture navigation), the arrow and "Go to Now" each landed on Now with the app still resumed, also with TalkBack on; `/schedule/session/42` and `/more/tracks/ai` still opened their screens and Back from the session went to Schedule. On iOS a Maestro flow saw the texts and no `nope`; the edge swipe left the page as it was, while the same swipe on `/now/session/42` popped to Now (the control); the arrow and "Go to Now" went to Now. Dark at 200 % (Android) and dark at the largest accessibility size (iOS) wrapped with nothing clipped. The review round (#486) gave the state a title of its own: rebuilt (the APK's `routes.pyc` and the iOS bundle checked as above), `/nope` showed "This page isn't in the app" / "The link may be old or mistyped." under "Not found" on both, `uiautomator dump` read the title and the body as nodes of their own inside `app.not_found`, "Go to Now" landed on Now, and light, dark at 200 % (Android) and dark at the largest size (iOS) wrapped with nothing clipped. |
| D-291 | **Deep links land on their target with the root below, cold and warm; a route change first closes the open dialogs and sheets; a link to search records where it landed** (settles #439, `P2-79`, under D-101; records ux.md §2.6's landing and architecture §5.6; F109, U89 #516).<br>• **Landing:** a link's path is the start route (cold, the boot gate included) or a route change (warm, F31), and the Router builds the stack from it (D-21, D-141): `/schedule/session/<id>` is `[Schedule, Session]`, Back pops one View per press to the root, the root goes to Now (D-142) and Now leaves the app. An unknown id shows its detail's not-found state over its root (D-186); an unknown path is D-270's one View. A link after Back on Now (F29) starts a new session at its own route, never through `Start` (D-143). Links only show screens: the query is a view option of the top View (`use_query()`, D-164), never an action. `tests/test_deep_links.py` opens every `PAGES` path and a session on every tab root, two More pages and search as a start route, quoted ids included, and walks Back to the exit; warm links from six places, search among them, build the same stacks.<br>• **Modal routes:** the open dialogs and sheets close before the new location shows. `ui/modals.py`'s `use_modals_close_on_route_change()`, which `AppScaffold` calls, puts `close_then()` in front of the Router's own `page.on_route_change` once (Flet runs a parent's mount effects before its children's, so a screen's effect finds the Router's handler). With nothing open it hands the change straight on; otherwise it sets `open = False` on each open dialog and sheet in the page's dialog list (`page._dialogs`, the only list, U89) and waits until Flet's dismissal wrapper has removed them (a look every 50 ms, at most `CLOSE_WAIT`, 1 s), then hands the change on; changes queue behind a lock, so two quick links keep their order. It holds for every route change, since the Router does not chain `page.on_route_change` (`router.py:660-667`) and Flet does not say where a change came from; in-app navigations meet an open modal only when the modal navigates itself.<br>• **Exceptions, each with a test (`tests/test_modals.py`):** SnackBars and Banners stay open (`KEPT`): messages over every page, not routes, so an Undo outlives a link (ux.md §4.4) and P2's reminder Banner (`P2-90`) will too. Date, time and date-range pickers are left as they are (`UNCLOSABLE`): Flet has no close path for them (F109), so waiting would only delay the link; a picker goes with its View, and Back closes it. A popup menu stays as Flutter leaves it: Python has no handle on it (U89); Back closes it (D-208).<br>• **Search:** a link to `/search` has no Search button to record its opener, so Back from it went to whichever page last opened search. `screens/search.py`'s `record_opening()` runs once as a Search View mounts: when the last location seen is not under `/search`, it becomes `search_base`, and a Search View that is the top View starts with an empty query; a View built below a result keeps the remembered query (D-193). The button records the same before it navigates.<br>• **First run:** until Welcome lands (#423, `P2-63`), a first-run link opens its target like any link; #423 records that Welcome waits for the next plain start.<br>• **Not chosen:** closing after the change, from the new top View's effect: Flet draws the dialogs in the new View without their route, so the close closes nothing (F109); `page.pop_dialog()` in a loop, which closes the top dialog, a SnackBar included (D-251); `can_pop=False` while a modal is open, which every opener would have to report (D-208's reason). | On my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, release arm64 APKs whose `app.zip` held this branch's bytecode): on `main`'s build a warm `pyconus://app/now` with the Schedule's filter sheet open showed Now under the sheet, `pyconus://app/schedule/session/31` showed the session under it, and the same link with session detail's menu open on `/schedule/session/105` showed the new session under the menu (F109); a warm `/search?scope=rooms` from the Map, after a search opened from the Schedule, went Back to the Schedule. With this change: cold `pyconus://app/schedule/session/31` showed the session, Back went to Schedule, Now, then the launcher; the same link after that Back exit (same process, F29) and sent within a second of Python's session starting, before the first screen showed (the boot gate), landed the same way; `/now/speaker/nope` showed the not-found state over Now; the sheet, Developer's "Clear the cached schedule?" dialog and the filter sheet over a pushed session each closed before the target showed, and the sheet opened again afterwards; a date picker was left for its View to take away; "Removed from My schedule · Undo" stayed over the linked session and its Undo saved the session again; the linked search went Back to the Map; a link with search open landed with Schedule below. With TalkBack on, a link to a detail spoke the window title "Conf Companion", then focused "Back. Button" (a link builds the Views a push builds): the heading is not read first (#517). On an iPhone 17 simulator of #439's own (iOS 26.5, a debug build whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), Maestro flows saw the same: cold and warm links, the unknown id, the sheet closing before Now and before a pushed session, the Undo SnackBar surviving, the linked search returning to the Map, and a link sent as Python's session started landing on the session over Schedule. |
| D-209 | *(The item's tile built by `ui/menus.py`'s `menu_item()` since D-233; the hook's module amended by D-267.)* **A track page's "Add all to My schedule": an overflow item that saves the whole track, then one SnackBar with Undo** (records SCHED-24 and ux.md §5.10's *(#103)* line; amends section M's `ui/` list; #103).<br>• **Where:** the track page's app bar (`screens/track.py`) gets an overflow, `PopupMenuButton(MORE_VERT, tooltip="More options")` (`track.menu`, merged into its node with `named()`), with one item, "Add all to My schedule" (`track.add_all`), its content a `ListTile` (the `PLAYLIST_ADD` icon as `leading`, the label as `title`, `min_height=48` and `horizontal_spacing=12` for the menu's row and gap), so the label wraps at 200 % text rather than running off the menu (F47), with the icon beside its first line by the theme's `TITLE_HEIGHT` (D-188). It is offered only where it can do something (ux.md §1, no dead control): on a page that lists sessions (not while the feed loads or after it failed, not on a track without sessions), not on the open spaces, whose slug is the Open spaces page's own route (`/more/tracks/open-spaces`, before the slug route, D-205), a page without an overflow, so no track page shows them, and not on the notices and breaks (`track.offers_add_all()`: the `logistics` group), whose rows have no star (D-31).<br>• **What it saves:** every session the page lists, `track_sessions()`'s list on every day (each poster, never the Posters row, D-199), not only what is on screen, in one `Favorites.add_many()` (D-172): one assignment, one notification, one write. No dialog first, since it is reversible (D-29). The selection click when it saved any, as a star's add gives (ux.md §4.5).<br>• **The SnackBar** (~~`ui/badges.py`'s~~ `ui/favorite.py`'s, D-267, hook `use_add_all()` and the pure `added_feedback()`; one SnackBar builder, `_show()`, for them and the star's `use_favorite_toggle()`, so floating, 4 s, and `persist` with a screen reader as D-184): "Added 16 sessions · Undo", or "Added 1 session · Undo", counting only `Change.added`, so the sessions saved before are neither counted nor removed by Undo; "All of them are already in My schedule" (`ALREADY_SAVED`) with no Undo when nothing was new, and nothing is written; "Couldn't save your change" with Undo when the write failed (ux.md §4.4). **Undo** is `Favorites.undo(change)`: the list is again what it was, sessions saved before included. It says nothing more, as a star's Undo of a removal says nothing; only a failed write speaks, "Couldn't save your change", with no Undo of the Undo (the menu offers the add again). On the iOS 26.5 simulator and the Android emulator it showed on the track page, whether the page was opened by deep link or pushed from the hub (#255; #197 tracks the pushed Views where a SnackBar was not seen). | The star's flow was the model: one store edit, one `Change`, one SnackBar. `_announce()` would answer the Undo with "Removed from My schedule · Undo" for the 16 sessions the user had just asked to drop again, a second message about the same tap; Material's Undo closes its SnackBar, and the stars on the page show the result. A count of the new ids only is what D-172's `Change` was shaped for, and an "Add all" on an empty or loading page, or on rows without stars, would be a dead control. An item in the overflow keeps the app bar to the back arrow and one icon, as session detail's overflow does (D-186), and leaves room for P2's "Add all to calendar". |
| D-217 | *(`KEYNOTE_TRACKS`' module amended by D-228; the snapshot check, which now also checks the sponsor credits in `load_year()`, by D-337; a panel's title type amended by D-229; the every-year test for a year without a snapshot by D-274.)* **Keynotes: their people and words are year data keyed by session id and checked against the bundled snapshot; a card per keynote and panel; session detail shows them** (records SESS-08 and SPKR-04; amends D-45's note, D-199's count, ux.md §5.5, §5.10 and §5.11, architecture §5.2 and §8, and section M's year modules, `core/`, `screens/` and routes; #104).<br>• **Year data** (`content.toml`'s `[[keynotes]]`, one entry per session): `session_id` (the feed's id as a string, D-42), `kind` (`keynote` or `panel`), an optional `title` (the talk's own: the feed names a keynote by its speaker) and `abstract` (Markdown), and `[[keynotes.speakers]]`: `name`, `role`, optional `photo_url` and `bio`. 2026: the four keynotes and the D&I and Steering Council panels, said in our own words, in US English, from the site's keynote-speakers page; the photos are the site's 165 px copies (`.max-165x165`), as its originals weigh up to 5.7 MB. The dataclasses and `check_keynotes()` (a known kind, one entry per session, someone on stage, no blank name or role, `https://` photos) are in `year_keynotes.py`, as the pages' are in `year_pages.py` (D-205): `config.py` had 416 lines.<br>• **Unknown session ids:** `load_year(check_sessions=True)` processes the year's bundled snapshot and refuses an entry whose session it lacks (`2026/content.toml: keynotes[0].session_id: '999' is not a session of the year's feed`), or whose session is on a track no keynote has (~~`config.KEYNOTE_TRACKS`~~ `year_tracks.KEYNOTE_TRACKS`, D-228, `Keynote` and `Plenary`: `'142' is a Talk session, not a keynote or a plenary`), since a typo that lands on a talk would put it on the page and replace its speakers on its detail; and a year with keynotes but no snapshot. A test loads every year this way (a year that bundles no snapshot, 2025, against its recorded feed, D-274), and `tools/update_snapshot.py` refuses a new feed that drops a keynote's session or moves it to another track, writing nothing. The app's own start does not ask: parsing the snapshot at every start would undo D-38's on-demand read, and at run time a keynote a newer feed drops simply has no card. A test also looks for every name and title of the year data in the package's modules: no keynote text in code.<br>• **The page** (`screens/keynotes.py`; `/more/keynotes`, and `/more/tracks/keynotes` under the hub, `HUB_PAGES["keynotes"] = "Keynote"`, D-207): `timeline.keynote_sessions()` lists every session of the keynotes' track and every session the year data names (the panels are plenaries in the feed), by `schedule_order`, memoised per feed. One memoised `Card(ELEVATED)` per session (`screens/keynote_card.py`, D-153), keyed by its id: a keynote's people, a 96 dp photo each (`speaker_avatar(radius=48)`, which now takes a keynote person too, decorative) over the name (`TITLE_LARGE`, a level-2 heading) and role (stacked: beside the photo, the largest text left the name a word a line); the talk's title; the bios cut at 4 lines with "Read more" / "Show less", read "Read more about <speaker>" (a panel's "about <title>"). A panel's: the feed's name (`LABEL_LARGE`, `primary`) over its own title (the heading), the abstract cut the same way, and its people as a wrapping row of 64 dp photos over their names, each name its own width so it breaks only between words. "Read more" shows for a text over 80 characters or with a paragraph break, about four lines at 200 % text, since Flet cannot say whether a text was cut (U16). Under a divider the time-and-room tile pushes the session from the page's View, beside its star. A keynote the year data lacks still gets a card, with its name. `screens/more_pages.py`, the last More stub, goes.<br>• **The hub** counts the Keynotes row as its page lists, the panels included (6 in 2026; D-199's rule), and its legend says so: "Featured talks and panels by invited speakers".<br>• **Session detail** (`screens/keynote_people.py`): a session with an entry lists the entry's people under Speakers ("Panelists" for a panel), a 48 dp photo beside the name and role, one node, then the whole bio, selectable, with no chevron (the feed links no speaker to them, so they have no page); About shows the talk's title over the abstract, which replaces the feed's description, a link to the site; without an abstract that link stays. | SESS-08's one data source, where Ionic kept two hard-coded copies matched by substring. Checking ids against the snapshot catches a typo or a dropped keynote on the Mac, in CI or in the snapshot tool, never on a phone, at no cost to the start. Counting the panels keeps D-199's promise that a row's count is its page's. On the shared iOS 26.5 "PyCon iPhone 17" (a simulator build whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), driven by taps: More › Keynotes showed the six cards in light, the photos loading over their initials; "Read more" on the Steering Council panel opened its abstract and turned into "Show less"; a card's tile opened the session, whose detail showed both Djangonaut Space speakers with their bios and About with the talk's title over the abstract, and Back returned to the page; Tracks & session types showed "Keynotes … Keynote, 6 sessions", its row opened the page and Back returned to the hub. On a simulator of #104's own, dark mode and the largest text size: beside a 96 dp photo the name broke a word a line, so the photo moved over the name. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, an arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): light, dark and 200 % text; `uiautomator dump` read the name as a `View` heading node, "Read more about Your AI Product Doesn't Have a Moat… Yet" and the tile "Friday, May 15 / 9:45 to 10:30 AM / Pacific Ballroom - Arena" as buttons and the star as a switch, and from the hub the Keynotes row then the Back key returned to the hub. |
| D-218 | **Search renders each type's first rows, with a "Show all" row for the rest** (settles #216; amends D-193's results; adds `first_results()` to section M's `core/search.py`).<br>• **The cut:** `core/search.py`'s pure `first_results(results, *, sessions, speakers)` keeps the first `sessions` sessions in `search()`'s order (the last day shown cut short, never an empty day), the first `speakers` speakers and every room (at most 34 in a year's feed); the days it leaves whole, and the results themselves when nothing is cut, are the same objects, so D-153's memoised path holds. `screens/search_results.py` shows `FIRST_SESSIONS = 50` and `FIRST_SPEAKERS = 20`.<br>• **"Show all":** under a cut type, a `ListTile` row whose words say the full count, "Show all 336 sessions" ("Show all 147 speakers"), in `BODY_LARGE` and `primary` from the rows' 16 dp line, with `EXPAND_MORE` at its end, 56 dp; one merged node read as its words and a button (`search.show_all.sessions`, `search.show_all.speakers`). A tap renders the rest of that type in the row's place; the other type stays cut. The count line keeps the full counts, so every result is counted and one tap away.<br>• **State:** `ResultList` holds which types are shown whole together with the results they were asked of: a keystroke that changes nothing keeps them open, other results start cut again, and so does the old query typed again after them (an effect forgets the opened types once other results show).<br>• **Not chosen:** a row cache across queries (#216's other option): the first broad query of a session still builds every row, and taking ~300 rows away again (a clear, or a narrower query) cost as much as a narrow query's render; "Show 50 more" steps: several taps to reach the end for the same rows. | #216: rendering every row of a one-letter query was the slow part, not the search. A throwaway timing build (never committed) logged D-159's handler → patch for the debounced search on my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (release arm64 APK, 2026 feed, All scope, "p", "a" and "e" typed and deleted 12 times each, two passes each way; the host shared with other agents' builds and emulators, load average 9 to 50, `Feed processed in` 84 to 520 ms against S2's 66): before, a one-letter query's render took a median **608 ms** (339 to 1,556; "p" 586, "a" 664, "e" 575) and clearing it 228 ms; after, a median **102 ms** (59 to 1,922, the outlier during a load spike; "p" 96, "a" 97, "e" 104), the band of queries that find a few dozen rows in D-193 (7 to 120 ms), and clearing it 36 ms. "Show all" on request: the speakers' 127 more rows a median 203 ms (157 to 275), the sessions' 286 more 441 ms (308 to 725). On the host (`tests/test_search_screen.py`'s harness, busy M4) "p" went from 428-1,193 ms to 53-56 ms and "a" from 693-923 ms to 37-46 ms. D-159's 16 ms budget is a keystroke's and a star's: a keystroke before the pause still re-renders no row, which a test checks with the list cut and opened. On the Android 14 emulator (the committed code, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`) `uiautomator dump` read each row as one clickable `Button`, `search.show_all.sessions` described "Show all 336 sessions"; a tap put "CTF for fun and profit" and the rest where the row was, light, dark and 200 % text (the words on one line, the icon beside them). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (a debug build checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps) the Sessions scope ended its 50 rows with the row and a tap rendered the rest in place; on an iPhone 17 simulator of #216's own, a throwaway build (never committed) that seeded the query and cut at one or three rows showed it in dark, and at the largest Larger Text its words wrapped to two lines with `EXPAND_MORE` beside the first, nothing clipped. |

Places replaces P1's Rooms chip: scope id `places`, and `?scope=rooms` is unknown and opens All, as any unknown scope does (D-180). The Map's search button opens Places. A room pin no feed room names (Room 103A, a part of Room 103ABC; Hall A) is not searched: its room is. `search_index(data, spots)` takes the spots from the year data, typed only under `TYPE_CHECKING`, as `core/expo.py` takes booths (D-67).<br>• **Results:** one type, "Places" (its heading; "3 places" in the count line, rooms and spots together), never cut (D-218): the rooms first, in natural order, each with its count line ("Sprints" for a sprint-only room) and pushing room detail (`search.room.<slug>`); then the venue spots in the file's order, each a `LinkTile` with `PIN_DROP_OUTLINED` (it opens a plan, not a detail), its label and its sublabel, pushing `…/plan/<plan>?pin=<key>` on top of search (`search.place.<key>`), so Back returns to the results with the query (D-19). The image is cropped to the floor (tables, the food and beverage area, the entrance), without the print's header and its PyCon US 2026 logo (D-36); the viewer's app bar names the plan.<br>• **Table markers are adopted, as their own issue** (#464, under #23): a ratio rectangle per printed table T1 to T20 on the cropped image, `plan/job-fair?table=T8` selecting it as `?booth=` selects a booth, and a "Show table T8 on the plan" button on each pane row and behind sponsor detail's "Job fair table T8". The measured height lives in `ExpoHall`, above the memoised card (F150).<br>• **Short panes (landscape):** a pane under 464 dp, where half the pane under the buttons' 160 dp column would push Zoom in past the `Stack`'s clipped top, lays the three buttons in a 48 dp row; and the box is the lesser of half the pane and what the buttons, the 16 dp gap, the card's 24 dp padding and the 16 dp insets above and below leave, the body's own 8 dp top padding counted when deciding whether it scrolls (`card_layout()`).<br>• **Roles line:** "Hiring · 15 open roles" when the sponsor's listings have roles, "Hiring" for a long-form listing, nothing for a placeholder: the sponsor has an Open roles section exactly then.
