| D-377 | **A booth picked from a link or a List row is brought into view above its card with the zoom layer's interim; `?booth=` seeds the expo pane, a booth off the plan opens the List under a live line, and More gains Expo hall** (#405, `P2-45`; amends EXPO-05's "without moving the camera", ux.md §6.2's `?booth=` bullet and the gaps doc's §5.7 interim).<br>• **When:** the hall places its camera once per mount (`screens/expo_camera.py`'s `use_camera()`): the pane's last zoom put back (D-312), unless the booth a List row or a `?booth=` link picked is out of sight there. A tap on the plan never moves it: the user is looking at that booth.<br>• **Where:** in sight means above the bottom column, the card, the zoom buttons and their insets (`booth_card.covered()`, from `card_layout()` and the card body's measured height, so the move waits for that measure, F51). The camera stays at the kept zoom if the booth shows there, else at the fitted plan if it shows there, else goes to the least zoom that can put the booth's centre in the middle of the band above the column, centred across, held to the viewer's maximum and its bounds (`in_view()`, pure: `s = (box − target) / (box − centre)`). A card so tall that the buttons' column would leave less than 112 dp of plan above it (`BAND`, the height the row gives back) puts the buttons in their 48 dp row, whatever the pane's height (`card_layout()`), so a booth always has a band to land in; the move waits for that measure alone, and without a booth to reveal the card's height is no dependency of the move, so a card measured mid-restore can't cancel it (F2). It is applied with `Zoom.restore()` (reset, zoom, pan, D-312), so two or three frames show the steps, until U3's `zoom_to_rect` animates it.<br>• **Links:** `locations.booth_href(base, number)` writes `…/plan/expo?booth=<n>` (`BOOTH_PARAM`); `ExpoPlan` reads it with `use_query()` (D-164) and `ExpoPane` seeds once, as it mounts (`expo_pane.seed()`): a booth the plan draws sets `expo_booth` and `expo_view = "map"`, as a row's tap does; any other number shows the List under "Booth 139 isn't on the map yet" (`expo.missing`, a polite live region) without writing `expo_view`, until the user switches or picks. Back pops the one level (D-17), so a sponsor's link returns to the sponsor. Same number twice selects again (no guard).<br>• **More:** Expo & sponsors is Sponsors, Expo hall (`more.expo`, `MAP_OUTLINED`, pushing `/more/plan/expo`, only in a year with booths), Job listings. | With a one-exhibitor card on a 382 × 560 dp pane at the fitted plan, 51 of the 61 2026 booths sit under the card or the buttons' column (computed with `in_view()`), so a pick from the List or a link showed a card for a booth the user could not see. The least zoom that clears the column is at most 2.5x there, which also makes the booth large enough to tap. EXPO-05 had deferred the move as hard to explain; it is one formula in a pure function with a test per booth, and U3 only changes how it is applied. Not chosen: a fixed zoom (it overshoots a booth already near the top); centring in the whole pane (the card hides its centre); measuring the bottom column (its height follows from the card body's). |
| D-312 | **Floor plans: 2048-px WebP, zoom to one image pixel per dp, one `Container` per booth, Map panes without a swipeable `TabBarView`, and a pane's zoom put back from `ViewMemory`** (S7, #361; amends ux.md §5.8, §6.1 and §6.2; keeps D-18).<br>• **Images:** each year's plans go through `docs/spikes/07-floor-plans/reencode.py`: the longer side 2048 px (Lanczos), **lossy WebP, quality 80**; the app bundles the `.webp` files. No `Image.cache_width`. The campus, job fair and expo plans carry PyCon marks, cropped or covered before they ship (D-36); the plans are credited in `NOTICE` (D-87).<br>• **Zoom:** `InteractiveViewer(min_scale=1, max_scale=natural_width / displayed_width)`, at least 1, so an image pixel fills at most one logical pixel (5.36x for the expo plan on a 382-dp plan).<br>• **Hotspots:** one positioned `Container` per booth in `Semantics(content=..., button=True, label=..., identifier=...)` (`content=` by name, F130); Flutter hit-tests them. No `GestureDetector` hit-tested in Python.<br>• **No double-tap** detector around a viewer whose plan has tappable areas (the expo map): it delays every tap by Flutter's double-tap timeout. Plans without hit areas (the floor viewer, the job fair plan) keep double-tap for 2x or reset; every viewer keeps the zoom buttons (WCAG 2.5.1).<br>• **Map panes:** `Tabs` with a `TabBar` and the selected pane alone under it, each pane with its own `key` (F130), no `TabBarView`, until U110 (#560) gives `TabBarView.physics`.<br>• **A pane's zoom:** the last `on_transform_changed` (scale, x, y) is kept in `ViewMemory` (D-18) and put back once the pane is laid out with `reset()`, `zoom(scale)`, `pan(x / scale, y / scale)`, until U3's `set_transform` (§5.7). `save_state`/`restore_state` are not used (F130). | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug), the expo plan with its 61 booths added about 10 MB to a 258 MB footprint and peaked 40 MB higher while decoding; the 4966-px source peaked 90 MB higher. WebP 80 is 110–200 KB a plan against 330–540 KB for JPEG 85, with no visible loss at maximum zoom, and every format decodes to the same 11–13 MB. Booth taps were right at 1x and 5.36x both ways; the containers built no slower than one detector and give a screen reader 61 named buttons. A double-tap detector made tap down to tap 326 ms instead of 5–30 ms. In a `TabBarView`, a finger-speed horizontal drag on a plan at 5.36x changed the tab instead of panning, and the only stop at the pin (`disabled`) disables the booths too. A pane's zoom was gone on return and `restore_state` brought nothing back; the remembered transform came back exactly (5.361, −321.7, −214.5), with the panes keyed. Low-RAM Android, the gate before the expo map ships: **passed on the 2.5 GB AVD** (third run, 2026-10-29, #453, a host with free swap): the reproducer's expo plan adds 13–17 MB PSS to the plain list's 133 MB at 1x and at 5.23x, levels off at 157 MB after 40 open/close cycles, pans at a 16.7-ms median frame interval, and all 61 booths are named nodes, hit right 61 of 61 at 1x; the app's release build holds 183 MB on Now and 197–204 MB on the expo map, zoomed and after 10 open/close cycles, with 1.1–1.2 GB of the AVD still available. The emulator leaves out the GPU's share (Graphics 0), so a real low-RAM phone stays unmeasured (`docs/spikes/07-floor-plans.md`). |
| D-279 | **The Sponsors list: three tiers by a level's place, the middle one in a `ResponsiveRow`, and one node per sponsor** (settles #395's proposal; records ux.md §6.5's list for SPON-01 and its new §4.6 row; amends §6.5's `GridView(max_extent=200)` and D-262's signature).<br>• **Tiers by position, never by name** (`screens/sponsors.py`'s `tier_of()`): the first level of `ConferenceData.sponsor_levels` as full-width outlined cards (a 96 dp plate as wide as the card, the name, "Booth 613"), the next two as logo cards (a 72 dp plate as wide as the card, the name, the booth), every level below as rows (a 48 dp plate, the name, the booth, `CHEVRON_RIGHT`), each level under a level-2 `SectionHeader` with its name, after the intro line. 2026: Visionary's 7 cards, Sustainability and Maintaining as logo cards, ten levels of rows.<br>• **The logo cards share an `ft.ResponsiveRow`** with `col={"xs": 6, "sm": 4, "md": 3}`: two to a phone's row, three from 576 dp, four from 768 dp (Flet's default breakpoints, read from the page's width). A `GridView` cannot size itself to its content inside the page's `ListView` (U32, #238), and a wrapping `Row` of fixed-width cards, the issue's first idea, leaves a ragged gap at the end of each run (51 dp with two 160 dp cards on a 411 dp phone). At the pin a `ResponsiveRow` is a Flutter `Wrap` (or a `Row` when its children fit) of children whose widths it computes from its own width (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/responsive_row.dart`), so it takes its content's height in a list, as a wrapping row does. A child's `col` sits on the item component's root control, since the client unwraps components (`models/control.dart`, `children()`).<br>• **`logo_tile(..., fill=True)`** leaves the plate's width to its parent: a `Container` with a centring alignment and no width is as wide as it may be. Every plate keeps a fixed height, so a logo that loads late or fails (offline, the initials) moves nothing.<br>• **One node per sponsor**, built like `SpeakerRow` (D-189) rather than as a `ListTile`, whose merged texts would leave the level out: `Semantics(label=..., button=True, exclude_semantics=True, identifier="sponsors.sponsor.<slug>")` inside the tapped `Container`, all in one `MergeSemantics`, read "Bloomberg, Visionary sponsor, booth 613" (`sponsor_phrase()`; no booth, no clause). The slug is the identifier's key as it is: sponsor slugs hold only `a`..`z`, digits and `-` (D-261). A tap pushes `…/sponsor/<slug>` on the list's View (D-17). Each item is a memoised component keyed by its slug (D-153), so a refresh that keeps the feed re-renders none.<br>• **States:** the status strip first (D-32); without data `NoDataState` (the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" and Try again); a feed with no sponsors "No sponsors announced yet" / "They show here as soon as the conference lists them." with `STOREFRONT_OUTLINED` and no button (D-175's refresh brings them), identifier `sponsors.empty`. More's **Expo & sponsors** section, between Programme and You, starts with Sponsors (`more.sponsors`, `STOREFRONT_OUTLINED`; D-169). | Ionic had one flat style, a 10 s blocking loader and no empty state (SPON-01); tiers give the top levels their weight without the rotating strip (D-37). A width from the screen fills a phone's row and a tablet's evenly where a constant cannot. The level is said with each sponsor because a screen reader that moves item by item never hears the heading again. |
| D-298 | **Now's sponsor spotlight: one `mobile_banner` sponsor picked at random once per app process, kept in `ViewMemory`, picked again only when it leaves the feed; one still card, last on Now in every season** (settles #398 for SHELL-08 and SPON-11; amends D-37's "per app open" and ux.md §5.1 item 10; adds `now_spotlight.py` to D-190's Now modules and `spotlight_candidates()`, `pick_spotlight()` and `thanks()` to section M's `core/sponsors.py`).<br>• **Candidates:** `spotlight_candidates(data)`, the sponsors whose `mobile_banner` is set, in level order (14 in 2026).<br>• **The pick:** `pick_spotlight(candidates, current, rng)` returns the sponsor whose slug is `current` while it is a candidate, else `rng.choice(candidates)`, else None. Now keeps the slug in `ViewMemory.spotlight` and draws from `ViewMemory.rng`, a `random.Random` a test replaces with a seeded one (left out of comparisons). Per process, not per open: on Android a relaunch after Back on Now is a new session in the same process (F29), and a pick per session would change the sponsor on every Back and return, which reads as the rotation D-37 removed. `Reset app` forgets it with the rest of `ViewMemory` (D-198).<br>• **The card:** an outlined `Card` after everything else on Now, in every season with data (the countdown, the conference days, the sprints and after): the logo on its 56 dp white tile (`logo_tile()`, D-262), "Thanks to NVIDIA, a Visionary sponsor" ("an" before a level that starts with a vowel) and a chevron; no timer, no animation. A memoised component keyed by the slug (D-153), so the minute tick never re-renders it. One button node, `now.spotlight`, whose label is the words; the tile is decorative. A tap pushes `/now/sponsor/<slug>` (D-17). No candidate or no data: no card. | A pick per session would show another sponsor after each Back exit on Android (F29), and a timer would bring back WCAG 2.2.2's problem. Last on Now keeps the "what should I do next?" content first; #222 settles how long the sections above it are. |

## D. Design system

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-22 | Colour: `ft.Theme(color_scheme_seed="#3B3EA9", surfaces=ft.ThemeSurfaces.NEUTRAL, color_scheme=...)` with brand overrides. Light: primary `#3B3EA9`, secondary `#680579`, tertiary `#A1009A` (Ionic's magenta `#DD04D2` darkened to 7:1 on white). Dark uses tuned light tones. Both platforms are identical. Ionic's default blue `#3880ff` and its iOS `#000` / Android `#121212` dark split are dropped. | The PyCon palette in M3, with the magenta derived for contrast (D-25). Flet builds `fromSeed`, neutralises surfaces, then applies the overrides (F9). |
| D-23 | *(The HC schemes recorded by D-265; what the switch stores, D-286.)* Four colour schemes: light and dark (P0), plus high-contrast light and dark (P2). The high-contrast schemes are hand-authored overrides with 7:1 for text. Settings has Appearance (System, the default / Light / Dark) and a High contrast switch. The switch defaults to the OS flag where the OS reports it (iOS, Android 34+), re-read on resume. | Accessibility. Flet has no contrast-level option, so HC is explicit overrides. Two settings are simpler than Ionic's four-way picker. |
| D-24 | A small frozen `Tokens` dataclass, read through `use_tokens()`, holds what M3 lacks: `live`, `warning`, `success` and `favorite`, plus the track palette. | M3 has no success or warning roles. |
| D-25 | Track badges are tonal pairs per track (container fill plus text colour, in light and dark), every pair at least 4.5:1, outlined in high contrast. Badges always carry text. The "En Español" badge is the tonal pair `#FFD7F3` / `#5E0058` (10.1:1) in light, inverted in dark (Ionic's `#DD04D2` is 4.18:1). A unit test checks every text/background pair in every scheme. | A quiet, content-first M3 look; colour is never the only signal. |
| D-26 | *(Settled by D-455.)* *(Font route amended by D-105.)* Bundle **Roboto** (400/500/700) as `Theme.font_family`. `page.fonts` maps a family to one static file, so the three weights are declared ~~as a `flutter.fonts` entry (family `Roboto`, three assets with `weight: 400/500/700`) through `[tool.flet.flutter.pubspec]` (F18)~~ in a local font-only Flutter package added through `[tool.flet.flutter.pubspec]` (F18, D-105). Packaged builds get all three; `flet run` falls back to the system font (accepted). ~~S5 verifies that the font assets resolve in the generated Flutter project and that the three weights render on iOS.~~ S5 verified that the three weights render on iOS and Android. A multi-weight `page.fonts` is upstream candidate U20. Text uses only the M3 type scale (`Text(theme_style=...)`), with no fixed heights around text. Layouts reflow at 200 % OS text, and long text is selectable. | Without a bundled font, iOS renders SF Pro (D-10). The M3 scale is tuned for Roboto, and its digits are tabular; `TextStyle` has no `font_features` (F8). |
| D-455 | **The font is Roboto, with no display face unless a 2027 brand appears** (#819; settles O-3 under D-101 as recommended; D-26 is no longer provisional). A display face for headings stays an idea for that brand: nothing is reserved for it, and a brand that wants one opens a new decision. | Roboto is what ships (`FONT_FAMILY` in `theme.py`, D-26, D-105), and no brand exists to choose a second face for. |
| D-27 | *(The tooltip's node amended by D-234.)* Material Icons only: outlined when unselected or decorative, filled when selected. Brand logos are bundled SVGs. Every icon-only button has a tooltip, which is also its accessible name, merged into the button's node (D-234). | One icon family; accessibility. |
| D-28 | Chrome: a surface-coloured `AppBar` whose title is always visible, and no gradient heroes on content screens. Brand colour is reserved for identity moments: Welcome, the Now status header and About PyCon US. | Heroes cost 150-200 px before any content, and hidden titles left screen readers without a page name. |
| D-29 | Feedback: a floating `SnackBar` (one action, Undo when reversible) for transient results; a `Banner` for persistent conditions that need action; an `AlertDialog` only before destructive actions. Results the user waits for appear in place. No top toasts and no blocking loaders. | Material-native; Flet's `SnackBar` is bottom-only (F11). |
| D-30 | *(The star's label for screen readers amended by D-184.)* Favourites have one control: a 48 dp star on every session row and in session detail, labelled "Add to / Remove from My schedule". Adding fills the star, with a haptic. Removing shows "Removed from My schedule · Undo". Swipe-to-favourite is dropped. | Discoverable and accessible, with Undo where a row can vanish. |
| D-210 | **A SnackBar shown from a pushed View is seen on that View: D-29's SnackBars serve every screen, and the "until #197" notes go** (settles #197; amends the notes on SnackBars in D-187, D-197, D-198 and D-206, ux.md §4.7, §5.5, §5.12 and §5.13, and the feature matrix's SESS-06 note; records F48).<br>• **No defect at the pin, in Flet or in our Views:** `page.show_dialog(SnackBar(...))` goes through the page's one `ScaffoldMessenger`, above the `Navigator`, which gives the bar to every root `Scaffold`, one with no `Scaffold` of the same messenger above it; every View, pushed or not, is a route with a `Scaffold` of its own, so the View on top draws it (F48). `AppScaffold` (D-165) and the View stack (D-141, D-144) need no change, and there is nothing to report upstream.<br>• **What needs nothing:** `report_errors`' "Something went wrong" (D-163), the Undo SnackBars (D-30, D-184), "Couldn't open the link" (D-182, D-197) and "Couldn't open the share sheet" (D-206) show on the View the user is on, session detail included.<br>• **What stays:** About this app's unlock and the Developer screen's outcomes are said in place (D-187, D-198), now for D-29's own reason, a result the user waits for, and no longer as a way around #197.<br>• **Guard:** `tests/ui/test_snackbar_on_pushed_view.py` stars and unstars a session on session detail and finds "Removed from My schedule" and its Undo: finders skip a covered View (testing-and-quality.md §5.1), so a bar drawn only on the root under it would fail the flow. | #197 was not seen again, at the same pin, with main or with #88's own commit. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds, each bundle checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps): session detail opened by a tap and by `--route` showed "Removed from My schedule · Undo" on itself; throwaway builds (never committed) showed a SnackBar on About this app with a `Prefs` save before it and after it (the save re-renders `App`, D-150) and on Developer after Apply and Reset; #88's commit `6032197`, rebuilt as it was, showed Reset's "Back to real time and the year's feed" on the Developer page, entered by `--route` and by a tap from More. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) session detail showed it as well. A Flutter widget test of Flet's shape (one messenger above a `Navigator` of `Scaffold` pages, material_ui 1.6.0) found the bar on the top page, also after that page's `Scaffold` was rebuilt under a new key and after a theme change. The first try on the simulator missed it although the log showed it built and timed out 4.9 s later: on a host at load 14 the simulator's screenshots trailed the app by seconds, the likely source of #88's 2-frames-per-second recording; two of this issue's own false alarms had plain causes, a tap on Undo sent seconds after the 4 s bar had gone, and a UI-flow tap that `pump(1 s)` did not delay, which reached the bar 5 dp into its entrance (testing-and-quality.md §5.2). A throwaway Dart probe (never committed) found the bar's two copies, the covered root's and the top View's, neither in a hero flight, the top one hit at its centre. The new flow passed on the iOS 26.5 simulator (an iPhone 17 of #197's own) and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`), each in light and dark: the bar, its Undo, the session saved again and removed again, all on session detail. |
| D-31 | Schedule layout: day tabs show dates, and you can swipe between days. Each time slot is one list item, with its time in a left gutter beside its rows; within a slot, rows are ordered by room. Breaks and notices are compact rows without a star. | Works without sticky headers (F8), gives `scroll_to` slot-sized targets, and room order matches the printed grid. |
| D-32 | *("While refreshing" amended by D-181; where the strip sits at the largest text sizes by D-345; pull-to-refresh recorded by D-449.)* Data state is always honest. The app renders the best local copy at once. A status strip appears only ~~while refreshing~~ while a refresh the user asked for runs (D-181), when data is stale (over 6 h on conference days, over 24 h otherwise) or when offline, and it offers Refresh. Skeletons appear only when there is no data at all, and there is never a full-screen error while data exists. Pull-to-refresh arrives once U1 is in our pinned Flet. | Offline-first; Flet has no `RefreshIndicator` (F8). |
| D-33 | *(Headings' levels amended by D-211.)* Accessibility baseline on every screen: every control has a name, role and state; each row reads as one phrase, with the star as its own stop; headings are marked (with a level, D-211); targets are at least 48 dp; contrast meets AA (AAA for text in high contrast); text works at 200 % without clipping; reduced motion is honoured; colour is never the only signal. Key controls get `Semantics(identifier=...)` for UI tests. | A showcase must be accessible; the APIs exist (F13, F24). |
| D-34 | Motion: M3 durations (150-400 ms) and no ambient or repeating motion: a pin pulses at most twice. All animation is off when the OS asks for reduced motion, which is read at start and on resume. *(How, and what "all animation" covers: D-173.)* | WCAG 2.2.2. Flet reports a snapshot, not changes (F13). |
| D-35 | Copy and time: US English, sentence case, verbs on buttons, no "successfully". Years, dates, names and URLs come from data. Times are always in conference time, with a "Pacific Time" note when the device's zone differs, and 12/24-hour format follows the device setting. | Consistency; Ionic hard-coded the year in about ten places. The device's 24-hour setting is readable (F23). |
| D-36 | *(O-4 settled by D-452.)* Identity and attribution: we say "Built with Python and Flet" proudly, and "Not an official PSF app" appears wherever the app names itself (Welcome, About this app). PyCon and PSF logos are not our app icon or branding. The name, icon and bundle id are open decision O-4. | "PyCon" is a PSF trademark, and Apache-2.0 grants no trademark rights. |
| D-452 | **The app's identity: Conf Companion, `io.github.ndonkohenri.confcompanion`, our own icon, `confcompanion://app` and the calendar domain `confcompanion.ndonkohenri.github.io`** (settles O-4 and #15; the owner chose the bundle id on 2026-11-02, and the name, deep links, calendar domain and icon follow the proposal they were shown; amends D-36, D-268, D-269; NATIVE-01).<br>• **Name:** `[tool.flet] product` and `APP_NAME` stay `Conf Companion`. It has no PSF mark, and "PyCon US" appears inside the app only as the event's name, next to "Not an official PSF app" (D-36). `pyconfcompanion` was weighed and left out: in a conference app it reads as "PyCon" with one more letter.<br>• **Bundle id:** `io.github.ndonkohenri.confcompanion` on both platforms: a reverse domain the owner controls, free of `pycon`, with nothing to host. The Python package stays `pycon_us`: users never see it.<br>• **Deep links:** `scheme = "confcompanion"`, and `host` stays `app`.<br>• **Calendar:** `CALENDAR_DOMAIN = "confcompanion.ndonkohenri.github.io"`, only an identifier in each `UID`, never fetched.<br>• **Icon:** `src/assets/icon.svg`, a white conference badge with a purple star, and `[tool.flet] icon_background = "#680579"`. The two other drafts are in `docs/icon-options/`. flet-cli reads the SVG itself (`find_platform_image` and `load_source`, at the pin), so no PNG is committed, and the splash falls back to it.<br>• **What can still change:** the name and the icon at any time. The scheme until a link is shared outside the team. The calendar domain until a build leaves our devices (D-268). The bundle id until the first build is uploaded to App Store Connect or a tester installs one: Apple won't change a record's id after an upload, and a removed app's id can't be reused once a build was uploaded (Apple's App information reference and "Remove an app", read 2026-11-02). On Android a new id is a separate app whose data doesn't carry over.<br>• **Old builds:** installed builds keep the old id and scheme, so reinstall them on emulators and simulators. An old build stays beside the new one as a second app until it is uninstalled. Spike folders and the research notes keep the old names, because they record what was run then. | The owner's call under D-101. An id free of `pycon` keeps the PSF's mark out of the one value we can't change later. |
| D-37 | *("Per app open" amended by D-298: once per app process.)* Sponsors: no rotating strip. Instead, a sponsor spotlight card on Now (one `mobile_banner` sponsor per app open), a tiered Sponsors screen, and credits from year data wherever a sponsor provides something (Wi-Fi, the Quiet room). | Ionic's strip took about 50 dp on every screen and rotated every 30 s with no pause control (WCAG 2.2.2). |
| D-150 | *(The high-contrast flag on `Tokens` added by D-285.)* **Screens read `Tokens` from a context** (records how `use_tokens()` works for D-24; #71). `theme.py` defines `TokensCtx = ft.create_context(LIGHT_TOKENS)`, and `use_tokens()` returns `ft.use_context(TokensCtx)`. `App` provides `build_tokens(dark=prefs.dark, high_contrast=prefs.high_contrast_on)` next to the store providers, so the tokens always match the theme `apply_theme()` shows (`Prefs.dark` follows the OS brightness under Appearance System, architecture §4.3). `Tokens` is a frozen value, not an observable, so reading it subscribes to nothing (F3): a scheme change re-renders `App`, which re-renders the components below it. A component wrapped in `ft.memo` takes the tokens it needs as a prop instead. | The same shape as `ServicesCtx`: one obvious way, explicit providers, no extra store. Deriving the tokens inside `use_tokens()` from `use_prefs()` would hide the brightness rule in a hook and subscribe every token reader to all of `Prefs`. |
| D-153 | **The Schedule memoises the whole path, not only the rows** (settles ux.md §3.9 and architecture §6.4; S2). A day list is a component wrapped in `ft.memo` whose props are its day, its query and **only its day's favourites** (`frozenset`, the favourites ∩ the day's ids); each slot item is a memoised component keyed by its slot key, given its slot view, its matching rows (a tuple) and only the slot's favourites; each `SessionRow` and `NoticeRow` is a memoised component keyed by session id, given its frozen row view and `is_favorite`. Props are values or stable callables: row views are built once per feed version (`use_memo` on `Conference.version`), and `on_toggle(session_id)` comes from `use_callback(..., [])`, the row binding its own id. A star tap re-renders one day list, one slot and one row. | On the busiest day (111 rows, 320 in five days) a star tap cost 145 ms of Python with plain rows, 158 ms with unmemoised row components and **38 ms with memoised rows alone**, because the parents still rebuilt and diffed every slot (F34); with the path memoised, **3 ms** and the star changed in the same frame as a change Flutter draws itself. A keystroke went 120 → 34 → 3 ms, a day switch 139 → 40 → 2 ms (`docs/spikes/02-day-list.md`). |
| D-154 | *(My schedule's list amended by D-191; the lazy lists after a text-size change by D-331.)* **Day lists are lazy; today's list builds every slot** (replaces ux.md §5.2's and §5.6's `build_controls_on_demand=False`; S2, F32). Each day's list is a `ListView` with the default `build_controls_on_demand=True`. Today's list, the one Jump to now targets (D-155), also gets `cache_extent=FULL_LIST_EXTENT` (`ui/`, 100,000 logical px, more than three times the busiest day at 200 % text: about 26,000, estimated from its 4:30 PM slot, the 21st of 26, at 21,234 px and the day's scroll extent of 12,314 px at 100 %), so Flutter builds all its slots and registers every `ScrollKey`; outside the conference no list has it. `build_controls_on_demand=False` is never used to make items reachable, and a day is never a `Column`. ~~My schedule's list, which opens at today's header, gets the same `cache_extent`.~~ My schedule's list does not scroll itself and gets no `cache_extent` (D-191). | `scroll_to(scroll_key=...)` only reaches a built item (F7), and `build_controls_on_demand=False` still builds only near the viewport (F32): 0 of 3 jumps to 4:30 PM, at both text sizes. A fully built list reached it 3 of 3 but costs on the emulator: a day switch's worst frame a median 49 ms (up to 50-70 ms) instead of at most 24 ms, a filter's list change up to 82 ms instead of 16 ms. A `Column` also stalled 220-227 ms when a fling hit either end of the day (stretch overscroll of one huge child); the fully built `ListView` did not. So only the list that needs the jump pays for it: with today's list alone built in full, the jump landed 3 of 3 at both text sizes, switches to the other days stayed at 18-20 ms frames (33-50 ms only when switching to today), and flings had no frame over 29 ms. |
| D-155 | *(Sequencing settled by D-192; the target amended by D-204.)* **"Jump to now" is one keyed `scroll_to`** (records how D-18's scroll happens; S2). The first display of today and the "Now" FAB call `await today_list.scroll_to(scroll_key=ft.ScrollKey(slot_key), duration=400)` (0 under reduced motion, D-34) on today's fully built list (D-154), with ~~`slot_key` from `current_slot_key()`~~ the target from `now_place()` (D-204) (architecture §7.5); never a computed offset. From another day's tab the FAB switches to today first. S2 verified the jump only on the shown list, already built (a tap 1-3 s after launch); both flows above add sequencing that #86 settles and verifies on Android and iOS: the jump waits until the render that shows today's list has been applied (a round trip, as in D-156), handles a `TimeoutException` from `scroll_to`, and confirms the landing with `on_scroll`'s scroll end. | On a fully built list it landed on the slot every time, on Android at 100 % and 200 % and on the iOS simulator. The two flows differ from that case: `scroll_to` waits for the list's widget to register its method listener (`initState`) and raises `TimeoutException` after its timeout; a key whose item Flutter has not built yet makes it a silent no-op; and `TabBarView` builds only the shown page, so today's list is unmounted while another day shows (`scrollable_control.dart`, `Control.invokeMethod` in `control.dart`). A two-step jump on a lazy list (an offset estimated from row heights, then the key) worked at 100 % and failed at 200 %, where rows are twice as tall and the first step lands hours early. |
| D-156 | **The shown day renders first; the other days in a second render** (S2). On the Schedule's first render only the selected day's list is rendered; the `TabBarView` pages of the other days are empty `Container`s. An effect of that first render awaits one round trip to Flutter (a service call; Flutter has then applied the first patch, though no frame is guaranteed) and sets a `rest_ready` state; the second render fills the other days. Later renders keep all days. | Rendering all five days in the first render costs about 150 ms of cold start on the emulator: 970 ms to the cached content against 815 ms with the deferred pass (865 against 1,037 at 200 %), Python's first render 245 ms against 105 ms. Rendering a day only on its first visit starts as fast (849 ms) but slides in an empty page when swiping to a day not yet shown. On the iOS simulator's debug build the second pass took 112 ms of Python for 209 rows. The adopted design (lazy lists, today's built in full, D-154) reached the cached content at **886 ms** (856-958) in a later window, its first render 111 ms of Python; the 70 ms over 815 ms had accrued before `main()` ran (762 against 701 ms after the intent), where the list plays no part, and `column/deferred` itself read 884 ms in an earlier window. So S2's numbers from different windows agree to about ±70 ms, and only same-window comparisons, such as the 150 ms above, carry a decision. The content line does not wait for a frame (D-112), so Flutter building today's whole list in the first frame is not in it; #86's screen recording shows it. |
| D-331 | *(The put-back amended by D-364; a filter change carries the place, D-371.)* **A text-size change keeps each Schedule day list's place, by key** (#337; amends D-18's offset restore and D-154's lazy lists for that case; F7, F149, U16). A list keeps its pixel offset, so when the text grows the rows above the top grow and earlier rows show: at Android's 1.3, Saturday's 12:00 PM slot gave way to 11:15 AM, today's 2:45 PM to 1:30 PM. `screens/schedule_place.py`, with `core/list_place.py`:<br>• **Measure:** each slot and the now line report their height (`on_size_change`) to the screen's `PlaceKeeper`, plain, so nothing renders from them and D-153's memo holds. `layout_probe()`, 0 dp high above the day pages, lays out an invisible "10:00" in the gutter's type and reports its width and the screen's: Flet has no text scale (U16), and Flutter lays the probe out before the pages' items, so its report comes first (F149).<br>• **Record:** at each scroll end, the item at the top and how far into it (`place_at()`), counted from the top when every item above has reported (today's list, D-154), else from the place before, since a lazy list reports the items the user scrolls through; unknown, the place is forgotten. It lives in `ViewMemory.schedule_places` with the feed version and the filter it was laid out from: after a filter or feed change it says nothing until the next scroll end.<br>• **Put back:** when the time's width, the screen's width or the time's position (D-216) changes, every height is forgotten and every place is marked stale. The shown list goes back at once: a keyed `scroll_to` (duration 0) to the item, then as far into it as before, scaled by the item's new height (`rescaled()`). When no keyed jump moves, because text that shrank left the item out of the built range (F7), the list first jumps to the item's old top, near it, and asks again. Nothing while the list scrolls: the place is forgotten. Another day with a stale place is built in full (`FULL_LIST_EXTENT`) until it shows, so its item is built for the keyed jump, which then replaces the offset restore once; it is lazy again after.<br>• **Left as on `main`:** the shifted rows show for a frame or two before the put-back; the scaled distance can land a row off inside the slot; a change made while the Schedule is not mounted, and a place lost to a filter change or a failed jump, keep the offset restore. Measured on the Android 16 emulator with release builds (#337's PR): at 1.3 and back to 1.0, the top row stayed 6 of 6 on Saturday and 7 of 8 on today's list (the eighth kept its slot, one row up); a change made while another day showed came back on both days' rows 2 of 2. `main` moved the top in 8 of 9 at 1.3, to an earlier slot in 6, and both days in the other-day case. |
