| D-173 | *(Note on #178 amended by D-183.)* **Reduced motion: one flag, one way to ask, one place for resume** (records D-34 for THEME-09 and sets its scope; #84).<br>• **The flag:** `Prefs.reduce_motion`, not persisted, is `reduce_motion or disable_animations` from `get_accessibility_features()` (iOS Reduce Motion; Android's Remove animations, i.e. `transition_animation_scale` 0). `bootstrap()` reads it before the gate opens, and `app.on_resume()` reads it again on every `RESUME`: the one place for what a return to the foreground does, which #82's refresh of a stale feed joins (D-40, D-175). A change is logged ("Reduced motion is on").<br>• **One way to ask:** `prefs.motion_ms(ms)` returns `ms`, or 0 under reduced motion. Every duration the app sets goes through it: `scroll_to` (the re-tap, D-166; jump to now, D-155), `animate_*`, `AnimatedSwitcher`, pulses and highlights.<br>• **Scheme changes keep Flutter's 200 ms crossfade, under reduced motion too:** `bootstrap()` sets `page.theme_animation_style` back to `None` when the gate opens (D-161), whatever the flag, and nothing changes it on resume. A crossfade changes colour and opacity, not position: WCAG 2.3.3 leaves those out of "motion animation", and iOS Reduce Motion itself replaces slides with dissolves. A hard swap was worse: on iOS the surfaces changed in one frame while Material's text-style tween (U26) still ran, so the list text was dark on dark for about two frames. On Android, `disable_animations` already runs the crossfade at 5 % (10 ms), like every other Flutter animation. So D-34's "all animation is off" means motion (movement, scrolling, scaling, pulses), not colour fades.<br>• **Page transitions stay the platform's** (D-148). Android's setting reaches Flutter as `disable_animations`, which already runs every `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller (routes, ripples, Material's implicit animations) at 5 % of its length, and the predictive gesture stays. On iOS, `PageTransitionTheme.NONE` would remove the edge swipe, which lives in the Cupertino builder, and would leave a popped page on screen for the route's 300 ms; the slide stays. Flutter's own implicit animations (Material's 200 ms text-style tween, radios, ripples) keep their length under iOS Reduce Motion: U26 (#179).<br>• **Progress indicators keep moving:** they show work running and stop when it ends. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), the three animation scales set to 0 with the app in the background, then a return, logged "Reduced motion is on" in the same process (one session, same pid), and back to 1, "Reduced motion is off". A change made while the app is in the background reached Flutter's `disable_animations` a few seconds after the return, after the `RESUME` read, in 6 of 8 tries (a change made in the foreground was current at the next resume), so on Android it applies at the following resume: #178, accepted until U17 (D-183). On the iOS 26.5 simulator, a throwaway `flet test` flow switched Appearance to Dark and back on More while recording: with motion on, the recording held crossfade frames (mean grey 152 and 39 between 249 and 19); with a first version that turned the crossfade off, after Reduce Motion was turned on with the app in the background (`defaults write com.apple.Accessibility ReduceMotionEnabled -bool true` and `notifyutil -p com.apple.accessibility.reduce.motion.status`, then a relaunch to the foreground), the surfaces swapped in one frame, in the same process, but the list text trailed by about 200 ms (Material's tween, U26) and was near-black on the dark surface for two frames. With the crossfade kept and Reduce Motion on (`get_accessibility_features` returned `reduce_motion: true`; the gate set `theme_animation_style` to null), two switches to Dark each held crossfade frames (mean grey 182 and 123, then 152 and 97, between 244 and 20), with the labels readable in every frame. An OS dark-mode switch on iOS crossfades the whole screen for about 450 ms whatever the app sets (seen with the app's crossfade off). The page-transition reasoning is read from the code, not timed on a device: Flutter's `animation_controller.dart` (`AnimationBehavior._enableAnimations`, false under `disableAnimations`, and the `0.05` scale in `_startSimulation`), Flet's `NoPageTransitionsBuilder` (`utils/theme.dart`) and `AnimatedTransitionPage`'s 300 ms route (`widgets/animated_transition_page.dart`). |
| D-174 | *(Why a label breaks only between words recorded by D-216.)* **Track badges: where the palette lives, how a badge is drawn, and Registration required outlined in `error`** (records D-24 and D-25; amends ux.md §3.4's palette and its Registration required fill; #80, THEME-08, SCHED-20).<br>• **Palette:** `Tokens` gains `tracks` (a read-only mapping from each key of the track registry, `"Open Space"`, `"Ai"`, `"Sponsor Presentation"`, to a frozen `BadgeColors(text, fill)`), `unknown_track` (the neutral grey of breaks and notices, for a key next year's feed adds), `spanish` (`#FFD7F3` / `#5E0058`, inverted in dark) and `registration`; `Tokens.track_badge(key)` applies the fallback. `tracks` stays out of the generated hash (`field(hash=False)`), so `Tokens` stays hashable.<br>• **Distinct fills:** ux.md §3.4's track fills are retuned onto one CIELAB lightness (87 light, 29 dark) and chroma (22), about 30° of hue apart, with Summit a quieter slate: any two fills, En Español and the Break grey included, differ by a CIE76 distance of 10 or more in each scheme, and every label reads at 6.4:1 or better on its fill. A test checks both.<br>• **Registration required** is outlined: label and a 1 dp border in `error`, no fill. So both schemes declare `error` / `on_error` with the seed's own values (`#BA1A1A` / `#FFFFFF`, `#FFB4AB` / `#690005`), and the contrast test checks the label on every surface of the neutral ramp (4.8:1 or better in light, the 4.76:1 on `surface_dim`; 6.6:1 or better in dark, on `surface_bright`).<br>• **Drawing:** `ui/badges.py`'s `TrackBadge(label, colors)` is a plain builder, like `LinkTile`: a `Container` with `LABEL_SMALL` text, 8 dp corners, 8/2 dp padding and a 1 dp border in its fill's colour (the text's when outlined), so every badge is the same height; no fixed size, and the label wraps inside the pill at large text. `session_badges(session, info, tokens)` gives a session's badges in Ionic's set and order (its track by `track_name()`, then "En Español", then "Registration required"; a charla shows both of the first two), and `badge_row()` lays them out in `Row(wrap=True, spacing=8, run_spacing=4)`, on ux.md §3.6's 4 dp grid.<br>• Until P1's real session detail (#90), the session stub shows the badge row under its title instead of the raw track key. | An outline marks a requirement as a different kind of badge from a category, as Ionic's danger outline did, and HC (P2) outlines every badge anyway. The first palette's fills were shades of a few hues: Security and Sponsor presentation were 2.0 apart in light, Keynote and Tutorial 2.1, Summit and the grey 2.6, and En Español sat 3.5 from Tutorial and 5.5 from the Keynote it follows on the Spanish keynote, so the second badge read as part of the first (#181's review; `error_container` was 16.4 from En Español). An even grid in a perceptual space spaces them by construction; 10 is clearly different at a glance, and 30° steps at chroma 22 give it while the fills stay pastel. A builder given its colours lets a memoised row (D-153), which receives the tokens as a prop (D-150), draw the scheme on screen. The seed's error values were computed with Flutter's `material_color_utilities` 0.13.0 (`SchemeTonalSpot` from `#3B3EA9`) and sampled on screen: the outline drew `#BA1A1A` on `#FFFFFF` and `#FFB4AB` on `#121212` on the iOS 26.5 simulator. On that simulator and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), light and dark, sessions of 14 kinds (10 on Android) showed their badges with Roboto labels and equal heights, the outlined badge beside the tonal ones; at 200 % text (Android) every pair still fit one line, and at the largest Larger Text (iOS) the second badge wrapped onto its own line, nothing clipped. After the retune, the same builds showed 16 kinds on iOS (a generic break with no place line among them) and 12 on Android, light and dark: each track in its own colour, Keynote and Charla clearly apart from the En Español beside them, 8 dp between badges, and a wrapped line 4 dp below at the largest text. |
| D-184 | *(A room's rows amended by D-194; the modules by D-200, D-227 and D-267; a speaker's rows by D-203; how long the Undo SnackBar stays by D-251 and D-356; what a tap says by D-355.)* **The row family: memoised rows keyed by session id, one spoken phrase per row, a toggle star and one star handler per list** (records D-30, D-31, D-33 and D-153 for SCHED-03 and SCHED-04; amends ux.md §3.9's sketches and §4.5's SnackBar; #81).<br>• **Modules:** the session rows are `ui/session_rows.py` and their views `core/row_views.py`, not `ui/rows.py` and `core/queries.py` as section M first said: those hold `LinkTile` (D-182) and the schedule queries (D-178, D-179), and each would pass 400 lines with the rows (amends section M). D-200 moved `TimeGutter` to `ui/time_gutter.py`, `semantics_key()` to `ui/semantics.py` and the phrases (`spoken_phrase()`, `notice_phrase()`) to `core/row_views.py`.<br>• **Row views:** `core/row_views.py`'s frozen `SessionView` (`session`, `when` "9:25–10:10 AM", `spoken_when` "9:25 to 10:10 AM", `place`, `speakers`) from the pure `session_view()` / `session_views()`; a list owner gets them from `ui/session_rows.py`'s `use_session_views(conference, ids, use_24h=...)`, memoised on the feed version, the ids and the clock format. A row says no day, so a list that spans days (a speaker's~~, a room's~~) puts a `SectionHeader` per day above its rows, grouped by `row_views.by_day()`; a room's has a tab per day and its rows leave out the room's own name (D-194).<br>• **Rows:** `SessionRow(view, is_favorite, on_toggle, tokens, *, screen)` and `NoticeRow(view, *, screen)` are plain functions that return an `ft.memo` component keyed by `view.session.id`, so no caller can forget the key (`ft.component`'s typing leaves out the `key=` its wrapper takes: one `pyright: ignore` each). A row's text is `Semantics(label=<phrase>, identifier="<screen>.session.<key>", button=True, exclude_semantics=True)` inside a `MergeSemantics` with the tappable `Container`: one button node, read as `spoken_phrase()` ("Talk. Title. 9:25 to 10:10 AM, Room 104A. Ana Lima."), whose action is the container's tap; `Semantics.on_tap` is not used, since at the pin it never reaches Flutter (#187). An identifier's key is `semantics_key(session.id)`, lower case with `-` for other characters, so a break's synthetic id (spaces, colons) is selector-safe. Supporting lines use `on_surface_variant`; the meta's time ("AM" kept to it by a no-break space, and the "·") and its place are two texts in a wrapping `Row`, so at large text the place moves down whole; no fixed heights.<br>• **The star:** ~~`ui/badges.py`'s~~ `ui/favorite.py`'s (D-267) `FavoriteButton(selected, on_click, *, color, identifier)`, a plain builder given `Tokens.favorite`: `MergeSemantics(Semantics(label="Save to My schedule", button=True, toggled=selected, identifier="<screen>.favorite.<key>", content=IconButton(STAR if selected else STAR_BORDER, icon_color=..., tooltip=Tooltip("Add to My schedule" / "Remove from My schedule", exclude_from_semantics=True))))`. The spoken name is stable and the state is said once, as the switch's on or off: the tooltip, which says what a tap does (D-30), stays out of the semantics (iOS reads a node's tooltip after its label), and the icon is swapped without `IconButton.selected` (its `Semantics(selected:)` would add "selected"). This amends D-30's label for screen readers; its visible tooltip is unchanged.<br>• **Notices:** a session whose track is in the `logistics` group of `conference.toml` (Break, Informational; `is_notice()`) is a `NoticeRow`: its track's registry icon, the title and meta on `surface_container` with 12 dp corners, padding 12 × 8 and margin 16 × 4 (its title starts 12 dp down, on the gutter's line), no star, tappable only with a description, its own merged node read as `notice_phrase()` ("Break. Coffee, 8:00 to 9:00 AM, Expo Hall AB."), a button when it opens, identifier `<screen>.notice.<key>`.<br>• **`TimeGutter(label)`** as D-158; a 24-hour label is one line over an invisible "00:00".<br>• **One handler per list:** `use_favorite_toggle(favorites)` returns `use_callback(toggle, [])`: the store's toggle, then `HapticFeedback.selection_click()` on an add (`Services.haptics`; a failed click is only logged), then the tap's one SnackBar from `feedback(change)`: none after a saved add, "Removed from My schedule" with Undo, or "Couldn't save your change" (Undo kept after a removal). Undo awaits `favorites.undo()` and reports its own failure the same way.<br>• **SnackBar persistence:** Flutter 3.47 keeps a SnackBar with an action on screen until it is used (`persist` defaults to true then), which would leave "Removed · Undo" over every later screen. So `persist` is true only for an Undo SnackBar while a screen reader is on (`Prefs.screen_reader`, Flutter's `accessible_navigation`, read at start and on resume with the other OS flags): 4 s otherwise (ux.md §4.5), and time to reach Undo for TalkBack and VoiceOver users; until the next favourites edit, which closes it (D-251).<br>• **Where rows are until their screens:** the Schedule stub's five sample sessions, the speaker stub's sessions and the room stub's sessions (by day, in gutter slots with notices) are rows, so the star works end to end; #86 builds the Schedule's memoised path, #94 My schedule, #99 and #101 the speaker and room details. | A row that owned its tap and its phrase in one merged node, and a star with its own name and state, is what S2's TalkBack tree lacked. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) with TalkBack on, `uiautomator dump` of the room stub showed one clickable `Button` node per row with the phrase as its description and `resource-id` `room.session.<key>`; the star as a separate clickable `Switch` node, checkable, 48 × 48 dp, named "Save to My schedule", checked after a tap and not `selected`; each gutter as one "5:00 PM" node; and each notice as its own node, `room.notice.break-expo-hall-ab-2026-05-15t08-00-00-07-00`. On the iOS 26.5 simulator, the debug build's semantics tree (`debugDumpSemanticsTreeInTraversalOrder` through the Dart VM service) gave each row one merged node with `isButton` and the phrase, and the star one node with `isButton`, `hasToggledState` / `isToggled`, the label "Save to My schedule", no tooltip and no `isSelected`; Flutter's iOS bridge (`SemanticsObject.mm`) appends a node's tooltip to its `accessibilityLabel` and maps `isSelected` to the Selected trait, which the earlier star had both of, so VoiceOver read its name twice and its state as "on" and "selected". In light and dark, and at 200 % text (Android) and Larger Text up to the largest (iOS 26.5 simulator), rows and notices reflowed without clipping, the place moving under the time whole ("5:00–7:00 PM ·" / "Expo Hall AB", where one text broke it as "· Expo" / "Hall AB"), and a notice's title on its gutter's line; at iOS's largest size, about 310 %, the wide gutter leaves the row so narrow that a long word can break inside itself and the time breaks after its dash. On the shared iOS simulator a star tap filled the star in `tertiary`, a second tap showed the floating "Removed from My schedule · Undo" for 4 s, Undo saved the session again (SharedPreferences held it), and the star was still filled after a relaunch. A unit test in a real Flet session sees a star tap re-render exactly one row body, and fails with `ft.memo` removed. Flutter's 3.47 default (persist with an action) was read in `scaffold.dart` and `snack_bar.dart`. |
| D-251 | *(The module and its tests amended by D-267; a screen reader's add by D-355; the SnackBar's persistence by D-356.)* **A favourites edit that says nothing closes the last favourites SnackBar: a screen reader is never left with "Removed from My schedule" about a saved session** (settles #334; amends D-184's SnackBar persistence and ux.md §4.4 and §4.5).<br>• **The bug:** with a screen reader on, D-184 keeps "Removed from My schedule · Undo" until it is used or replaced. Saving the session again, by its star on any screen, shows no SnackBar (§4.4), so nothing replaced it: TalkBack found it on session detail and back on Search, 95 s later, beside a filled star, and its Undo did nothing.<br>• **The rule:** ~~`ui/badges.py`'s~~ `ui/favorite.py`'s (D-267) `_report()` reports every favourites edit, the star's (`use_favorite_toggle()`), its Undo, "Add all" (`use_add_all()`) and that Undo. An edit with a message shows its SnackBar, which replaces the last one, as Flet's `SnackBarControl` removes the current SnackBar before it shows one (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/snack_bar.dart`, line 109). An edit that says nothing (an add, an Undo that was saved) closes the last favourites SnackBar the page showed, while Flet still reports it `open`: `open = False` and `update()`, which `SnackBarControl` turns into `ScaffoldMessenger.removeCurrentSnackBar()` (lines 124-129). The page's last one is kept in a `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the page, so a star on session detail closes the SnackBar a star on Search showed; its value is a `weakref.ref` to the SnackBar, since the SnackBar holds its page (its Undo's closure, the dismiss handler `page.show_dialog()` wraps) and a strong value would keep a page whose session has ended, with its stores and services. The page's dialogs own the SnackBar while it shows (`base_page.py`'s `show_dialog()`), so the reference lives as long as there is something to close. Any edit closes it, not only one of the same session: the SnackBar does not say which session it removed. Edits overlap: Flet runs each event as its own task (`flet_socket_server.py`), so a second tap can finish while the first still awaits its write or its haptic click. Each handler takes a number from a per-page counter before its first await (`_begin()`, another `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the page), and only the page's newest edit reports: an older add or Undo that finishes late never closes a newer removal's SnackBar, and an older removal whose write answers after a newer add began shows nothing over the add's silence. The newest edit writes the whole set (`Favorites`), so its result is the one that counts; the SnackBar's `ref` alone could not mark the order, since it changes only when a SnackBar shows. D-184's `persist` is unchanged: a SnackBar still stays for a screen-reader user until it is used, replaced, or closed by the next edit.<br>• **Rejected:** `page.pop_dialog()`, which closes whatever dialog is on top, a filter sheet included (ux.md §4.5); closing before every show as well, since the close and the show, sent together, each remove the current SnackBar after the frame, in an order Flet does not promise, and the close could take the new SnackBar away; ending `persist` for screen readers, which would take Undo out of their reach again (D-184).<br>• **Tests:** ~~`tests/test_badges.py`~~ `tests/test_favorite.py` (D-267) (a real Flet session with a screen reader on): saving the session again closes its "Removed" SnackBar and sends it, Undo closes it, saving another session closes it, a removal's SnackBar stays until the next edit, another SnackBar on the page stays open, one Flet already reports gone is not sent again, and a page with an Undo SnackBar is released when its session ends (it fails with a strong value), an older add held in its haptic click, or an older Undo held in its write, leaves the newer removal's SnackBar open, and an older removal held in its write shows no SnackBar once a newer add has saved the session again; `tests/test_track_add_all.py`: "Add all"'s Undo closes its SnackBar. | On my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, TalkBack 14.2 driven by its keyboard shortcuts written to the emulator's keyboard device, read from its verbose speech log; release arm64 APK of this branch, light, default text size), on Search ("pathlib"): removing showed "Removed from My schedule · Undo", which TalkBack read (`TYPE_WINDOW_CONTENT_CHANGED`) and which stayed over 40 s, as D-184 wants; Alt+Enter on the star again saved the session and the SnackBar went at once, focus staying on the star; opening the row then showed session detail with the star on and no SnackBar. Undo, reached and activated with TalkBack, saved the session and the SnackBar went (TalkBack's focus moved to Back, as when a focused node goes). Removed on session detail, the SnackBar followed to Search on Back (D-210) and went when the star there saved the session again. |
| D-355 | **The star's tap sends no "clicked" event; with a screen reader on, an add says "Added to My schedule"** (settles #342 under D-101; amends D-184's spoken state after a tap, D-251's silent add for screen readers and ux.md §4.4; records F173 and U153, #605).<br>• **The bug:** TalkBack sometimes said the star's old state right after a tap ("on" after a removal, 3 of 10 taps on Android 14). Flutter's `InkResponse` sends Android's `TYPE_VIEW_CLICKED` at the tap, before Python has the event, and TalkBack reads the clicked node's checked state when it handles that event, about 200 ms later; Flet changes `toggled` only after the round trip, so a slow patch loses the race (F173). Neither known mechanism was the cause: the star is one `Switch` node with the tap and the state (no `enabled` conflict, U34), and no live region is involved (F61).<br>• **The rule:** `FavoriteButton`'s `IconButton` has `enable_feedback=False`, so the tap sends no click event and no Android click sound (the add keeps its selection haptic, ux.md §4.5). The tap's word then comes from the favourites SnackBar, a live region spoken once the edit is made: `feedback(change, screen_reader=...)` says "Added to My schedule" (`ADDED`) for an add, an Undo's included, while `Prefs.screen_reader` is on, 4 s and no action; a removal still says "Removed from My schedule" with Undo (D-184); without a screen reader an add stays silent and closes the last SnackBar (D-251). The added SnackBar replaces a "Removed" one, so D-251's aim holds. iOS: `Feedback.forTap` plays nothing there, so only the SnackBar is new.<br>• **Rejected:** Flutter's API 36 `CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_CHECKED`, which the bridge sends for a toggle change but which reaches TalkBack 16 folded into a subtree change and is not spoken; a per-state label on a live-region star (D-184's stable name); a landing note per list (D-241's pattern), a node on every screen; a faster round trip, which narrows the race but cannot win it. The real fix is upstream: a toggle whose state flips on the client at the tap (U153).<br>• **Tests:** `tests/test_favorite.py`: the star sends no feedback, `feedback()`'s table, a screen reader hears an add and an Undo, the add's SnackBar replaces "Removed"; D-251's closing tests run without a screen reader. | On my own `pycon_pixel6a_api36` emulator (Android 16, TalkBack 16.0, its verbose speech log on, release arm64 APKs, Search "pathlib", Alt+Enter on the focused star, a tap every 4 s): `main` said the old state once in 60 taps ("off" after a save), its rule `EventTypeViewClickedFeedbackRule: ttsOutputRule= checkedState` running 220 ms after the click; this branch said "Added to My schedule" or "Removed from My schedule" on all 40 taps (20 every 4 s, 20 every 2.5 s), no "on" or "off", focus staying on the star. |
| D-356 | **A SnackBar that a screen reader keeps closes once no screen reader is on: while it shows, after its 4 s, the app reads the OS's flags again every 2 s in the foreground, until U17** (settles #343; amends D-184's and D-251's SnackBar persistence, ux.md §4.5, and D-183's "not done" for this one case).<br>• **The bug:** D-184 gives an Undo SnackBar (and D-255's Review one) `persist` while `Prefs.screen_reader` is on. Flutter decides once: `ScaffoldMessengerState.build()` arms one timer for the SnackBar's `duration`, which returns when `SnackBar.persist` is true and is not armed again (`packages/flutter/lib/src/material/scaffold.dart`, lines 612-627, Flutter 3.47.7). The app reads `accessible_navigation` only at start and on resume (F13: a snapshot, no change event; U17 #183), so TalkBack turned off with the app in front (the volume-key shortcut) left "Removed from My schedule · Undo" up for good, over More's About row.<br>• **The interim:** `ui/snack_bars.py`'s `keep_while_screen_reader(bar, svc, prefs)`, called by `ui/favorite.py`'s `_report()` and `screens/changes_review.py`'s listener right after they show a kept SnackBar (only one with an action: D-355's "Added to My schedule" has none, so it is not kept, times out in its 4 s and is not watched), starts one task in the caller's context (a store write needs its page) that waits the SnackBar's 4 s (`SNACK_BAR_MS`, which moves there), then, while the SnackBar still shows (`open`, which Flet sets False once it has gone), calls `read_accessibility()` every `READ_EVERY_S` (2 s). Once no screen reader is on it closes the SnackBar as D-251 does (`open = False`, `update()`): it has had the 4 s anyone gets. The watch ends when the SnackBar has gone (used, replaced, closed), and when the session closes (`svc.closed`, D-147); it waits while the app is in the background (`svc.foreground`), since the resume reads the flags itself. It holds the SnackBar weakly and the running tasks in a module set (the loop keeps tasks weakly). `read_accessibility()` moves from `app.py` to `prefs.py` (then to `accessibility.py`, #737) so the UI can call it without importing `App`; reading every flag, not only the screen reader's, keeps `Prefs` honest about all of them.<br>• **Cost:** one `get_accessibility_features()` round trip every 2 s, only while a screen reader keeps a SnackBar and the app is in front; none for anyone else. A screen-reader user turning it off waits at most 2 s more for the SnackBar to go.<br>• **Rejected:** a finite `duration` for screen-reader users instead of `persist` (WCAG 2.2.1: they need the time to reach Undo); a read on the 60 s tick (D-183's "not done": up to a minute of a stuck bar, and reads for everyone); a polling loop for the whole session; a Flet change, which is U17's (D-212, D-213).<br>• **U17 retires it:** once `on_media_change` reports `accessible_navigation`, `Prefs.screen_reader` follows it at once and the SnackBar closes on that change instead of a read. | Honest at the pin: it reads the same snapshot the app already trusts at start and on resume, only while the one thing that depends on it is on screen; and it fixes the reported case without waiting for an upstream release. |
| D-216 | *(Words wider than their line amended by D-229; the width beside the rail by D-277; the capped list by D-305; the probe's answer also steps detail titles down, D-328.)* **At large text a slot's time goes above its rows: the gutter may take at most a fifth of the width, measured by a probe on every screen** (amends D-158 and D-174 for the largest sizes, D-69's boot gate, ux.md §3.9's `TimeGutter` and `TrackBadge` and §5.2's day list, and section M's `ui/time_gutter.py`; #224, #214).<br>• **The rule:** `time_above(gutter_width, width)` is true when the gutter, padding included, would take more than `MAX_GUTTER_SHARE` (0.2) of the width. Then every list with slots (the Schedule, My schedule, room detail, Open spaces) shows each slot as `Column[TimeAbove, Column[rows]]`: "2:00 PM" on one line in the gutter's `TITLE_SMALL`, 16 dp in and 20 dp down (the rows keep their own 12 dp under it, so the time sits with the rows it heads, not midway between them and the divider or now line over it), the same heading read once (`header=True`, `label`, `exclude_semantics`), and the rows take the whole width. Otherwise the slot keeps D-158's gutter. `slot_layout(label, rows, *, above)` builds either (the Schedule's keyed box wraps it, D-155), `TimeSlot(..., above=)` adds the divider; `above` is a required argument, so no list can forget it, and it is a prop of the memoised day lists and slots (D-153), which re-render only when it flips.<br>• **The measurement:** Flet cannot read the text scale (U16), so `GutterProbe`, a component in `ui/time_gutter.py`, lays out an invisible copy of the widest gutter ("10:00" / "AM" at opacity 0, the gutter's padding) in a 0 dp high box as wide as the screen and takes both widths from `on_size_change` (F51). The box is the View's width, beside the body's `SafeArea`: only in landscape on a phone with a notch or a cutout is a slot narrower, by the side insets (62 dp a side of 874 on the iPhone 17), and there the largest gutter takes 18.0 % of the 750 dp left, still under a fifth, so the answer is the same; accepted rather than wrapping every screen's body in one more column. It writes the answer to `Prefs.time_above`, a device fact beside `reduce_motion`, not persisted and kept by `reset()`, and only once it has both widths, since every screen mounts a probe of its own; an observable notifies only on a change, so a resize with the same answer re-renders nothing. `AppScaffold` puts it under every body (`View(spacing=0)`, so it takes no space) and `BootView` holds it too, under the boot gate's `PrefsCtx`, and the gate waits for its first answer (`Prefs.time_measured`, amends D-69's gate), or at most `RING_DELAY` (1 s, then the ring shows and the lists keep the gutter until a screen's probe answers): the answer is in before the first screen renders, and it follows a text-size change and a rotation while the app runs.<br>• **Badges** (D-174) need no rule of their own: the badge row's `Wrap` already moves a whole badge to a line of its own, and Flutter breaks a label inside a word only when that word is wider than the whole row, which happened only beside a gutter at the largest sizes. With the rows at full width "Registration required" wraps between its words; a word wider than the row (a 320 dp phone at the largest size) would still break, which clipping would make worse (ux.md §3.4).<br>• **Not chosen:** a gutter capped at a fixed share, which broke "10:00" inside itself as the 64 dp gutter had (D-158); the time inline in every row's meta at every size, which drops the scannable gutter where it works; a share of a quarter, under which iOS's first two Accessibility sizes and Android's 200 % kept a gutter of 21 % to 23 % and titles of two or three words a line ("Running Large / Language / Models on Lap…"). | Words breaking inside themselves ("Lightnin" / "g…", "Registratio" / "n required") read as broken at the sizes people choose because they need them (D-33). A fifth separates the sizes cleanly: measured on an iPhone 17 simulator (402 dp, iOS 26.5) the gutter takes 15 % at the default size, 18 % at the largest standard size (72.6 dp), then 20.7 %, 23.3 %, 26.9 % and 33.7 % (83.0 to 135.3 dp) from the first to the fifth Accessibility size; on the Android 14 emulator (411 dp) 14.6 % at 100 %, 19.5 % at 150 % (80.3 dp), 21.0 % at 180 % (86.4 dp) and 22.0 % at 200 % (90.5 dp), and the same 200 % on a 360 dp width (`wm density 480`) 25.1 %; in landscape (874 dp) even the largest gutter takes 15 %. On those devices (release APK and simulator builds checked against `build/python-app`; a throwaway build, never committed, started the fake clock at Friday, May 15, 2:01 PM and logged the probe's widths): at the default sizes the Schedule, a speaker page and a room page look as before; at Android's 200 % and iOS's Accessibility sizes the slots put "2:00 PM" above their rows, titles break between words and "Registration required" wraps as "Registration" / "required"; a font scale changed while the app ran re-sent the widths and the slots returned to the gutter without a restart. A first build placed the probe over the body in a `Stack` (`left`, `top`, `right`), which drew Flutter's grey error box over every screen (F51); a second, with the probe only under the body, opened the Schedule straight from `--route` with the gutter and switched a moment later, after its jump to 2:00 PM had already run against the first layout, so the list stood one slot too far; with the probe in the boot view too it still did at the Accessibility sizes, because each screen's own probe heard the gutter's width before the screen's and answered "beside" for about 0.2 s, under the jump; a probe that answers only once it has both widths fixed that, and a gate that waits for the boot view's answer fixed the start on a loaded host, where the gate had opened before the first answer came (0.7 s after the session started). |
| D-229 | *(Session detail's title at large text amended by D-328; the summary's inset by D-373.)* **At large text a time range breaks only after its dash, a talk's title on a card is `TITLE_MEDIUM`, and the filter summary's "Show all" keeps its inset** (settles #281 and #283; amends D-186's time tile, whose range "never breaks in two", D-217's panel title, D-216's words wider than their line, D-195's summary, and ux.md §3.6's text scaling, §5.2's summary, §5.5's time tile and §5.11's panel title).<br>• **Time ranges: one helper, no word joiner.** `timefmt.on_screen(label)` is how every line shows a time or a range: session detail's time tile, the Keynotes tile, `SessionRow`'s meta line, room detail's "Now" line and `TimeAbove`, which each made their own no-break copy before. Its spaces become no-break spaces, so "AM" keeps to its digits; Unicode then allows a break in "9:45–10:30 AM" only after the en dash (class BA), and Flutter breaks there only when the whole range is wider than its line. A range always starts its own line, so a word joiner at the dash (#281's suggestion) never keeps one whole that would not fit anyway: it only takes away the dash's break, and Flutter then breaks the run inside a time ("9:00 AM–12" / ":30 PM", seen in D-186). Shared text (`share_text()`) and spoken labels keep plain spaces.<br>• **Why a range cannot always stay whole.** Measured on an iPhone 17 simulator (402 dp, iOS 26.5): on the Keynotes tile, beside the 48 dp star, the text column is about 247 dp, and "9:45–10:30 AM" in `BODY_MEDIUM` stays on one line at 100 % and at iOS's 200 % (`accessibility-large`) but breaks after its dash from the fourth Accessibility size on (about 269 dp at the largest); in session detail's tile (`BODY_LARGE`, about 297 dp) "11:45 AM–12:15 PM" breaks after its dash at 200 % and at the largest size, where "11:45 AM–" alone is 245 dp and "12:15 PM" 211 dp. On the Android 14 emulator (411 dp) both ranges stay on one line at 200 %, since Android 14 scales large text less. Keeping them whole would need smaller type, which Flet cannot pick by the text scale (U16); "9:45–" / "10:30 AM" is the break style guides allow.<br>• **Words wider than their line.** Flutter has no hyphenation, so a word wider than its line breaks inside itself (D-216's badges). On a card, a talk's title is `keynote_card.TALK_TITLE`, `TITLE_MEDIUM`, a keynote's and now a panel's: in `TITLE_LARGE` "Community" took about 361 dp of the panel card's 338 dp column at iOS's largest size ("Comm" / "unity"); in `TITLE_MEDIUM` it takes 255 dp, and every 2026 keynote and panel title breaks between its words at every size. People's names keep `TITLE_LARGE`, as their words are short. Session detail's `HEADLINE_SMALL` title is not changed: about 80 of the 345 sessions of 2026 have a word of twelve letters or more, some without a hyphen ("Telecommunications", "Interoperabilidad"), which no heading size above body text would hold at the largest size on a phone; a heading that steps down at the Accessibility sizes, as iOS's own text styles do, waits for the scale (U16) and is a question of its own (#305).<br>• **The filter summary's "Show all"** (`schedule_filter.SHOW_ALL_INSET`, 8 dp): Material shrinks a text button's side padding as text grows (12 dp at 100 %, 8 at 200 %, 4 from 300 %), so a "Show all" on a run of its own started right of the words above it (#283). The button's horizontal padding is pinned at 8 dp, with no vertical padding (Material's 8 dp goes at large text too, and at 100 % the 40 dp minimum height holds the label), and the words start 8 dp into the wrapping row, which has no spacing of its own, where the 8 dp gap after the mark was; the row's right margin is 12 dp, so at 100 % the label still ends 20 dp from the edge. The 48 dp target, `schedule.show_all` and the label are unchanged. | Checked on a simulator build whose bundle matched `build/python-app` (an iPhone 17 simulator of #281's own and, for scrolling, the shared "PyCon iPhone 17") and an arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched it on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`), light and dark. The summary's left edges, measured on the screenshots: at iOS's largest size "space" and a wrapped "Show all" at 45.0 and 48.7 dp before, 45.0 and 44.7 dp after (the "S" and "s" glyphs' own side bearings); at Android's 200 % on a 360 dp width (`wm density 480`) 44.3 and 43.0 dp; at 100 % "Show all" ends 21.3 dp from the edge as before. The D&I panel's title wraps between its words at 100 %, 200 % and the largest size on iOS and at 200 % on Android. The ranges broke as above, after the dash only. |
| D-328 | **At large text a detail page's title steps down from `HEADLINE_SMALL` to `TITLE_MEDIUM`, when `GutterProbe` says a slot's time goes above its rows** (settles #305 under D-101; amends D-229's words wider than their line, D-186's title, the titles of D-199's track page, D-203's speaker detail and the sponsor detail, ux.md §3.6's scale and text scaling, §5.5, §5.7, §5.10 and the sponsor detail; U16's interim).<br>• **The rule:** `ui/scaffold.py`'s `PageTitle(text, *, identifier, large_text, centred=False, selectable=False)` is the level-1 heading of session, track, speaker and sponsor detail (`<screen>.title`): `HEADLINE_SMALL` (`PAGE_TITLE`), or `TITLE_MEDIUM` (`PAGE_TITLE_AT_LARGE_TEXT`) when `large_text`, which each screen passes as `Prefs.time_above` (D-216). `large_text` is required, so no detail page can forget it. Welcome's title, About this app's name and Now's header keep `HEADLINE_SMALL`: their words are short ("Welcome to PyCon US 2026", "Day 4 of 5 · Saturday") and fit at every size.<br>• **How the app tells the sizes apart:** Flet cannot read the text scale at the pin: `PageMediaData` carries padding, insets, the pixel ratio, the orientation and the 24-hour flag, and nothing else (`packages/flet/lib/src/widgets/page_media.dart:56-63`; U16). The probe's answer already separates the sizes, and it is the right question: whether a word fits depends on the width as much as on the scale. On a 402 dp iPhone 17 it turns at the first Accessibility size (the gutter takes 20.7 % of the width), on a wider phone a size or so later, on a tablet only near the largest size if at all, where the line holds the words; it follows a text size change while a page is open. On Android it turns at 180 % on a 411 dp phone (21.0 %, D-216), where the title then takes a row title's type too: one rule on both platforms, as the slots.<br>• **Why `TITLE_MEDIUM`, two steps rather than one:** measured with the bundled Roboto against session detail's 370 dp line on a 402 dp phone, taking each of 2026's 180 distinct session titles' longest word (a line may break after a hyphen or a slash), `HEADLINE_SMALL` breaks 1, 4, 36 and 70 titles inside a word at the second to fifth Accessibility sizes, `TITLE_LARGE` (one step down, as the issue asked) 0, 3, 16 and 43, `TITLE_MEDIUM` 0, 0, 0 and 3; `BODY_LARGE` breaks the same 3 ("subrepresentadas", "Interoperabilidad", "Communications" at the fifth size), and body text keeps the user's size (D-33). `TITLE_MEDIUM` is as large as body text and heavier (500), as iOS's own headings come down to body size at its Accessibility sizes (Headline is body size, semibold); still the page's level-1 heading for screen readers, and first on the page.<br>• **Accepted:** between the largest standard size and the first Accessibility size the title gets smaller (on iOS 32.5 → 26.4 dp) while body text grows (23 → 28 dp); from there it grows with the text again. A word wider than a body-text line still breaks inside itself (D-229).<br>• **Not chosen:** waiting for U16 (a factor alone does not say whether a word fits: the width decides, and the probe answers that today); one step down to `TITLE_LARGE`, which leaves 43 titles broken at the largest size; a title that grows less than body text, as iOS's do, which needs the factor, since Flutter scales every `size` again (U16's upgrade); measuring each title's longest word, a probe per page and a second layout for a rule the probe already gives. | Words breaking inside themselves read as broken at the sizes people choose because they need them (D-33), and a session's title is the first thing its page says. The counts are Pillow's advances of the bundled `Roboto-Regular` and `Roboto-Medium` at Flutter's M3 sizes and iOS's factors (body 17 pt to 23, 28, 33, 40, 47 and 53 pt), which agree with D-229's on-device widths to 4 % ("Community": 351 dp computed, about 361 measured, in `TITLE_LARGE`; 258 and 255 in `TITLE_MEDIUM`). Compared on an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) from one `ios-simulator` build, with the bundle's compiled `ui/scaffold.py` swapped for each option (#566): at the third Accessibility size "Interoperabilidad de hardware…" (session 137) broke as "Interoperabilid" / "ad" in `HEADLINE_SMALL` and "Interoperabilida" / "d" in `TITLE_LARGE`, and held whole in `TITLE_MEDIUM`; at the fourth "procrastination" (session 47) broke as "procrastinat" / "ion" in `HEADLINE_SMALL` and held whole in `TITLE_MEDIUM`; at the fifth "Interoperabilidad" breaks in `TITLE_MEDIUM` too, as computed. Set back to the largest standard size with the page open, the title returned to `HEADLINE_SMALL` without a restart, and grew again from the third to the fifth size as the probe followed. |
| D-345 | **At the largest text sizes the Schedule's filter summary, status strip and zone note head each day's list and scroll away with it** (settles #331 under D-101; amends where D-20 puts the summary, "under the day tabs", and where D-32's strip and D-240's zone note sit, at those sizes only; ux.md §5.2).<br>• **The rule:** when `Prefs.time_above` (the probe's answer that a slot's time goes above its rows, D-216, the signal D-328 uses), `screens/schedule_head.py`'s `ScheduleHead` (the summary with Show all, the strip, the zone note) is the first item of every day's `ListView` (`DayList(head=...)`, `ListHead`: under a `ScrollKey("head")` anchor and reporting its height, so D-331's places count it first and a place at the head is put back by key), and the column under the tabs holds an empty placeholder in its place, so the day pages keep their index and are never rebuilt (#313). Below those sizes nothing changes: the head is fixed between the tabs and the pages. The strip's state is the Schedule's own (`use_strip(auto_refresh=True)`, one tick and one refresh check), and each day's head draws only its `Strip` value (`strip_view()`): nothing under a memoised day list may re-render on its own (F150). Flutter's `TabBarView` builds only the page on screen, so a screen reader hears one live region. What the head says is a frozen `Head(summary, zone_note, strip)`, equal from render to render, so a star tap still re-renders one slot (D-153).<br>• **Accepted:** at those sizes the summary and the strip are out of sight once the user scrolls down a day; the filter button's dot still says when the filter differs from the default (D-20), and a re-tap of the tab scrolls back to them. A strip that appears or goes while a day is scrolled moves the rows under it by its height, as the fixed strip moved the list's top edge before.<br>• **Not chosen:** shortening the lines to one ellipsised line each (truncated words at the sizes people choose because they need them, D-33, and three rows of the largest text still fixed above the list); moving them into the overflow or a sheet (D-32: the data state is always shown while it is not fresh); putting the head only in the shown day's list (it would pop in after each swipe); a collapsing app bar (no `SliverAppBar` or `NestedScrollView` in Flet at the pin). | At iOS's largest Larger Text on an iPhone 17 the fixed app bar, tabs, summary (four lines with Show all) and strip (three lines and Refresh) ended about 1,540 of 2,622 px down and the bottom bar took the last 880, leaving about 200 px, less than a session title's line, for the day (#331); D-194 solved the same for a room's page. |
| D-182 | *(Copy goes through `copy_text()`, and its failure log names what was not copied, D-263; the icon alignment of a `LinkTile` with a subtitle amended by D-188; the Copy identifier on Help & safety by D-281.)* **The link rule, the shared states and where a link opens** (records ux.md §3.9's `EmptyState`, `SkeletonList` and `LinkTile`'s external form, and §4.7 for INFO-14; #83).<br>• **One rule, decided by the target, in a module of its own:** `ui/links.py` (used by `ui/rows.py`'s `LinkTile` and, from #91, by `Markdown`'s `on_tap_link`; `rows.py` is where P1's rows go, D-66). Its pure `link_kind(target, *, app=False)` returns a `LinkKind`: a location starting with `/` is a page (pushed, `CHEVRON_RIGHT`); `http(s)` is a web page (`LaunchMode.IN_APP_BROWSER_VIEW`), or, with `app=True`, a social profile or a map (`EXTERNAL_APPLICATION`); `geo:` is an app; `tel:` and `mailto:` are a phone or an email (`EXTERNAL_APPLICATION`); any other scheme raises `ValueError`, and so does `app=True` on anything but a web address or a `geo:` link, so the flag never does nothing silently. The caller marks a profile or a map: a host cannot say it (Mastodon has many instances). `open_link(svc, target, app=...)` applies the rule for rows and for `Markdown`'s `on_tap_link` (#91); a launch that raises shows "Couldn't open the link", and the log names the kind and the error's class only (`Could not open the email link: RuntimeError`), never the target nor the error's text, which repeats it (Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` names the intent, a timeout the call's arguments; a `mailto:` holds an address, D-72).<br>• **External rows:** one merged node, `Semantics(link=True)`, whose `OPEN_IN_NEW` icon has the `semantics_label` "Opens in browser" ("Opens in another app"), read after the title: the icon says it visually, the label says it to screen readers, whatever their hint setting. Not `Semantics.hint_text`, which never reaches Flutter at the pin: the Dart control reads `hint` (#187). Phone and email rows have no trailing icon (their title is the verb, "Call the Code of Conduct hotline") and a 48 dp Copy `IconButton` beside the merged node, a stop of its own (`<identifier>.copy`), which copies the number or the address (`contact_of()`) and says "Phone number copied" ("Email address copied"), except on Android 13 (API 33) and later, whose own overlay confirms every copy (`page.get_device_info()`; Android 12 and earlier, and a version that cannot be read, get the SnackBar); a failure says "Couldn't copy the phone number" ("…the email address") on every platform, logged like a launch. No `can_launch_url` probe until U6.<br>• **Icons beside the first line:** link rows use `ListTileTitleAlignment.TITLE_HEIGHT` (a 56 dp row's icons centred; a 72 dp row's leading icon, and past 72 dp both icons, 16 dp from the top), and Copy is top-aligned 4 dp down, level with the leading icon, so at 200 % text no icon drifts to the middle of a four-line title.<br>• **Services:** `Services` gains `launcher` (`ft.UrlLauncher()`) and `clipboard` (`ft.Clipboard()`), as architecture §4.2 planned. The in-app `LinkTile` stays a plain builder, cheap in long lists; its external form is a component (`_ExternalLinkTile`), because a tap needs `use_services()` and a builder called in a loop cannot call hooks (D-70).<br>• **`EmptyState(title, *, icon, body=None, action=None, on_action=None, disabled=False, identifier=None)`** (`ui/states.py`): a 48 dp outlined icon in `on_surface_variant` (decorative), the title in `TITLE_MEDIUM` as a heading node of its own (`Semantics(header=True, container=True)`: without `container`, Flutter merges the flag and the body's text into the live region, and the whole state reads as one heading), an optional `BODY_MEDIUM` line, both in one `Semantics(container=True, live_region=True)` carrying the identifier, and at most one `FilledTonalButton`, a stop of its own, `disabled` while its action runs; centred, no fixed height. `action` and `on_action` come as a pair (`ValueError` otherwise). Every empty state names a way out: the button, or its sentence where no button can help (ux.md §4.6's search miss and My schedule unavailable).<br>• **`SkeletonList(label="Loading schedule")`**, a component: five session-shaped placeholder rows in `surface_container_highest` under one `Semantics(label, container=True, exclude_semantics=True)`, in `ft.Shimmer(period=prefs.motion_ms(1500), highlight_color=surface_container_low)`; under reduced motion there is no `Shimmer` (D-173). Like a progress indicator it shows work running and goes with it, so it is not D-34's ambient motion. A screen without data shows it until the first fetch has failed (`problem is None`), then `EmptyState` "Can't load the schedule" with "Try again", its body by `problem` (ux.md §4.6), which stays during a retry with its button disabled (`disabled=refreshing`): swapping it for the skeleton would remove the focused button and lose a screen reader's place; the strip's bar shows the work. | A hint would be the natural slot, but the one Flet sends never arrives, and an icon label is read whatever the screen reader's hint setting. Copy beside the row, not only after a failure: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a `tel:` tap opened nothing and raised nothing, so a failure SnackBar alone would never show. Checked with a throwaway build (not committed) whose More list had a web, a profile, a `tel:` and a `mailto:` row and whose year had no snapshot and a feed URL that never answered: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a tap on the web row opened us.pycon.org in the in-app Safari view, the profile row handed off to Safari ("◀ Conf Companion"), and Copy put `+1-555-010-0199` and `coc@example.org` on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) with "Phone number copied" / "Email address copied"; on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) the web row launched Chrome (its first-run screen), Copy showed the SnackBar, and `uiautomator dump` read the web row as one node "PyCon US website, us.pycon.org, Opens in browser" with `resource-id` `preview.web` and Copy as "Copy phone number" (`preview.phone.copy`; its button is the same tooltip-and-button pair as the app bar's Search). On both, the Schedule showed the skeleton for the 10 s the fetch waited, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again; with motion on, two frames 0.8 s apart differed in 754 (iOS) and 808 (Android) rows of the skeleton, and under iOS Reduce Motion or Android's animation scales at 0 ("Reduced motion is on") in none. Light, dark and the largest text (iOS) or 200 % (Android): every state and row reflowed with nothing clipped, and the Copy icon lines up with the rows' `OPEN_IN_NEW` once its button sits 12 dp from the edge (8 dp put it 4 dp out). The review round (#189) rebuilt it with a feed host that accepts and never answers: at the largest text (iOS) and 200 % (Android) every link row's icons, Copy included, sat beside the title's first line, where M3's default had centred them against four-line blocks; a tap on Try again left the state in place with the button disabled (`uiautomator dump`: `enabled="false"`, same bounds) and the strip saying "Updating the schedule…"; the dump shows the title as a node of its own inside `schedule.no_data`, which now carries only the body; and on the Android 14 emulator Copy brought the system's clipboard overlay and no SnackBar. The log rule: in a probe on the previous code, Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` text and Flet's timeout text put the address in the log twice through `exc_info`. |
| D-226 | *(The hook's text and `Ref` amended by D-243; its `focusable` added by D-330.)* **The list filter's text, Clear and count line are shared, and the count has one padding** (settles #249; amends D-189, D-193 and D-202 where they build the field's state, Clear and the count, and section M's `ui/fields.py`).<br>• **The hook:** `ui/fields.py`'s `use_field_query(initial="")`, beside `SearchField`, returns a frozen `FieldQuery`: `value`, the controlled text (D-157); `changed`, the field's `on_change`; `ref`, a fresh `Ref` each render, which holds this render's field, as the screens' copies did; `clear()`, which empties the text and then focuses the field, since a Clear goes away when used (D-182); and `focus()` alone, for search's "Show all results". A plain function that calls one `use_state`, so the screens call it once per render, before any early return (D-70). Speakers and Sprints pass its handlers to the field and their no-match "Clear filter" as they are; search wraps `changed` and `clear` in handlers of its own that also write `ViewMemory.search_query` and, for Clear, the searched query at once (D-193). No callback parameter: one screen keeps extra state, and its own handlers say so where they are read.<br>• **The count:** `found_count(line, *, identifier)`, the one copy of the three screens' `_found_count()`: a polite `Semantics(live_region=True)` line (`<screen>.count`) in `BODY_SMALL` and `on_surface_variant` (ux.md §9). The screen still words it ("5 speakers", "3 projects", search's `summary()`) and shows it only while the text finds something; a miss has its no-match state, which announces itself.<br>• **One padding:** 16 dp from the sides, 4 dp under the field or the chips and **8 dp over the list**, Sprints' padding: the line is pinned and the list scrolls under it, so a card's edge, an avatar or a row's ripple never touches the text. Speakers and search had no bottom padding, so their first heading or row now starts 8 dp lower while a filter applies; nothing else moves. Not chosen: no bottom padding on all three (a scrolled card touched the line, the reason Sprints had it), or 4 dp (under the 8 dp ux.md §3.6 keeps within a group).<br>• **Matching unchanged:** `filter_directory()`, `filter_projects()` and `search()` are as they were; what each screen counts and when it shows the line too. | The pinned filter was written three times nearly verbatim (the hint and Clear constants, `changed`, the per-render `Ref` with an async `clear()` that refocuses, and `_found_count()`), and the copies had drifted: only Sprints' count had a bottom padding (#249, raised by #243's second opinion). One hook and one builder put the D-182 refocus and the live region in one place, where #250's iOS live-region fix will land too. In real Flet sessions (`tests/test_fields.py`) the probe's field keeps every keystroke, Clear empties it and focuses that render's field, `focus()` keeps the text, and the count is a live region with its identifier, style and padding; `tests/test_speakers.py`, `tests/test_sprints.py` and `tests/test_search_screen.py` pass unchanged (the count's text, its live region, Clear refocusing). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (iPhone 17; debug builds checked against `build/python-app`), the filtered lists of a throwaway build that started each filter with a query ("mu", "python web", "chen"; never committed) and of the same patch over main differ only below the count line on Speakers and search, by 8 dp, at the default size and at the largest Larger Text, and not at all on Sprints; on the shared simulator, driven by taps, "m", Clear, then "u" left "u" in the field with "59 speakers", "Clear filter" and search's Clear kept the cursor in the field, and "python web" showed "5 projects". On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` read `speakers.count`, `sprints.count` and `search.count` under their fields, and after each Clear the field was empty and focused, and the next key went into it. |
| D-263 | *(`check_info()` takes the conference too, D-282.)* **The info pages share a year-data module, a body and a copy helper: `year_info.py`, `ui/info_page.py`'s `InfoBody` and `ui/links.py`'s `copy_text()`** (records INFO-05 and DATA-23's mechanism for the six info pages of ux.md §6.7; amends D-182's Copy and its log line, and section M's top-level, `ui/` and `links.py` entries; #375).<br>• **`year_info.py`:** the info pages' `content.toml` tables (Wi-Fi, Help & safety, Venue & hours, About PyCon US, About the PSF, Social), each a dataclass built by `year_schema.build()` as a field of `config.Content`, and ~~`check_info(content, file)`~~ `check_info(content, conference, file)` (D-282), which `config._check_content()` calls on every load, after the other pages' checks, so a table's rule fails the load with a `YearDataError` naming the file and the key. It takes the built `Content` (imported for its type only, since `config` imports the module), so each page's issue adds a field and its rules ~~without changing the call~~ (the venue hours added the conference to it, D-282). No page has a table yet: each one's issue adds its own. A module of its own, as `year_pages.py` (D-205) and `year_keynotes.py` (D-217) are: `config.py` has 335 lines and six tables would take it past D-66's ~400.<br>• **`InfoBody(blocks, *, block, screen, empty)`:** the body each info page hands `AppScaffold`, one `expand=True` `ListView` (8 dp above, 16 below). With the page's blocks from its year data it lists them; without them its one item is `FeedBlock`, which shows the feed's own block for the page (`ConferenceData.content_md[block]`, converted once per feed version, D-130: `wifi`, `code-of-conduct`, `venues-hours`, `about`, `about-psf`, `social-media`) through `feed_markdown()` (`<screen>.feed`, links by D-197's rule); a missing or blank block, or no feed on the device yet, shows the page's `EmptyState`, which names the way out (D-182). Never a skeleton or a loader: what is on the device renders at once (INFO-05; Ionic held each page behind "Fetching latest…" for up to 10 s). `InfoBody` is a plain builder with no hooks, and only `FeedBlock`, a component, reads `Conference`, so a page whose year data gives its blocks is not re-rendered by a refresh (D-165's rule). **Gutters:** `GUTTER`, 16 dp, for texts and cards (the feed block's inset); rows keep their own `ListTile` padding, so their ripple runs edge to edge, as M3 lists do. The reading width (720 dp, D-09) is the shared width class's once #440 lands, never a cap of the page's own.<br>• **`copy_text(svc, text, copied, failed)`:** the one copy. It confirms once, with `copied`, in the app's SnackBar, except from Android 13 (API 33) on, whose own overlay confirms every copy (D-182's rule, now for every copy); a failure says `failed` on every platform and logs `<failed>: <error class>`, our words and never the value, which the platform's error text repeats (D-72). `copy_contact()` is `copy_text()` with D-182's words, so its failure line reads "Couldn't copy the phone number: TimeoutError" where it read "Could not copy the phone link: TimeoutError"; nothing on screen changes. A caller's `failed` names what was not copied ("Couldn't copy the password"), never the value. | Six pages built in parallel need one place for their tables, one body and one copy, or each would grow its own loader, fallback and confirmation. Bundled data first and the feed's block second keeps one source per fact (DATA-23) and a page that works offline from the first launch. Tests: `tests/test_info_page.py` (the blocks listed with no feed block; without them `FeedBlock`; the 2026 feed's `wifi` block as selectable Markdown 16 dp in; a blank block, 2025's feed without `wifi` and no feed at all show the empty state), `tests/test_year_info.py` (every year's load passes its content and file to `check_info()`, whose error is the load's) and `tests/test_links.py` (`copy_text()` copies as given, confirms on iOS and Android 12, not from Android 13 on, says a failure on iOS and Android 14 and logs no value). A throwaway build (never committed) routed `/more/wifi` and `/more/missing` to probe pages over `InfoBody([])` with a Copy action calling `copy_text()`. On an iPhone 17 simulator of #375's own (iOS 26.5, debug build checked against `build/python-app`): the 2026 feed's Wi-Fi block rendered at once ("Conference Wi-Fi", the SSID and password as code spans) 16 dp from the sides; a Maestro tap on Copy put `pyconLB2026` on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) and "Password copied" showed; in dark, the missing block showed the empty state. On my own Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read the block as `probe.feed` with its text and Copy as one node `probe.copy` "Copy password"; after a Maestro tap on Copy no "Password copied" SnackBar showed, as the system confirms the copy from API 33. |
| D-281 | **Help & safety: the year's `[[safety_contacts]]` and `[help]` tables, the Report a concern card first, then the places and the Code of Conduct; a contact ships only from a public page** (records INFO-07, INFO-11 and ux.md §6.7's Help & safety under D-45 and D-263; amends D-182's Copy identifier for this page, ux.md §6.7's order and architecture §8.1's example; #411, P2-51).<br>• **Year data** (`year_info.py`): `[[safety_contacts]]`, each a `SafetyContact(label, kind, value, source, checked)`: `label` what a tap does ("Email the Code of Conduct team"), `kind` `tel`, `mailto` or `https`, `value` the number in E.164 (`+15625550100`), the address or the URL, `source` the public `https` page that shows it, `checked` the day it was last checked there. The loader refuses a contact without `source` or `checked` (a missing key), a source that is not `https://` with a host, a `tel` value that is not E.164, an address that is not one, an `https` value that is not one, an unknown kind, a blank label and two contacts of one kind, whose rows would share an identifier. D-45's yearly re-check is a test, not a loader rule (the device has no reason to read last year's data): each bundled year's `checked` dates are on or before today and after both the year before's `last_day` and its check dates, so a folder copied forward with last year's dates fails; a rollover re-checks each contact on its `source` and updates `checked`. `[help]` is a `HelpInfo` of seven fields: `in_person` (the card's last line: when someone is in danger, local emergency services first, any staff member or volunteer helping find the number; then reporting to any staff member, who can help reach hotel or venue security or the police), `info_desk`, `lost_and_found` and `quiet_room` (a line each), `coc_summary` (in our words) and `coc_url` and `procedures_url` (`https://`); the loader refuses a blank line and a link that is not `https://`. Both tables are optional, but `[help]` needs at least one contact (the loader refuses it otherwise): the page's first stop is the card, and `in_person` lives on it. `Content` gains `safety_contacts` and `help`.<br>• **2026 lists the report email alone.** The hotline Ionic's page called (a Long Beach number in `coc.page.html` and `help.page.html`) is on no public page: on 2026-10-20 neither the PSF's Code of Conduct (`policies.python.org/us.pycon.org/code-of-conduct/`) nor its Procedures for Reporting Incidents lists a phone number, and the 2026 feed's `code-of-conduct` block says to find a staff member or email `pycon-us-report@python.org`. So the email and the staff line ship, as #411 asked; a number joins as a `tel` entry the year a public page shows it. The staff line opens with the danger pointer because the Procedures page does ("contact the appropriate crisis number, non-emergency number, or police number", a volunteer or staff member helping find it; "PyCon US is not equipped to handle emergency situations"), and an email is the wrong channel in an emergency; hotel or venue security and the police are the Code of Conduct page's ("help participants contact hotel/venue security or local law enforcement"). A wording choice settled on #411 under D-101 after the second opinion.<br>• **The page** (`screens/help.py`, `/more/help`, `InfoBody` with `block="code-of-conduct"`): first the **Report a concern** card, `Card(OUTLINED)` with a 1 dp `error` edge and 12 dp corners, 16 dp in (`GUTTER`), not one node (`semantic_container=False`, D-211): a level-2 heading (`help.report`) with `REPORT_OUTLINED` in `error`, so colour is not the only signal (D-33); a `LinkTile` per contact, title the action and subtitle the value (`contact_line()`: a North American number in groups, `+1 562-555-0100`, which a screen reader reads by groups; a web page by its host), one node read as action plus value, with Copy beside a phone or an email (`copy_contact()`, D-182, D-263); then `in_person`. Then "At the venue" with the three places, each a `ListTile` that opens nothing, laid out as a link row with a subtitle (`SUBTITLED_TOP`) and merged into one node through `named()`; then "Code of Conduct", the summary as selectable `BODY_LARGE` text and two external `LinkTile`s, "Read the Code of Conduct" and "How reports are handled", with the host as subtitle. A year without either table shows the feed's block (`help.feed`), and without that "No safety details yet" (`HEALTH_AND_SAFETY_OUTLINED`, `help.empty`) with no button. Ionic's "Report a Website Issue" is dropped; the location cards (#418) and "Report a problem with the app" are other issues.<br>• **Identifiers:** `help.report` (the heading), `help.report.<kind>` (a row) and `help.copy.<kind>` (its Copy, `<screen>.<element>.<key>` as section M has it, where D-182's `<identifier>.copy` would have been four parts: `LinkTile` gains `copy_identifier=`), `help.info_desk`, `help.lost_and_found`, `help.quiet_room`, `help.coc_summary`, `help.coc`, `help.procedures`.<br>• **More:** an "At the venue" section after Programme with Help & safety (`SUPPORT_OUTLINED`, `more.help`); Venue & hours and Wi-Fi join it with their pages (D-169). | One place to look under stress, and its first stop is how to report (Ionic put the report rows after a page of reading, on two pages). A contact is the one thing on the page that must never be wrong, so each names where it was checked and when, and the loader refuses one without them. The order of the rest follows the stress: where to go in person, then the rules. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK): `uiautomator dump` read the title, then `help.report` "Report a concern" as the first node after it, `help.report.mailto` one `Button` "Email the Code of Conduct team / pycon-us-report@python.org", `help.copy.mailto` a `Button` "Copy email address", the staff line, the level-2 "At the venue", each place one node read as its name and line, "Code of Conduct" and the summary; a tap on the email row handed off to Gmail, Copy put the address on the clipboard (the keyboard's clipboard suggestion offered it in Search's field) with no SnackBar, as API 34 confirms copies itself; in airplane mode More › At the venue › Help & safety showed the whole page at once and Back returned to More; dark at 200 % text reflowed the card, the places and the two links with nothing clipped. On an iPhone 17 simulator of #411's own (iOS 26.5, debug build checked against `build/python-app`): the page as on Android; a Maestro tap on `help.copy.mailto` put `pycon-us-report@python.org` on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) with "Email address copied"; a tap on the email row opened nothing and said nothing, since the simulator has no Mail app and Flet drops url_launcher's `false` (U79, #498; D-182 saw the same for `tel:`), so Copy is the way there; at the largest Accessibility size in dark the heading wrapped to two lines, its icon moved beside the middle of them, hence the icon top-aligned to the first line. |
| D-282 | **Venue hours are year data, `[[venue_hours]]` in `content.toml`, and one pure rule says per service what is open at an instant: `core/hours.py`'s `hours_status()`, `None` outside the days the services open** (records MAP-09's structured hours and DATA-23 for Venue & hours, the data #413's page shows; amends D-263's `check_info(content, file)`, which takes the conference too, and section M's `core/`, `year_info.py` and `year_schema.py` entries; #412).<br>• **The data:** each `[[venue_hours]]` entry is a `core.hours.ServiceHours`: `service` (the name, unique in the year without regard to case), optional `location` (where the venue signs it), `pin` (a `map.toml` key, checked there once the year has a `map.toml`) and `note` (one more line), and `open`, an array of `Opening`s (`date`, a conference-local day; `opens` and `closes`, TOML local times such as `07:30:00`, which `year_schema` now builds as `datetime.time`). The dataclasses are `core`'s, as `core/rules.py`'s `DisplayTimeOverride` is, since the pure rule reads them and `core` imports nothing above it; their rules are `year_info.check_venue_hours()`, which `check_info()` runs.<br>• **The rules**, each failure a `YearDataError` naming file, entry and field (`2026/content.toml: venue_hours[0].open[1].closes: 18:00:00 is not after opens (18:30:00)`): no blank text; no service twice; an opening on a day of the conference (`first_day` to `last_day`), its times in whole minutes (seconds `00`, since the labels and the rule work to the minute), opening before it closes, the same day (no opening runs past midnight), and not overlapping the service's other openings that day, though one may close as the next opens; a service with no `open` has a `note` saying why. The first rule that needs the conference's facts, so `check_info()` takes them, called with the content and the conference by `_check_content()`.<br>• **The rule at an instant:** `hours_status(hours, now, zone, *, use_24h)` gives, by service name in the data's order, each service with openings its `HoursStatus` (`kind`, `label`, `spoken`): "Open now · until 6:00 PM" (spoken "Open now, until 6:00 PM"; `open`, from `opens` up to, not including, `closes`: D-179's half-open span, so the opening minute is open and the closing minute closed); else its next opening, "Opens 7:30 AM" later today, "Opens tomorrow at 7:30 AM", or "Opens Fri, May 15 at 9:00 AM" (spoken "Friday, May 15"); else "Closed today". A service without openings has no status. It is `None` off-season: on a conference-local day before the first or after the last of `days(hours)`, the dates any service opens on, where every label would say "Closed" or name a day a week away, so the page shows the hours alone. Hours are wall times in the conference's zone (D-43): an instant in any zone gets the same answer. The words follow the device's 12/24-hour setting (D-35).<br>• **2026** is transcribed from the bundled feed's `venues-hours` block (captured 2026-10-11): Registration, Information desk, Swag pickup (with its Sunday T-shirt note) and Quiet room with their hours; Lost & found (Room 203AB) with a note and no hours of its own, since the feed sends attendees to the information desk; and Luggage check with no location and the note that there is none this year, which answers the question an attendee leaving on Sunday asks. `tests/test_year_hours.py` reads the snapshot's block and fails when the two disagree: a section added, renamed or reordered, a location or an opening changed, or a line besides them (the T-shirt line, Lost & found's, Luggage check's) changed without its note, so a snapshot update asks for the year data to follow. 2025 has no table, and its page shows the feed's block (D-263). | MAP-09: Ionic split the site's HTML and never said what was open; a page that answers "is it open now?" offline needs the hours as data and one rule, tested to the minute, which #413's page, a quick-info button on Now (#419) and the location cards (#418) can share. One call for all the services keeps the season one decision rather than one per card. A next opening named outright ("Opens tomorrow at 7:30 AM") says more than "Closed", and "Closed today" is kept for a service with nothing left. Tests: `tests/test_hours.py` under both machine zones (the opening minute open, the closing minute closed, "Opens 7:30 AM" before opening, the next opening after closing, today or a later day, "Closed today" with none left, `None` before, after and a year on, the same answer in UTC and in Kiritimati, 24-hour words, a lunch break, a naive instant refused) and `tests/test_year_hours.py` (2026 against the snapshot's block, section by section and line by line, 2025 without a table, each rule refused with its message, a time with seconds among them, touching openings and the conference's first and last days accepted). No device check: no screen shows the hours until #413. |
| D-313 | **Venue & hours (`/more/venue`, `screens/venue.py`): an address card with Directions and Copy address, then a card per service whose status is a non-interactive label, re-rendered on the minute; Directions opens a maps app at a point from `conference.toml`** (records MAP-09's page and MAP-11 on D-282's rule; amends ux.md §6.7's "status chip" and D-45's `conference.toml` list; #413).<br>• **The data:** `conference.toml` gains two optional entries: `address` (2026: the feed's `venues-hours` block's "300 E Ocean Blvd, Long Beach, CA 90802") and `[directions]`, a `year_info.Directions` of `label`, `latitude` and `longitude` (TOML floats; `year_schema` now builds `float`), checked by `check_directions()`: no blank label, a latitude in ±90 and a longitude in ±180. 2026's point is Ionic's Pine Ave entrance (33.764444, -118.191722, "Long Beach Convention Center (Pine Ave Entrance)"), which an OpenStreetMap reverse lookup puts at the convention center's west end, 300 E Ocean Blvd (checked 2026-10-21).<br>• **Directions** (`venue.directions`, a `FilledTonalButton`, a link read "Directions, Opens in another app"): by `page.platform` at the tap, Ionic's two forms, `https://maps.apple.com/?q=<label>&ll=<lat>,<lng>&dirflg=w` (walking) on iOS and `geo:<lat>,<lng>?q=<lat>,<lng>(<label>)` on Android (`directions_url()`), through `open_link(app=True)`, so a launch that raises says "Couldn't open the link" (D-182); one the platform refuses (url_launcher's `false`, no maps app) opens nothing and says nothing, since the pin drops that result (U79, #498), and Copy address is the way there until a pin returns it and `open_link()` handles `false`. **Copy address** (`venue.copy_address`, an `OutlinedButton`) goes through `copy_text()`: "Address copied" / "Couldn't copy the address" (D-263). Both sit in a wrapping row under the venue's name (a level-2 heading, `venue.address`) and the selectable address; a year without `[directions]` has Copy alone, and one without `address` no card.<br>• **A service's card** (outlined, so `live` sits on the surface; not one node, D-211): its name as a level-2 heading (`venue.service.<key>`, the name's letters and digits joined by `_`: `lost_found`); its status, when `hours_status()` gives one: a decorative 18 dp icon (`CHECK_CIRCLE_OUTLINE` open, `SCHEDULE` later today, `EVENT_OUTLINED` another day, `DO_NOT_DISTURB_ON_OUTLINED` closed today) and `LABEL_LARGE` words read as the rule's `spoken` (`<key>.status`), in `live` for Open now and `on_surface_variant` otherwise; **never `ft.Chip`**, since a tap does nothing; its location; a line per opening, "Thu, May 14 · 7:30 AM–6:00 PM", breaking at the "·" or after the range's dash (D-229), never inside the day or a time, one node read "Thursday, May 14, 7:30 AM to 6:00 PM", today's in bold and read with ", today"; its note.<br>• **Time:** the page calls `use_minute()`, so labels, today's bold and the zone note follow `clock.now()` (the Developer fake clock too); off-season no labels (D-282). One render a minute for the whole page is cheap (six cards) and keeps the season one decision (D-282). A point rather than the street address lands the maps app at the door attendees use without geocoding, and Ionic's forms were already proven on both platforms.
