| D-283 | **About the PSF: `content.toml`'s `[psf]`, the mission once, one Donate button, and three sections of external rows each led by an icon or a brand's mark** (records INFO-06 for ux.md §6.7; adds the first table to D-263's `year_info.py`, an `icon` key beside the issue's `brand`, a control as `LinkTile`'s leading and More's About section, D-169; #415).<br>• **The table:** `[psf]` has `mission` and `donate_url`, and `[[psf.links]]` has `section` (`get_involved`, `python_resources` or `follow`, `year_info.PSF_SECTIONS`, the page's order), `label`, `url` and, optionally, `brand` (a `ui/brands.py` key, D-264), `icon` (an outlined `ft.Icons` name, as a track's icon is) and `app` (a profile, which opens in its own app, INFO-14). Built by `year_schema.build()` as `PsfInfo`/`PsfLink`, the `Content.psf` field. `check_psf()`, called by `check_info()` on every load, refuses a blank mission or label, any URL that is not `https://` with a host (so the feed's `http://pyfound.blogspot.com/` is https in the data), an unknown section, two labels that give one row key (the row's identifier is `psf.link.<semantics_key(label)>`, so "PSF blog" and "PSF Blog" clash) or a label whose key is empty, and a link with both a brand and an icon. For that check `semantics_key()` moved to `core/slugs.py`, which has no Flet, and `ui/semantics.py` re-exports it (amends D-200's module). The loader imports no Flet, so a test checks every bundled year's brands against `BRANDS` and its icons against `ft.Icons`, as the tracks' icons are checked. 2026 has 15 links (mission and links from the feed's `about-psf` block and Ionic's page, every URL answering 200 on 2026-10-20); 2025 has none.<br>• **The page** (`screens/psf.py`, `/more/psf`): `InfoBody` with a level-2 "Mission" heading, then the mission once (`psf.mission`, `BODY_LARGE`; Ionic had it in its card and again in the feed's block under it) and `FilledButton("Donate to the PSF")`, which keeps its own width with an `OPEN_IN_NEW` icon. The button is `links.external()`'s link (`psf.donate`) and is read "Donate to the PSF, Opens in browser", as the lightning talks' sign-up is (D-205). It sits under the mission, so the call to action is on the first screen, and it is the page's only donate link (Ionic had Donate as a row and as the button). Then "Get involved", "Python resources" and "Follow the PSF", level-2 `SectionHeader`s over external `LinkTile`s (`psf.link.<semantics_key(label)>`); a section without links has no heading. A year without `[psf]` shows the feed's `about-psf` block, and without that the empty state "Nothing about the PSF yet" / "It shows here as soon as the conference publishes it." (`INFO_OUTLINE`, no button: the refresh may bring the block, D-175).<br>• **Every row has a leading mark:** its brand's (Python, PyPI, Mastodon, X, LinkedIn) or an outlined Material icon (members `PERSON_ADD_OUTLINED`, volunteering `HANDSHAKE_OUTLINED` and `BADGE_OUTLINED`, sponsorship `STOREFRONT_OUTLINED`, grants `PAID_OUTLINED`, work groups `GROUPS_OUTLINED`, partners `PUBLIC_OUTLINED`, docs `MENU_BOOK_OUTLINED`, website `LANGUAGE`, blog `ARTICLE_OUTLINED`). With these, every title starts in the same column and no section is a mix of indented and flush rows. The issue named only `brand`; Ionic gave each row an icon. `LinkTile(icon=...)` also takes a control, used as it is, for `brand_icon()`'s image.<br>• **More:** an About section between Programme and You (D-15's order), starting with About the PSF (`VOLUNTEER_ACTIVISM_OUTLINED`, `more.psf`). | Year data keeps every URL and word out of the code (D-35, D-45), and the loader's rules catch an http link or a typo before a build ships. The page is Ionic's page without the repeats, with one call to action. On my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, release arm64 APK) and my own iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5, `flet build ios-simulator`, whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), in light and dark, the page opened at `/more/psf`, and every brand mark, X included, read on its surface in both schemes. `uiautomator dump` read each row as one `Button`, its title then "Opens in browser" (the profiles "Opens in another app"), with the identifiers above; Donate read "Donate to the PSF, Opens in browser" (`psf.donate`); each heading and the mission were nodes of their own; and More read "About", then "About the PSF" (`more.psf`). A tap on Mastodon opened Chrome in a task of its own, and Donate opened Chrome, which on that image stops at its first-run screen (as in D-182). On iOS, Maestro taps opened the blog in the in-app Safari view and handed the X profile to Safari ("◀ Conf Companion"). At Android's 200 % the mission wrapped, Donate kept one line and two-line titles kept their icons beside the first line, with nothing clipped. At iOS's largest text the mission and the rows wrapped the same way, and Donate's label took two lines inside its button, which grew to fit, with nothing clipped (captured in the review round with slower Maestro swipes); there "documentation", wider than its line, broke inside itself, as Flutter breaks any such word (D-229). PyPI's mark is Simple Icons' design, whose wireframe half draws in thin strokes: at 24 dp that half is faint in both schemes, and the brand contrast test (#479) measures only the tint. The mark is kept: it is decorative (out of the semantics, and WCAG 1.4.11 exempts logos) and the row's title names PyPI. |
| D-183 | *(Its "not done" amended by D-356 for a SnackBar a screen reader keeps.)* **A background change of Android's Remove animations applies at the following resume until U17** (settles #178 under D-101; amends D-173's note on #178).<br>• **No fix at the pin:** nothing in the pinned Flet reports a change of an accessibility flag. `get_accessibility_features()` is a snapshot (F13); `page.on_media_change` fires only when a `PageMediaData` field changes, none of them is an accessibility flag, and its builder (`packages/flet/lib/src/widgets/page_media.dart`) reads `MediaQuery.paddingOf()` and the like, so it does not even rebuild on one; no Page or service event follows `didChangeAccessibilityFeatures`.<br>• **`app.on_resume()` keeps its one read.** On Android a change made in the background can reach Flutter after that read; the app's own durations (`prefs.motion_ms()`) then follow at the following resume. In practice that is the scrolls (the re-tap, D-166; jump to now, D-155): Flutter runs `scroll_to` on an unbounded `AnimationController`, whose `AnimationBehavior.preserve` ignores `disableAnimations`, so until the next resume they keep their full length. Flutter's own animations follow the late value at once, because each `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller (routes, ripples, Material's implicit animations, and the app's `AnimatedSwitcher` and `animate_*`) reads `disableAnimations` when it starts, so they already run at 5 %. A change made in the foreground, and iOS Reduce Motion, apply at the first resume. The high-contrast default (D-23) is read the same way; its Android timing is not measured.<br>• **Not done:** a second read a few seconds after `RESUME`, and a read on the 60 s tick.<br>• **U17 is the fix** (#183): accessibility fields in `PageMediaData` make the existing `on_media_change` fire whenever Flutter's value changes, late or not (flet-gaps §5.4). The app needs it from P1 (THEME-09), no longer P2; it keeps its place in D-98's group 6, one XS PR with U16, and `Prefs` follows it in its own PR after the pin bump (D-64). | #178's measurements on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK): the `RESUME` read returned the previous `disable_animations` in 6 of 8 tries, and the late value came 3 to 10+ s after the return; on the iOS 26.5 simulator Reduce Motion applied at the first resume, both ways. Flutter's `animation_controller.dart` (3.47.7, `AnimationBehavior._enableAnimations` and the `0.05` scale): an `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller, the default constructor's, reads `SemanticsBinding.instance.disableAnimations` at each start, so only the app's own motion lags, by one return. `AnimationController.unbounded` defaults to `AnimationBehavior.preserve`, which never reads it; `scroll_to` (`scrollable_control.dart`: `animateTo`, `Scrollable.ensureVisible`) runs as a `DrivenScrollActivity` on such a controller (`widgets/scroll_activity.dart`), so a scroll keeps its length under `disableAnimations` and only `motion_ms()` shortens it. A fixed delay is a guess that still misses some returns and adds a timer to cancel on pause and close (D-147); the tick is up to a minute late and calls the platform every minute for a rare change. The real fix is an XS change in Flet. |
| D-186 | *(The time tile's range breaks amended by D-229; the title at large text by D-328; the other rooms' fold by D-354; the status line's no-op cleanup dropped by D-387; the folded rooms amended by D-191, the overflow for open spaces by D-206 and the one room tile by D-349.)* **Session detail: an unknown id stays on its level, the status is a leaf with its own tick, one room tile, and a dropped session keeps its content** (records ux.md §5.5's top half for SESS-01..03; amends D-21's unknown ids and section M's `ui/` list; settles #90's question under D-101).<br>• **Unknown ids:** every detail (session, speaker, room, and later sponsor) shows its not-found `EmptyState` on its own drill level, under the back arrow, with its list as the way out ("Open schedule", ux.md §4.6), never a redirect with a SnackBar (D-21's root, architecture §5.3's parent): nothing moves, the reason stays on screen in a live region, and Back pops the one level the user entered. A P2 deep link with an unknown id is "its root with the target pushed" as any link is, so Back lands on the root.<br>• **Layout** (`screens/session.py`): the app bar holds `FavoriteButton` (`session.favorite`, `use_favorite_toggle()`'s handler and Undo SnackBar, D-184) and, only when `Session.url` is set, an overflow (`session.overflow`, "More options") with "Open on <host>" (the host from the URL), opened in the in-app browser view and, as it leaves the app, a link read "Open on <host>, Opens in browser" (the words on the text: the leading icon's label was read first); sessions without a public page (plenaries, keynotes, summits, breaks, notices, posters) have no overflow (D-60), and Add to calendar joins it in P2. The app-bar title stays empty; the page's heading is the session's name in `HEADLINE_SMALL`, `selectable`, `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=1)` (`session.title`; on Android one `View` node holding the text, not an `EditText`, F40), 8 dp under the badge row, which leaves out a badge that repeats the title (a break's "Break"). The sections sit 24 dp apart (ux.md §3.6); a section's `SectionHeader` takes `level=2` and `top=0`, as the list's gap already stands above it. Speakers stay plain links until #91; Share and posters are #92's.<br>• **When and where** (`ui/when_where.py`, a module of its own so the screen stays under D-66's 400 lines and Now can reuse its status line): an outlined card whose time tile shows the day over the time range (a line each: on one line, at large text the range broke in two, "9:00 AM–12" / ":30 PM", even with no-break spaces and a word joiner) and reads as one phrase ("Saturday, May 16, 10:30 to 11:00 AM, Starts in 25 minutes", the range's dash spoken as "to", `session.when`), with "Times are Pacific Time (PDT)" (`timefmt.zone_note()`) when the device's offset at the session's start differs from the conference's (D-35). Then the room tiles from the pure `row_views.places()`: the session's one place (`display_location`) as a `LinkTile` to `room/<slug>?highlight=<id>` (`session.room`; a place the feed has no room for is shown and opens nothing), and its other rooms, each once, in an `ExpansionTile` (`session.other_rooms`: "+4 more rooms", or "In 5 rooms" for a break with no one place; each room `session.room.<slug>`), which opens in `prefs.motion_ms(200)`. `OtherRooms` is a component that holds `expanded` itself (as D-157 does for text fields): left to Flutter, the tile folded up again after a room's detail and Back. Breaks and notices get no star, as their rows have none (D-31).<br>• **Status** (`timefmt.time_status(start, end, now, zone)`, pure): counted from the start the tile shows (`display_start`: the Job Fair opens at 9:30 though the Schedule groups it at 10:00, DATA-11, so at 9:45 it is "Happening now", not "Starts in 15 min"); before it, on the session's own conference-local day only, "Starts in 25 min" / "Starts in 1 h 5 min" (minutes rounded up, so never "0 min"), spoken in words ("Starts in 25 minutes", D-165's reason); "Happening now" in `Tokens.live` from the start up to, not including, the end; "Ended" from the end on (D-179's half-open span). A notice (no duration) counts down but never says "Ended". `StatusLine` is a leaf component that ticks every `TICK_SECONDS` through `Services.run_every` with `stop_ticks` as its cleanup (D-175, F39), reading `clock.now()`, so the screen around it does not re-render and #88's fake clock drives it. The status and the zone note sit in the tile's title column, styled as supporting lines, not in a `subtitle`: an empty subtitle kept a two-line tile's height, a blank under the range, in the commonest case (another day, on Pacific time).<br>• **Registration** (DATA-09): a filled card, "Register separately" / "Your conference ticket doesn't sign you up for this session." (what to do, as the badge above already says "Registration required"; true of a paid tutorial and of a summit's free sign-up alike), then a `LinkTile` "Check your registration" to `conference.toml`'s new optional `registration_url` (2026: the registration dashboard, from us.pycon.org/2026/attend/information/); a year without one shows the card without the link.<br>• **Removed while open** (ux.md §4.1, brought forward from P2): the component remembers the session it last showed (`use_ref`), for that id only: the same View given another id in place (the Router reuses a level's component) forgets it. When a newer feed lacks the id, the content stays under an inline note on `tertiary_container`, "Removed from the latest schedule" (D-47's words), a live region (`session.removed`). Reopened later, the id is unknown like any other. | A redirect hides the reason after 4 s and moves the user unasked (ux.md §1); a detail level that explains itself keeps Back honest, and the speaker and room stubs already behave so. A status component of its own keeps the minute tick out of the screen's render, as the strip's does (D-165). One tile for the place an attendee walks to, with the others folded, replaces Ionic's up to seven inline links (SESS-02). A countdown on other days repeats the date beside it.
