The handler awaits it all, so it returns only after the return.<br>• **Where:** `open_link(svc, target, opener=e.control)` from every button that opens a link (Sign up for a lightning talk, Propose an open space in the app bar and in the empty state, Submit a sprint project in the empty state, a sprint card's Project page, Donate to the PSF, a sponsor's Website, the venue's Directions), and `focus_back()` around session detail's Share, which re-enables the button before the focus comes back (a disabled button takes none). A handler that needs the opener takes the event (`e: ft.Event[ft.Button]`); `EmptyState`'s `on_action` is typed as Flet's `ControlEventHandler[ft.Button]`.<br>• **Not yet:** a `LinkTile`, an overflow menu item (Sprints' links, session detail's Open on us.pycon.org, the map's 3D venue tour, Add to calendar's browser on iOS) and the Report a problem rows: `ListTile` and `PopupMenuButton` have no `focus()` at the pin (F54), so their windows return to no node on TalkBack 14.2 (#302) until U182 (#625); TalkBack 16 put a row's focus back by itself on the emulator (F192). A link inside a feed text has no control to focus either.<br>• **iOS:** the in-app browser and the share sheet are presented inside the app, which may not go `inactive` under them; then `back_since()` gives up after `LEAVE_WITHIN` and nothing moves, and VoiceOver's own behaviour stands. Not checked on the iOS simulator in this PR (the device time went to TalkBack; the Simulator has no VoiceOver either); the iPhone with VoiceOver (#261, D-102) is deferred by the owner. | The return is the one moment the app knows the user is back; waiting for it, rather than a fixed delay after the call (#302's experiment), works for a browser that returns at once and a sheet whose call ends only when it closes. One small module per job: the lifecycle fact in `services`, the focus in `ui`. On my own Android 16 emulator (`pycon_pixel6a_api36`, TalkBack 16.0, a release arm64 APK of this branch), TalkBack's focus put on Share by a touch and Share activated by a double tap (both through touch exploration, `sendevent`), then Back from the share sheet, and TalkBack's focus read every half second through the `tools/a11y_tree.py` probe: on no node right after the Back, then on Share (with the input focus) from 0.7 to 1.5 s, in 8 of 8 trips with a 1 s settle, fresh Views and repeated trips alike; Sign up for a lightning talk, back from Chrome's window, was on the app bar's Back first and on Sign up from 1.2 s. A 0.5 s settle left TalkBack on Back in 2 of 10 trips. |
| D-265 | *(The tokens amended by D-285; the navigation bar's selected state settled by D-306.)* **The high-contrast schemes: every role by hand in `theme_hc.py`, four rules for the roles ux.md §3.2 leaves out, and the colours Flet derives from the seeded scheme named from the scheme shown** (records ux.md §3.2's HC columns for THEME-04 and THEME-05; amends D-151 for high contrast; #377).<br>• **Every role:** `HC_LIGHT` and `HC_DARK` set all 46 `ColorScheme` roles, so none falls back to the seed. Beyond §3.2's table: one surface (`surface_bright`, `surface_dim`, every `surface_container*` and `surface_tint` equal `surface`); strong roles are their own containers (`error_container` = `error`, like `primary_container`); lines are ink (`outline_variant` = `outline` = the text colour); the inverse roles swap the schemes (`inverse_primary` is the other HC scheme's `primary`); the unused fixed roles are one neutral pair (`#E9E7E1`, dim `#D6D3CB`, under `#1A1D24`) in both. `tests/test_high_contrast.py` checks that no role is unset, that Flet's `parseColorScheme` reads each one, text pairs and text on every surface at 7:1, and both outlines at 3:1.<br>• **The rest of the theme is the standard one:** font, seed, transitions, tiles and the bars' icon brightness. ~~`build_tokens()` keeps the standard tokens until P2-60 (#420).~~ HC has tokens of its own (D-285).<br>• **The navigation bar names its colour in HC:** Flet resolves a role in `system_overlay_style` against the seeded, neutralised scheme, before the overrides apply (`parseTheme`, `utils/theme.dart:163-178`), so `SURFACE_CONTAINER` gave the grey `#F2F2F2` / `#202020` under HC's `#FBFAF6` / `#0B0F17` `NavigationBar`; `overlay_style(dark=..., high_contrast=True)` passes HC's own `surface_container` hex. The standard schemes keep the role (D-151).<br>• **Every theme names `scaffold_bgcolor`, `canvas_color` and `card_bgcolor` (= `surface`) from its scheme, and the HC themes also name `divider_color` (= `outline`),** until U64 (#483): Flet builds `ThemeData` from the seeded scheme and applies the overrides with `copyWith`, which re-derives none of them. The standard schemes set no `outline`, so their divider stays Flet's derived one, as before. The text theme keeps the seeded `onSurface` (`#1B1B1B` / `#E7E7E7`, 16.5:1 and 15.5:1 on the HC surfaces; tested at 7:1).<br>• ~~**The `NavigationBar`'s selected state is knowingly below WCAG 1.4.11 until P2-97 (#457):**~~ *(Settled by D-306: a 2 dp `outline` ring round the pill.)* Its pill (`secondary_container` on `surface`) is 1.18:1 in HC light and 1.25:1 in HC dark, under the 3:1 a state indicator needs, and selected and unselected icons and labels are the same ink (`on_secondary_container` = `on_surface_variant` = `on_surface`). No role value fixes it: a pill at 3:1 on the cream surface leaves ink on it at about 5.4:1. The filled versus outlined icon (D-27) is the remaining non-text cue, except on More, whose `MORE_HORIZ` has no filled form.<br>• **The OS flags:** iOS Increase Contrast; on Android, Color contrast at medium or high (F84), not High contrast text. A background change is read on resume (D-173); on Android it can apply at the following resume, as D-183 says of Remove animations. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, 3-button navigation) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, Increase Contrast through `simctl ui increase_contrast`): with the flag on at a cold start, a recording went from the boot purple straight to the cream HC screen (no standard frame), and Now, Schedule and session detail showed HC light and dark. Before the named colours, the page body stayed `#FFFFFF` under cream bars on both platforms. With the overlay's hex, Android's 3-button bar read `#FBFAF6` / `#0B0F17`, the `NavigationBar`'s own fill, with dark and light icons. Of six background changes of Color contrast on Android, five applied at the first resume and one at the second; two more recreated the activity, and the new session started in the right scheme. |
| D-286 | **Settings: Appearance's radio rows and a High contrast switch that shows what applies, and goes back to following the OS when set to the OS's value** (records ux.md §6.9's Appearance for THEME-01 and STORE-08; ends D-169's interim; #421).<br>• **The page:** `screens/settings.py` at `/more/settings`, pushed from More, whose You lists Settings (`SETTINGS_OUTLINED`, `more.settings`), About this app, then Developer options. "Appearance" is a level-2 heading (D-211) over the System, Light and Dark radio rows that More held until now (`ListTile(leading=Radio, toggle_inputs=True)`, `settings.appearance.<value>`), then the High contrast row (`settings.high_contrast`): a `ListTile(toggle_inputs=True)` with a decorative `CONTRAST_OUTLINED` icon, centred in a 48 dp leading slot (a `Radio`'s padded width) so its title starts where the radio rows' do, and a trailing `Switch`, one merged node through `named()` (D-214). Reminders (#27) and Data (P2-62) join it later.<br>• **The switch shows `Prefs.high_contrast_on`:** under `system`, the OS flag, re-read on resume (D-23), with the subtitle "Follows your device setting". **A change to the OS's value stores `system`; any other stores `on` or `off`** (`Prefs.high_contrast_setting(on)`), and the subtitle goes. Where the OS reports no flag (Android before 34), the flag reads as off, so off is `system` there.<br>• **Saving:** both controls apply at once and call `Prefs.save()`; a failed write keeps the choice on screen for the session and shows a floating SnackBar, "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). The theme follows through `App`'s effect on `prefs.theme` and `prefs.high_contrast_on`, as before. | One switch with two states can't show three settings, so it shows what the user sees, and the stored value follows from it: a user who turns the switch to match the device is asking for the device's behaviour, which then keeps up with it, while a user who turns it against the device has made a choice the device must not undo. The other options were worse: a third "Use device setting" control is the four-way picker THEME-01 replaces, and storing `on`/`off` on every change would leave a user who once touched the switch out of step with Increase Contrast forever, with no way back short of Reset app. Checked (#421) on release builds at `15854a0ac`, the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, arm64 APK) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (iPhone 17), driven by local Maestro flows: Dark and HC applied at once in light, dark, HC light and HC dark, and both held after a relaunch; switching off with the OS flag off showed "Follows your device setting" again; with the setting at `system`, iOS Increase Contrast (`simctl ui increase_contrast enabled`) and Android's Color contrast (`contrast_level 1.0`), each turned on with the app in the background, turned the switch on at the first resume, subtitle kept; on Android, switching it off then stored `off` (the row read "High contrast", unchecked). `uiautomator dump`: each row is one node with its identifier, checkable with its state, 56 dp (the switch row 72 dp). At 200 % text on 360 dp nothing clipped, and Back from Settings returned to More at the same scroll place. |
| D-264 | **Brand marks are Simple Icons 13.21.0's SVGs, drawn by `ui/brands.py`'s `brand_icon()`: decorative, 24 dp, in the brand's own colour where it reads at 3:1 on the page in light and dark, else in `on_surface`** (records D-27's brand logos for THEME-12; amends ux.md §3.7, the matrix's THEME-12 and INFO-10 notes and section M's `ui/` list; #376; F83, U63).<br>• **Source:** the eight marks THEME-12 names (Python, PyPI, Mastodon, Bluesky, X, LinkedIn, YouTube, Discord) are Simple Icons' one-colour 24 × 24 SVGs, unmodified, at **13.21.0** (CC0 1.0), the last release with all eight: LinkedIn left the set in 14.0.0. They are `src/assets/icons/brands/<key>.svg` (section M's `src/assets/icons/`), which `flet build` bundles with `src/`. `NOTICE` names the source, the version (`brands.SIMPLE_ICONS_VERSION`, a test keeps them equal) and the licence, and says the marks stay their owners' trademarks, shown only beside a link to that brand (D-36).<br>• **The builder:** `brand_icon(key, size=24)` returns an `ft.Image` `size` dp square, `exclude_from_semantics=True` (the link beside it names the brand), tinted with `BRANDS[key]` over the whole mark (`SRC_IN`, Flet's default for an SVG); an unknown key raises `KeyError`. A plain builder, no hook: each tint is the same in both schemes, a hex or a scheme role, so it needs no `Tokens` (D-150).<br>• **Tints:** the brand's own colour (Simple Icons' hex) when it reads at 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11) on every surface of `BRAND_SURFACES` in light and dark: Python `#3776AB` (4.84 light, 3.87 dark on `surface`), PyPI `#3775A9`, Mastodon `#6364FF`, Bluesky `#0285FF`, LinkedIn `#0A66C2` (5.69, 3.29), YouTube `#FF0000` and Discord `#5865F2`; X in `ON_SURFACE`, since its black reads 1.12:1 on the dark surface (Ionic's vanished there). The 3:1 check covers each mark's tint as a solid fill, not hairline detail inside a mark: PyPI's outline cubes are 0.078-unit strokes (about 0.2 px at 24 dp), which render at about 1.2 to 1.3:1 in light and dark at any practical size (still under 0.5 px at 48 dp). The solid cubes carry the mark, which stays Simple Icons' unmodified drawing. Not a tone of each brand's hue tuned per scheme (D-22's way with the magenta): a shade of our own would be a new version of someone else's mark, where its own colour or the text colour shows it as its owner draws it.<br>• **Where:** `BRAND_SURFACES` is the page's `surface`, where the info pages' link rows sit (ux.md §6.7). On the container surfaces several brand colours fall under 3:1 (Bluesky 2.93 on light `surface_container_highest`, Python 2.69 on dark), so a mark placed on another surface adds it to `BRAND_SURFACES` first, and the test then says whether each tint still holds; one that does not would take `on_surface` there.<br>• **Bytes, not the asset path (U63):** at the pin, Flet draws an SVG asset file without `exclude_from_semantics` (F83), so the mark's image flag joined the link's node. `brand_icon()` reads the bundled file once per process (`functools.cache`) and sends its bytes, whose branch passes the flag. When the pin has the fix, it takes `icons/brands/<key>.svg` in its own PR (D-64).<br>• **Out:** the marks on the pages (About the PSF, Social: #415, #416), and high contrast (E5-2 extends the test). | Brand marks are the one place D-27 allows something other than Material Icons, so their source, licence and colours are pinned and tested. On a throwaway preview build (never committed) that put the eight marks at 24 dp in link rows (a merged `ListTile`, the mark as `leading`, `OPEN_IN_NEW` at the end), on my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`) and my own iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5, debug build whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), light and dark: every mark drew whole in its 24 dp box (PyPI's hairline outline cubes faint in both schemes; X's glyph, which spans the box's width, measured 61 px at Android's 2.625 and 70 px at iOS's 3x, 23.2 and 23.3 dp) in its brand colour beside the row's title, and X in near-black on light and near-white on dark. `uiautomator dump` read each such row as one `android.widget.Button` named by its title; a row whose mark came from the asset path read as an `android.widget.ImageView`, and one with a Material icon as a `Button` (F83). |
| D-285 | *(The line under the app bar, the chips, the selected pill and the per-screen lines added by D-306.)* **High contrast changes structure through `Tokens`: a `high_contrast` flag, every badge outlined 1.5 dp, flat bordered cards and a line above the bottom bar** (amends D-150; records ux.md §3.3's HC tokens and §3.4's HC badges for THEME-06 and THEME-08; #420, P2-60).<br>• **The flag rides on `Tokens`:** `Tokens.high_contrast` is true in `HC_LIGHT_TOKENS` and `HC_DARK_TOKENS`, which `build_tokens(dark=..., high_contrast=True)` returns. A component whose structure changes in HC (a line instead of a fill or a shadow) reads `use_tokens().high_contrast`, and a memoised row gets it in the tokens prop it already takes (D-150, D-153); none reads `Prefs` for it.<br>• **The HC tokens:** `live`, `warning` and `success` (= `live`) are `#0F5A26`, `#7A4A00` in light and `#30D158`, `#FFD60A` in dark (`theme_hc.py`); `favorite` is `on_surface`. Every track badge, the unknown track's and En Español are outlined in `on_surface`; Registration required stays in `error` and New track in `primary`; all are `BadgeColors(fill=None, border_width=1.5)`, so every HC badge is the same height. Outside HC every badge keeps its 1 dp border and nothing changes. `tests/test_high_contrast.py` checks each text token and each outlined label at 7:1 on every HC surface.<br>• **Cards:** the HC themes set `card_theme` (`hc_card_theme()`): elevation 0 and a 12 dp `RoundedRectangleBorder` with a 1 dp side in the scheme's `outline`, named as a hex because Flet resolves a role in a component theme against the seeded scheme (U64, #483). It applies to every variant, since no card of the app sets its own shape or elevation; a filled card is then the surface with a border.<br>• **The bottom bar:** in HC, `AppScaffold` ends a tab root's body with a 1 dp `Divider` in `OUTLINE` (`bar_divider()`), right above the `NavigationBar`, which is the page's colour there. Material's `NavigationBar` has no border (Flet's `border` is the Cupertino bar's), and the `Scaffold` gives the body no bottom inset when it has a bar.<br>• **Where:** `BadgeColors`, the tonal palette and `HC_BADGE_BORDER` move to `theme_badges.py`, so that the HC tokens leave `theme.py` near D-66's 400 lines (404); the HC token colours and `hc_card_theme()` are `theme_hc.py`'s, and `theme.py` builds the HC tokens from them (`theme_hc.py` cannot import `Tokens`: `theme.py` imports it). Per-screen fixes, such as fills that drew a boundary, are P2-97's (#457). | ux.md §3.3, §3.4 and THEME-06 asked for structure in HC, and one surface leaves outlines as the only way to tell a card, a badge or the bar from the page. A flag on the value every component already receives reaches memoised rows without a new prop or a subscription to `Prefs`. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (iPhone 17, `ios-simulator` build, Increase Contrast on), light and dark: Schedule's Tutorial badges were ink outlines beside a Registration required outline in `error`, both the same height; session detail's outlined and filled cards were the surface with a 1 dp ink border and no shadow; a Charla showed "Charla" and "En Español" outlined; the Tracks hub showed New track outlined in `primary`; and on Schedule and Now a 1 dp line sat right on the bar's top edge. With Increase Contrast off, session detail kept its tonal badge, its grey filled card and no line. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, Color contrast medium), light and dark, Schedule, session detail and Now showed the same outlines, bordered flat cards and line above the bar. |
| D-284 | **Social media (`/more/social`) shows the year's `[social]` table: hashtag chips that copy, then the PyCon US, PSF and blog links with their brand marks; profiles open in their app, blogs in the in-app browser** (records INFO-10 and DATA-23's social handles; amends ux.md §6.7's Social bullet, §4.6 and the matrix's INFO-10; section M's `year_info.py`; #416).<br>• **Data:** `content.toml`'s optional `[social]`, `year_info.py`'s `SocialInfo` (`hashtags`, `links`) as `Content.social`, with `[[social.links]]` as `SocialLink` (`section`, `label`, `handle`, `url`, optional `brand`). `check_social()`, from `check_info()`, refuses a hashtag that is not `#` then letters, digits or `_`, a repeated one (case-insensitively), a table with neither, a `section` outside `SOCIAL_SECTIONS` (`pycon`, `psf`, `blogs`, in the page's order with their headings), a URL that is not `https://` with a host, a blank label or handle, a brand that is not a lower-case key, and two links of a section whose labels give one identifier. The loader stays free of Flet, so a test, not the loader, checks that each brand is a key of `ui/brands.py`'s `BRANDS`, as `test_config.py` checks the track icons. 2026 has `#PyConUS` (the feed's) and `#PyConUS2026`, the PyCon US profiles (Mastodon, Bluesky, X, YouTube), the PSF's (Mastodon, Bluesky, X, LinkedIn) and four blogs (PyCon US, PSF, Python Insider, PyPI), checked on 2026-10-20 against the feed's block, the site's footer, the Mastodon and Bluesky lookups and the pages themselves. The feed's LinkedIn link, `linkedin.com/company/thepsf]`, is the PSF's page with a stray `]`: the data has it without, under the PSF, whose profile it is.<br>• **Page:** `screens/social.py`, in More's new About section (`more.social`, `LANGUAGE`), its body `InfoBody` (D-263): a level-2 "Hashtags" heading, one line ("Tap one to copy it, then paste it into your post."), then a wrapping row of chips; then a level-2 heading per section with links. Each chip is one node, a button read "Copy hashtag #PyConUS" (the label `Text`'s `semantics_label`), `social.hashtag.<n>` from 0, and a tap copies the hashtag through `copy_text()` ("Hashtag copied", "Couldn't copy the hashtag"). The label draws the `CONTENT_COPY_OUTLINED` icon (18 dp, `primary`, scaling with the text) and the side padding, as the Schedule's filter chips do (D-249, F69), so a hashtag wraps instead of fading. Each link is an external `LinkTile` whose leading is the brand's mark (`brand_icon()`, D-264; a blog without one `RSS_FEED_OUTLINED`), titled by the network or blog with the handle as subtitle, `social.link.<section>_<label words>` (`social.link.pycon_mastodon`); a profile opens with `app=True` ("Opens in another app"), a blog as a web page ("Opens in browser"). `LinkTile`'s `icon` takes a control as well as an `IconData`, for the mark. A year without the table shows the feed's `social-media` block, and without that the `EmptyState` "No social links yet" / "The website, us.pycon.org, links to PyCon US's profiles.", no button.<br>• **Out:** embedded posts. | Ionic's chips looked tappable and did nothing, its handles were in the template, and the feed's LinkedIn link was broken. A profile belongs in the app that follows it; a blog is reading, which the in-app browser keeps in context (ux.md §4.7). Tests: `tests/test_social.py`, `tests/test_year_info.py`, `tests/test_rows.py`. On my own iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5, `flet build ios-simulator` whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), driven by Maestro: the page showed at once with both chips, the three sections and every mark; a tap on `#PyConUS2026` put it on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) with "Hashtag copied"; the Mastodon row handed off to Safari ("◀ Conf Companion"), the PyPI blog opened in the in-app Safari view; in dark the X mark followed the text; at the largest Larger Text a first build faded "#PyConUS20…" in its chip, so the label now draws its icon and padding, and the hashtag wrapped whole, with every handle wrapping in its row. On my own Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read each chip as one `Button` "Copy hashtag #PyConUS" (`social.hashtag.0`), 126 px tall at 2.625, 48 dp, and each row as one `Button` whose name is its three lines, "Mastodon", "@pycon@fosstodon.org" and "Opens in another app" (`social.link.pycon_mastodon`); after a chip tap no SnackBar showed (the system confirms from API 33), and a paste into Search's field gave `#PyConUS`; the profile row started Chrome in a task of its own; at 200 % and in dark nothing clipped. |
| D-306 | **High contrast draws with a line every edge a fill drew: a 2 dp ring round the selected pill, outlined chips, fields, bands, notes, frames and circles, lines under the app bar, and the header's icon in its ink; brand moments keep the purple** (records ux.md §3.1's brand-moment rule, §3.3's per-screen lines and §9's boundary line for THEME-04..06; settles D-265's navigation-bar note; amends D-285's bar line; #457, P2-97).<br>• **The rule:** in HC every `surface_container*` equals `surface` (D-265), so a part that a tonal fill set apart from the page takes a 1 dp line in `outline` there (7:1 or more on every HC surface, where WCAG 1.4.11 asks 3:1), and keeps its fill and no line elsewhere. Never a hard-coded colour on a control: a control names the role (`ft.Colors.OUTLINE`), which resolves against the scheme on screen; a component theme names the hex (U64, as `hc_card_theme()` does). `theme_hc.py`'s `hc_border(high_contrast)` is the one `ft.Border` for a container, read from `Tokens.high_contrast` (D-285).<br>• **The theme (`with_hc_components()`, which `build_theme()` applies to the HC themes):** the cards of D-285; `chip_theme.border_side` 1 dp, so every chip is outlined, selected or not (M3's selected filter chip has no border, and its `secondary_container` fill is 1.2:1 on the surface); `navigation_bar_theme` and `navigation_rail_theme` `indicator_shape` a `StadiumBorder` with a 2 dp (`HC_INDICATOR_BORDER`) side in `outline`. Flutter's `NavigationIndicator` paints its shape as a `ShapeDecoration`, so the side shows; the pill keeps its fill. That is D-265's non-text cue at 3:1 or more on every tab, More included (its `MORE_HORIZ` has no filled form): 16.2:1 in HC light, 17.5:1 in HC dark. Not an ink pill, which would need a `surface`-coloured selected icon per destination, nor a bolder label, which moves the label. Thicker than a badge's 1.5 dp: on More it is the only cue.<br>• **Lines under the bars:** `AppScaffold` opens every View's body with `bar_divider()` in HC, so the `AppBar`, whose scrolled-under colour (`surface_container`) is the surface there, keeps an edge, pushed Views included; D-285's line above a root's bottom bar stays. Both lines keep their slots in the View's controls, hidden (`visible=False`, no widget) outside HC: a line inserted before the body would shift it, and Flet would replace the body's whole subtree, a list's scroll included, when the OS contrast setting changes (D-18). An `AppBar` `shape` would draw all four sides, and Flet has no `LinearBorder`.<br>• **Per part:** `SearchField(high_contrast=...)`, a 1 dp ring on its pill, focused or not; the status strip, 1 dp lines above and below; `SkeletonList`, unfilled 1 dp shapes that the shimmer paints in `outline` (a fill would be painted whole); My schedule's open "Earlier …" folds, Flutter's own `ExpansionTile` lines in the theme's divider colour (`FoldTile(lines=True)`); `NoticeRow` (which now takes the tokens, as `SessionRow` does, D-150), session detail's removed note and an open space's image frame, `hc_border()`; the filter sheet, a 1 dp `outline` side on M3's 28 dp top corners (its scrim leaves the page under it nearly the sheet's colour); the Tracks hub's 40 dp circle, a ring of its outlined badge's width and ink (the Sprints row's takes the unknown track's); Now's header icon, the header's ink in HC (gold was 1.03:1 on HC dark's yellow `primary`). Other fills stay: the room highlight and the elapsed bar's track are transient or a hint, and the next-session card and every other card already have D-285's border.<br>• **Brand moments (reversible, D-101):** the boot View and Welcome keep the splash's `#680579` in HC too (white text, 11.1:1), so the launch never flashes; Now's header and any About band use the HC roles (`primary` / `on_primary`). About has no band today (D-187).<br>• **Tests:** `tests/test_hc_boundaries.py` (the theme's ring and chips, with the ring's contrast; the line under the app bar, and the body kept mounted across a contrast change in a real session; the field, the circles, the header icon, the sheet and the fold), with the strip's and the skeleton's in their own modules. | One surface makes the fill a part was told apart by invisible (1.0:1); a line in `outline` restores the edge at 7:1 without a new colour, and only in HC. A theme carries what Flutter lets it (cards, chips, the pill); the rest reads the flag D-285 put on `Tokens`. The issue's other options for the pill were worse: an ink pill needs per-destination icon colours, and a bolder label shifts the bar. |

## E. Data and offline

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-38 | The app bundles a feed snapshot per year (`data/<year>/conference.json`, updated by `tools/update_snapshot.py` before builds), so it works offline from the first launch. The network refresh runs in the background. | The cheapest, most visible improvement, and the best demo opener. |
| D-39 | *(Data path confirmed by D-108; a refresh that brings the published feed again publishes nothing, D-224.)* One `Conference` store refreshes with a single in-flight request. Only after a successful parse is the raw feed written to the durable app data directory (`FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA`, set by packaged builds and by `flet run`), through a temp file and `os.replace`. A corrupt cache is deleted and the app continues from the snapshot. A refresh never deletes the cache first. | The cache directory can be purged by the OS (F14). This also fixes Ionic's bug where an offline refresh wiped the cache. |
| D-40 | *(Triggers, interval and ticks recorded by D-175.)* The feed refreshes on start, on resume when older than 15 min, every 15 min while Now or Schedule is visible, and on demand. Time-dependent views recompute every 60 s, and only while visible. | Fresh enough at the venue, with no work in the background. |
| D-41 | Feed processing keeps Ionic's rules, each with a named test: the placeholder filter (which exempts posters and breaks), collapsed Break and Lunch rows and Lunch spans, the Job Fair row (grouped at 10:00, labelled 9:30), poster details, speakers linked by name, seeded sprint rooms, a start time on every time group, and bad slots skipped and logged. Ionic's display quirks are fixed as `improve` rows, not in the parser. | The data semantics are the product. |
| D-42 | Ids are strings and stay stable, including Ionic's synthetic ones (`conf_key+9000`, `poster-detail-<k>`, `{kind}-{room}-{start}`). They are URL-quoted in routes. | Favourites, the change log and the mock `mobile_state` all key on them. |
| D-43 | Time: datetimes are timezone-aware, in the year's IANA zone via `zoneinfo` and bundled `tzdata`. There is one `clock.now()`: a test override first, then the Developer fake clock, then real time. Day buckets are conference-local dates. `core` functions take `now` as an argument. Tests run under two machine time zones. | Ionic had two definitions of "today" and used a fixed UTC offset. |
| D-44 | *(Converter scope, escaping and rendering recorded by D-114..D-116; where it runs, its API and its boundaries by D-130 and D-131.)* Feed HTML is converted to Markdown once per feed version by a converter built on stdlib `html.parser`. It makes links absolute and falls back to plain text for unknown tags. The result is shown with `ft.Markdown(selectable=True, on_tap_link=...)`, and links open in the in-app browser. Search and filters match text, never markup. | Flet has no HTML control (F8), and this avoids heavy wheels. S3 measures coverage. |
| D-45 | *(The keynotes' entries and their session check recorded by D-217; `conference.toml`'s list amended by D-313.)* Year content is bundled data, not code, in three files under `data/<year>/`:<br>• `conference.toml`: name, dates, time zone, venue, URLs, feed URL, track names.<br>• `content.toml`: keynotes, Wi-Fi, safety contacts, venue hours, links, event reminders, sponsor credits.<br>• `map.toml`: floor plans, room pins, booths.<br>`tomllib` loads them into frozen dataclasses, and the loader validates with clear errors. A unit test loads every year. Safety contacts come only from a verified public source and are re-checked each year. A content fix needs a new build (accepted). Rolling over means adding `data/2027/` and setting `config.YEAR`. | No hosting or remote config. The loader is the schema: stdlib only, and the files can carry comments. |
| D-280 | **Wi-Fi: the network and password are year data, shown as selectable monospace text that a screen reader reads with its label, and copied through `copy_text()`: Copy beside the network, one "Copy password" button** (records INFO-08 and the Wi-Fi table of D-45 and D-263; ux.md §6.7; #410).<br>• **The table:** `content.toml`'s optional `[wifi]`, `network` and `password`, built as `year_info.WifiInfo` into `Content.wifi`. `check_info()` refuses a blank value with the file and the key (`2026/content.toml: wifi.password: is empty`), and the schema a missing one. 2026's values are the feed's own `wifi` block (the bundled snapshot captured 2026-10-11), which a comment beside them names with its date; values the feed changes later need a new build (D-45). 2025 has none. The credit ("Wi-Fi by …", INFO-09) is #417's.<br>• **The page:** `screens/wifi.py` at `/more/wifi`, the first row of More's At the venue, which starts here (`more.wifi`, `WIFI`; D-169). Its body is `InfoBody` (D-263): with the table, the network block and the password block; without it, the feed's `wifi` block (`wifi.feed`); without that, `EmptyState` "No Wi-Fi details yet" / "Ask at the information desk for the network and its password." (`WIFI_OFF_OUTLINED`, `wifi.empty`), whose sentence is the way out. With the table the page reads nothing of the feed, so it shows the same offline on a fresh install.<br>• **A value:** its label in `BODY_MEDIUM` `on_surface_variant` over the value in `TITLE_LARGE`, selectable, in `monospace` with `Menlo` as the fallback (the feed's code spans' pair, `theme.MARKDOWN_STYLE`), where `l`, `1` and `I` differ. For screen readers one node, `Semantics(label="Network, PyConUS26", container=True, exclude_semantics=True)` with the identifier `wifi.network` (`wifi.password`): a selectable text is otherwise a read-only text field of its own (Flutter's `RenderEditable` flags every editable a text field), apart from its label.<br>• **Copy:** a 48 dp `IconButton` beside the network ("Copy network name", `wifi.copy_network`, merged by `named()`, D-234), 12 dp from the edge as a row's trailing icon sits, and a `FilledButton` "Copy password" under the password (`wifi.copy_password`), the one way to copy it, with 10 dp above and below its label as the Lightning talks sign-up has. Both go through `copy_text()`: "Network name copied" / "Couldn't copy the network name", "Password copied" / "Couldn't copy the password" (the SnackBar on iOS and Android 12 and earlier, the system's overlay from Android 13). | Wi-Fi is the first thing attendees look for, often with no signal, and P2's exit asks someone to copy the password unaided. Ionic fetched the page and showed a hard-coded copy, copied only the password and assumed the copy worked. Tests: `tests/test_year_info.py` (2026's values are the feed block's, 2025 has none, a blank or missing value fails the load with its key) and `tests/test_wifi.py` (both values from the year data with no feed; each value one labelled node over selectable monospace text; each Copy copies its value with one SnackBar; a failure says what was not copied and logs no value; 2025 falls back to the feed's block, then the empty state). On my own Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), with Wi-Fi and mobile data off before a fresh install: the page showed both values at once from More › At the venue › Wi-Fi; `uiautomator dump` read "Network, PyConUS26" (`wifi.network`), "Copy network name" (`wifi.copy_network`), "Password, pyconLB2026" (`wifi.password`) and "Copy password" (`wifi.copy_password`), each one node; Copy brought the system's clipboard overlay showing `PyConUS26`, then `pyconLB2026`, and no SnackBar; the values' glyphs were all one width (DroidSansMono, Android's `monospace` family). On my own iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5, the bundle checked against `build/python-app`): Maestro taps on both Copy buttons put `PyConUS26` and `pyconLB2026` on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) with "Network name copied" and "Password copied"; Maestro's hierarchy read the same four labels. Dark at 200 % (Android) and the largest accessibility size (iOS): the values wrap inside the gutter, Copy stays beside the network, nothing clips, and "Copy password" keeps room around its label. |
| D-46 | *(Folding, matching and result order recorded by D-180; the Speakers list's letters and filter by D-189; "places" amended by D-351.)* Search ignores case and accents (NFKD plus casefold) and requires **all words** to match (AND). It covers session titles and description text, speaker names and rooms, plus sponsors and places from P2. It debounces for 250 ms and groups results by type. The Speakers list sorts by displayed name, folded the same way. | Results narrow as you type. Ionic's OR matching over raw HTML both missed and over-matched. |
| D-47 | *(Which refreshes are diffed, who hears of a batch and the screen's route amended by D-255.)* Change log (`PyCon/pycon-us-mobile#312`, P2): after each successful refresh, a pure `diff(old, new)` by session id compares start, end, room and title, and records added and removed sessions. Changes are stored as JSON in the data directory. They show as a "Changed" chip for 24 h, a "Schedule updated · Review" SnackBar and a Schedule changes screen with saved sessions first. The wording is "Removed from the latest schedule", never "cancelled". ~~There is no diff on first install~~ A refresh over the bundled snapshot (a first install) is diffed only when sessions are saved, and only for them (D-255), and a parser change cannot cause a false one: the cache keeps raw bytes, so both sides of a diff are parsed by the current code. | A real complaint about the 2026 app, solved without a database. The feed's `cancelled` flag is always false. |
| D-48 | *(So are the staff choices and the outbox, D-466.)* Favourites are local-first (a `list[str]` in SharedPreferences). They need no account and survive sign-out. They are stored per conference year (`pycon.<year>.favorites`), and so are the feed cache and the change log. | Attendees should not need an account to plan their day. Ids are only guaranteed unique within one year's feed, so a 2026 favourite must never mark a 2027 session. |
| D-466 | **A conference year's staff data is per year too: the sponsor, the door session and the swag categories chosen, and the outbox** (extends D-48; amends D-108, D-414, D-425, D-433, D-459's rows on a device, D-198's Reset app and section M's keys and files; #842, the rollover rehearsal).<br>• **The keys:** `pycon.staff_<year>_sponsor`, `pycon.staff_<year>_door_session` and `pycon.staff_<year>_swag_categories`, built by `staff_sponsor()`, `staff_door_session()` and `staff_swag_categories()` in `pref_keys.py`. They stay under `STAFF_PREFIX`, so a sign-out, an account switch and Clear staff data remove every year's (D-414). The scanner consent stays app-wide: it holds no id.<br>• **The file:** `outbox-<year>.db`, one per year as the feed cache and the change log are, so every query and every send stays within the year with no column or condition added. That covers D-459's key, its "Also in your leads for" and a note's wait for its lead, and D-410's `maybe_sent`: a lead of 2026 is no repeat in 2027, and a lost answer says nothing of the same badge's lead there (`tests/test_year_rollover.py`).<br>• **A row not synced when the year changes stays in its year's file:** it is neither sent, since its sponsor and products would be credited at `/2027/`, nor deleted, since no confirmation named it. Clear staff data and Reset app work on the active year's file; another year's stays until the app is uninstalled. Nothing in the app can delete it: accepted because its rows are the mock's (staff tools stay hidden without a mock URL, D-51). If staff tools ever hold real attendees' data, these two confirmations must count every `outbox-<year>.db` and delete them once confirmed, which keeps D-414's rule.<br>• **Reset app also deletes other years' `feed-<year>.json` and `changes-<year>.json`** (`Storage.delete_other_years()`): caches nothing reads, and it already removes every year's preferences.<br>• **Not kept:** a team device's `outbox.db`, from before this decision, is left unread, the rows D-459 kept as they were included (`tests/test_outbox_existing_rows.py`). No build outside the team exists, and its rows are the mock's.<br>• **The mock server and its badge sheet follow `config.YEAR`:** the seed's `year` stays 2026 for the tests.<br>• **Rejected:** a `year` column on the outbox, which every query, its Python twin (`core/outbox.py`) and the state machine's proof would have to carry for the same result; clearing the staff keys and rows when a new year starts, which would delete unsynced rows without asking. | Sponsor, product, category and badge ids are only guaranteed within one year's data, the reason D-48 gives. The rehearsal showed a 2026 scan still pending would go to `/2027/` under its 2026 sponsor, and 2026's synced rows would fill 2027's lead list and door check, against D-56's "never lose or misattribute a scan". D-457's stage demos are where that shows. |
| D-49 | Storage:<br>• SharedPreferences, keys prefixed `pycon.` and listed in one table in `services/storage.py` (dicts JSON-encoded): prefs, favourites, flags.<br>• Files in the durable data directory: the feed cache and the change log.<br>• `flet-secure-storage` (P3): mock credentials.<br>• sqlite (P3): the staff outbox only. One connection, used only from the asyncio loop thread (no `to_thread`), with WAL mode and `PRAGMA user_version` migrations. | One store per need, and explicit thread ownership. SharedPreferences accepts only simple types (F12). |
| D-120 | **Storage failures are logged and returned, not raised** (refines D-39, D-49 and D-71; #68). In `services/storage.py`:<br>• a failed SharedPreferences write (including a timeout or a client error) or feed-cache write returns `False`; the cache write removes its temp file and leaves the previous cache, and the caller still publishes the data it parsed;<br>• a store that saves what the user chose (settings, favourites) keeps the new value in memory and says so on `False` (a SnackBar such as "Couldn't save your change"), so nobody believes a change is saved when it will be lost on relaunch;<br>• an unreadable cache file reads as `None` (the app continues from the snapshot), and a preference that cannot be read, or whose stored value is not of its type, reads as its default;<br>• atomic writes take turns (one lock), because a refresh whose task was cancelled can still be writing in its thread;<br>• each case is logged with the key or file name, never the value.<br>The exception is a data directory that cannot be created: `data_dir()` raises, since that is a broken install. Without `FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA` (pytest, a plain `python` run), the data directory is `.flet/storage/data` under the working directory, the layout `flet run` uses. | The data in memory is correct for the session, and the next refresh or save writes again, so a storage error is worth a log line (`console.log`, D-72), not `report_errors`' "Something went wrong" or a lost refresh. Packaged builds and `flet run` always set the variable (F14); the fallback keeps what tests and scripts write in the git-ignored `.flet/`, as `flet run` would. |
| D-121 | **2025's nested posters are posters** (settles DATA-27 for poster slots; #58). A `poster` slot's `sessions` list (2025: the 19 posters of "Posters Session", `conf_key` 154, which has no top-level `posters`) is decoded like the top-level `posters` list: `conf_key` → `Poster.id`, `name` → title, `description` (HTML) → description, `speakers` registered as poster authors; both lists share one id space, so a nested poster whose id is taken is skipped. So both years get `poster-detail-<conf_key>` sessions (DATA-07), and the slot itself folds into the Posters row as any poster slot does. `sessions` on any other kind stays unported (DATA-27 `drop`): it is logged as a warning and ignored. | They are real posters, with titles, abstracts and authors. Ionic never read them (poster slots collapse before its shared-session branch), so dropping them would hide 19 sessions and their speakers in the 2025 fixture; mapping them costs one small decoder. |
| D-122 | **Venue names in Ionic's rules are year data** (#58). Ionic prefixes a room named `Hall …` with `Expo ` (in collapsed rows, and from P0-12 in plenary-like names), because the 2026 feed writes `Lunch (Hall AB)` for the hall it otherwise calls `Expo Hall AB`. The prefix is `hall_prefix` in `content.toml`: `"Expo "` in 2026, `""` in 2025, whose feed calls its halls `Hall A`, `Hall B` and `Hall C` everywhere. `FeedSettings` carries it, with the year's `site_url` (which must end in `/`; open-space links and images resolve against it). | Rules that name a venue belong to the year (D-45). With Ionic's literal, 2025's halls would split in two rooms: `Hall A` for notices and `Expo Hall A` for lunches and events. |
| D-123 | **Where Ionic's filtering and collapse rules were unclear** (#58; DATA-04..07):<br>• **Accounting:** every record that does not become a session of its own is listed by rule in `ConferenceData.dropped` (`available-open-space`, `open-space-duplicate`, `placeholder`, `posters-plenary`, `blank`, `collapsed`, `unscheduled-open-space`) and counted in one INFO log line, so "no session lost" is checked exactly (D-77).<br>• **Open spaces** without a start, an end or a room are proposals: dropped as `unscheduled-open-space`, not skipped as broken. Their location is `room_display`, or `room` when that is empty (Ionic checked both but showed only `room_display`).<br>• **Collapsed ids** keep Ionic's form `<kind>-<room>-<start>`, the start written in ISO 8601 at the conference's offset. That is the feed's own string whenever the feed writes the conference offset, as both recorded feeds do, and stays stable if a feed ever writes UTC.<br>• **Poster details need a Posters row:** they take its time, day and place. Without one they are not created and a warning is logged (Ionic created them with no time, on "Sun", in `Expo Hall AB`). The posters themselves stay in `ConferenceData.posters`.<br>• **A session id already taken** (e.g. an open space's `conf_key + 9000` equal to a slot's `conf_key`) is skipped and recorded in `skipped`, never silently overwritten. | Each choice keeps Ionic's output on both recorded feeds (2026: 345 sessions, 170 speakers, the same 14 collapsed rows and day counts as the Ionic simulation in `docs/research/ionic/data-layer.md` §3.7) while making the edge cases explicit instead of accidental. |
| D-124 | **The lightning talks' location is year data** (#59; like D-122). Ionic shows every lightning-talks row at `Pacific Ballroom - Arena`, whatever rooms the slot lists, because 2026's talks are given in the Arena and streamed to five rooms. The place is `lightning_talks_location` in `content.toml`: the Arena in 2026, empty in 2025, whose lightning talks list their one room (Hall B) and so show it. The rooms the slot lists still get the session (DATA-16). | A venue's name is year data (D-45); Ionic's literal would put 2025's talks in a hall that year's venue did not have. |
| D-125 | **Where Ionic's name, speaker and room rules were unclear** (#59; DATA-08, DATA-15, DATA-16):<br>• **Tags in names:** only real tags (`</?letter…>`) are stripped, keeping their text, so 2025's `CANCELLED: <s>…</s>` reads `CANCELLED: Introduction to Sprints Workshop` while a title such as `DuckDB <3 Python` keeps its `<3`. Whether a cancelled row shows as such is a separate UX question (the feed's `cancelled` stays false).<br>• **Pseudo-speakers:** a speaker record whose name starts with `Hosted by` (the lightning talks' hosts, both years) is not a person: it stays out of the directory and shows as text from `Session.speaker_names`, which keeps every author. Ionic listed it as a speaker.<br>• **Linking by name:** each author name links the first directory speaker of that name; a session lists each person once. Poster details link their authors by id, as Ionic did, and keep every author's name as text too.<br>• **Room order:** a room's sessions are sorted by start, then name; Ionic sorted by start only and kept whatever order its arrays had. Rooms are sorted by `slugs.room_sort_key`: numbers by value, case and decomposable accents (`é`, not `ø`) ignored, close to Ionic's `localeCompare(…, {numeric: true, sensitivity: "base"})` for English room names. | Each keeps Ionic's output on the 2026 feed (the 28 rooms with sessions and their counts in `docs/research/ionic/data-layer.md` §3.7, 170 speakers less the pseudo-speaker) and makes the edge cases deterministic. |
| D-126 | **The Schedule's days are the conference's dates** (#60; DATA-17, SCHED-01). `queries.days(data, first_day, last_day)` returns every date from `conference.toml`'s `first_day` to its `last_day`, widened to any schedule day outside them, as an immutable tuple: a day's index is its position and never moves for a given feed and year. Sprint days, which the feed has no sessions for, get a day (SCHED-01 shows them with an empty state that links to Sprints). Filters play no part. | Ionic built its day list from the sessions and sorted it in place, so sprint days had no tab and indices could shift under another screen. Taking the dates from the year data is the one obvious source, and widening keeps any session reachable if the feed strays outside them. |
| D-130 | *(Model fields and `conference.content` handling recorded by D-140.)* **Feed markup is converted in `feed.process()`, once per feed version, never at render time** (records D-44's "once per feed version" and architecture §7.3 rule 16; #67). `core/html_md.py` has two public functions:<br>• `to_markdown(value, site_url)`: the D-114/D-115 Markdown; a value with no tag is plain text.<br>• `to_text(value)`: the text-only form for search (D-46): the visible text with blocks, list items and line breaks as spaces, link targets, `script` and `style` left out, whitespace collapsed, not yet folded.<br>Processing converts each HTML field (descriptions, bios, sprint and job texts, the `conference.content` blocks) after Ionic's rules, so the placeholder rule still reads the raw description as Ionic does. The model keeps the raw HTML (SPON-09's roles and MAP-09's sections read it) beside the Markdown, and `search_text` is built from `to_text`. Screens and queries never convert. The wiring is #134 (after #58 and #59, which change the same constructors); its time counts in `feed processed in N ms` (#60). | Converting on render would repeat the work on every rebuild of a detail screen, and search over raw HTML is Ionic's bug (D-46). The 2026 feed's 464 non-empty HTML values convert in about 20 ms on the host (M4), well inside D-109's budget. |
| D-131 | **Converter boundaries beyond D-114** (#67; none occurs in the 2025 or 2026 feeds, each has a named test in `tests/test_html_md.py`):<br>• A code span's edge spaces stay outside it, and an empty span adds nothing: the renderer trims one space at each edge, which joined the words around it.<br>• Content written directly in a `ul` or `ol` becomes an item, so its text shows (S3's prototype dropped it).<br>• An `a` without `href`, inside another `a`, or with an `href` that cannot be parsed (`http://[`), is text: a missing `href` would join to the site URL, links cannot nest, and conversion runs inside feed processing, where a field must never raise.<br>• Emphasis or code that directly follows an element of the same kind (`<em>a</em><em>b</em>`, `<b>` then `<strong>`) continues it: rendered apart, the markers touch (`*a**b*`, `` `a``b` ``) and show literally.<br>• Consecutive `br`s give one hard break, and breaks at a paragraph's edges are dropped: a blank line would end the paragraph and split emphasis or a link across it.<br>• A bare URL needs something after `://`; curly closing quotes end it like straight ones.<br>• A block element inside an inline one (a `p` inside an `a`) is set off by spaces, so words never join.<br>• Emphasis flanking (D-114) treats neighbouring markers of the same character as one delimiter run, as CommonMark does (`a<strong><em>x</em></strong>b` stays bold italic), and is checked again after a pair is dropped. | Found while writing the production converter and by hypothesis properties over plain text and random tag mixes (testing-and-quality §3.4). Each keeps the visible text intact or avoids markers shown literally; all are reversible. |
| D-135 | **The bundled snapshot** (records D-38's details; #69, NEW-03).<br>• `src/pycon_us/data/<year>/conference.json` holds the public feed's bytes unchanged, so the current parser reads them (D-47). Only the active year bundles one. `conference.toml`'s optional `snapshot_captured_at` (an aware date and time) says when it was fetched; a year has the key exactly when it has the file (a unit test).<br>• `YearData.read_snapshot()` returns the bytes and that time on demand, never at `load_year()`. It reads relative to the package (`config.DATA_DIR`), which works in packaged builds, where `src/` is copied, not installed (F15): an arm64 APK on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` read and processed it (728 sessions, no skipped record).<br>• It is a separate file from `tests/fixtures/feeds/conference-<year>.json`, even while the bytes are equal (as on 2026-10-12; the repository then stores one blob).<br>• `tools/update_snapshot.py` fetches the year's `feed_url` and refuses a feed that `feed.process` rejects, that has a record the parser skips, or whose days fall outside the conference dates: the same bar as the test of the committed snapshot. Then it writes both files with `write_atomic` (only the `snapshot_captured_at` line of `conference.toml` changes; a new year gets it after `feed_url`) and prints each count before and after. | The fixture and the snapshot have different jobs: the fixture is frozen test data whose exact counts the tests assert, while the snapshot is refreshed before every build, so one shared file would make each refresh rewrite the tests. A skipped record is tolerated at run time (D-41), but the snapshot is our own build input, so it must parse cleanly, and the tool never writes one that CI would reject. The capture time is the data's age for bundled data (D-32): a file's modification time does not survive a checkout or `flet build`. |
| D-140 | *(The fold amended by D-180; the sprints' text by D-202; the job listings' by D-278.)* **Where converted markup lives in the model** (records D-130's wiring; #134, DATA-23..25). `feed.process()` converts last, after Ionic's rules and the grouping, inside the timed span of `Feed processed in N ms`:<br>• `Session.description_md`, `Speaker.bio_md`, and `description_md` on `Poster`, `Sprint` and `JobListing`, each `to_markdown(raw, site_url)`; the raw field stays beside it. An open space's `description_md` is its description unchanged: it is Markdown already (DATA-25), and converting it would escape its markup. Its links stay as the feed writes them, not joined to the site URL: the one relative destination in either feed is 2026's `[pytest](pytest.org)`, an author's slip that `urljoin` would turn into a wrong `https://us.pycon.org/2026/pytest.org`; SESS-06's link handler (#91) decides what a tap on a relative link does (D-197: it opens `https://pytest.org`).<br>• `Session.search_text` = `queries.fold(name + to_text(description) + speaker_names)`, joined by spaces: the target shape of architecture §7.4. An open space's text comes from its Markdown instead: each inline link `[text](destination)` keeps its text only and the `*` and backtick markers are removed before `to_text`, so a search never matches a link destination or a marker (D-46); `_` stays, since these descriptions use it inside identifiers (`ic_ocr_cataloger`), never for emphasis. `fold` (~~NFKD without combining marks, then `casefold`~~ D-180's fold) is the one D-46 fold; a query folds the same way. ~~Sprints and job listings get their text when their search lands (P2).~~ Sprint projects get `description_text` and `search_text` for the Sprints page's filter (D-202); ~~job listings get their text when their search lands (P2)~~ job listings get their `roles` and `search_text` (D-278).<br>• `ConferenceData.content` holds the feed's `conference.content` HTML blocks by key, as the feed gives them (MAP-09 reads `venues-hours`' sections), and `content_md` their Markdown. `conference` and `content` are optional; either one that is not an object raises `FeedError`, as an optional section that is not a list does, and a block that is not a string is a skipped record.<br>• Each distinct value converts once per `process()` (a poster's description is also its poster detail's). On the host, conversion takes about 30 ms of the 2026 feed's 40 ms (`to_markdown` about 20 ms as D-130 measured, `to_text` about 5 ms); #121 measures the whole on the iPhone against D-109's 150 ms.<br>• The `*_md` fields and `search_text` default to empty, so the rules' constructors stay as they are; only `process()` fills them, and the outline snapshot shows every content block and one changed value of each other field.<br>• A converter boundary beyond D-131: a link destination holding a lone surrogate (JSON's `\ud800`, which UTF-8 cannot encode) is percent-encoded as its surrogate bytes (`%ED%A0%80`) instead of raising, since a raise would now stop `process()`. The D-131 hypothesis property found it; it has a named test. | One pass at the end keeps the rules reading the feed as Ionic did (the placeholder rule needs the raw description) and gives screens and search ready values. A fold in the model makes every search a substring test. Keeping the raw HTML costs memory but is the only source of SPON-09's role links and MAP-09's sections. |
| D-145 | *(What a good refresh publishes amended by D-224.)* **The `Conference` store** (records D-39's single flight and architecture §6.2; #70). In `state.py`:<br>• Its methods take what they use, `storage` (`Storage`) and `year` (`YearData`), not the whole `Services` bundle; P0-20's bootstrap passes `svc.storage, svc.year`.<br>• **Single flight:** `start_refresh()` returns the refresh in flight, or starts one; `refresh()` awaits that task through `asyncio.shield`, so concurrent callers share one request, and cancelling one caller (an effect cleaned up on unmount) leaves the refresh running for the others. Once it has ended, the next call starts a new one.<br>• **`fetched_at` is an aware datetime** (D-43), not epoch seconds: the cache file's modification time, the snapshot's `snapshot_captured_at` (D-135), or `clock.now()` after a refresh, so the Developer fake clock moves the data's age too. `age()` is never negative: data stamped after a fake "now" counts as just fetched.<br>• A refresh publishes nothing until the feed has parsed; any expected failure (D-146) leaves `data`, `source`, `fetched_at`, `version` and the cache as they were and sets `problem`. A good refresh clears `problem`; a cache write that fails still publishes (D-120). A good refresh whose bytes are the published feed's publishes nothing new: only `fetched_at`, `source` and `problem` change (D-224).<br>• The load order is fixed: the cache when it parses, else the snapshot (NEW-03); the cache wins even when an update installs a newer snapshot, and the next refresh replaces both. | One shared task is the smallest correct single flight; shielding it is what makes sharing safe, since awaiting a task directly cancels it with its awaiter. Narrow parameters keep the store testable with a temporary directory and a fake SharedPreferences, without building Flet services. Aware datetimes are the one time type of the data core, and `clock.now()` is the one "now". |
| D-146 | *(Status order amended by D-181; the thresholds' module by D-231.)* **Refresh failures and the data status** (records D-32's states and D-53's errors for the feed; #70).<br>• `services/api.fetch_feed(url)` is an unsigned GET through `httpx.AsyncClient` with a 10 s timeout, following redirects. Every `httpx.TransportError` (connection, DNS, timeouts, a reset or dropped connection) is a `NetworkError`; an HTTP error status is an `ApiError(status, message)`, and an answer that cannot be read (a redirect loop, an undecodable body) an `ApiError(None, message)`.<br>• `NetworkError` sets `problem = "offline"`; `ApiError` and `FeedError` (a captive portal's HTML page, a truncated or malformed feed) set `problem = "server"`.<br>• `Conference.status(info)` is what the status strip (#82) says, the first that holds: `refreshing` while ~~a refresh runs~~ a refresh the user asked for runs (`refresh()`; an automatic one is quiet, D-181); `offline` or `problem` after a failed refresh; `stale` when there is no data yet, or when it is older than 6 h on a conference day (`first_day` to `last_day` inclusive, tutorials to sprints, in conference time) or 24 h on any other day; otherwise `fresh`. The thresholds are `STALE_ON_CONFERENCE_DAYS` and `STALE_OTHERWISE` in ~~`state.py`~~ `feed_refresh.py` (D-231). | A dropped connection on venue Wi-Fi is "no answer" to the attendee, while a bad answer is not fixed by finding a better network, so the two read differently. A running refresh is the newest fact, and a failed one matters more than the data's age. The strip needs one value; computing it in the store keeps the rule in one tested place. |
| D-172 | **The `Favorites` store** (records D-48 and architecture §6.1's `Favorites`; #79). In `favorites.py`, with `FavoritesCtx` and `use_favorites()` (D-160):<br>• **`ids: frozenset[str]`**, not §6.1's sketched `list[str]`: one value is both the in-memory set for O(1) lookups and what a memoised list is given (`ids & day_ids`, equal while nothing under the list changed; D-153, F34). It is saved sorted, as `list[str]` (F12), under `storage.favorites(storage.year)` through `Storage.read` and `Storage.set`. Ids no longer in the feed are kept.<br>• **Edits:** `toggle(storage, session_id)`, `add_many(storage, ids)`, `remove_many(storage, ids)` and `undo(storage, change)` each assign `ids` once (one notification) and return a frozen `Change(added, removed, saved)`. `added` and `removed` hold only what the edit changed, so Undo never removes a session saved before and "Added 16 sessions" (SCHED-24) counts only new ones; `saved` is D-120's flag. An edit that changes nothing neither notifies nor writes; `add_many` and `remove_many` refuse a lone string.<br>• **Shown first, saved after** (unlike `Prefs.save`): an edit assigns `ids`, then writes the set as it is before its first await. Flet sends a service call before awaiting its answer (`Session.invoke_method`), so the writes arrive in the order of the edits and the last one holds them all.<br>• **A failed read is not an empty list** (refines D-120 for this key): `load()` reads through `Storage.read()`, which raises a SharedPreferences failure that `Storage.get()` turns into the default. The store is read-only on the device until a read succeeds: `loaded: bool` starts False and turns True only after `Storage.read` returns, and while it is False edits show but are not written (`saved=False`). That covers a read that failed and a start that broke before the read (D-161 opens the gate anyway). `loaded` is public, so after the gate My schedule tells "can't open" from "nothing saved" (ux.md §4.6). An absent or unreadable value still reads as none, and counts as read.<br>• **Feedback for #81:** when `Change.saved` is False, the one SnackBar says "Couldn't save your change" in place of the removal or count message, keeping Undo when there was one (ux.md §4.4).<br>• **Start:** `App` builds it with `use_memo(Favorites, [])` and provides it next to `PrefsCtx`; `bootstrap()` loads it after the theme and before the feed, so the gate opens with the stars in place.<br>• **Rows never subscribe:** only a list's owner calls `use_favorites()`. It hands memoised day lists and slots `ids & their_ids`, rows `is_favorite`, and one `on_toggle(session_id)` from `use_callback(..., [])`, which can close over the store and `Storage` because both live as long as the session. | Saving before showing would wait a SharedPreferences round trip before the star changes (D-153 wants it in the same frame) and would lose an edit: two taps during one write both start from the old set, and the second assignment drops the first (a unit test interleaves two taps). A default after a failed read is harmless for a setting, but the next star tap would write that empty set over the user's whole list; a flag that turns True only after a read also keeps a store that was never read from writing, and a public one lets My schedule say so instead of showing a false empty state. A frozenset needs no second set kept in step with a list. A `Change` per edit gives #81's SnackBar and SCHED-24's count what they need without the store knowing about the UI. A row that subscribed would re-render on every tap anywhere, undoing D-153's memoised path. |
| D-175 | *(The cleanup bullet amended by D-387.)* **The refresh triggers and the strip's minute tick** (records D-40 for the feed and the status strip; #82).<br>• **Due rule:** an automatic refresh is due when none has started in the session, or when the last one *began* 15 min ago or more by `clock.now()` (`REFRESH_EVERY`), whatever its outcome; a clock set back before that start (the Developer fake clock) makes it due too. None is due while one is running: that one is the attempt. `Conference.refresh_due()` says so, `start_refresh_if_due()` acts on it, and `start_refresh()` records each start (`_started_at`). D-40's "older than 15 min" counts from the last attempt, not from the data's age, and the strip's 6 h / 24 h thresholds (D-146) are no refresh trigger.<br>• **Foreground:** `Services.foreground`, an `asyncio.Event` set at creation; the lifecycle handler clears it on `pause` and sets it on `resume`. `Services.run_every(seconds, action)` is the one loop for D-40's ticks: it calls `action` at once, then every `seconds` while `foreground` is set, does nothing in the background, calls once at once on `resume` when a call fell due, and returns when `closed` is set (`close()` also sets `foreground`, so a loop waiting for a resume that will never come ends).<br>• **Resume:** `app.on_resume(svc, conference, prefs)`, D-173's one place for what a resume does, has a line per consumer: the ticks, `start_refresh_if_due()`, then the accessibility flags (D-23, D-34, D-173).<br>• **The 15-min timer is the status strip's:** `StatusStrip(auto_refresh=True)` on Now and Schedule. Every strip ticks every 60 s (`TICK_SECONDS`) while it is mounted and the app is in the foreground, and re-renders when the minute has changed (its state is the epoch minute), so its age keeps up and it appears when the data turns stale; with `auto_refresh` the tick also calls `start_refresh_if_due()`. "Visible" means the tab's stack is on screen: a tab switch unmounts the root (one View key, D-142) and stops the loop, while a detail pushed over Now or Schedule keeps its root mounted, so the timer goes on for that tab.<br>• **Cleanup:** the strip's effect is an `async def` with a sync `cleanup=` that does nothing itself: its presence is what makes Flet cancel the task on unmount (F2), and a cleanup returned by a sync setup would be lost at the strip's next render (F39). | Counting from the attempt keeps an offline phone (venue Wi-Fi) from requesting on every tick while its data is days old, and retries every 15 min as D-40 intends. Putting the timer in the strip runs it on exactly the screens D-40 names, with the strip's own hooks (D-165), and keeps it out of the screens' renders. One resume function, which #84 started (D-173), lets P3's sync worker join without touching the handler. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (a debug build whose feed URL, changed only for the check, pointed at a local address that never answered): on Now, after a failed start refresh at 22:18:38 the strip's tick started the next one at 22:33:39, with no request in between; in the background from 22:36 the app made none, and the resume at 22:48:59 started one at once. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, the public feed): with the device clock moved 16 min ahead, Now refreshed on its next tick; Map, with a refresh due, made no request in 150 s; with the clock 25 h ahead, My schedule's strip appeared on its next tick saying "Updated 1 day ago", with no refresh. |
| D-178 | *(Module and `Slot`'s key amended by D-185; `FILTER_USERS` by D-195.)* **The schedule queries: one filtering query, schedule order and a memo per feed** (records architecture §7.5's `timeline` and `track_sessions`, and D-20 and D-31 for the data; #85, DATA-18, DATA-20, DATA-30, SCHED-17). In ~~`core/queries.py`~~ `core/timeline.py` (D-185):<br>• **Shape:** `timeline(data, day, excluded)` returns a tuple of ~~`Slot(key, start, sessions)`~~ `Slot(start, sessions)`, a frozen value, one per time slot; `key` ~~is~~, derived from the start (D-185), is `"slot-<iso>"`, the slot item's `ScrollKey` (D-155). Rows are the model's own `Session` objects, so a memoised row compares them by identity; the row view (formatted time, badges) belongs to the row family (#81), built once per feed version (D-153). A day without sessions (the sprint days) and a filter that hides every track give `()`; a screen tells the two empty states apart by asking for the unfiltered day: `timeline(data, day, ())` is `()` only when the day has no sessions at all (ux.md §4.6: the sprint-day state, or "No sessions on Thursday match your filters" with "Clear filters").<br>• **Order** (`schedule_order`): the shown start (`display_start`: the Job Fair, shown at 9:30 in the 10:00 slot, leads it), then the shown place by `slugs.room_sort_key` (natural, ignoring case and accents: D-125's room order), then name, then id; a row without a place (a notice, a generic break) comes first in its slot. Feed processing's `TimeGroup` order (the raw `location` string) stays as it is; the query sorts its own tuples.<br>• **Filter:** a session passes unless its track key is in `excluded` (SCHED-10); a slot the filter empties is left out; a lone string raises `TypeError`, as in `Favorites.add_many`. `timeline` is the only query that takes `excluded`, and only `screens/schedule.py` names `timeline` or `excluded_tracks` (besides their definitions); two tests check both, so My schedule, Now, Search and the track pages cannot filter (D-20). The filter sheet (#87) adds its module to the test's list if it gets one. "Jump to now" picks its key from the filtered slots, because a slot the filter leaves out has no item to scroll to (D-155): `current_slot_key(slots, now)` takes the shown slots, not the day (architecture §7.5).<br>• **Track pages:** `track_sessions(data, track)` returns a tuple of `DaySessions(day, sessions)`, each day in `schedule_order`, whatever the filter (open spaces show on their own page); the Posters track lists its poster details, not the Posters row (SCHED-22); until the feed publishes posters, the row itself is listed, so the page is never empty while the Schedule shows one.<br>• **Memo:** per feed, in a `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the `ConferenceData` (one object per `Conference.version`), then by the day and the excluded set (order-free), or the track. The same arguments return the same objects; a slot the filter leaves alone is the same object filtered or not; a filter that hides nothing on a day returns the whole day's tuple. Favourites are in no key: they reach the memoised lists as `ids & their_ids` (D-153, D-172). | D-153's memoised path skips work only when its props are the same objects or equal values (F34): identity per feed lets a filter change re-render only the slots it changed, and a remounted Schedule (a tab switch unmounts it, D-142) gets the same objects back. A weak key frees a replaced feed's results (a test checks it) without `core/` knowing about versions (D-68). The shown start first keeps a slot in reading order; natural room order is the printed grid's (D-31), which the raw string order broke on both feeds (2026's 4 PM break sat between `Room 102C` and `Room 202A`; 2025's `garden outside 315` after `Room 3xx`). One filtering query makes D-20's "My schedule, Now and Search ignore them" a property of the code that a test holds, not a review rule. Ionic sorted shared arrays in place and wrote `hide` flags onto shared sessions (DATA-30, SCHED-17). |
| D-179 | *(Module and result shapes amended by D-185.)* **Now & Next and My schedule: the phase, happening, up next, your next session and overlaps** (records D-14's seasons, architecture §7.5's `now_next`, `next_for_me` and `my_schedule`, SCHED-25 and NEW-01; #93). Pure functions in ~~`core/queries.py`~~ `core/agenda.py` (D-185) that take `now` (aware, any zone, D-43) and the saved ids (`Favorites.ids`, D-172) as arguments and never the Schedule's filters (D-20):<br>• **Phase:** `now_next(data, now)` places `now` against each day's span, from its first start to its last end over every session listed under it: `before` the conference's first start; `day` inside a span, between slots included; between spans, `after_day` ("That's a wrap for today", ux.md §5.1) while the conference-local date of `now` is earlier than that of the next day's first start, and `before_day` ("First up today at 9:00 AM") once it is the same, from midnight; `after` from the conference's last end. The sprint days, which the feed has no sessions for, are `after`; Now (#95) names them from `conference.toml`.<br>• **Happening and Up next** count the timeline only (`data.days`), so the poster details stay inside their Posters row: happening is `start <= now < end`, so a session that ends at `now` is over and a notice (no duration) is never on; Up next is the first time slot that starts after `now`, on any day, with its start (~~`next_start`~~ `up_next.start`: the Schedule's own `Slot`, D-185). Both take the Schedule's unfiltered slots, with each slot's sessions in its order (`schedule_order`, D-178), and use `start`, the grouping start, not the Job Fair's earlier label (DATA-11), to tell what is on.<br>• **Your next session:** `next_for_me(data, favorites, now)`: the first saved session that starts after `now` and within 3 h (`NEXT_SESSION_WINDOW`, inclusive); when none does, the saved session that is on and started last; else None. A session that is on never hides the next one; ties go to the agenda's order; poster details count.<br>• **My schedule:** `my_schedule(data, favorites)` lists every saved session in the feed by `Session.day` and start, in the Schedule's order within a start (`schedule_order`, D-178), whatever the filters hide; saved ids not in the feed are left out (the store keeps them). `overlaps` maps each saved session that overlaps another to those it overlaps, in agenda order, from one sweep over the sessions sorted by start. Two sessions overlap when each starts before the other ends, so back-to-back sessions do not, a chain A-B-C does not join A and C, and a notice overlaps nothing. Sessions with the same location, start and end are one visit and do not overlap: the posters of one poster session, its Posters row and the Job Fair listed with it (Expo Hall AB, 9:30 AM to 1:00 PM in 2026). Folding what has ended is the screen's (#94), since it depends on `now`.<br>• **Values:** `NowNext`, `Agenda`, `AgendaDay` and ~~`AgendaSlot`~~ `Slot` (D-185) are frozen dataclasses that compare by value and ~~name sessions by id, as `TimeGroup` does~~ hold the model's sessions (D-185); `Agenda.overlaps` stays out of the generated hash. `now_next` takes `data`, not §7.5's sketched `sessions`, since the timeline is `data.days`. | Ionic kept the last slot that had started "Happening now" until midnight, skipped groups without a start and read the Schedule's hide flags (SCHED-25). A strict end is the one rule under which a talk that ends at 10:30 is gone at 10:30 while the one starting then is on, and the same half-open interval makes back-to-back sessions free of conflict. Spans taken from the sessions hold for a day that runs past midnight and give 2025's and 2026's feeds their own phases; only the evening and the morning between them need a date, compared in the conference's zone (the next start's) in `core`, where the two-zone tests pin it (D-43), so Now (#95) only maps a phase to its copy. An Up next that crosses days gives "That's a wrap for today" its tomorrow without a second query. One order (D-178's) for the Schedule, Now and My schedule means a slot reads the same on every screen. "Your next session" answers "what should I do next?" (D-14): with "on comes first", a saved summit, tutorial or poster session (the 2026 feed has 55 sessions of 3 h or more) would hold the card for hours and hide the talk the attendee has to walk to; the session that is on stays in Happening now with its star. Saving several posters is an expected path, and flagging them against each other would bury the real conflicts that My schedule is for (ux.md §5.6). ~~Ids in value types are what D-153's memoised path compares~~ Values are what D-153's memoised path compares: an answer recomputed every 60 s equals the last one until something changes~~, and row views are keyed by id~~ (sessions, not ids: D-185). Fixed-now tests on the 2026 feed (and 2025's phase turns), under two machine zones, cover each boundary, and each comparison was checked by mutating it (each mutant failed the tests). |
| D-180 | *(`DaySessions` and the order's key amended by D-185.)* **Search: the fold, the index and the results** (records D-46 for DATA-18, DATA-19 and NEW-02; amends D-140's fold; #96). `fold` stays in `core/queries.py`, where feed processing and the Speakers sort take it; the index and the matching are a new `core/search.py` (amends section M's `core/` list; architecture §7.5):<br>• **`fold(text)`** is NFKD, `casefold`, NFKD again (the Unicode Standard's compatibility caseless match, so folding a folded text changes nothing), then: apostrophes and their look-alikes (`' ‘ ’ ‚ ‛ ′`, the modifier letters `ʼ ʻ`, and `´`, which goes before the first NFKD since NFKD makes it a space) are removed, so `don't`, `don’t` and `dont` all fold to `dont` and `O’Brien` sorts as `obrien`; typographic double quotes (`“ ” „ ‟`) and dashes (hyphen, figure dash, en and em dash, horizontal bar, minus) become `"` and `-`; the letters NFKD leaves whole become base letters (`ł ø đ ħ ı ŧ` → `l o d h i t`, `æ œ` → `ae oe`); and marks and format characters (Unicode categories Mn, Me and Cf: accents, the soft hyphen, zero-width spaces and joiners, bidi controls) are removed. `ß` already folds to `ss` and `ﬁ` to `fi`. `Session.search_text` keeps D-140's parts and takes this fold.<br>• **`search_index(data)`** pairs each session's `search_text`, each directory speaker's folded name and each room's folded name with what it finds, already in result order, and logs `Search index built in N ms: <n> sessions, <n> speakers, <n> rooms`. It is built once per feed version, never per keystroke: #97's `Search` holds it in a `use_memo` on `Conference.version`.<br>• **`search(index, query, scope)`** folds the query and splits it on whitespace; an entry matches when every word occurs in its text (AND) anywhere, as a substring, not only as a prefix or a whole word, as architecture §7.5 sketched. An empty or blank query finds nothing. `scope` is one of `SCOPES` (`all`, `sessions`, `speakers`, `rooms`); any other raises `ValueError`, so #97 reads an unknown `?scope=` as `all`.<br>• **Results** are a frozen `SearchResults`: `sessions` as `DaySessions(day, sessions)` groups (the track pages' type, D-185), oldest day first and each day in the Schedule's order (D-31, DATA-30: the start a row shows, then its place in natural order by `slugs.room_sort_key`, a row without a place first, then name and id: D-178's `schedule_order` with the day first; poster details at the Posters row's start; ~~#194 makes search use that key itself~~ search sorts by `(session.day, schedule_order(session))` itself, D-185), then `speakers` by `queries.speaker_order` (folded name, then id: the one key for D-46's Speakers order, which the Speakers screen uses too), then `rooms` in their natural order (D-125); `session_count` counts the sessions. Every session is searched, poster details and collapsed rows included, on all days and whatever the Schedule's filters (D-20); a pseudo-speaker ("Hosted by …") is found through its sessions only (D-125). Equal results hold the same objects, so a memoised list (D-153) skips a keystroke that changes nothing. | The sessions' text and the names hold `’` 166 times (2025) and 162 times (2026), `—` 41 and 72 times and, in 2026, 26 U+2010 hyphens, and iOS's smart punctuation types `’` and `—` into the field: with D-140's fold, `don't` and `don’t` never met, and people typing on a phone often leave the apostrophe out (`dont`, `oreilly`), so apostrophes go rather than becoming `'`. `Łukasz Langa` (2025) could not be found as `lukasz`, since `ł` has no decomposition.
