A format character is invisible but splits a word (2025 has zero-width spaces and bidi controls). Checking only the non-ASCII runs for marks made the fold faster than D-140's despite the extra steps: over the 2026 sessions' 256,000 characters, 3.7 ms against 7.2 ms (best of 30, M4 host), and `feed.process` went from 43-45 to 39 ms. A substring lets `async` find `asyncio` and `typing` find `typing.List`, and the list narrows as the user types. On the host the index takes 0.5 ms, mostly the natural room keys (2026: 345 sessions, 169 speakers, 28 rooms; 2025: 369, 164, 34) and a search 0.12 ms (median of nine queries from `p` to `python typing`, 0.5 ms at most). testing-and-quality.md gives no host-to-emulator factor; `feed.process` read about 40 ms on the host (D-140) and 66 ms on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (D-159), so on the emulator a search should stay near 0.5 ms and the once-per-version index near 1 ms, far inside S2's 3 ms keystroke (D-159). A module of its own because search is one concern (the index, the matching and the result types, about 160 lines) and `queries.py`, which holds the schedule queries (D-178) and Now's and My schedule's (D-179), is already past D-66's 400 lines (about 600 with this fold); `fold` stays where its callers already import it. |
| D-181 | **Automatic refreshes are quiet** (settles #177 under D-101; amends D-32, D-146's status order and D-165's strip; applies to D-175's triggers).<br>• **Quiet:** a refresh the app starts by itself, the start refresh (`bootstrap()`'s `start_refresh()`, the first one over the bundled snapshot included), the resume and the strip's 15-min tick (`start_refresh_if_due()`), never changes the strip's line, icon or button. Over fresh data the strip stays hidden; over a strip that shows (stale, offline, "Couldn't update") only the 2 dp bar runs, in the slot the strip keeps for it.<br>• **Asked for:** only a refresh the user asked for, `Conference.refresh()` (the strip's Refresh, the Schedule stub's Refresh and its "Try again", later pull-to-refresh and the Developer screen's "Refresh now"), makes `status()` say `refreshing`: "Updating the schedule…", Refresh disabled. Refresh during a quiet refresh joins it (single flight, D-145) and makes it the user's at once, so the line changes on the tap, not when the request ends. Every button that calls `refresh()` takes `disabled` from `status() == "refreshing"`, as the strip's does, so none greys out during a quiet refresh.<br>• **The outcome stays honest:** a good refresh hides the strip, a failure ends in "Offline · …" or "Couldn't update · …" (D-146). A failure like the last one sets `problem` to the value it has, which notifies nothing (F5), so the strip's live region changes only when the outcome changes.<br>• **In code:** `Conference._asked`, private so setting it notifies nobody, is set by `refresh()` and cleared when the refresh ends; `status()` returns `refreshing` only while `refreshing and _asked`. When `refresh()` joins a running quiet refresh, it calls `notify()` itself, since `refreshing` is already set. `refreshing` stays "a refresh runs", which the bar reads (`visible=conference.refreshing`), and the strip takes its line, icon and button from `status()`. Nothing new subscribes to the store, so a refresh still re-renders only what shows the feed (D-153, D-165).<br>• **The first start refresh is quiet too:** it is automatic, and the strip already says the honest fact, the bundled schedule's age ("Updated 5 months ago"), while the bar shows the work; the outcome replaces it. A first-start exception would be a second rule with no gain: the user has asked for nothing yet. | Each automatic refresh changed the strip with no action from the user (#177, from the review of #175): over fresh data the strip appeared for the length of the request and pushed the content down by its height, every 15 min and on most resumes; over a showing strip the line swapped to "Updating the schedule…" and back, so TalkBack and VoiceOver announced the live region twice per attempt, and offline that was every 15 min. Delaying "Updating…" by about 1 s (#177's option 3) would hide fast refreshes but not offline ones, which last the 10 s timeout. Checked with check-only builds (never committed) whose feed URL pointed at a local server that held each request, then served the feed or dropped the connection, with the interval cut to 1 min: on the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build) and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), light and dark, Now kept its strip hidden while the start refresh and a tick's refresh ran over fresh data; over the offline strip a tick's refresh showed the 2 dp bar under an unchanged line, icon and enabled Refresh, and ended on the same line; Refresh tapped during such a refresh changed the line to "Updating the schedule…" at once, and the server saw one request; at the largest Larger Text (iOS) the wrapped offline line kept its bar below, nothing clipped. With TalkBack on (Android), every utterance takes audio focus (`MediaFocusControl`, usage `ASSISTANCE_ACCESSIBILITY`). In a re-run against the public feed with the emulator's Wi-Fi turned off, its logcat excerpt kept in #188, the change from fresh to "Offline · …" took focus 0.19 s after the failed refresh, and the next four quiet refreshes over the offline strip took none (one held 6 s, three failed at once), so the strip is announced when its outcome changes and never for a quiet attempt. On the Schedule stub (Android), a quiet refresh over the offline strip left both Refresh buttons enabled, and a tap on the strip's Refresh during it disabled both and showed "Updating the schedule…". |
| D-224 | **A refresh that brings the published feed again publishes nothing** (settles #290 under D-101 as its option 1 recommended; amends D-39 and D-145's publish rule and D-204's button; applies to D-175's triggers and D-181's quiet refreshes; architecture §6.2).<br>• **Same bytes, same data:** `Conference._digest` is the SHA-256 of the raw feed that `data` was processed from: `load_local()` (the cache's bytes or the snapshot's), `reset()` (the snapshot's) and a refresh that publishes set it. A refresh whose fetched bytes have that digest does not process them, since `feed.process` is pure and the year's feed settings never change in a process, and publishes nothing: `data` and `version` stay. So every `use_memo` on the version keeps its view models and every `SessionView` its identity (D-153), no day list, slot or row re-renders, no `ScrollKey` box is given a new `GlobalKey` and remounted, `Shown` logs no new "Content shown" line (D-112), and the Schedule's `Landing` still holds, so "Now" stays hidden (D-204).<br>• **The freshness still updates:** `_confirm()` sets `fetched_at = clock.now()`, `problem = None` and `source = "network"`, also when the bytes were the snapshot's, since the network confirmed them. The strip and the Developer screen read the outcome of a good refresh (D-146): stale data turns fresh, "Offline · …" goes. Logged as "Feed refresh: the same feed as shown; nothing new to publish", with no "Feed processed" line.<br>• **Still cached:** the bytes are written like any good feed's (D-39), so a refresh over the snapshot creates the cache, and the cache file's mtime, the next start's `fetched_at` (D-145), is the refresh's.<br>• **A changed feed** publishes as before, a new version that brings the button back (D-204); the digest is the shown feed's, so a feed that changes and then comes again the same publishes once.<br>• **Rejected:** comparing the raw bytes (the 800 kB feed kept twice in memory, for the same answer); option 2, a `Landing` that compares a content fingerprint of today's layout instead of the version (sessions compare by identity, D-70 rule 6, so it needs a fingerprint of what each row shows, and every list would still re-render). | Every cold start with a cache published the same feed twice: `Content shown: cache feed, version 1`, then `network feed, version 2` 1.1 s later, on `flet_probe_api34` with `-cores 1` (#290's logcat), which re-rendered today's whole list a second after the jump to now and brought "Now" back over the now line it had just shown; a 15-min refresh during the day did the same to whichever screen showed. The feed changes a few times a day at most, so most refreshes bring the same bytes. A digest costs 0.3 ms for 2026's 822 kB on the host (M4) and saves `feed.process`'s 40 ms there and the 200 ms of #290's slow emulator. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34` with `-cores 1`, a release arm64 APK whose build-only patch, never committed, started the fake clock at Friday 2:31 PM): the first launch processed the snapshot once and its start refresh, the same bytes, logged the new line and no second `Feed processed`; a cold start over the cache, with the emulator's network delayed by 1.5 s (`emu network delay 1500`) so the refresh ended 1.9 s after "Jumped to now (try 1)", as in #290, logged `Content shown: cache feed, version 1` and no version 2, and 20 s later the list still showed "Now · 2:31 PM" at its top with no "Now" button (`uiautomator dump`: `schedule.now_line`, no `schedule.jump_now`). |
| D-244 | *(Which feed URL changes delete the cache, and the change log with it, amended by D-255.)* **The `Conference` store owns the Developer feed URL's cache rule; a refresh from a feed URL no longer in effect writes nothing; a URL change starts no refresh** (settles #206; amends D-187's feed URL and architecture §6.2 and §17).<br>• **The rule:** `async Conference.set_feed_url(url, storage)` deletes the feed cache when a Developer feed URL in effect gives way to the year's feed (`_feed_url` set, `url` None), ~~and keeps it from one URL to another or from the year's feed to a URL~~ and on any other change of the URL once the feed has loaded, with `changes-<year>.json` (D-255). `bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()` (async now), `Conference.reset()` and the Developer screen's Apply and Reset await it; `developer.leave_test_feed()` goes. At a start the store is on the year's feed, so it deletes nothing; Reset app with a URL set logs two deletions, the rule's and its own, which is harmless.<br>• **The race D-187 recorded:** `Conference._refresh` checks, after the fetch and again after the cache write, that the URL it fetched is still the one in effect (`_url(year)`), as it checks a closed session (F30). When it is not, it logs "Feed refresh ended after its feed URL was left; nothing written" and writes neither the cache nor the store, not even `problem`, which would be the old URL's. `set_feed_url` changes the field **before** it deletes, and `Storage.delete_feed()` waits for every `write_feed()` asked for before it, a cancelled caller's too (each write is a task of its own in `Storage._writes`), so a URL left during the write, or Reset app, sees the write land, then the deletion, and nothing published. The worker threads' `_write_lock` alone gave them turns, not order: a write whose thread was still queued for the lock landed after a deletion asked for later and brought the test feed's cache back (found in the D-100 review).<br>• **No refresh on a URL change**, as D-73's "from the next refresh": `bootstrap()` starts one right after, Reset app starts its own (D-198), and after Apply, Refresh now is on the same screen. Starting one in `set_feed_url` would need the year, the transport and `Services.closed` there, and while a refresh from the old URL runs, single flight would hand back that one, which now writes nothing. For the same reason a Refresh now that joins a refresh still running from the URL just left publishes nothing; tapped again, it fetches from the URL in effect. | The store already holds the URL in effect and the new one, so the rule needs no copy of `Prefs.feed_url` in the screen, and every caller (the screen, the start, Reset app) gets it. Checking the URL where the refresh writes closes the race without cancelling anything: a cancel (as `reset()` does) would also end a user's `refresh()` with `CancelledError`, for a change the user made on the same screen. Tests in `tests/test_state.py` over a real `Storage` in `tmp_path`: the rule's four transitions; a refresh held on a feed URL (`HeldFeed`), then the URL left for the year's or another, then released: no cache, version unchanged, `refreshing` off; a URL left during the cache write: the cache deleted after it, nothing published; and a URL left, or Reset app, while the write's thread waits for the lock (`tests.fakes.QueuedWrite`): the deletion waits and no cache is left, with `tests/test_storage.py`'s cancelled-caller case under it. Each check, removed, fails its test. |
| D-255 | *(The store and the hand-off recorded by D-301; the Changed badge's colour and rows by D-315.)* **The change log: Schedule changes is a drill level, `changes/<year>`, pushed on the View that opens it; a refresh is diffed only against a feed from its own feed URL, and over the bundled snapshot only for saved sessions and only until the install's first network feed for the year; any feed URL change after the load deletes the cache and the log; a SnackBar per batch that changes something, Now's card for saved sessions, the Changed badge for time, room and title; feed pairs built in `tests/feeds.py`** (settles #366 under D-101; amends D-47's first-install rule and route, D-141's drill kinds, D-187's and D-244's feed URL rule, D-198's Reset app, section M's Routes, Drill, Storage and Tests, ux.md §2.5, §4.2, §5.1, §6.4 and UQ-2, architecture §5.2, §5.3, §6.2 and §7.9, testing-and-quality.md §2 and §3.4, and NEW-04's notes).<br>• **The route:** `changes` is a sixth drill kind (`DRILL_KINDS`, D-141), whose id is the conference year, as the log is kept per year (`changes-<year>.json`, D-48): `Drill` returns `ScheduleChanges` for it. Every opener pushes it on its own View with `drill_href(view_path, "changes", str(YEAR))`. Now's card pushes `/now/changes/2026`, the Schedule's overflow `/schedule/changes/2026`, Settings › Data `/more/settings/changes/2026`, and the SnackBar's Review pushes it on whatever View is on top: a root, a detail, a More page or search. Its items push `…/changes/2026/session/<id>`. Back pops one level everywhere through `parent_of()`, which already drops any drill pair, so `PAGES` and the Back code do not change, and a deep link gets the list as any drill target (D-21). An id that is not the year shows the list's not-found state on its own level (D-186). Not a fixed `/schedule/changes` (#366's option a), whose Back from Now's card or Settings lands on the Schedule; not a global modal (option c), a second modal with its own pop rules (F6, U18) for a list that is not a search; not a `changes` child of a few roots (option b as first sketched), since a SnackBar shown over a detail would then switch the tab or drop that detail.<br>• **A removed session's item** is not a link: its id would open D-186's not-found state. It shows the session's last title, time and room, kept in the batch, with "Removed from the latest schedule" inline (D-47's words), read as one sentence, and keeps its star (`FavoriteButton`, with Undo as everywhere): a saved session the feed dropped stays saved (D-172 keeps the id), the list is where the user can let it go, and if the feed lists it again it is in My schedule again.<br>• **Which refreshes are diffed:** only one that publishes (D-224: the same bytes publish nothing and record nothing), and only against a feed from the feed URL it fetched from: the cache, or an earlier network feed. The bundled snapshot counts as the year's feed URL's, and a refresh over it is diffed only when sessions are saved, the batch then holding only their changes (a time, room or title change or a removal, never an addition): someone who installed and saved offline is told that a talk moved, while a first install with nothing saved gets no log of an old snapshot's differences. That holds only until the install's first network publish for the year, from any feed URL, which saves a marker, `pycon.<year>.fetched` (a bool in section M's key table; Reset app clears it with every `pycon.` key). Once it is set, a refresh over the snapshot publishes without a diff, as after a feed URL change: a start falls back to the snapshot after Clear cached feed (D-198), a cache that does not parse (`load_local()`'s `delete_feed()`) or a feed URL change, and the snapshot's values are not what the user last saw, so a diff over them would announce again changes already in the log or already seen. After a feed URL change, the next refresh publishes without a diff, in the same session (the shown feed is the old URL's) and after a relaunch (the cache is gone, and the snapshot is diffed only by the rule above), and the ones after it are diffed again. A refresh that writes nothing records nothing: a closed session (D-147), a URL left (D-244); nor does one whose URL is left, or whose session closes, after it publishes and before its batch is written (below).<br>• **The feed URL rule (amends D-244):** any change of the feed URL once the feed has loaded (Developer options' Apply and Reset, Reset app) deletes both the cache and `changes-<year>.json`, not only a return to the year's feed; the start's restore of the stored URL runs before the feed loads (`bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()`, then `load_local()`) and deletes nothing. So the cache always holds the feed of the URL in effect and is a fair base for the next start's first diff, and the log never mixes two feeds. The log keeps the cache's order (D-244): its writes are tasks that `Storage` keeps with the feed writes (`Storage._writes`), and the deletion `set_feed_url` and Reset app ask for waits for every write of either file asked for before it, a cancelled caller's too. The hand-off that records a batch (`_record_changes`) checks the URL in effect (`_url(year)`) and a closed session again after its last await, just before it writes, so a refresh whose URL was left by then records nothing, though it has published. Tests: a URL left while the log write is queued (`tests.fakes.QueuedWrite`), and a URL left between the publish and the log write, each leave no `changes-<year>.json`. Reset app also deletes the log and lists Schedule changes among what is lost (D-198).<br>• **Who hears of a batch:** one floating SnackBar (D-29) whose action, **Review**, pushes the list on the current View: "2 saved sessions changed" when the batch touches saved sessions, else "Schedule updated · 3 changes", counting moves, retimes, renames and removals. Additions are listed but neither counted nor announced alone: a batch of additions only shows no SnackBar. The SnackBar follows a refresh the app started too (D-181 keeps the strip quiet about the work; this is its outcome) and the Undo SnackBar's timing: 4 s, kept for a screen reader until used or replaced (D-184), and the next SnackBar replaces it. **Now's card** shows only unseen changes to saved sessions detected in the last 24 h ("2 saved sessions changed · Review", pushing `/now/changes/<year>`), and goes once they are seen: an item is seen as ux.md §6.4 says (2 s after the list shows it), or by Mark all as seen, and the card counts only unseen items, so leaving the list sooner keeps it. **The Changed badge** (~~`tertiary_container`, ux.md §3.4~~ outlined in `Tokens.warning`, D-315) marks the rows whose time, room or title changed in a batch of the last 24 h, by detection time, seen or not; none for an addition or a removal.<br>• **When the Review SnackBar goes:** besides use and replacement (D-184), the page keeps its last Review SnackBar in a weak reference keyed by the page, as D-251 keeps the favourites one. The list closes it when it is shown, whatever opened it (Now's card, the Schedule's overflow, Settings › Data, Review, a deep link), and so does Mark all as seen, while Flet still reports it `open`: `open = False` and `update()`, never `page.pop_dialog()` (D-251). Review on a View whose last drill pair is already `changes/<year>` pushes nothing and only closes it. A Review SnackBar already replaced is left alone, and one shown after the list opened (a newer batch) stays until it is used or replaced, or Mark all as seen. Opening the list shows no SnackBar of its own, so D-251's rejected close-before-show race does not arise.<br>• **Feed pairs:** `tests/feeds.py` builds each change-log pair from the recorded 2026 feed, `recorded(2026)` and `changed(2026, edit)`, with named edits (move, retime, rename, add, remove; #378), and the device check's laptop server (#460) serves pairs from the same builder; nothing is committed under `tests/fixtures/feeds/pairs/`. | Ionic swaps the schedule silently (`PyCon/pycon-us-mobile#312`). The SnackBar shows wherever a refresh lands: at the start, on a resume from any screen, or on the 15-min tick while a detail is pushed over Now or the Schedule (D-175); only a drill level is one pushed View from every opener, as D-17 asks of every cross-link, with no change to `parent_of()` or `PAGES` (read at `main`: `locations.parent_of()` drops any `DRILL_KINDS` pair). The same-URL rule needs to know where the shown feed came from, and under D-244 a URL applied and then the app closed before a refresh left the old URL's cache to be read at the next start as the new URL's; deleting it on every change after the load closes that, at a Developer-only cost (after entering a test URL offline, the next offline start shows the bundled snapshot), and `app.bootstrap()` already restores the URL before it loads the feed. Open spaces are added all through the conference and are hidden by default (D-20), so a SnackBar for each batch of additions would teach people to ignore the one that matters, and a New badge would mark most of the open-space board. Five pairs of the 822,356-byte 2026 feed would commit about 8.2 MB of near-identical JSON; a named edit says exactly what differs and cannot drift from the recorded feed, and the fixtures README already builds edge cases from deep copies. The marker, not "the snapshot is the base", decides the snapshot diff, and the log keeps the cache's order: Clear cached feed, a corrupt cache and a feed URL change all leave the snapshot as the next start's base without deleting the log, and a log write that lands after a URL change's deletion would mix two feeds, as a late cache write brought the test feed back before D-244 (the D-100 review of #469 found both gaps). The list closes the Review SnackBar for the reason D-251 closes the favourites one: with a screen reader on, D-184 would otherwise keep "Schedule updated · Review" over the list it points to and every screen after it. Docs only: the code is #378 and #430 to #438. |

A `ListTile` reads its title and subtitle as separate texts, and the phrase needs its own label, as the session rows' does. In a real Flet session (`tests/test_speakers.py`) a keystroke that narrows the list re-renders no row and only the sections it changed, and one that finds the same speakers re-renders nothing; both fail with `ft.memo` removed from either level. Python's share of a keystroke on the host (M4, busy with other builds, medians of five): 1-3 ms for one that narrows a few rows, 16-20 ms for the first letter (most sections go), 47-51 ms for clearing the filter, when all 169 rows render again, as S2's restored rows did; hiding rows instead of removing them halved the clear but made the first letters dearer, for 169 more controls, so rows are removed. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) the host was so loaded (load average 14 to 84, `Feed processed in 803 ms` against S2's 66) that its numbers are not comparable with D-159's: handler to patch mostly 15-45 ms (9-166) for a keystroke that narrows, about 200 ms for the first letter and 250-720 ms for a clear, over two runs (the first two keystrokes after the launch, while the refreshed feed arrived, took 3.7 and 5.1 s); #121's iPhone release numbers decide whether the clear needs more. On the iOS 26.5 simulator and that emulator, in light and dark: letters, rows, "+1 more", accent-insensitive matches (`mu` finds Mike Müller), the no-match state and its Clear filter, and on iOS a tap pushing `/more/speakers/speaker/<id>` and Back returning to the list; at 200 % (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS) every row reflowed with the title cut at two lines, and at the largest the hint wrapped instead of clipping. `uiautomator dump`: each row one clickable `Button` named by its phrase with `resource-id` `speakers.speaker.<id>`, each letter a node of its own. After the reviews (#203): with "mu" typed, `uiautomator dump` showed the field's one `EditText` node with `resource-id` `speakers.filter` (Flutter puts a text field's label in its hint text, `BaseRoleConfigurator`, which `uiautomator` does not print), the Clear button a node of its own beside it, and `speakers.count` read "5 speakers"; a labelled `Semantics` without the merge was a second node ("Filter speakers") above an unnamed `EditText`, and a merge with the Clear button inside the field took the button into the field's node (its class became `Button`), hence the `Stack`. Neither platform drew a focus ring; Android's keyboard showed no suggestion strip; after Clear filter the field kept the input focus (typing went straight into it on iOS); 200 % text kept the count on one line. |
| D-201 | **`core/html_md.py` splits along its inline layer: the HTML side stays, the Markdown it becomes is `core/markdown_inline.py`** (records D-66 for the converter; amends section M's `core/` list; #198).<br>• **The seam:** `html_md.py` keeps `to_markdown()` and `to_text()`, which every caller imports from it, and the HTML side: the tag tables, the tree (`_Element`, `_TreeBuilder`, `_parse()`, `_text_of()`), plain-text values, the blocks and lists, and `_inline()`, the walk over inline elements. `markdown_inline.py` holds what those pieces become in Markdown: escaping (`escape()`, `escape_line_start()`, with the URL, specials and destination rules of D-114 and D-115), the tokens (`Emphasis`, `Token`), `around()`, `link()`, `code()`, and `paragraph()`, which resolves emphasis by CommonMark's flanking rules (D-114, D-131) and joins a run into one paragraph. HTML's whitespace is defined once, as its `SPACE_CHARS`: `paragraph()` and `around()` strip that set and `html_md.py` builds the pattern that collapses it from the same constant, so the two can't drift apart.<br>• **One way only:** `html_md.py` imports `markdown_inline.py`, never the reverse; `_inline()` stays with the tree because it reads `_Element`, `_text_of()` and the tag tables, and moving it would make the two modules import each other. Nothing else imports `markdown_inline.py`; the names `html_md.py` uses are public in it (no leading underscore), its own helpers stay private. Two tests in `tests/test_html_md.py` hold the rule, scanning imports as D-67's guard does: only `core/html_md.py` imports the module, and it imports nothing from `pycon_us`.<br>• **The name:** `core/` modules are named after their concern, in plain words and with no leading underscore (D-185 split `queries.py` into `timeline.py` and `agenda.py`), so not `_md_inline.py`.<br>• **A move only:** the output is unchanged; `tests/test_html_md.py`, `tests/test_html_md_feeds.py` and `tests/test_feed_markup.py` test the converter through `to_markdown()` and `to_text()` and pass unchanged; only the two boundary tests above are new. | `html_md.py` had grown to 475 lines, over D-66's 400. The inline layer is the one seam the code already had: it is about Markdown's syntax, not HTML, and the dependency runs one way. Converted feed output was compared before and after on both feed fixtures, the bundled snapshot and 20,000 random tag mixes: no value differs. |
| D-261 | **Sponsors by level: the model, tolerant decoding, Ionic's slug and `core/sponsors.py`** (records DATA-22 and SPON-02, architecture §7.4 and §7.8; amends section M's `core/` and tools lists; #373).<br>• **Model:** `Sponsor(name, slug, level, level_order, logo_url, description, booth, job_fair_table, mobile_banner, external_url=None)` and `SponsorLevel(name, order, sponsors)` in `core/models.py`, frozen with `eq=False`. `ConferenceData.sponsor_levels` holds the levels sorted by `order` once in `feed.process()` (a stable sort, so ties keep feed order), and no screen sorts them again. A level's `name` is the feed's key and its `order` its first sponsor's `level_order`, as Ionic's `levelOrder` read `value[0].level_order`; its sponsors keep feed order.<br>• **Decoding:** `records.sponsor_levels(document, skip)`, one call in `process()`. A feed without `conference.sponsors` has no levels. Unlike `conference.content` (D-140), a `sponsors` that is not an object does not raise `FeedError`: it is a skipped record, as are a level that is not a list and a sponsor that cannot be read (`conference.sponsors['Visionary'][2]: …`). A level left without sponsors is not listed. `level_order` is a required integer and the name's slug must hold a letter or digit (a blank name, or one of spaces and marks, slugs to dashes alone and is skipped); a missing or null `mobile_banner` is False; `booth_number` and `job_fair_table_number` read as text (a number as its digits, as Ionic's `String()`), trimmed, and blank or null as None; `description` stays plain text (SPON-03) with its CR LF and CR as line feeds; `external_url`, absent from the live feed, is None unless given.<br>• **Slug:** `slugs.sponsor_slug(name)` is Ionic's `name.toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, '-').replace(/[^a-z0-9-]/g, '')`, with JavaScript's `\s` written out, since Python's also matches U+001C..U+001F and U+0085 and misses U+FEFF. Nothing is trimmed and double dashes stay (`qube-research--technologies`, `code--supply`).<br>• **Query:** `core/sponsors.py` holds `sponsor_by_slug(data, slug)`, a scan of the sorted levels; on a collision the first wins, as Ionic's first match did.<br>• **Invariants:** `sponsor_slugs_are_unique` (each slug is its name's, and none is shared) and `sponsor_levels_are_sorted_and_not_empty` (sorted, each level once, none empty, all of a level's sponsors of its order, each sponsor's `level` its level's). `outline()`, with `markdown_samples()` and `clock()`, moves to `tools/feed_outline.py`, since `tools/feed_invariants.py` had passed D-66's ~400 lines, and lists the sponsors by level. | Every sponsor screen, search, the spotlight and the credits read this model (SPON-01..06, SHELL-08, INFO-09, MAP-10); sorting once is DATA-22's "compute once". Routes keep Ionic's slugs, so a link written for the original app still lands. Sponsors are presentation data: a malformed sponsor section should cost the Sponsors screen, not the schedule's refresh, and the weekly check and `tools/update_snapshot.py` still fail on any skipped record. About a hundred sponsors need no index. Both recorded feeds decode whole: 2026 13 levels and 105 sponsors, 2025 13 and 93, with no slug collision. |
| D-278 | **A job listing's roles: anchors and lone bare URLs become linked roles, a listing with a heading is long form and has none, a placeholder has none and no description, and a sponsor's listings are those that name it exactly** (records SPON-09, SPON-05's matching and SPON-07's text, architecture §7.5 and §7.7; amends D-140's job listings, D-197's home of `feed_link()`, the matrix's SPON-09 sketch and section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists; #394).<br>• **`core/jobs.py`'s `parse_roles(html, site_url)`** reads the listing with `html.parser`, in the listing's order: each `<a href>` is a `Role(title, url)` titled by its text (whitespace collapsed), or by its URL when the text is empty or is itself a URL; a `<p>` or `<li>` with no anchor that holds exactly one bare `http(s)` URL (D-114's pattern, sentence punctuation after it dropped) is a role titled by the text before the URL with its separators trimmed (`Research Engineer \| https://…` → "Research Engineer"), else by the URL. A URL's title is its last path segment, decoded, dashes and underscores as spaces, first letter upper-cased ("Careers", "Python jobs"), else its host: Ionic's `shortenUrl`. Text outside a `p` or `li`, a block with two URLs and a URL beside an anchor make no role of their own. As a browser shows it, a `br` or a block's edge separates words and URLs (two URLs a line apart are two, `Senior<br>Engineer` is "Senior Engineer"), and `script` and `style` text is not read, as `html_md` drops it (`DROPPED_TAGS`); a role found when its block ends still sorts by where its link appears, so a nested item's role keeps its place. A listing with a heading (`h1`..`h6`) is **long form**: a written job description (2026: SerpApi, marimo), whose prose anchors (`marimo`) and "Apply:" lines are not role titles, so it has no roles and its Markdown shows instead. Old Mission's single anchor over five paragraphs has no heading and is one role.<br>• **Links:** a role's link is what `feed_link()` makes of the `href` (D-197: a site path joins the year's site, a scheme-less host gets `https://`), kept only when it is `http` or `https` with a host: `mailto:`, `javascript:`, page-relative links and `http://` alone are no role. `feed_link()` is pure and now feed processing needs it, so it moves, unchanged and with `FEED_LINK_SCHEMES`, from `ui/links.py` to a new `core/links.py` (D-67: core never imports `ui/`); `ui/links.py`'s `open_feed_link()`, the Sprints card and the tests import it from there, nothing is re-exported.<br>• **A placeholder** (`is_placeholder(text)`): the listing's visible text, folded, without punctuation and spaces, is empty, `na`, `none`, `tba` or `tbd` (OpenEDG's `N/A`). It gets no roles and an empty `description_md`, so a screen has nothing to show but the sponsor. Text in any script is not a placeholder (`\W` is Unicode).<br>• **The model:** `JobListing` gains `roles: tuple[Role, ...]` and `search_text`; `Role` is frozen with value equality, being small. `read_listing(listing, site_url, markdown)` fills them with `description_md` in `feed.process()`'s conversion step, once per feed version (D-140), with `feed.py`'s per-value Markdown cache. `search_text` is `fold()` of the sponsor's name, the role titles and `html_md.to_text()` of the description, so it holds no tag, attribute or anchor destination (`nofollow`, `avature` match nothing); a bare URL written in the text stays, as it is visible text. A placeholder's is the sponsor's name.<br>• **Sponsors:** `listings_of(data, sponsor)` returns the listings whose `sponsor_name` equals the sponsor's `name` exactly, in feed order (SPON-05); a scan of 13 needs no index. The invariant `job_listings_name_a_sponsor` (the thirteenth) reports a listing whose name no sponsor has. | Ionic's `parseRoles` linked a role only when its URL was visible text (Bloomberg: 15 roles, 0 links), and the matrix planned anchors only, which would have lost Hudson River Trading's five `Title \| URL` roles and three careers pages. Reading both, every 2026 listing does at least as well as Ionic's: Bloomberg 15 linked roles, Temporal 9, Hudson River Trading 5, Sentry 5, NVIDIA 2, Old Mission and Tower Research 1 each, Anaconda, Capital One and Qube 1 each titled from the URL, SerpApi and marimo none with their Markdown, OpenEDG none and nothing to show (`tests/test_jobs.py`). A heading is the simplest sign of a written description that holds for both feeds; counting text instead would also take Old Mission's one role. Moving `feed_link()` keeps one rule for what a feed link means, where a copy in `core/` could drift from the one taps use. |
| D-449 | **Pull-to-refresh on the main lists: `ui/scrolling.py`'s `pullable()` wraps the Schedule's day lists, Sprints' and Job listings' lists in `ft.RefreshIndicator`, a pull runs the status strip's refresh, and the strip keeps Refresh** (records D-32's and D-64's swap for U1, in the pin since `22a6dcb16`, #805, once U279 was fixed at `a9ef26fdb`, #1010; DATA-03, SCHED-13, EVT-07, SPON-08; #806, closes #20).<br>• **The lists:** one indicator around the Schedule's `TabBarView`, with `handle_nested_scrolls=True`, for all the day lists, whose memoised `DayList`s (D-153) don't change: Flutter's indicator starts only from a vertical drag at a list's top and refuses horizontal scrolls (`material_ui-1.9.0`'s `refresh_indicator.dart`, `_shouldStart` and `_start`), so a swipe between days never pulls: with finger-sized touch steps on the emulator, swipes up to 30° from level changed the day with no refresh, and from 40° they were pulls that left the day alone (#811). Not one on each page: changing day during a refresh disposes the pulled page's widget while Python keeps its control, and Flet's `finish_refresh` then fails with an unhandled timeout that ends in the full-screen error (F299, U285, #1020). The Schedule's no-data list has its own; the Sprints list and the Job listings list, every state of each (the no-data and empty states are in the same list). Other screens keep the strip's Refresh alone; adding one is a one-line `pullable()` in its own issue.<br>• **The handler:** `pullable(content, on_refresh)` takes the refresh the screen already runs: the strip's `Strip.on_refresh` on the Schedule (stable, from `use_callback`), the same on its no-data list, where a pull is Try again, and `use_refresh()`, the strip's own hook, on Sprints and Job listings. All await `Conference.refresh()`, whose single flight shares one request (D-145): a pull during a refresh, the user's or a quiet one, joins it, and its spinner lasts as long as that one; joining a quiet refresh makes it the user's, so the strip says "Updating the schedule…" as for its button (D-181). Offline, the refresh ends with `problem == "offline"` and the strip's line, never an error SnackBar. Flutter's Material indicator, its colours, displacement and localized "Refresh" label are the defaults.<br>• **What stays:** the `ListView` keeps its ref, key, `ScrollKey`s and `on_scroll`, so Jump to now, the put-back (D-331) and D-166's re-tap scroll reach it as before. The strip's Refresh and Settings › Data's "Refresh now" stay: a pull is a gesture no screen reader makes and nothing on screen announces (D-33, ux.md §9).<br>• **The spinner:** Flet ends it through whichever instance holds the indicator's id once `on_refresh` returns, so a render during or after the refresh leaves no spinner behind (F299, U279, fixed at the pin `a9ef26fdb`); `pullable()` needs no code of its own for that. | The four rows asked for it since P1, and the pin has it. One helper keeps the three screens alike; the shared refresh is the one the strip runs, so a pull adds no second path to the data (D-145). It waited for U279: at `22a6dcb16` a pull on Sprints or Job listings, whose screens re-render as a refresh starts, never stopped its spinner, and the only way round in the app was Flet's private API, so the strip stayed the interim until the fix was in the pin (#811). |

## F. Mock backend and private API

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-50 | `tools/mock_api/` implements the private API:<br>• a pure `handle(state, method, path, headers, body) -> (status, headers, body)` over an in-memory `MockState` seeded from `tests/fixtures/mock/` (fixed accounts per role, sponsors, products, attendees), which verifies signatures and the timestamp window;<br>• a stdlib `ThreadingHTTPServer` that serves it to phones on the LAN;<br>• the same `handle` behind `httpx.MockTransport` in tests (`transport.py`);<br>• a printable sheet of mock-badge QR codes (`badges.py`, P3).<br>`tools/mock_api/README.md` documents the contract, citing Ionic's `pycon-api.ts`. | One implementation and no dependencies. The Ionic client is our only specification. |
| D-457 | *(The stage rig, and how the script meets "no Wi-Fi": D-463.)* **Stage demos use the LAN mock from the laptop (D-50); there is no in-app backend unless a reason appears** (#819; settles O-8 under D-101 as recommended, before the P3 exit because #833 and #855 work from the answer).<br>• **What it means on stage:** the laptop runs the mock. Of the demo's beats only the staff scan needs it: the rest of the app is offline first, and D-51 hides private-API features until a mock URL is set. A phone can't reach the laptop's loopback, so with no Wi-Fi it joins a laptop hotspot, with the server on `--host 0.0.0.0` and the laptop's address on that network as the Mock API base URL. An emulator or simulator on the laptop reaches the mock through loopback (development.md §6).<br>• **Not tried yet:** no phone has run against a laptop hotspot, and iOS local-network behaviour with the LAN mock is still open (development.md §10, #121). #855 owns the demo script's details and its dry run.<br>• **What reopens it:** the script finding a beat this can't serve, such as a demo with no laptop or a hotspot that fails. The answer then is O-8's alternative, the pure mock `handle()` behind the Developer screen. | `tools/mock_api` sits outside `src`, so it isn't bundled, and nothing needs it to be yet. An in-app backend would ship the mock's seeded accounts and a code path that exists only for a demo, which a laptop can serve today. |
| D-463 | **The demo runs on simulators on the laptop, over loopback; a phone on a hotspot is an upgrade once rehearsed, and stage never opens GitHub from the app** (#855; settles under D-101 where `docs/demo-script.md`, a draft until the UI freezes, conflicts with P4's exit 3; keeps D-457).<br>• **What counts as a run:** the laptop has no internet (its Wi-Fi off, or serving only the hotspot; no cable or tether). All six beats happen live; a beat that falls back to a recording fails that run for exit 3. A page opened before the Wi-Fi went off counts as live.<br>• **The rig:** simulator A runs the packaged build, simulator B the companion client, with an Android emulator as the spare. The mock runs on the laptop at `http://localhost:8000` (`http://10.0.2.2:8000` on the emulator), so the default rig needs no hotspot.<br>• **A phone** takes beats 1, 2, 3 and 5 only after two rehearsals on the phone that will be on stage, over a laptop hotspot with the server on `--host 0.0.0.0` (D-457). None has been tried; the script lists the unknowns. It also needs #121.<br>• **The scan** is manual entry until E1 (#520, #726). A simulator has no camera, so a camera scan on stage also means a phone.<br>• **Live edit** stays on a simulator over loopback: a phone can't reach it, and no 1.1 companion is in the stores (D-81).<br>• **View source** is shown in the menu, not tapped (#834 settles what it says offline). The laptop's browser shows the same file from a page opened beforehand; the fallbacks are the file in the editor, then a recording (#856). Until #835 makes the repo public, that page would 404 in a window with no GitHub session, so the editor stands in.<br>• **O-8 stays settled:** no beat needs an in-app backend. It reopens as D-457 says. | Simulators are the one setup where every beat has a route with no outside network, and pieces of it have run on devices; a phone on a hotspot has run nowhere. A demo that depends on the least-tried path fails in front of the room, and the phone remains possible once it has earned its place. Reversible: the dry run on #855 can change any of it. |
| D-51 | A structural guard: private-API features stay hidden unless a mock base URL is set in the Developer screen, and the API client refuses any `/api/v1` request to a `*.pycon.org` host (covered by a unit test). | We must never call private PSF endpoints, not even by accident. |
| D-52 | Request signing is byte-exact with Ionic: hex `sha1(secret + ts + METHOD + path_with_query + body)`. The body is serialised once (`separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`) and sent as exactly those bytes; the timestamp is rounded the JavaScript way; each endpoint encodes its query as Ionic does. Test vectors covering Unicode, escaping, query order and rounding are produced once by running Ionic's own signing code, with their provenance recorded. Client and mock share `core/signing.py`. | Without vectors from outside our code, the client and mock could agree on a wrong answer. |
| D-53 | The API client is `httpx.AsyncClient` (already a Flet dependency, F19) with Ionic's per-endpoint timeouts. It has two error types: `NetworkError` (connection failure or timeout) and `ApiError(status, message)` with `.is_auth`. Clock skew is learned from the `Date` header, with one retry. | Callers only branch on "network or not" and "auth or not". |
| D-54 | Sign-in (P3) uses an `AutofillGroup` with email and password hints, an in-flight guard, and distinct errors for a wrong password, being offline and a timeout. Key and secret go in SecureStorage. Role flags come from `check_features` on start and resume. Staff entries come from a pure `staff_entries(flags)` that keeps Ionic's gating, with an exhaustive truth-table test. | Correct and accessible; Ionic said nothing on network errors. |
| D-55 | Favourites sync (P3): the first sync after sign-in takes the union of local and server lists. After that, each change sends a debounced, idempotent full-list PATCH and sets a `dirty` flag, which is retried on start and resume. On a pull, a dirty local list is pushed first; otherwise the server list is adopted. Sign-out keeps the local list. | Never overwrite an unsent edit, and no queue is needed. |
| D-56 | Staff outbox (P3):<br>• sqlite rows of kind `lead`, `note` or `door`, each with owner, sponsor snapshot, status, tries and an idempotency key (door: code + product + session);<br>• synced rows are kept, for duplicate detection and "Already checked in";<br>• one retry cap: 10 counted failures make a row `failed`, with "Retry failed". Network errors never count, and a note waits for its lead;<br>• one `SyncWorker` runs every 60 s, on connectivity change, on resume and on "Sync now".<br>Swag pickup and CoC capture are online-only, with explicit messages. Signing out with unsynced rows asks first. Door inventories and the CoC directory stay in memory only. | Never lose or misattribute a scan. The outbox is property-tested (D-77). |
| D-57 | The mock injects faults on demand: connection errors, timeouts, "server committed, response lost", expired auth and a skewed clock. It also offers both idempotent and non-idempotent capture semantics. The guarantee we test: under idempotent semantics each record is applied exactly once; under non-idempotent semantics no record is lost, and a duplicate can occur only after a lost response, where it is detected and shown. | Client-side dedupe cannot prove the server applied a record exactly once, so we test against both behaviours. |
| D-391 | **The mock-badge sheet is one self-contained HTML page of inline SVG QR codes made with `segno`, a dev dependency only** (settles #666's open question under D-101, its option (a); names D-75's "one QR library"; D-50's `badges.py`; NEW-08, LEAD-14).<br>• **Library:** `segno` (pure Python, no dependencies), in `pyproject.toml`'s `dev` group, never in the app's dependencies nor bundled by `flet build`.<br>• **Output:** `python -m tools.mock_api.badges [--out FILE]` writes one HTML page (default `build/mock-badges.html`): a card per seeded attendee with the QR code of `<access code>:<validator>` as inline SVG (50 mm, four-module quiet zone), the full name and the payload in text for manual entry; no scripts or external files. Two columns of 88 mm cards in 12 mm margins print on A4 and Letter.<br>• **Rejected:** `qrcode` (needs Pillow for images); `segno` writing a multi-page PDF (not text-diffable, and a fixed page size). | No transitive dependency; the output diffs as text, prints at any scale from any browser, and carries the names and codes a person types when a camera can't scan. Reversible: only `badges.py` and the dev group change. |
| D-398 | **The mock account stays signed in when Developer's mock API URL is cleared or changed** (settles #684's open question under D-101, its option (a); D-51, D-54).<br>• **Cleared:** `pycon.account` and the keystore's key and secret stay. With no URL, `Services.api` is None, so nothing is sent, and the private-API features stay hidden (D-51); set again, the same account shows with no new sign-in. `bootstrap()` restores the account from local reads whether or not a URL is set.<br>• **Changed:** the account stays, and the new client signs with the same key (`PrivateApi` hands every client `Account.credentials`). A server that refuses the key takes #686's revoked-key path ("Sign in again").<br>• Sign-out, and Reset app (D-198), are the only ways the account goes.<br>• **Rejected:** (b) signing out on any URL change.<br>Pinned by `tests/test_account_sign_in.py`'s `test_the_account_outlives_a_mock_url_cleared_or_changed`. | The mock's keys are fixed seed values, so a changed URL usually reaches the same accounts. Signing out would drop the owner context of unsynced staff rows (#38), and would sign out a developer who only fixed a typo in the URL. |
| D-403 | **The role flags follow the server at start, on resume and on the Account screen's mount, one call at a time; a refused key makes the account `expired`, never signed out** (records D-54's refresh and D-57's revoked key for API-03, AUTH-04 and SHELL-15; amends section M's top-level modules; #686).<br>• **The call:** `Account.refresh_flags(api, storage, *, closed)` starts one signed `check_features` as a task the store keeps (`_refresh`) and returns it; a call while it runs returns the same task, and nothing starts signed out, expired or with no client (`Services.api` None, D-51). `bootstrap()` calls it right after `restore()` without awaiting it, so the gate never waits on the network; `on_resume()` has one line for it (D-173, D-175); the Account screen (#688) calls it on mount.<br>• **The reply:** `lead_retrieval`, `door_check_in` and `mask_violation`, an absent one False; `is_speaker` is never touched (sign-in only, STORE-05). Only a change is assigned and saved to `pycon.account`, so the store notifies only then. No answer, another error status or another body keeps the flags as stored.<br>• **A refused key** (`ApiError.is_auth`, the mock's `revoked`): `expired` turns True. The account stays signed in, its key, secret, profile and flags kept, so what it recorded keeps its owner (#38); the refresh, and the sync worker #38 adds, start no private call while it is set; More's profile row says "Sign in again" (`more.sign_in_again`, the email under it), which opens Sign in with the email filled in and a line saying the sign-in expired. A sign-in clears it (another email is an account switch, #38), and so do sign-out and Reset app. It is not stored: the next start's refresh finds it again.<br>• **Writes take the account lock** after the reply, and write nothing for a session closed meanwhile (D-147), nor once the account signed out or signed in again while the call was out: the store compares the secrets tuple it started with by identity, so even the same key from a newer sign-in wins.<br>• **Modules:** `account_profile.py` holds what an account is made of, split from `account.py` at D-66's ~400 lines: `FLAGS`, `REFRESHED`, `Profile`, `secrets_of()`, `refreshed_flags()` and `log_unexpected()`. | One refresh in flight keeps a resume during a slow start from doubling the calls, and a kept task lets the Account screen await the one already running. Keeping the account signed in when the key is refused is what D-57's scenario needs: unsynced staff rows keep their owner and nothing is deleted. Storing `expired` would add a fourth part to `restore()`'s all-or-nothing rule for a state the next call finds anyway. Pinned by `tests/test_account_flags.py`, `tests/test_app_handlers.py`'s role-flag tests and `tests/test_sign_in.py`'s revoked-key tests. |
| D-406 | **Favourites follow the account by a saved three-state sync: the first sync after a sign-in takes the union, then a clean list adopts the server's, `[]` included, and a dirty one is pushed; one loop in `services/favorites_sync.py` sends one request at a time** (records D-55's sync for API-04, AUTH-12 and SCHED-12; settles #687's two open questions; replaces section M's planned `pycon.<year>.favorites_dirty` with `pycon.<year>.favorites_sync`; #687).<br>• **The state** (`core/favorites_sync.py`'s `SyncState`, saved per year as `pycon.<year>.favorites_sync`): `merge`, the first sync after a sign-in has not happened (the key's absence, so an install signed in before this change unions once); `dirty`, a local edit has not reached the server; `clean`, the server had this device's last list. A bool `favorites_dirty` could not say `merge`: a sign-in whose first pull failed would then adopt the server's list over the device's at the next start.<br>• **The reconcile** (`reconcile(local, server, state)`, pure): `merge` keeps the union, `dirty` the local list, `clean` the server's; whatever differs from the server's list is pushed as one full-list PATCH (idempotent). `server_ids()` reads `{"favorites": [...]}`, integers as strings; any other body is not adopted. **A server `[]` on a clean list is adopted** (the matrix said it never wipes local favourites): clean means the server had our list, so `[]` is a removal made on another device; the union of the first sync is what protects pre-login favourites (Ionic's bug).<br>• **The loop** (`FavoritesSync`, `Services.favorites_sync`): `App`'s `use_favorites_sync()` runs it once `prefs.loaded` (after the favourites' load and the account's restore) until the session closes; it subscribes to `Favorites.ids` and `Account.signed_in`. It pulls at start, on resume (`on_resume()`'s `request()`) and at each sign-in. A local edit makes `clean` `dirty`, saved at once even while a request is out (state writes take turns, and the loop awaits them before each request), and schedules a PATCH 1 s later (`DEBOUNCE`), restarted by each edit. A pull's reconciled list is `dirty` from the reconcile until its PATCH settles, so an edit during the first sync's PATCH is pushed, never unioned again. After each await a sync goes on only for the same account, signed in, not `expired`, in an open session: a reply after a close or an account change adopts, saves and sends nothing, and the next account's sign-in pull does its own union. A pull asked for while a request is out runs after it, so it never reads a list older than the one just sent; an edit during a pull is `dirty` when the reply is reconciled, so it is kept and pushed. A request settles the state to `clean` only when no edit and no sign-in or sign-out happened while it was out. No answer or an error keeps the state for the next start or resume.<br>• **When nothing is sent:** signed out (favourites are only local, D-48), `expired` (D-403), with no client (D-51) or with favourites that could not be read (D-172). A sign-in and a sign-out set `merge` (the key removed); the local list stays.<br>• **Stores:** `Favorites` makes no request: it gains `replace(storage, ids) -> bool`, the server's list shown first and saved after (D-172), without an Undo; the sync tells its own assignment from an edit by a flag set just before it, since `replace` assigns before its first await and Flet notifies synchronously. `Account.favorites_dirty` says whether the account lacks this device's list, for the Account screen (#688). | The union at the first sync and a kept dirty list are D-55's two guarantees; saving `merge` makes the first one survive a kill. Adopting `[]` only on a clean list is what makes a removal on another device reach this one, which "never wipe" would block forever. One loop with one request in flight is simpler than locks and is what keeps a pull from adopting a list older than the PATCH before it. Keeping the network out of `Favorites` leaves the P1 store and its D-172 semantics as they were. Pinned by `tests/test_favorites_sync_core.py` (the table, and hypothesis: no local id lost at the first sync, a dirty list never overwritten), `tests/test_favorites_sync.py` (through the mock: union at sign-in, five stars one PATCH, a failure kept and sent on resume and at the next start, `[]` and integer ids, edits during a pull and a PATCH, sign-out, expired, a close during a pull, an account switch during the union's save, an edit during a pull then a kill, a removal during the first PATCH) and `tests/test_favorites.py`'s `replace` test. |
| D-407 | **One staff sync loop per session, kept by `App` while a mock client exists and the account is signed in and not expired; it trusts transport errors, and a refused key waits uncounted** (records D-56's `SyncWorker` for LEAD-12, API-10, API-11 and DOOR-07; architecture §12.3; #692).<br>• **Who runs it:** `services/sync.py`'s `use_sync_worker()`, called by `App` before the boot gate, runs `supervise()` in one effect once `prefs.loaded`: it subscribes to `Account` and `Prefs` and keeps exactly one `SyncWorker.run()` task while `sync_target()` names a client (`Services.api`, D-51) and an owner (signed in, not `expired`, D-403). A new client, a sign-out, another account or `expired` cancels the worker before another starts; `close()` (D-147) and the effect's unmount end both. Signed out, `Staff` is cleared, with or without a worker before (an expired account or no client); an expired account keeps its counts until then.<br>• **When it passes:** at once, on `Services.sync_wake` (`kick_sync()`: an enqueue, "Sync now", `on_resume()`, and `ft.Connectivity`'s change to anything but `NONE`, `kick_on_reconnect()`) and every 60 s, only while `Services.foreground` is set. `ft.Connectivity` only kicks: connectivity is not reachability, so the transport errors decide.<br>• **A pass:** `Staff.syncing` makes it one at a time. It sends the owner's due rows with `send()` (each row's own sponsor snapshot, STORE-11) and reads the due rows again until none is left, so a note goes in the pass its lead synced in; a row is sent once per revision per pass, so an edit goes too and a failed save cannot loop. The account and `Services.foreground` are checked before each row, so no row goes with another owner's key or in the background; `ApiClient.request()` reads the credentials once, so its clock-skew retry carries the first attempt's key even if the account changes in between. `NetworkError` ends the pass uncounted; an `ApiError` counts (`RETRY_CAP`); a **401** (the key refused, the mock's `revoked`) ends the pass uncounted and asks `Account.refresh_flags()`, which sets `expired`. A 403 (role or signature) counts.<br>• **Payloads, as #39 and #40 enqueue them:** a lead `{"badge_validator"}` (the capture query is built at send time with the row's `sponsor_id`, none for a sponsor's own account), a note `{"accessCode", "note"}` (the snapshot's `sponsor_id` added at send), a check-in the `redeem` body as sent.<br>• **Time:** outbox times are real epoch seconds (`time.time()`), like the signatures; the Developer fake clock moves the schedule, not the queue.<br>• **The mock:** under `capture="idempotent"` a note that repeats the sponsor's last note for the lead records nothing, so D-57's exactly-once test covers every kind of row (`tools/mock_api/README.md`, Unknown). | A loop per page visit (Ionic) sent each row several times; one loop owned by the session's root, restarted only when what it may send changes, is the simplest shape that can never send with the wrong key. Counting a refused key against rows would fail every row of an account whose key expired, though nothing is wrong with them (D-57's revoked-key scenario: rows wait). |
| D-410 | **A row whose send may have reached the server without an answer is flagged for good, and says "may be recorded twice"** (settles #693's open question under D-101, its recommended option; how D-57's duplicate is "detected and shown"; API-10, DOOR-07, LEAD-07; architecture §10.2, §12.1, §12.2).<br>• **The signal:** `NetworkError.maybe_sent`, set by `NetworkError.of()`: False only for the transport errors raised before a connection existed (`services/api.py`'s `NOT_SENT`: `ConnectError`, DNS failures included, `ConnectTimeout`, `PoolTimeout`, `ProxyError`, `UnsupportedProtocol`); True for every other one (a read or write error or timeout, a protocol error), after which the server may have applied the request. A reply that came but can't be read is the same signal for a write: `ApiError.unreadable` (`status` None: a 2xx that is not JSON or has no envelope, a shape the caller can't parse; an error status or an error envelope under 200 stays a refusal, which never flags; #874).<br>• **The flag:** `Row.maybe_sent`, a column (`OUTBOX_V2`: `maybe_sent INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`), not a key in `payload_json`, which is the request as sent plus the answer. `outbox.after_network_error(row, now, maybe_sent=...)` and `outbox.after_error(row, message, now, maybe_sent=...)` set it (an unreadable reply still counts towards `RETRY_CAP`: it answered, and a server that always answers so must not be resent to for ever, #874), and nothing clears it: a later success, a refused connection, an `ApiError`, an edit or "Retry failed" keep it. The worker also stores it by id whatever the revision (`OutboxDb.flag_maybe_sent()`), so an edit while the row was sent, which drops the send's outcome (D-56), keeps the flag (a rescan of a pending lead only makes it due now, a new `next_at` but no new revision, so the send's outcome saves and a success is never resent, #741), and every save writes `maybe_sent OR` the stored one; a worker cancelled mid-send (D-407) flags its row the same way, as the server may have had it, and leaves it pending, its tries unchanged. A refused key (401) answered, so it never flags.<br>• **The words:** `core/outbox.py`'s `status_text(row)`, the one place for them: `STATUS_WORDS` (Queued, Confirmed, Failed, ux.md §7.2), then " · may be recorded twice" (`MAYBE_TWICE`) on a flagged row, so a synced one reads "Confirmed · may be recorded twice". "May": an idempotent server records it once, and the client cannot tell which kind it talks to. The queue sheet and the tools' lists (#694, #39, #40) show it with an icon beside the words.<br>• **Rejected:** (b) never resending a flagged row on its own and asking "Send again?" (rows would wait through the conference rush); (c) showing nothing (breaks D-57).<br>• **Modules:** `services/endpoints.py` holds the endpoint table and the Ionic query builders, split from `services/api.py` at D-66's ~400 lines. | The client cannot see the server's records (Ionic's API has no list endpoint), so the only duplicates it can know of are the resends after an answer that may have been lost; flagging exactly those keeps D-57's promise without slowing the queue. Pinned by `tests/test_sync_faults.py` (under both semantics, every row the mock records twice is flagged, a rescan while sent or a cancel after the commit included, and a failure before the request left flags none), `tests/test_api_faults.py` (which errors are `maybe_sent`), `tests/test_outbox.py`, `tests/test_outbox_machine.py` (flagged exactly from the first lost or unreadable answer on) and `tests/test_outbox_db.py` (a v1 file migrates unflagged). |

| D-423 | **The Lead scanner asks for consent once per account, on a page of its own, and reads the answer from storage as it opens: it is in no store** (#739; LEAD-04, STORE-06; ux.md §7.3; amends architecture §6.1's planned `Account.scanner_consent`).<br>• **The key:** `SCANNER_CONSENT`, `pycon.staff_scanner_consent` (bool, default False), a staff key (D-414), so a sign-out, a sign-in as another account and Clear staff data remove it, and "Sign in again" as the same expired account keeps it.<br>• **The page:** past D-416's rule, `screens/staff_lead.py`'s `LeadTool` reads the key for the signed-in owner (an empty page under the title meanwhile; an answer read for another owner never stands) and shows `screens/lead_consent.py`'s `LeadConsent` until it is True: a level-1 heading "Scan only with consent", the explanation (adapted from Ionic's, naming the Code of Conduct), "You're asked once for this account, and again after you sign out.", a `ListTile` with a leading `Checkbox` "I'll only scan with consent" (`lead.consent.check`, one node read with its state, toggled anywhere on the row), then Cancel (`lead.consent.cancel`, goes to `parent_of()`, as Back) and Continue (`lead.consent.continue`, disabled until ticked), which saves the key and shows the scan screen in place. A failed save still opens the scanner for the session and says "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). The scan screen is not mounted before consent, so a HID scanner's typing goes nowhere.<br>• **Rejected:** a `Prefs` field, as #739 first proposed (the staff prefix's clear at sign-out, a switch and Clear staff data would have to reset it in memory too, three more paths to keep in step); an `Account` field (its sign-in clears and re-applies the profile, so a same-account "Sign in again" would have to carry it over); the `Checkbox`'s own `label` (Flet lays it out in a `Row` with no room to wrap, so the label ran off the screen at 200 % text). | Storage is the one truth for a key that three paths remove, so no copy can outlive it; a read as the page opens is a few milliseconds. Pinned by `tests/test_staff_lead.py` (the gate, Continue's state, Cancel, a relaunch, the staff keys' removal, a switch, an expired account, a failed save) and `tests/test_staff_data.py` (the table walk and the removals, with the real key). |
| D-425 | *(The choice is per year, D-466.)* **Door staff choose the sponsor they scan for on a pushed page, `…/staff/lead/sponsor`; the choice is a staff key read from storage, like the consent, at each change of location; the list is fetched each time the page opens and never kept** (#740; LEAD-05, LEAD-06, API-09, STORE-11; ux.md §7.3, architecture §12.4; extends D-423).<br>• **The data:** `core/lead_sponsors.py`'s `parse_sponsors(reply)` reads `lead_sponsors` into `LeadSponsor(id: str, name, booth, logo_url)` sorted by folded name: one `"` stripped from each end of a name (Ionic's `/^"|"$/`), an entry without an id or a name left out, a repeated id kept once; a reply without a `sponsors` list is an `ApiError` (`staff_sponsor.fetch_sponsors()`). `filter_sponsors()` keeps the sponsors whose folded name or booth holds every word (D-46). The choice is `STAFF_SPONSOR`, `pycon.staff_sponsor`, `{id, name, logo_url}` (`to_stored()`, `from_stored()`), a staff key (D-414), so a sign-out, a sign-in as another account and Clear staff data remove it.<br>• **The picker:** `screens/lead_sponsor.py`'s `SponsorPicker`, a child route of both Lead scanner routes (`/more/…` and `/now/…`), so Back returns to the scanner; it re-checks `may_open(LEAD)` and `scans_for_sponsor()` with a signed-in account and a mock URL on every render, else "Not available for this account". A search field (`lead.sponsor.search`), then one memoised row per sponsor (`lead.sponsor.<id>`: `logo_tile()`, name, "Booth 101", a tick and `secondary_container` on the chosen one), one node read "Example Corp, Booth 101, selected". A tap saves and goes back; a save that fails stays on the page with "Couldn't save your change", since the scanner reads storage. Cancel (a close icon, `lead.sponsor.cancel`) and Back keep the choice. States: a spinner, "Couldn't load sponsors" with Retry (offline or any error, D-120), "No sponsors available".<br>• **The scanner:** `screens/staff_lead.py`'s `LeadTool` reads the consent and the sponsor for the owner, again whenever `ft.use_route_location()` changes, so the choice shows when the picker goes back; a consent kept for the session after a failed save stays given. Until that read lands, a `scans_for_sponsor()` account's scan screen is `paused` with "Checking the sponsor…", as Door check's is (#873): `Read` carries a token of the arrival it was read for, a new one (`use_memo`) in the render of each change of location, so out and back with the reads held, which Flet cancels, the read from before the picker doesn't count as current; the old sponsor stands until then, while a row keeps its sponsor for good (D-56). A sponsor's own account has no sponsor to read again, so it never pauses. For `scans_for_sponsor()` it opens the picker by itself once per visit when none is chosen (after consent, only from the top View); until one is chosen the scan screen is `paused` with "Choose a sponsor to start scanning" and a "Choose sponsor" button (`lead.sponsor.choose`); then `ScanScreen`'s `context` (now `str | ft.Control | None`) is an outlined chip "For: Example Corp" (`lead.sponsor_chip`, read "Scanning for Example Corp, change sponsor") that opens the picker: a `Container` drawn as M3's assist chip whose text wraps, since Flutter's `Chip` kept its label on one line and cut it at 200 % text on the iOS simulator. A `lead_retrieval` account never sees either.<br>• **Rejected:** a field in a store (D-423's reasons: three removal paths would have to reset it in memory); a callback or a `ViewMemory` field from the picker to the scanner (a second copy of the choice that could outlive the key); a dialog over the scan screen instead of a page (LEAD-05 asks for a full-screen list, and a route gives Back for free); caching the list (only needed to change sponsor, and stale lists are how Ionic misattributed). | Storage stays the one truth for the staff keys, and a read is milliseconds; an id, never a name, says which sponsor is chosen, so the quoted names that fooled Ionic cannot. | D-426 | *(One row per owner, code and sponsor since D-459.)* **A lead scan goes through the outbox, and its result is the row as the worker's pass leaves it: the handler enqueues, kicks the `SyncWorker`, waits up to 3 s and reads the row back by id; it never sends** (settles #741's open question under D-101, its option (a); amends D-416's handler; D-407 unchanged; D-56, D-57, D-410, D-120; LEAD-07, LEAD-09, STORE-11; ux.md §7.3, architecture §12.5).<br>• **The handler:** `lead_capture.capture()`, the Lead scanner's `on_badge`, enqueues `scan_lead` (one row per owner and code; payload `{"badge_validator": badge.lead_validator}`; the sponsor chosen now, or None for a sponsor's own account, snapshot in the row, so a later switch leaves it going to the first). `ALREADY` (a synced row) answers at once and sends nothing. Otherwise it calls `Staff.update_counts()`, subscribes to `Staff`, kicks (`Services.kick_sync`) and re-reads the row (`OutboxDb.row(id)`, new) at each `Staff` change, until a send has moved it (synced, failed, a try counted, or `next_at` put off) or `WAIT_SECONDS` (3.0: the capture's 2 s timeout plus margin) have passed: driven by the worker's reports, never by polling.<br>• **The words:** synced → accepted "Lead captured", the `first_name` the answer stored, the code; `ALREADY` → duplicate "Already in your leads", the name, the code; pending with no try counted (offline, a backlog, the wait ran out) → queued "Saved. Will sync.", the code; pending with a try counted (a 404: wrong validator, unknown attendee) → denied "Not captured", the server's message, "It will retry. See the sync queue."; failed → denied "Not captured", the message, "Retry it from the sync queue."; an enqueue that failed (logged) or no outbox → denied "Not captured", "Couldn't save this scan on this device." A flagged row adds "May be recorded twice" (D-410).<br>• **Late results:** `ScanScreen` drops a handler's result that comes after it unmounts or while another View is on top (a mounted ref and `is_top_view()`, as #751's picker), so a slow capture never buzzes or speaks over the sponsor picker; the row and the chip still say it.<br>• **Rejected:** (b) the handler sending the new row itself and saving through `OutboxDb.save()`'s revision guard (faster under a backlog, but a second sender: a pass already running can take the same pending row, so one capture is sent twice, a real duplicate under non-idempotent semantics, against D-57 and D-407); polling the row every 100 ms as Ionic did. | The worker stays the only sender, so a row is never sent twice at once and "may be recorded twice" means only a lost answer; a long offline backlog honestly says "Saved. Will sync.". Pinned by `tests/test_lead_capture.py` (each outcome against the mock, a staff sponsor, offline then back after a switch of sponsor, the tenth refusal, `non_idempotent` with `lost_response`, no send without the worker, a failed enqueue), `tests/test_staff_lead.py` (the queued row, the cooldown and the repeat, the staff snapshot) and `tests/test_scan_screen.py` (a late result under another View). |

A search field (`lead.leads.search`, the name or the code, folded, D-46); for `scans_for_sponsor()` with a sponsor chosen, a filter chip "Only Example Corp" (`lead.leads.only_sponsor`, off by default, read with its selected state) comparing `sponsor_id`s, a `Container` drawn as M3's filter chip whose text wraps, as D-425's sponsor chip; while the search or the chip narrows the list and finds some, a live count line "3 leads" (`found_count()`, `lead.leads.count`), so a screen reader hears each result from the field (ux.md §9); then every lead in a lazy `ListView`, newest first, no cap, each a memoised row keyed by its code (D-153), one node (`lead.leads.row.<code>`) read "Ada, Confirmed, 5 minutes ago, Example Corp, has a note": `status_icon()` (the queue sheet's), `status_text()`'s words with `WARNING_AMBER` beside a flagged row's (D-410), the first name or else the code (a pending lead has only its code), the time ago, the sponsor snapshot on a staff account's rows, a note icon. A tap opens `use_note_sheet()` for the lead. States: "No leads yet", "No leads match", and "Couldn't read your leads on this device." (D-120).<br>• **The data:** `lead_list.leads(db, owner)` filters `OutboxDb.rows(owner, every status)` to its `lead` rows, newest first, each with whether a `note` row exists for its code, or None on a failed read; `lead_count()` is the button's number ("Leads" alone when unreadable). Both are read in `use_memo` on `[owner, Staff.revision]` (D-69), as the queue sheet reads; `core/lead_views.py` is pure (`lead_view()`, `phrase()`, `filter_leads()`; `first_name()` moved there from `lead_capture.py`). `OutboxDb` gains nothing (#755 splits it).<br>• **Why:** `ScanScreen` stays as the four tools share it (D-416); a HID scanner types into the focused scan field, which a Leads tab's search would take; Back returns to the scanner (its field is not yet focused again after Back: #775). It costs one route more than tabs.<br>• **Rejected:** (b) `Tabs` Scan · Leads inside `ScanScreen`, a slot only the Lead scanner uses, with the entry's focus to give back at each tab change and a HID scan going into the search while Leads shows. | One more route keeps the shared scan screen simple and a scanner's typing where it belongs; the list follows the outbox like the queue sheet. |
| D-430 | **Every confirmed CoC incident is recorded with the POST and `force: true`, from the name search and from a scan alike; the per-frame GET is not called** (settles #758's open question under D-101, its option (a); D-56, D-57, D-72, D-410; API-08, MASK-03, MASK-04; architecture §10.4, §12.5).<br>• **The call:** `conduct.record(api, code, notes)` sends `COC_REPORT`, POST `mask_violations/capture/` with `{"attendee_access_code", "notes"?, "force": true}`, the notes only when they hold more than whitespace (stripped), and returns `core/conduct.py`'s `Recorded` (the names, the email, the new count, None when the reply has none: the incident is recorded all the same). `COC_SCAN`, Ionic's GET with the code in the query, stays in `ENDPOINTS` as API-08's contract and the mock still serves it, but the app never calls it.<br>• **A double record is the screen's to stop:** the review dialog disables Record while the call is out (MASK-03, MASK-04). No dedupe on the client and none asked of the server.<br>• **Errors:** a 403 (no `mask_violation`), a 404 (an unknown code) and an unreadable reply are `ApiError`s; no answer is a `NetworkError` whose `maybe_sent` says the incident may be on record (D-410). CoC never queues (D-56).<br>• **Rejected:** (b) Ionic's split, the GET for a scan and the POST from the name path (a scan's notes would need the POST anyway, and a reviewed scan has no per-frame repeat left to dedupe); (c) the POST without `force` for both, relying on the server's guard (a second, real incident within its window, the mock's 60 s, would be dropped without a word). | Every record is a deliberate, reviewed act now that both paths confirm first, so a server-side repeat guard can only lose a real report. The POST also keeps the access code and the notes out of every URL, which `httpx` logs at INFO and a proxy may keep (D-72). Pinned by `tests/test_conduct_calls.py` (two records within the window are two forced incidents under both semantics, notes kept; the body; no query; 403, 404, a lost reply; no log line holds a code, a name, the notes or an email). |

A status line heads the scan screen (loading, the count with Reload, "Couldn't load the attendee list" with Retry, "Not available for this account" on a 403); offline, scanning is paused with "CoC capture needs a connection" and Retry and Reload are disabled.
