| D-434 | **A CoC scan opens the review dialog, and the scan screen awaits it; a code the loaded directory lacks is reviewed and may be recorded all the same, the server's 404 shown in the dialog** (settles #760's open question under D-101, its option (a); D-56, D-65, D-70, D-72, D-208, D-410, D-416, D-420, D-421, D-430, D-432; MASK-03, MASK-04, MASK-05, SHELL-06; ux.md §7.2, §7.3, architecture §12.5).<br>• **The dialog:** `ui/coc_review_dialog.py`'s `review_dialog()`, an `AlertDialog(modal=True, scrollable=True)` shown through `ft.use_dialog` by `ui/coc_review.py`'s `use_review(on_recorded=)`: a level-1 heading "Record an incident", the name (or the code), "Badge: Grace S." when it differs, the count in words with its level's icon (`coc.review.count`, one node), or "Not in the loaded attendee list" for a code the directory lacks; `TextField(label="What happened?", hint_text=…, multiline=True, max_length=2000, capitalization=SENTENCES)` (`coc.review.notes`); Cancel (`coc.review.cancel`) and Record incident (`coc.review.record`), a ring and "Recording…", disabled, while the call is out, Cancel still enabled. The notes live in the field only: sent once, never stored or logged, gone when it closes.<br>• **The scan path:** `CocTool`'s `on_badge` awaits `ReviewDialog.open(code, attendee)`, a future the dialog resolves, so the scan screen's single flight drops every submit while it is open (their text kept), and `paused` says "Paused while the review is open"; the 4 s cooldown stops a held badge from reopening it after it closes, and the focus goes back to the field. Record sends `conduct.record()` once (a second tap while it is out sends nothing, D-430); the reply closes the dialog with `ScanOutcome.RECORDED` ("Incident recorded", the reply's name, "2 on record": neutral fill, `TASK_ALT`, a light haptic, polite; never the green of a check-in) and sets the directory row's count (`with_count()`). Cancel or Back gives `ScanOutcome.NOT_RECORDED` ("Not recorded" and the name: neutral, `REMOVE_CIRCLE_OUTLINE`, no haptic); Cancel while the call is out, or after a call that may have landed, gives "Not confirmed" with the D-410 line, and the reply, if it comes, still sets the count; each opening's Cancel and dismissal answer only that opening, so an earlier dialog's late dismissal never closes the next. Leaving the tool resolves a waiting review as closed.<br>• **Errors keep the dialog and its notes**, said in `coc.review.problem` (a live region; an announcement on iOS): a refusal is "Not recorded." and the server's message (the mock's 404 "Unknown attendee access code."); no connection before the request left is "No connection. Nothing was recorded."; a `NetworkError` with `maybe_sent`, or an answer that couldn't be read (`ApiError.unreadable`, #874), is "The incident may have been recorded. Check the count before recording again." and Record becomes "Record anyway"; that stays until a reply, so a later "No connection" keeps it. Awaiting the dialog in the handler reuses the scan screen's one-at-a-time flight, so no second review can open under the first. | D-438 | **The scan screen's badge field never loses `can_request_focus`, and the screen puts the focus back in it when Back pops a page pushed over it, as when a pause ends** (#775; amends D-420's pauses and, for the scan screen only, D-330; D-416, D-425, D-428, D-433; ux.md §7.2, §9).<br>• **The cause:** the field took `use_field_query()`'s `focusable` as its `can_request_focus` (D-330), so it let the focus go as the leads list, the sponsor picker or the door's session picker opened, and Back returned with nothing focused: a HID scanner's next code went nowhere. A focus asked for as `focusable` turns back on is dropped (F288), which is why #770's effect failed on the emulator.<br>• **The fix:** `ScanScreen` passes no `can_request_focus` (Flutter's default, true) and keeps one `ready` flag, no pause and its View on top (`FieldQuery.focusable`); its effect on `[ready]` focuses the field when `ready` turns true again, after a sheet, a tool's dialog or a pushed page. The covered field still loses the primary focus to the pushed route's scope, so typing on that page never reaches it, and Flutter's own route focus restoration agrees with the explicit request on Back. A result that comes while covered is still dropped (D-426).<br>• **Why not D-330 here:** D-330 keeps a search field's keyboard from coming back over results on Back; on the scan screen the focused field, keyboard and all, is what Back should return to (D-428's "Back returns focus to the entry"). | Every tool's pushed page benefits, from the shared screen. Pinned by `tests/test_scan_screen.py` (fails with the field taking `focusable`: it is false while covered and nothing asks for the focus on Back). On the Pixel 6a AVD (API 36, release APK) as `sponsor@example.com`: Leads (1), then Back, `uiautomator dump` showed `scan.entry` `focused="true"`, and `adb shell input text MOCK0001:v4l1d01` then `KEYCODE_ENTER` gave "Lead captured, Ada". On the iOS 26.5 iPhone 17 simulator: after Back the field had its focus ring and the keyboard, and Maestro's `inputText` of a payload with a newline, with no tap, captured MOCK0002. |
| D-471 | **A submit the scan screen drops, while a handler runs or scanning is paused, goes with its text and puts the focus back; after a drop for a running handler the field's line says "Scanned too soon: scan that badge again", until the next submit that is read** (settles #875's open question under D-101; amends D-416 and D-420's "its text kept"; F274, U254, D-426).<br>• **Why:** Enter has to leave the field ready for the next scan, and a HID scanner only types after what the field holds. Kept text would join the next scan, and `parse_badge()` accepts the join: `MOCK0002:v2MOCK0003:v3` reads as code `MOCK0002` with the lead validator `v3`. Since #830 Android's Enter leaves the focus in the field, so the join would come from the very next scan.<br>• **The code:** `ScanScreen.submit()` calls `FieldQuery.clear()` on a drop, which empties the field and asks for the focus (iOS's Enter took it, F274).<br>• **The line:** the kept text was the only sign of a drop, so the helper line takes over from it for a running handler; a pause's own line already says why. It is visual, and a screen reader hears it when it reads the field.<br>• **Under a modal sheet** nothing changes: the sheet's route takes the focus and the keyboard goes (seen on both platforms with the queue sheet), so a scan goes nowhere and no submit comes. The drop on a pause is for the pauses with no modal, such as Door check with no session.<br>• **"Not a badge code" went the same way** in D-473 (#941): it too kept its text, so a scan after it joined it.<br>• **Rejected:** selecting the kept text so the next scan replaces it (`TextField.selection` stays on the control and Flet applies it again at each rebuild, `textfield.dart:206-210`, so a stale selection could swallow a later scan's first keys unless a handler reports every caret move); keeping it and refusing a joined payload (a badge may have more than two segments, so a join can't be told from a badge); queueing the scan to run after the handler (no scan lost, but it changes the one-at-a-time contract, and a tool's sheet can open in between); a denial's haptic on a drop (a second pull on the same badge would buzz a denial over the result on its way). | Each scan is read alone, and nothing that was scanned too soon reaches the server under another badge's name. The cost: someone who typed a code during a send types it again. Reversible: one line in `submit()`. Pinned by `tests/test_scan_screen.py` (scanning through a slow send and past a pause, typed after whatever the field holds) and seen on an Android emulator and an iOS simulator (#875's PR). |
| D-473 | **A refused entry ("Not a badge code") goes with its text like every other submit, and the panel gives the text back through an "Edit code" button** (settles #941 under D-101, its first option with a way to correct by hand; amends D-416 and D-471; F274, U254).<br>• **Why:** the field kept the text "for correction", but a HID scanner types after whatever the field holds, and `parse_badge()` accepts the join: `NOCOLON` then `MOCK0005:v4l1d05` queued a lead for `NOCOLONMOCK0005`, and the badge scanned is lost (seen on a Pixel 6a AVD against the mock). A scanner and a typist look the same to the field, so one of the two had to pay.<br>• **The code:** `ScanScreen.submit()` calls `FieldQuery.clear()` once it has parsed and before it acts on the verdict, so the field is empty whatever the text; for a refused one it then shows `NOT_A_BADGE` with the trimmed text as the title ("Not a badge code", the text, "Check the code and try again."). While that result shows, the screen's own `FilledTonalButton` "Edit code" (`scan.edit`, `ui/tool_controls.py`'s `tool_button`, before the tool's `result_actions`) calls `FieldQuery.put(text)`, which sends the text to the field and puts the focus after it, as a Clear sends "" and focuses (`clear` is `put("")`; the same race handling), and drops the result with its button, since the text is back in the field. An empty entry has no text, so no button.<br>• **Told apart by a gesture, not by timing:** a scanner never taps; a typist who wants the text back does. A rule on how fast the keys come would fail on a slow emulator or a Bluetooth scanner, and cannot be pinned by a test. After "Edit code" the text is in the field on purpose, so a scan then joins it, as hand-typing would: that is the typist's choice.<br>• **Rejected:** keeping the text selected so the next keys replace it (D-471's hazard: `TextField.selection` stays on the control and is applied again at each rebuild); keeping it and stripping it from the front of the next submit (a typist's correction that appends starts with the same text, so it would lose its first part, and the two cannot be told apart by the text); clearing it and nothing more (a typist retypes from memory, with no way to see the text again once the panel is replaced).<br>• **Details:** the refused text travels as `ScanResult.entry` (trimmed, empty for every other result, so an empty entry has no button), and the tool's `clear_after` (the Code of Conduct's 15 s) skips a refused entry, which holds no one's details and whose button must not vanish under a typist; the app going to the background still clears it. A very long text is cut to its first 60 characters, which is what is spoken, and the panel shows two lines of that: a deliberate exception to D-33's no-clipping rule, since "Edit code" gives back the whole text (#965, `core/badges.py`'s `entry_title()`). Tapping "Edit code" while a scan's handler runs and submitting before it ends is dropped as D-471 says. | Each scan is read alone after a refused entry too, and a typo is one tap from being fixed. The cost: one more button, shown only after a refused entry. Reversible: `submit()` and one button. Pinned by `tests/test_scan_screen.py`; seen on an Android emulator and an iOS simulator against the mock (#941's PR). |
| D-475 | **At large text the scan screen leads with its badge field, then the result, and the panel stacks its icon above its words** (settles #955 under D-101; amends D-416; D-216, D-328, D-229, D-33; ux.md §7.2, §9).<br>• **When:** from `Prefs.time_above`, the size where a slot's time leaves its gutter (iOS's first Accessibility size on a 402 dp phone, Android's 180 %); below it the screen keeps its order and the panel its icon beside the words.<br>• **Why:** at iOS's largest text everything above the field (the sync chip, the context line, the camera note, the panel) was four screens tall. Flutter scrolls a focused field into view, so the screen opened at its end, and with the keyboard up the result's heading ended above the app bar; and the 32 dp icon, grown with the text, took a third of the card, so "Allergy" and "Vegetarian" broke inside themselves.<br>• **The code:** `ScanScreen` builds the body as field, panel, the tool's line for the result, the result's buttons ("Edit code" first) and the tool's buttons, then the sync chip, the context and the camera note; reading order follows the visual order. `result_panel(large_text=)` puts a `Column[icon, words]` in the card, the icon 40 dp and not grown with the text (stacked, it would fill the screen; D-373 rule 3, a mark beside no line stays fixed), and the words, no longer `expand`ed, take the card's whole width.<br>• **Rejected:** keeping the order and shrinking the panel (the name and the allergy chip must stay large, D-229); moving only the context below the field (the sync chip and the camera note would still push the panel down); a second, fixed field above the keyboard (D-471 and D-473 rest on one field).<br>• **Cost:** at large text Door's session chip and headcount, and Swag's categories, are a scroll below the result, not above it. | The outcome is the first thing on the screen with the keyboard up, and tag words stay whole. Reversible: the body's order and one flag in `result_panel`. Pinned by `tests/test_scan_screen.py` and seen on an Android emulator at 200 % and an iOS simulator at the largest text (#955's PR). |
| D-476 | **The CoC tool says "attendee list", as Door check and its own failure line do** (amends D-439's quoted words; #991). "Loading attendee list…", "Reload attendee list" and "Type a name to search the attendee list." The code's name for it, `Directory`, and identifiers such as `coc.directory.reload` stay, so flows don't change. |
| D-477 | **A badge payload is `code:rest` with a code of URL-unreserved characters (`A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ ~ -`) that is not the first word of a known QR format (`BEGIN`, `MECARD`, `MATMSG`, `WIFI`, `SMSTO`, `SMS`, `TEL`, `MAILTO`, `GEO`), a `rest` that does not start with `//`, and no control character anywhere; anything else is "Not a badge code"** (settles #978 under D-101; amends D-416; notes D-473, D-471, D-65).<br>• **Why:** Ionic splits on `:` and checks nothing (feature LEAD-03 kept that rule; this narrows it), so `https://example.org/a` read as code `https` and a vCard as code `BEGIN`: the text reached the tool's handler and the panel said "Not registered", or a Lead scanner queued a lead for `https`. A HID scan of any QR code that is not a badge usually has a colon.<br>• **The evidence for the code's shape:** the original app puts the access code unencoded into request paths, queries and storage keys (`fetch_products/?attendee_access_code=` + code, `lead_retrieval/` + code + `/note/`, `pending-scan-` + code), which would break on a code holding `/ ? & #` or `%` (two other calls do `encodeURIComponent` it, `pycon-api.ts:224, 298`), so the server's codes are very likely URL-safe. The unreserved set is a conservative choice inside what that allows: `@ ! * ' ( ) , ; = $` would also survive in a path. The mock's `MOCK0001` and the original's dev simulation `SIM12345:SIMULATED` fit. Nothing in this repo or the original's source states the real format.<br>• **The rules, in `parse_badge()`:** the access code (segment 0) must match `CODE_SHAPE`; the text after the first `:` must not start with `//` (a URL's authority); the code must not be a QR format's first word, compared in lower case (a HID scanner types a vCard one line per Enter, so its first line has no newline; a Wi-Fi code's password would otherwise become the lead validator); the trimmed text must hold no newline or other control character (a contact card in one text, a multi-line paste). The split is unchanged: door = segment 1, lead = the last, CoC the code only. Validators are otherwise free.<br>• **A refused scan** behaves as D-473 says: the field is cleared, the panel shows the text, "Edit code" gives it back.<br>• **The risk, and why the rule is loose:** a real code outside the set would make that badge unscannable, by camera and by hand, which is worse than the bug (a URL scan shown as "Not registered"). So the set is wide and the validators are free. Not caught: the other lines of a vCard typed line by line (`FN:Ada`, `VERSION:3.0`), `otpauth:` and any scheme off the list, which have a badge's shape; the server answers "not registered". The format list is a short backstop for the common ones, not a complete deny-list of schemes, which is what was rejected. If the real codes fall outside the set, `CODE_SHAPE` is the one line to widen.<br>• **Rejected:** keeping Ionic's rule (the bug stays; the repo's own #965 text assumed these were refused); an allow-list of the mock's `[A-Z]+[0-9]+` (it would refuse a real code that differs); a complete URL-scheme deny-list (it is never complete). | A scanned URL or contact card is told apart from a badge at the door and in the lead booth, and no real badge the evidence points to is refused. Reversible: one regex and two tests. Pinned by `tests/test_badge_codes.py` and `tests/test_scan_screen.py`. |

The flows open `confcompanion://app/more/app`, with `adb` on Android (Maestro reading the tree would change what the app does, F72) and `maestro/subflows/open_link.yaml` on iOS (it answers the "Open" prompt, F31). Reversible; both go when #288 lets a flow scroll. |

It is the same "over" as Welcome's thanks and Now's `Season.OVER`.<br>• **Where it shows:** with listings only (a feed without them says "No job listings yet" and nothing could have closed), above the cards whatever the filter keeps or misses, and, at large text (D-216), after the strip, field and count that head the list there. It is evaluated when the page renders, with no tick: a render comes with every refresh and keystroke, and the page open across the day's change at the venue is not a case worth a timer.<br>• **Not chosen:** a list of dead links in `content.toml` whose rows hide (it goes stale, and a role's words without its link confuse); hiding Jobs after the conference (loses a showcase page whose links mostly still work); checking links at run time or editing the snapshot (D-135, #869's scope). | 2026-11-02, one GET with a phone's user agent: 27 of the feed's 44 role links returned 404 (Bloomberg 15 of 15, Temporal 9 of 9, Sentry 3 of 5), 5 of Hudson River Trading's 403, probably bot blocking. | D-439 | *(Its "attendee directory" became "attendee list" by D-476.)* **The CoC name search is a full-height modal `BottomSheet` over the scan screen, opened by "Find by name" under the badge field; a row's tap opens #760's review dialog, and its result reaches the panel through `ScanScreen(posted=)`** (settles #761's open question under D-101, its option (a); D-46, D-56, D-70, D-72, D-208, D-420, D-430, D-432, D-434, D-438; MASK-02, MASK-03; ux.md §7.3, architecture §12.5).<br>• **The button:** `ui/coc_search_sheet.py`'s `find_button()`, an `OutlinedButton("Find by name", icon=PERSON_SEARCH)` (`coc.find`), disabled while the directory isn't loaded or the device is offline, its name then saying why in #759's words ("Find by name. CoC capture needs a connection", "… Loading attendee directory…"; `staff_coc.find_reason()`).<br>• **The sheet:** `find_sheet()`, `BottomSheet(scrollable=True, show_drag_handle=True, use_safe_area=True)` whose column holds the level-1 heading "Find attendee" with Close (`coc.search.close`), the `SearchField` "Search by name" (`coc.search`, `autofocus`), and an expanding `ListView`, so it takes the screen's height with the field pinned. Under the field: "Type a name to search the attendee directory."; "No attendees match “x”" with Clear search; or a live line ("3 attendees", `coc.search.count`; "Showing 25. Refine your search.", `coc.search.more`) and the rows of `core/conduct.py`'s `search()`, one node each (`coc.search.row.<index>`, never the access code), "Margaret Test, badge Maggie, 3 incidents on record", the count drawn by the review's `count_row()` (its level's icon and colour).<br>• **The hook:** `ui/coc_search.py`'s `use_find(attendees=, on_pick=)` keeps a `Finding(typed, query, clears, opening)` in `use_state` beside a `latest` ref the handlers read (#756); `query` follows `typed` 250 ms after typing pauses (a coroutine effect Flet restarts at each keystroke, D-46). The field is built from what Flutter last reported, through a `Ref` made for each opening and each Clear (D-243), so the next opening starts empty. Each opening's token is `Finding.opening`: a dismissal for another one closes nothing. A tap sets the search to None, then awaits `on_pick`, in one handler, so the review opens in the same render and the pause never lapses between them.<br>• **The record:** `CocTool`'s `picked(attendee)` awaits `use_review().open(code, attendee)` and hands its result to `ScanScreen(posted=Posted(result, serial))`; `ScanScreen` shows each new `Posted` as a scan's result (haptics, announcement) unless it has unmounted or is not on top. Paused reasons: the review's, then "Paused while the search is open", then offline's.<br>• **Privacy:** the query and the rows live only in the hook's state while the sheet is open, never stored or logged (D-56, D-72).<br>• **Rejected:** (b) a pushed page `…/staff/coc/find` fetching its own directory, a second download of a large personal list per search; (c) tabs "Scan · Find by name" on the shared `ScanScreen`, where a HID scan on the Find tab would type into the search (#743's reason). | One directory in one component's memory; the pause and the focus come from the scan screen's existing `paused=`, and the record path is the scan's. Pinned by `tests/test_coc_search.py` (the button's reasons; the sheet's states, the 25-row cap over 3,004 attendees, a nickname and an accented name; a HID submit dropped while open; a row's tap, Record, the panel and the new count; Cancel; Close and Back forget the query and refocus the field; a late dismissal; the debounce; no access code in an identifier, no query or code in a log). |
| D-440 | **A scan result can clear: `ScanScreen(clear_after=, clear_in_background=)` drops it from state that many seconds after it shows and when the app goes to the background; the CoC tool uses both (15 s), closes its name search on the same `pause` and keeps an open review dialog with its notes; a recorded result's email stays hidden until "Show email"** (#762; O-7, MASK-05; D-56, D-65, D-70, D-72, D-173, D-416, D-420, D-439; ux.md §7.2, §7.3, architecture §12.5).<br>• **The timer** (`ui/scan_clear.py`) starts at the render that shows the result, which is when the reply arrived: Ionic started 5 s when the request left, so a slow reply could vanish unseen. It is the effect's coroutine on `[serial, after]`, so the next result restarts it and unmount cancels it. The age line counts down in words ("Read 3 seconds ago. Clears in 12 seconds"), never colour or motion.<br>• **The background:** one loop per mounted screen waits on `Services.background` (set on `pause`, D-420) and calls the latest action; the CoC tool's own loop closes Find by name, query and all.<br>• **Not said:** the panel is a live region only while a result shows, so the clear's "Ready to scan" is not announced (Flutter's Android bridge sends a live-region event only when the node is still live as its label changes, `AccessibilityBridge.java:1705`), and a clear gives no haptics.<br>• **The open review stays** in the background, with its notes: losing a half-written report is worse than a dialog that shows a name and a count, and it shows no email.<br>• **The email** rides in `ScanResult.email` (out of `spoken` and the repr). "Show email" (`coc.show_email`, a result action) reveals it on its own line under the panel (`result_note`, `coc.email`), outside the live region and above the badge field, so the keyboard the field keeps up never covers it; the reveal is the result's serial, which a ref keeps unique, so the next result starts hidden. `result_actions` takes the `Shown`, and a `Posted` result is handed over once, so no tool keeps a copy after the clear.<br>• **Rejected:** clearing the open review in the background (a lost report); the email under the field with its button (the keyboard covered it on both phones); keeping the panel live after a clear (TalkBack would say "Ready to scan" at every clear). | A result about a person leaves the screen on its own, and when the phone is put away; a secure-screen flag that also covers the OS snapshot is upstream (U270, #786). Pinned by `tests/test_scan_clear.py` (the timer from the result with a reply 4 s late, a restart per result, the clear on `pause`, other tools unchanged, the countdown words, the hand-over) and `tests/test_coc_clear.py` (15 s from a slow capture reply, the search closed and the dialog kept on `pause`, Show and Hide email, the next result and the clear hiding it, no email in a log through `RedactingFormatter`). `maestro/journeys/coc_capture.yaml` passed on the Pixel 6a AVD (API 36, release APK) and the iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) against the local mock. |
| D-441 | **Door check admits on the device and doesn't wait for the worker; a `redeem_products` reply of 200 with `errors` syncs the door row, its message kept and shown in the queue sheet as "Confirmed · Already redeemed"** (settles #766's open question under D-101, its option (a); the door's counterpart of D-426; D-56, D-57, D-407, D-410, D-120, D-421; DOOR-04, DOOR-07, STORE-12, API-07; ux.md §7.3, architecture §12.2, §12.5).<br>• **The decision:** `core/admission.decide(inventory, code, product_ids, kept)` (pure) says whether the code is on the preloaded list for one of the session's products, with what it bought of them, and whether one of the account's door rows for the code (pending, synced or failed; `OutboxDb.door_rows()`) covers one of them: Ionic's per-product overlap, read from the kept rows (STORE-12), not a key of its own. A badge off the list is looked up online (`fetch_attendee_products(door=True)`): what is left to redeem admits; bought with none left is "Already checked in" · "Checked in on another device"; nothing bought or a 404 is "Not registered for this session"; no answer is "Can't check this badge". A failed read or write of the outbox is "Not checked in" (D-120), never "no check-ins".<br>• **Optimistic, not awaited:** `door_check.check_in()` enqueues `admit` (one row per `code|sorted products|visit`, the visit a uuid made on mount; payload `redeem_payload(..., swag=False)`; no sponsor), updates the `Staff` counts, kicks the worker and returns "Checked in" at once ("Admits 2" over one; "Saved. Will sync." added after a pass that ended offline). Unlike the Lead scanner (D-426) it never waits: the decision is local, the line can't wait 3 s a person, and the server's answer can't change who is at the door. The sync chip and the queue sheet say how the row syncs. A rescan only reads: no row, no count update, no kick.<br>• **A reply with `errors`:** `after_success()` as for any 200, the reply stored under `"result"`; `core/admission.redeem_errors(row)` reads its messages (`parse_redeem_reply()`), and `ui/queue_rows.row_tile()` shows the row as "Confirmed · Already redeemed" with `WARNING_AMBER` and the server's messages under it (after " · may be recorded twice" when flagged).<br>• **Entries:** `BUILT_TOOLS` and `QUEUED_TOOLS` gain Door check (D-421), so More's row and Now's card open it, with the outbox's counts.<br>• **Rejected:** (b) an `ApiError`, retried every 60 s and failed at 10: the same refused payload ten times, and "Failed" though the server has a record; (c) synced and silent, which hides a duplicate entry the server saw (against D-57 and D-410). | The person is already inside and resending can't change the server's answer; saying it once, where the rows are, is honest without stopping the line. Pinned by `tests/test_door_check.py` (every outcome, two products in one row, a rescan, another session's own row, offline then a relaunch syncing once, a reply with `errors`, a lost answer flagged and nothing lost, the handler never sending), `tests/test_admission.py` (`decide()`, `admitted()`, `redeem_errors()`), `tests/test_queue_sheet.py` (the row's words and icon), `tests/test_staff_door.py` and `tests/test_door_preload.py` (the screen, the 4 s cooldown) and `tests/test_staff_entry.py` (the entries). |
| D-442 | **Door check's headcount is the signed-in account's check-ins on this device for the chosen session's products, read from the outbox: the distinct access codes among its `door` rows (pending, synced or failed) whose admitted products overlap the session's; a door result carries the attendee's allergy and dietary needs as `ScanResult.tags`** (settles #767's open question under D-101, its option (a); D-56, D-65, D-120, D-306, D-416, D-420, D-441, O-7; DOOR-05, DOOR-06, STORE-12; ux.md §7.2, §7.3, architecture §12.5).<br>• **The count:** `core/admission.headcount(rows, product_ids)` (pure, the same `admitted()` that "Already checked in" reads) over `OutboxDb.rows(owner, every status)`; `screens/door_headcount.use_headcount()` re-reads it on each `Staff.revision` (each admission and each sync pass). So it survives a restart and a trip to another tool, comes back when the session is chosen again, and never moves on a duplicate; a new account starts from its own rows (D-414 purges synced ones at a switch). It is per device: the API has no aggregate. Shown under the session chip as "42 checked in" (`DISPLAY_SMALL`, `door.headcount`, one node read "42 checked in for this session", not a live region); a failed read says "Check-in count unavailable", never 0 (D-120).<br>• **The tags:** `Tag(text, warning=False)` is data (D-416), so the other tools are unchanged; `core/admission.tags(attendee)` gives "Allergy: peanuts" (or "Allergy" when none is named; an allergy named without the flag still counts) as a warning, then the diet with a capital ("Vegetarian"), from the preloaded list or the online lookup, on "Checked in" and on "Already checked in" (an allergy matters on a second pass). `ui/scan_result.tag_row()` draws them right under the name, a wrapping row of chips, the warning first, larger (`LABEL_LARGE` bold, `WARNING_AMBER` 20 dp) with the card's colours swapped, the others outlined in the label's colour (`RESTAURANT_OUTLINED`); every chip has a 1 dp edge in the label's colour, so it reads at the card's own contrast on every fill (AA; 7:1 in high contrast, D-306). They are said after the name, one line: "Allergy: peanuts. Vegetarian." (under the name rather than under the details, so reading order follows the visual order, ux.md §9).<br>• **Rejected:** (b) Ionic's in-memory counter, reset on any change of category or session: a restart or a look at the Lead scanner resets it to 0 halfway through a session. | One source of truth with "Already checked in", so the two never disagree, and nothing new is stored. Pinned by `tests/test_door_headcount.py` (the rule, the tags, each seed attendee's chips, the count after a duplicate, a relaunch and a change of session), `tests/test_scan_tags.py` (words, order, icons, edges and contrast on every fill in light, dark and high contrast) and `tests/test_door_check.py`. |
| D-456 | **The staff tools are all four, in the order lead, door, swag, CoC; all four are built, and none moved to `later`** (#819; settles O-7 under D-101 as recommended).<br>• **The UX defaults O-7 named are built too:** attendee names at the door, available swag items pre-ticked, and the CoC email hidden until revealed (ux.md §7.3).<br>• **Built is not the P3 exit:** the scripted scenarios on devices stay with #833, and the camera still waits on E1. | `BUILT_TOOLS` in `core/staff_tools.py` holds the four keys in this order, and the epics #39, #40, #41 and #42 are closed, so O-7's option of letting swag and CoC slip no longer applies. |
| D-443 | **The expo hall's zoom buttons go in their 48 dp row wherever their 160 dp column, at the fitted plan, would reach over the plan; the column stays where the plan leaves it room** (#791; amends D-370's placement, keeps D-377).<br>• **Why:** the column sits 16 dp in from the pane's bottom right and the plan is centred in the viewer, so on a phone in portrait the column reached over the plan's lower right corner: at 1x with no card Zoom in covered the lower 11 px of booth 709 on the 720 x 1600, 280-dpi AVD (#790) and all of it on the iPhone 17 (the column's top at y 584, the booth at 590-605). At 1x the viewer cannot pan (its translation is held to `[box (1 - s), 0]`), so the camera cannot move the plan out from under it, and insetting the plan's fit would move every viewer's geometry; the row, 64 dp from the bottom, clears the plan on both (the iPhone's plan ends at y 640, the row starts at 696).<br>• **How:** `screens/expo_layout.py` (the bottom column's arithmetic, out of `booth_card.py`, which had passed D-66's ~400 lines) gains `over_plan(box, shown)`, true when the column's rectangle meets the fitted plan's; `card_layout()` and `covered()` take it as `crowded=`, so a booth brought into view lands above the row (D-377). The choice depends on the viewer and the fitted plan only, never on the zoom, so the buttons don't move while the user zooms; a tablet in portrait keeps the column. | Pinned by `tests/test_expo_layout.py`: every 2026 booth, at the AVD's and the iPhone 17's measured hall sizes, lies clear of the buttons at 1x (booth 709 failed on both with the column). |
| D-444 | *(The card's semantics amended by D-447: one node labelled by its words.)* **The floor viewer's label card never grows over its pin: its height is bound by what the fitted plan leaves under the pin, and its words scroll inside it** (#793; amends ux.md §6.1 item 3).<br>• **How:** `screens/plan_label.py`'s `label_room(box, shown, pin)` (pure) is the viewer's height less the 16 dp inset and the pin's ring at its widest (`PIN_BOX / 2` under its point) at the fitted plan, never under `LABEL_MIN` (72 dp, above the card's height at the default size, so it never scrolls there); its two lines are a `Column` measured with `on_size_change` (F51; the height kept in `PlanViewer`'s state), and only when they and the card's 12 dp padding above and below are taller than the room (`scrolls()`) does the `Card` take the room as `max_height` (Flet's `LayoutControl` constraint, a `ConstrainedBox`) and their outer `Column` scroll, as the booth card's body (D-370). Always scrolling would stretch a short card to the room: at the pin, `ScrollableControl` gives a scrolling `Column`'s content the bound as its least height (`scrollable_control.dart`, `_InnerConstraintsEnforcer`), so Room 103ABC's two lines stood 314 dp tall on the iPhone 17 (Codex's review of #798). Fixed by the fitted plan, never the zoom, so the card does not resize while the user zooms (as D-443). The job fair's table card is unchanged.<br>• **Not chosen:** a camera move as the expo's (D-377), which leaves the pin under the card at fit; the label alone at the largest sizes, which drops what happens in the room and can still cover a low pin. | On the iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) at `accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large` with Bold Text, Room 103ABC's card covered the plan's left half and the pin (its top at y 427 pt, the pin at about 479); with the bound its top sits at 504, the pin in sight above it, and a swipe on the card scrolls "Concourse level · Talk track / Security track" into view; Room 203C's, the lowest Upper pin (59 %), likewise. Maestro's hierarchy shows one node, "Room 103\nConcourse level · Talk track / Security track", after the plan's. `tests/test_plan_label.py` checks every 2026 pin at the iPhone's and the AVD's viewer sizes: none hits the floor, so every pin stays clear of the card in portrait. |
| D-447 | *(The expo hall's bar still while its Map shows, D-479.)* **The floor viewer's label card is one node labelled by its words, and the floor viewer's app bar keeps its colour when something in its body scrolls** (#800; amends D-444).<br>• **The id:** `label_card()` wraps the card in `Semantics(label="<name>\n<plan · sublabel>", exclude_semantics=True)` inside `named("plan.label", ...)`, at every size, so the scrolling `Column` never joins the identifier's node. Merged in, it made that node a scrollable one, and iOS shows a scrollable node as a `FlutterSemanticsScrollView`, which forwards the label but not `accessibilityIdentifier` (F297): Maestro saw the words without `plan.label`. The label is what the merge read before, so the journeys' assertions are unchanged; a screen reader reads the whole label without scrolling.<br>• **The bar:** `AppScaffold(still_bar=True)` gives the `AppBar` `bgcolor=SURFACE` (its unscrolled colour) and `elevation_on_scroll=0`, so neither the scrolled-under `surface_container` nor the surface tint of its elevation applies (F297). `PlanViewer` passes it always: its plan is an `InteractiveViewer`, which sends no scroll notifications, so its bar has no page scroll to show; the expo hall passes it while its Map shows (D-479). Not chosen: an `upstream` issue for `AppBar.notification_predicate`, since the two existing properties settle it. | On the iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) at the largest text, `?pin=103abc`'s card scrolled and, after a swipe inside it, Maestro's hierarchy still showed `plan.label` with its words and the bar stayed white; on the Pixel 6a AVD (API 36) at 200 % `uiautomator dump` showed `resource-id="plan.label"` with the words, `scrollable="false"`. Pinned by `tests/test_plan_label.py` and `tests/test_scaffold.py`. The expo hall's booth card scrolls the same way under its own bar (D-370), but that View's List pane scrolls the page too, so it was not this choice; D-479 makes it per view. |
| D-450 | **A search field shows the keyboard's Search key on Android and iOS: `SearchField` passes `TextInputAction.SEARCH` whenever it has an `on_submit`, and keeps the default text keyboard** (#807, U30; amends D-193).<br>• **Search:** the global search's field, the only `SearchField` with an `on_submit`, shows Search: Android's `imeOptions` carry `IME_ACTION_SEARCH` and Gboard draws the magnifier; the iOS 26.5 simulator's key reads "search". The filter fields (Speakers, Sprints, Jobs, Expo, the staff lists, CoC's sheet) narrow the list at every keystroke and have no `on_submit`, so they keep Flutter's default Done.<br>• **No `KeyboardType.WEB_SEARCH`:** Flutter's `webSearch` is iOS's `UIKeyboardTypeWebSearch`, whose return key is Go whatever the action asks (seen on the iOS 26.5 simulator: the key read "go" with `SEARCH`); on Android it changed nothing visible (`inputType` stays text with no suggestions). So `SearchField` lost its `keyboard_type` argument and every field uses the text keyboard.<br>• **Other fields:** only Sign in's email field changes, to `TextInputAction.NEXT` (the password comes next; its `on_submit` still focuses the password). The password keeps Done (it signs in), the scan screen's badge entry keeps Done (it looks up one code, and HID scanners send Enter), and multiline notes keep Newline. |
| D-451 | *(Ended by D-481: no checkout to keep at a pin. The main checkout's `.flet-src` is an optional clone or nothing.)* **The main checkout's `.flet-src` is the agents' own clone of flet-dev/flet, kept at the pin; whoever merges a pin bump moves it right after** (#812; the owner's direction, 2026-10-31: the agents are responsible for this repo and don't wait on him; amends D-81's setup and D-219).<br>• **Before:** it was a `git worktree` of the owner's Flet repo (development.md §2.2), so only he could move it, and after #808 it stayed at `08b89cce5`: the main checkout's app and checks ran a Flet without `RefreshIndicator` and `TextInputAction`, which `main` already used. Each worktree's APFS clone kept its `.git` file, so a git write in a clone moved the owner's Flet worktree, hence the rule that no clone takes a git write.<br>• **Now:** a standalone `git clone --filter=blob:none`, detached at `.flet-ref`. The bump PR moves its own worktree's clone (a fetch and a detached checkout, which now touch nothing else, only after checking that `.flet-src` is no link and has a `.git` folder, since a copy made before D-451 still holds the owner's `.git` file; then `find .flet-src/sdk/python/packages -mindepth 2 -maxdepth 2 -type d -name build -exec rm -rf {} +` (a form that also works in zsh, #849), the wheel build folders of `flet` and the bundled extensions, whose stale files a checkout leaves and a wheel would ship), and after the merge the merger moves the main checkout's the same way, then `uv sync --locked` and `rm -rf build` there, and checks the pin (development.md §2.2). The companion client's three iOS files are restored with `git restore` in each clone, never copied from the main checkout, which may be at another pin. Nobody edits Flet's files in any `.flet-src` (D-213).<br>• **The owner's worktree was moved, not deleted:** to `~/PycharmProjects/flet-dev/flet-pycon-worktree`, re-registered with `git worktree repair`, its HEAD and its 346 uncommitted changes intact. | On 2026-10-31 the main checkout's `.flet-src` read `08b89cce5` against `.flet-ref`'s `22a6dcb16` and had no `refresh_indicator.py`; after the switch, the §2.2 pin check printed "Flet pin OK", `test -d .flet-src/.git` held, and `uv run python -c "import flet as ft; ft.RefreshIndicator, ft.TextInputAction.SEARCH"` ran. `git worktree list` in the owner's Flet repo shows the moved worktree at `08b89cce5`. A clone of the agents' own needs no one else to move it, and copies of it can't reach anyone's Flet work. |
| D-459 | *(A team device's `outbox.db` from before this is left unread since D-466; the key holds within each `outbox-<year>.db`.)* **A lead belongs to a sponsor: an account keeps one lead row and one note row per (owner, code, sponsor), so a scan of an attendee for a second sponsor queues its own capture, and every result, list and sheet says which sponsor** (settles #829 under D-101, its option 2; amends D-426's one row per owner and code, and D-428's row key; D-56, D-57, D-410; LEAD-05, LEAD-07, LEAD-08, LEAD-10, LEAD-11, STORE-11; ux.md §7.3, architecture §12.1, §12.2, §12.5).<br>• **Why:** Ionic's `storeScan` (`pycon-api.ts` 410-422) dedupes by access code alone, so a second sponsor's scan is a silent no-op and that sponsor never gets the lead, though `syncScan` (297-301) carries `sponsor_id` per request. A staff member scanning for two sponsors is the case; asking on the result ("Add for B too?") adds a step at a busy booth for a rare case.<br>• **The key:** `core/outbox.py`'s `scan_lead()` and `save_note()` find a row by (owner, kind, code, `sponsor_id`). The same sponsor again is a repeat, as before: `ALREADY` on a synced row, sending nothing; a pending row made due now; a failed one pending from scratch. Another sponsor, after a row in any status, is `QUEUED` with its own snapshot and leaves the first row as it is. A sponsor's own account has one sponsor id, None, so it behaves as it did.<br>• **A note follows its lead:** a note's sponsor is its lead's, given by the caller from that lead's result (`ScanResult.sponsor`) or row (`LeadView`), never the sponsor chosen now, so `use_note_sheet()` no longer takes one. A note saved after a switch would otherwise wait for ever on a lead that sponsor doesn't have. `due()` and `DUE_SQL` release a note only when the same sponsor's lead is synced, and `forget_synced()` and `DELETE_SYNCED_SQL` keep only that lead for it (`sponsor_id IS`, as a sponsor's own rows hold NULL).<br>• **What it says:** `lead_capture.attributed()` starts a staff lead's details with "For Beta Corp" and, when `Enqueued.others` names another sponsor the account has the attendee in its leads for, "Also in your leads for Acme", so a scan for the wrong sponsor shows at once. The note sheet says "For Beta Corp" (`lead.note.sponsor`), the leads list has a row per lead, each naming its sponsor (`LeadView.key`, `lead.leads.row.<code>.<sponsor id>`; a sponsor's own stays `lead.leads.row.<code>`), "Leads (42)" counts both, and the queue sheet's rows add the sponsor. A sponsor's own results are unchanged.<br>• **The flag goes by the row:** the worker flags `maybe_sent` by id, and `result_of()` reads only the scan's row, so a lost or unreadable answer for one sponsor's lead flags that row and says "May be recorded twice" and "Not confirmed" of it alone; the other sponsor's lead stays as it was (D-410, #874).<br>• **Rows on a device:** no schema change, no migration, `user_version` stays 2. No column changed, and every existing row already fits the new key: the old key was stricter, and a note always took its lead's sponsor (`tests/test_outbox_existing_rows.py` writes a version 2 file by hand). A staff rescan that earlier builds kept on another sponsor's row was never sent, and can't be recovered.<br>• **The mock's contract:** no code change: it already keeps leads and notes per (sponsor, attendee), in both D-57 modes. Its README states this as the mock's choice; the real server's handling of a repeat is unknowable.<br>• **Left as it is:** an account whose role changes between a sponsor's own and staff has a None row beside a named one, even for its own sponsor, as the app doesn't know which sponsor is its own; a repeat there is the server's to dedupe, and the result still names the sponsor it went to. | Each sponsor gets its lead, and a repeat stays a repeat. The second lead costs the staff member nothing, and says what it did. |

## G. Notifications, native gaps and capabilities

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-58 | *(Where the keynotes' moments come from amended by D-274.)* No push. The notification feature is local scheduled reminders (P2): "Remind me before sessions in My schedule" (5, 10 or 15 min) and fixed "conference moments" ~~from `content.toml`~~ (lightning-talk and open-space sign-ups from `content.toml`, keynotes and panels derived from the feed, D-274). | Reminders need no server and are what attendees value most; push needs the PSF's Firebase project. |
| D-59 | *(Amended by D-221 and D-222 after S6; the event reminders' ids by D-274.)* A pure `plan(favorites, conference, prefs, content) -> list[Reminder]` builds reminders with deterministic ids: the numeric session id, or 1,000,000 + crc32(id) % 1,000,000 for synthetic ids; event reminders at 8000-8099 (8000-8049 written by hand, 8050-8099 derived from the keynotes, D-274); the Developer test reminder at 8999. `apply(plan)` cancels ~~everything~~ every pending reminder (D-222) and schedules the plan, at most the next 60 reminders (iOS keeps 64 pending; `plan()` applies the cap, D-222), with ~~inexact timing by default~~ exact timing on Android when the user allows it and inexact otherwise (D-221). It re-runs whenever favourites, settings or the feed change, and on every start and resume (D-222). | "Cancel all, reschedule" is the obvious correct algorithm. |
| D-60 | *(The `.ics` bullet amended by D-272: the alarm holds on iOS only.)* Until scheduled notifications (E3) are in our pinned Flet, the interim is:<br>• the reminder settings say "Reminders aren't available in this build";<br>• Now's "Your next session" and an in-app `Banner` cover the time the app is open;<br>• `.ics` files ~~with an alarm~~ cover calendars, with an alarm on iOS only: Android's calendar import drops it (D-272). | An honest interim, with no controls that do nothing. |
| D-61 | *(The notification status on iOS, when the primer shows and its gate amended by D-395; the denial's `Banner` replaced by Settings' card by D-401.)* Permissions are asked in context, after a one-sentence primer: notifications on the first favourite or the first reminder switch, the camera on the first staff scan. Never at launch. After a denial, a `Banner` offers "Open settings". Notification permission is requested through the notifications extension (E3, `request_permission()`) on both platforms; `flet-permission-handler` checks status, opens the app settings and handles the camera. | Ionic prompted blindly at boot. Flet's iOS template has no notification macro for permission_handler (F17), and one request path is simpler. |
| D-62 | *(How the file reaches the calendar amended by D-272: never the share sheet.)* Calendar: a pure `core/ics.py` (UTC times, stable UID, escaping and line folding) ~~shared with `await ft.Share().share_files([ft.ShareFile.from_bytes(ics_bytes, name="<slug>.ics", mime_type="text/calendar")], share_position_origin=...)` (a non-empty list; the origin anchors the sheet on tablets, D-09)~~ handed to the calendar as D-272 says (iOS: the in-app browser over a loopback server; Android: a saved file), for one session or the whole of My schedule (P2). Native add-to-calendar (E4) comes later. | Small, pure and useful. |
| D-268 | *(`CALENDAR_DOMAIN` is `confcompanion.ndonkohenri.github.io` by D-452.)* **What a calendar file holds: one function, a `UID` that never changes once shipped, and the time and place the app shows** (records D-62 for SESS-05; #380).<br>• **One function:** `core/ics.py`'s `calendar_file(sessions, *, year, now, alarm=ALARM) -> bytes`, for one session (session detail, #425) or every saved one (My schedule, #426): one `VCALENDAR` (`VERSION:2.0`, `PRODID:-//<APP_NAME>//EN`, from the identity constant O-4 swaps, never a PSF mark, escaped as TEXT like the other values) with one `VEVENT` per session in `timeline.schedule_order`. At least one session, an aware `now` and a lead of zero or more, or `ValueError`.<br>• **`UID`:** `<year>-<session id>@<CALENDAR_DOMAIN>`, the id percent-encoded (a collapsed row's id holds spaces, commas and colons), so it is the same after every refresh and next year's session with the same id is another event. `CALENDAR_DOMAIN` sits beside `APP_NAME` in `pycon_us/__init__.py`: ~~`conf-companion.invalid` until O-4 (#15)~~ `confcompanion.ndonkohenri.github.io` (D-452); once a build leaves our devices it never changes, or each re-export duplicates every event.<br>• **Times and place:** `DTSTART` is the start the app shows (`display_start`: the Job Fair's 9:30, DATA-11), as Share's text (D-206); Ionic's file used the feed's 10:00. `DTEND` is the end, left out for a notice, which ends when it starts (RFC 5545 wants `DTEND` after `DTSTART`). All in UTC (`…Z`). `LOCATION` is the place the row shows (`display_location`: the lightning talks' Arena, D-124, not the five rooms they are streamed to), none for a generic break.<br>• **Text:** `SUMMARY` the name; `DESCRIPTION` the authors as the row lists them (the lightning talks' "Hosted by …" too), then the public page, on separate lines; `URL` only when the feed gives a page (none for synthetic ids, keynotes or notices, D-206). Escaping per RFC 5545 §3.3.11 (`\`, `;`, `,`, newlines), with other control characters dropped; lines folded at 75 octets between characters, never inside a UTF-8 sequence.<br>• **Alarm:** each event has a `VALARM` (`ACTION:DISPLAY`, the name as its `DESCRIPTION`, `TRIGGER:-PT10M` by default; `alarm` gives 5 or 15 min, zero is `PT0S`).<br>• **Checked independently:** both recorded feeds' whole-agenda files parse in `icalendar` 7.3.0 and the stricter `ical` 14.3.0, every session's name, place, description, start and end and the `PRODID` of a name with `;` and `,` read back unchanged, each event with its alarm. ~~The parsers are not dependencies: `tests/test_ics.py`'s two parser tests skip without them and run with `uv run --with icalendar==7.3.0 --with ical==14.3.0 pytest tests/test_ics.py`.~~ The parsers are dev dependencies, locked at those versions, so `tests/test_ics.py`'s two parser tests run in every full check (#823). | The `UID` is the one thing a shipped build can't take back, so its form and its domain are fixed now; the shown time and place keep the calendar, Share and the rows in agreement. |
| D-63 | Capability gating is a set of import-time constants in `capabilities.py`: `available(module) = flet.utils.is_embedded() and importlib.util.find_spec(module) is not None`. One module per capability imports the extension and owns its fallback (`services/scanner.py`, `services/reminders.py`). There are no probes, no observable capabilities and no remote veto. | The companion app (`flet run --android/--ios`) runs Python on the laptop and is never embedded, so it always gets the interim (F14). It is the smallest mechanism that is still correct. |
| D-64 | A Flet core gap's interim is replaced only after a PR bumps the pinned Flet (D-81); the swap is its own tested PR (D-92), never runtime detection. | We pin one Flet version, and an upgrade is a tested change. |
| D-65 | Badge scanning (P3) uses one shared scan screen for all four tools:<br>• manual entry of the full `code:validator` payload and USB/Bluetooth HID scanners always work;<br>• the E1 camera preview appears when the extension is bundled;<br>• one accepted result per code every 4 s;<br>• outcomes are shown as colour, icon and word, with haptics and a live region;<br>• the screen stays awake while scanning. | It works without E1 and with hardware scanners, and it is accessible. |
| D-220 | *(The landed API and its adoption: D-389.)* **E3 is the official local-notifications extension plus scheduling, and it carries its own platform setup** (spike S6, #111; amends `docs/flet-gaps-and-extensions.md` §4.1, which holds the details; F52, F53).<br>• **Nothing exists at the pin:** `.flet-src` at `15854a0ac` has no local-notifications package, so E3 stays the D-60 interim until a pin bump brings `flet-dev/flet#6948` v1 and scheduling (#19; written up for the owner in #294).<br>• **The API:** v1's `initialize`, `request_permission`, `get_permission_status`, `show`, `cancel`, `cancel_all`, `get_launch_details` and `on_notification_tap`, plus the scheduling PR's `zoned_schedule(id, title, body, at, *, payload, android_schedule_mode)` (one aware instant, sent as ISO and scheduled in UTC: no time-zone database), `get_pending()` and `cancel_all_pending()` (gaps doc §4.1). **What S6 ran** over `flutter_local_notifications` 22.3.1: initializing with the launch details, the permission request and status, the tap event, `zoned_schedule` (with a boolean `exact` for the mode), `get_pending()`, `cancel_all()` and `cancel_all_pending()`. `cancel_all_pending()` removed the pending reminder and left the one already shown, on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator; the plugin's `cancelAll()` also removes shown ones. S6 did not run v1's `show` or `cancel(id)`: they wait for the adoption PR's checks. Exact scheduling without the permission is an error the caller sees (`exact_alarms_not_permitted`), never a silent fallback. E3 adds no exact-alarm permission API: `flet_permission_handler.Permission.SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` exists at the pin for the status and the settings page (S6 read the state through the plugin's `canScheduleExactNotifications()` instead; the adoption PR checks the two agree).<br>• **Android setup in the extension:** an Android library module (a plugin class in its pubspec, F52) whose manifest declares the plugin's `ScheduledNotificationReceiver`, `ScheduledNotificationBootReceiver` (boot, quick boot, package replaced) and `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, and a monochrome status-bar icon with a `tools:keep` rule. The template needs no `<receiver>` hook.<br>• **iOS setup in the extension:** a Swift plugin class that makes the app delegate `UNUserNotificationCenter`'s delegate (F53); without it, iOS never reports a tap.<br>• **From Flet's template:** core library desugaring in `:app` (U7), without which no Android build succeeds; written up for the owner in #293.<br>• **R8:** the template's default rules (`-keepnames class * { *; }`) keep the plugin's GSON models; no extra rules unless an app sets `proguard_default_rules = false`. | S6 delivered and opened reminders with the app killed, locked, rebooted and moved on Android, and killed on iOS, through exactly this shape; each piece it lists was missing in a run that failed without it. |
| D-221 | **Reminders use exact alarms on Android when the user allows them, and say so when they can't** (amends D-59's inexact default, ux.md §6.9 and NATIVE-06; spike S6).<br>• **Why:** Android 14 gives an inexact alarm a window of 75 % of the time left when it is scheduled, and delivers it anywhere in that window: at its end when nothing else wakes the device. S6 measured +90 s on a 2-minute lead, +180 s on 4 minutes, +80 s on 10 minutes and +370 s on 30 minutes. Outside forced Doze, exact alarms fired within seconds (+0.3 s to +5.4 s over three runs); in forced deep Doze an exact one came +59.3 s late (inexact: +90.1 s), so exact is sooner, never guaranteed on time, and the copy promises no more. The plan is applied hours ahead, so an inexact "10 minutes before" can arrive after the session started.<br>• **How:** declare `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` (never `USE_EXACT_ALARM`, which is for alarm and calendar apps). After notification permission (D-61), when "Remind me before sessions" is on and `Permission.SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` is not granted (`flet-permission-handler`), Settings › Reminders shows a card: "For more timely reminders, allow Alarms & reminders" with `OutlinedButton("Open settings")`, which requests it (Android opens the app's "Alarms & reminders" page). `apply()` schedules `EXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE` when allowed, `INEXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE` otherwise, and the card's wording is honest: "Without this permission, reminders may arrive late, including after a session starts." (an inexact reminder planned hours ahead can come hours late) Re-checked on resume; never asked at launch.<br>• iOS has no such setting; its reminders fired on time on the simulator. | A reminder that comes after the talk started is worse than none; exact alarms cost the user one switch, asked in context. |
| D-222 | **The cap of 60 is the plan's, `apply()` cancels only pending reminders, and it runs on every start** (amends D-59; spike S6).<br>• **The cap:** iOS kept 64 of 70 reminders and dropped the six **soonest** (the first six added), so the app must never hand it more: `plan()` returns at most `MAX_PENDING = 60`, soonest first, and `apply()` schedules what it gets. Android kept all 70; one rule for both.<br>• **Cancel pending only:** `apply()` calls `cancel_all_pending()`; `cancel_all()` would also clear a delivered reminder from the shade at every favourite (seen in S6). S6 checked `cancel_all_pending()` with one reminder shown and one pending: on both platforms the pending one went (Android's `dumpsys alarm` had no alarm left) and the shown one stayed.<br>• **On every start and resume:** a force-stop (Settings › Force stop) clears an app's alarms on Android, and the OS may drop them in other ways; re-applying the plan at start and resume brings them back. Applying is idempotent, ids are deterministic. | The OS limit drops the reminders that matter most, cancelling must not touch what the user has not read yet, and only the app can restore what the OS cleared. |
| D-389 | **E3 schedules through one `Scheduler` per session, applied at start, on resume and at every watched change; the newest apply wins** (adopts D-220's extension at the pin `08b89cce5` with D-221 and D-222; records the landed API; NOTIF-05, NEW-05; #447, P2-87).<br>• **The landed API**, whose names differ from those D-220 and the gaps doc proposed: `Notifications.schedule(id, *, when, title, body, payload, schedule_mode)` (no `zoned_schedule`: an aware `when` keeps its instant); D-221's exact flag is `schedule_mode=AndroidScheduleMode.EXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE` or `INEXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE`; `cancel_all_pending()` and `get_pending()` as proposed (never `cancel_all()`, D-222); `can_schedule_exact_alarms()` and `request_exact_alarm_permission()`. Failures are `NotificationException`s; an exact schedule without the access raises `NotificationPermissionException` (S6's `exact_alarms_not_permitted`).<br>• **Where:** only `services/reminders.py` imports the extension (D-63). `start(permissions)` builds the session's `Scheduler` in `Services.create()` when `capabilities.REMINDERS`, else `None`, so the companion app keeps the interim (D-60). `Scheduler.apply(plan, *, exact)` calls `cancel_all_pending()`, then `schedule()` for each reminder, its route as the payload (for P2-90's tap).<br>• **When:** `reminder_schedule.use_reminder_schedule()`, called once by `App`, has one effect keyed on `prefs.loaded`: once the start has read the settings, the favourites and the feed, it asks for an apply and runs `Scheduler.keep_applied()`. A change of the feed's `version`, the favourites' `ids`, `Prefs.reminders` or the change log's `batches` asks again, and so does `on_resume()`; other changes (a refresh starting, the theme) ask nothing. A request during an apply stops it before its next call and the loop applies a fresh plan: the newest wins, and two applies never interleave. `Services.close()` asks too, so a replaced session's loop wakes and returns (D-147).<br>• **Exact, re-read before each apply:** the plugin's `can_schedule_exact_alarms()` decides, since it is what schedules; on Android, `flet_permission_handler`'s `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` status is read as well and a disagreement is a warning in the log (D-220's check). An exact schedule the OS refuses is logged and scheduled inexact, as is the rest of that plan.<br>• **Failures are logged, never raised** (D-120): a failed cancel still schedules, a failed reminder still schedules the rest, a failed exact check reads as inexact, and a planning error (reported by `report_errors`) cancels nothing.<br>• **Real time:** the plan is made at `clock.real_now()`, which skips the Developer fake clock: the OS fires at real instants, and a fake May clock in October would plan reminders no OS fires. Real-time checks use P2-23's shifted feed (`tools/shifted_feed.py`).<br>• `capabilities.REMINDERS_LIVE` stays `False` until #449, so screens keep the interim. With the default settings (no session lead, D-275) a packaged build schedules the conference moments only, and the OS shows nothing until notification permission is granted (#448). | Applying from one loop is the simplest way to make "cancel pending, reschedule" safe against itself: an apply cut short by a newer one leaves nothing the next one does not replace, since it starts with a cancel. The plugin's answer is the one that holds when scheduling, and the permission handler's is what Settings will show (D-221), so a disagreement is worth a log line, not a choice between them. The checks ran on my own Android 16 emulator (`pycon_pixel6a_api36`, release arm64 APK) and iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5), with the keynote moment 8050 timed by the shifted feed. On Android, a fresh install applied inexact (`window=+3m21s`), and its reminder posted 29 s into that window with the app swiped from Recents and no process left. After `appops ... SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM allow`, a cold start and a resume each applied exact (`window=0 exactAllowReason=permission`), and both readings agreed in each state. A served move took 8050 from 03:53 to 04:03 under the same id. After `adb reboot`, the boot receiver restored it without the app being opened, and it fired once, at 04:03:00.03, with nothing at 03:53. On iOS, `Deliver local notification` came at the second, with the app terminated (#679's comments). iOS needs authorization first, which #448 asks for: that build carried a temporary `request_permissions()` call that was never committed. |
| D-395 | **The notification primer: a star's first saved add shows it while the OS can still ask, in any build with E3; Android's status comes from `flet-permission-handler`, iOS's from E3** (amends D-61's status check and ux.md §4.8; settles which capability gates the primer; records F245; NOTIF-06, NOTIF-07; #448, P2-88).<br>• **Trigger:** the star handler (`ui/favorite.py`'s `use_favorite_toggle()`), after an add that was saved, never a removal, an Undo or "Add all", while `ReminderSettings.primer` is `unasked` (D-275). It calls `ui/reminder_primer.py`'s `ask()`, the one flow, which the first reminder switch in Settings reuses (#449). Two quick stars show one sheet.<br>• **Status** (`Scheduler.access()`, in `services/reminders.py`, the one module that imports E3, D-63): on Android, the handler's `NOTIFICATION` status mapped as §4.8 says (`GRANTED`, `LIMITED`, `PROVISIONAL`: allowed; `DENIED`: askable; `PERMANENTLY_DENIED`, `RESTRICTED`: blocked; `None`: unknown). On iOS the handler always says `DENIED` (F245), so E3's `are_notifications_enabled()` answers: allowed, else askable. E3 has no "not asked yet" status at the pin, and none is needed: the primer's own record says whether the app asked, and a request after an iOS refusal shows nothing and returns False. A failed read is unknown and logged.<br>• **When it shows:** only while askable. Allowed already (Android 12 and older, or a permission given in system settings): there is nothing to ask, so no primer and nothing changes; session reminders stay off until Settings (#449) turns them on. Blocked or unknown: no primer either; Settings' card offers "Open settings" (#449).<br>• **The answers:** Allow closes the sheet, then asks through E3's `request_permissions(badge=False)` (D-61, F17); when the OS allows, `session_lead` becomes 10 (D-275), which the scheduler applies as any change of `Prefs.reminders` does (D-389). `primer` is `accepted` whatever the OS answers. Not now, a tap outside the sheet, a drag down or Back save `not_now` and ask nothing. Either way the favourite stays saved and a star never shows the primer again. A failed write keeps the answer for the session and says "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). The sheet is a modal route, so the focus returns to the star (D-225), except on Now, where the add moves the session into Your next session and the card's star replaces the one tapped, an opener that does not stay (amends D-225's "every opener today stays"): `ui/focus_back.py`'s `star_of()` notes the star before the add, and once the answer is in (after the OS's prompt too), `refocus_star()` waits `SETTLE_SECONDS` and calls `focus()` on the session's star mounted on the top View, only when the one tapped has left the page, so it never races Flutter's own refocus. On the Android 16 emulator with TalkBack (#697), Back and Allow then the OS's Allow each left TalkBack's focus on `now.next.favorite.123`; with the card scrolled out of view, its star has no node and the focus stays on the title, as before.<br>• **Layout:** the sheet may take the screen's height (`scrollable=True`, not 9/16 of it), and its column scrolls inside a `Row(intrinsic_height=True)`, so it is as tall as its content until that outgrows the screen (200 % text in landscape), then scrolls to both buttons; a bare scrolling `Column` would take all the height it is given (Flet's `scrollable_control.dart`, #6450).<br>• **Gate:** `Services.reminders`, which exists when `capabilities.REMINDERS` does, not `REMINDERS_LIVE`: what the primer asks for is the permission that the reminders #447 already schedules need, and it offers no control Settings would lack. Settings and Welcome keep the interim until #449 sets `REMINDERS_LIVE` (D-287). The companion app has no `Scheduler`, so no primer.<br>• **Copy** (ux.md §4.8): "Get a heads-up 10 minutes before your saved sessions start, and before keynotes and sign-ups?", with "Not now" and "Allow". One sentence names everything Allow turns on: the session lead and the conference moments, which default on (D-275). | The issue asked for the permission handler's status, or E3's on iOS if F17 blocks it, and F17 does block it (F245). A primer exists to explain a prompt, so it shows only when a prompt can follow: an OS that already said yes, or that won't ask again, gets nothing to tap through. That also keeps the Maestro flows, which launch with every permission granted, free of the sheet; the primer's own flow (`maestro/journeys/reminder_primer.yaml`) launches with notifications `unset`; `deny` gives iOS's refused state, where the request shows no prompt. Checked on my own Android 16 emulator (release APK) and iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5): the first star's primer, Allow and the OS's Allow (`session_lead` 10, `primer` `accepted` in the stored record; `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` granted), Allow and Don't allow, Not now (`not_now`, no prompt), no primer on a second star; with TalkBack, Back from the sheet gave the focus back to the Schedule's star (#697's comments). |

A payload that is not a path from `/` opens nothing and is logged.<br>• **Cold start:** `services.reminders.start()` passes `on_tap` to the `Notifications` constructor (F246). `bootstrap()` awaits `Scheduler.read_enabled(LAUNCH_WAIT)` (3 s at most) after the feed and before `prefs.loaded`: E3 answers a call only after its start has handed over the tap that launched the app, so the tap is held when the gate opens. `Start` then navigates to `locations.launch_target(tap, last_location, search_base, *, onboarded)`: the tap when there is one, else `start_target()`. On the Android 16 emulator the wait cost nothing measurable (E3 had answered within 5 ms of the call).<br>• **Removed sessions:** a session the feed has dropped opens its not-found state over My schedule (D-186); Back returns to My schedule. `tests/test_reminder_taps.py` checks that every route the 2026 plan can produce names a page the app has.<br>• **The Banner rule** (`ui.reminder_banner.os_reminds()`): the in-app Banner (D-60, #428) hides while the OS shows the saved sessions' reminders itself, that is when E3 schedules them (a `Scheduler`), the OS shows the app's notifications (`Scheduler.enabled`, `are_notifications_enabled()`, read at the start and before each apply, so a resume after a change in system settings sees it) and session reminders are on. Notifications denied, session reminders off, or no E3 (the companion app) keep it. A settings change asks again at once, and so does a read that changes `Scheduler.enabled` (`Scheduler.watch_enabled()`).<br>• **Module split (D-66):** the Banner's pure `due_session()`, `DueSession` and `BANNER_LEAD` move from `services/reminders.py` to `ui/reminder_banner.py`, beside the rule; `services/reminders.py` keeps the plan, the `Scheduler` and the extension import (D-63). | The launch tap must not show Now or Welcome first, and a tap is the user asking for one session: it outranks where they were. Waiting for E3 before the gate is the only point where the launch tap is known to be in, and costs nothing once E3 has started. Two reminders for one session (the OS's and the Banner) are noise; the Banner stays where the OS is silent. Reversible: one helper, one wait and one rule. |
| D-401 | **Settings › Reminders is live in every build with E3: switch rows that save silently, a card only while the OS needs the user (in Settings a card, elsewhere no prompt), a calendar alarm at the chosen lead, and Settings again under My schedule** (amends D-61 and ux.md §4.8, whose denial showed a `Banner`, architecture §14.3 and SESS-05's fixed 10-minute alarm; adds `/my-schedule/settings` to section M's routes; removes `capabilities.REMINDERS_LIVE`; NOTIF-01, STORE-10, NOTIF-10, NEW-01; #449, P2-89).<br>• **Gate:** `Services.reminders` (a `Scheduler`, `capabilities.REMINDERS`), as the primer's (D-395). `capabilities.REMINDERS_LIVE` is gone: since #447 schedules and #448 asks, nothing is left that a build with E3 lacks, so Settings' live section and Welcome's reminders row (D-287) follow `REMINDERS`. Without E3, the companion app included, D-60's interim stays (`interim_section()`).<br>• **Rows** (`screens/settings_reminders.py`'s `RemindersSection`; ux.md §6.9): "Remind me before sessions in My schedule" (`settings.reminders.sessions`), its 5 · 10 · 15 min `SegmentedButton` (`settings.reminders.lead.<minutes>`) only while on; a level-3 "Conference moments" over "Keynotes and panels", "Lightning-talk sign-ups" and "Open-space sign-ups" (`settings.reminders.keynote`, `.lightning`, `.open_space`). Each is a `ListTile(toggle_inputs=True)` whose change is one `Prefs.save(reminders=...)`, which the scheduler applies as one plan (D-389); nothing is said unless the write fails (D-120). Turning the switch on saves 10 minutes, then, while the primer is not `accepted` (never answered, or Not now), shows it through `reminder_primer.ask()` (D-61, D-275); the primer's Allow then keeps a lead already on, and turns 10 on only from off.<br>• **Cards** (`card_for()`), only while a reminder is on, under the heading: `askable` → "Reminders show only once you allow notifications." with "Allow notifications", which asks the OS directly (the card is the explanation; the primer is then `accepted`); `blocked` → "Notifications are off in system settings." with "Open settings" (`open_app_settings()`); on iOS, `askable` once the app has asked (`primer` `accepted`) is `blocked` too, since iOS shows its prompt once and says denied the same way before and after (F245); on Android, with notifications allowed, session reminders on and exact alarms refused, D-221's "For more timely reminders…" with "Open settings", which opens Alarms & reminders (`Scheduler.request_exact()`), then re-reads and applies again. iOS never shows it. `unknown` shows nothing (§4.8). On Android the handler says `permanentlyDenied` only once it has read a `denied` that the OS would explain (after a first refusal) and then a second refusal: two refusals in the app, the primer's or the card's, reach the blocked card, but a refusal fixed from outside the app (`pm set-permission-flags … user-fixed`) still reads `denied`, so the card offers Allow notifications and its tap shows nothing. Elsewhere in the app a denial shows nothing: Settings is the one place (amends D-61's `Banner`).<br>• **Re-checked:** the status is read when the section mounts, after a change of `Prefs.reminders`, after a card's button and whenever the `Scheduler` reads a change, which it does before every apply, so at every resume: `Scheduler.exact` now keeps `exact_allowed()`'s answer, and `Scheduler.watch()` (renamed from `watch_enabled()`) calls back when `enabled` or `exact` changes.<br>• **Calendar:** `ReminderSettings.calendar_alarm` is the session lead while on, else `core.ics.ALARM` (10 min); session detail's Add to calendar and My schedule's export pass it (SESS-05).<br>• **My schedule:** its overflow adds "Reminder settings" (`my_schedule.reminder_settings`, `NOTIFICATIONS_OUTLINED`) after Export in a build with E3, in every state of the screen, since the moments remind with nothing saved; Export still shows only with something to export (D-289). It pushes `/my-schedule/settings`, Settings again under My schedule (D-207's pattern), so Back returns there; `locations.PAGES` has it. | The issue's scope, settled here (D-101). One place for the permission's state is easier to find than a Banner on whatever screen is open, and the switches it serves are beside it. iOS's single prompt makes "askable" after an ask a dead end; the primer's record is the one thing that tells them apart. Saving the switch before the primer keeps what the user turned on even when they say Not now, and the card under it says what is missing. A calendar alarm that disagrees with the app's own reminder would be two heads-ups at different times. At 200 % text on Android, nothing clipped. |

Without E3 (the companion app, D-63) they are disabled with "Not available in this build".<br>• **Order of the checks, and the outcome line** (`developer.reminder_outcome`, a live region): **denied** first, `Scheduler.access()` not `allowed` (askable, blocked and unknown each say so), since Settings can't fix it; then **muted**, that kind off in `Prefs.reminders` (a session lead of 0); then **scheduled**: `Scheduler.schedule_test()` in `services/reminders.py`, the one module that imports E3, exact when Android allows it, else inexact with a warning that it may come late, or refused (logged). Muted and denied schedule nothing, so the line explains a reminder that would not have come.<br>• **Time:** `datetime.now(UTC)` + 5 s, never the fake clock's: the OS fires by its own clock. It is taken after the exact-alarm check (`schedule_test()` calls back for the reminder), so a slow check can't leave it in the past, which E3 refuses. It is outside the apply loop, so a re-plan's cancel may remove it while pending, as DEV-06 accepts. | Testers see why a reminder did or didn't fire, not only that one was scheduled, as Ionic's did; the real reminders' own checks, in the same order, give the answer. |
| D-275 | **The reminder settings are a frozen `ReminderSettings` record merged over its defaults: session reminders off until the primer is accepted, the three kinds of conference moment on, and the primer unasked** (records STORE-10 and NOTIF-03; amends D-160's `reminders` dict and section M's top-level modules; #382).<br>• **Fields and defaults:** `session_lead` 0 (off), 5, 10 or 15 minutes, default 0; `lightning`, `open_space` and `keynote` (the lightning-talk sign-ups, the open-space sign-ups, the keynotes and panels; named as the year data's moment categories, #381), each a bool, default `True`; `primer`, the notification primer's answer (D-61): `unasked`, `accepted` or `not_now`, default `unasked`. Nothing else: no push categories, no master toggle (D-58).<br>• **Why those defaults:** no reminder fires before notification permission, which is asked after the primer (D-61), so the moments can default on: the primer names them (#448), and they are a handful a year. A session reminder is per saved session, so it stays off until the primer is accepted; the primer's "Allow" then turns on the lead its sentence names (10 min, ux.md §4.8). "Not now" leaves it off, and the first reminder switch in Settings asks again (D-61).<br>• **Where:** `reminder_settings.py`, a top-level module (`prefs.py` would pass D-66's ~400 lines): `ReminderSettings`, `SessionLead`, `PrimerAnswer`, `SESSION_LEADS` and `PRIMER_ANSWERS`. `Prefs.reminders` is a `ReminderSettings`; a change is `dataclasses.replace()` passed to `Prefs.save(reminders=...)`.<br>• **Checked both ways:** building a record with a value outside its field's set raises `ValueError` (a bool is no lead, 1 is no bool), as `save()` does for an unknown Appearance (D-160), so a bad record is never saved. Reading `pycon.reminders` (`ReminderSettings.from_stored()`, in `Prefs.load`) merges it over the defaults: a missing key, or one whose value is not one of its field's values in type and value, reads as its default, and an unknown key is dropped; both are logged in one line that names the keys, never the values (D-120). A stored value that is not a JSON object reads as the defaults (`Storage.get`).<br>• **Stored:** the whole record, every field by name, as one JSON object (`to_stored()`), so a field added later reads as its default from an older record. A failed write returns `False` and the record holds for the session (D-120). Reset app returns the defaults (D-198); sign-out (P3) does not clear it. | A typed, frozen record lets pyright check every reader and writer and keeps a stored value the app can't use from ever reaching the plan, while one bad key costs only that key, never a start. The defaults follow the issue's scope and D-61: nothing reminds anyone before they said yes to notifications. |
