| D-272 | **Add to calendar never goes through the share sheet: on iOS the app serves the file on the loopback and opens it in the in-app browser, whose "Add All" sheet adds the events with their alarm; on Android, until U71, it saves the file and says how to open it, and promises no alarm** (settles #364, P2-04; amends D-62's share call, D-60's `.ics` bullet and section M's `services/` list; SESS-04, SESS-05; F91, U71).<br>• **Why not the share sheet:** no calendar takes a shared `.ics`. Android 14's chooser for `text/calendar` lists Nearby Share, Drive, Messages, Bluetooth and Gmail: Google Calendar imports through `ACTION_VIEW` only. iOS 26.5's sheet offers Copy and Save to Files, its app list is empty, and Files' Quick Look shows an `.ics` with no add action.<br>• **iOS:** a stdlib `ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `127.0.0.1` on port 0, started per tap, serves only `/<slug>.ics` as `text/calendar; charset=utf-8`; the app opens the URL with `UrlLauncher.launch_url(url, mode=LaunchMode.IN_APP_BROWSER_VIEW)`. iOS shows "N Events" with Add All; the events keep their UTC time, `VALARM` and `UID` (opening a file again adds no duplicate). The call raises `RuntimeError` although the sheet showed (F91), so the server's GET is the success: the handler sets a `threading.Event` on a 200 GET (its thread writes no store, D-70), and the app reads it only after `launch_url` returns or raises, which url_launcher_ios does only after SFSafariViewController's `didCompleteInitialLoad`, so after the GET; set is success, unset says "Couldn't open the calendar file". The server answers every request until then, 60 s at most, then shuts down: `shutdown()` and `server_close()` run off the event loop (`await asyncio.to_thread(...)`), since `shutdown()` waits up to `serve_forever`'s 0.5 s poll. Never `EXTERNAL_APPLICATION`, which opens Calendar's "Add Subscription Calendar" for a URL that stops answering. No calendar permission, usage string or ATS exception is needed. Measured on the iOS 26.5 simulator (a debug build, whose server stayed up for the session); the per-tap server and the requests it gets are checked on the simulator in #425, and the release build, the alert firing, airplane mode and VoiceOver on the iPhone (#121); if the loopback route fails there, this reopens with the spike's Fallback (Save to Files with Android's SnackBar, or E4).<br>• **Android:** `FilePicker.save_file(file_name=f"{slug}.ics", src_bytes=...)` opens the system save dialog: the user picks the folder (the spike's image opened Downloads; another phone or a later save may start elsewhere), and the call returns an opaque document path (`/document/12`), so the app cannot name the folder. `initial_directory` is not passed: it is only a starting hint the user can leave. `None` is a cancel and says nothing; a save says "Saved {slug}.ics. Open it from the Files app to add it to your calendar." (the name the app offered). Opened from Files, the file reaches Google Calendar's import (Add all) with the right times, when the device has a calendar account to add to (with none, Calendar opens Google's sign-in; spike); Google Calendar 2023.18.3 drops the `VALARM`, so no Android copy promises an alarm. U71 (#487: open a local file in the app that handles its type) replaces the save with one tap to the import screen; E4 stays the native route.<br>• **Shared:** `services/calendar.py`'s `async add_to_calendar(svc, slug, data)` takes `core/ics.py`'s bytes for a session (#425) or My schedule (#426) and owns both routes: the iOS loopback server and launch, the Android save. It reads the platform from `page.platform`, not `capabilities` (no extension is involved, D-63), and uses `svc.launcher` and a new `svc.file_picker` (`ft.FilePicker()`, architecture §4.2), as `ui/links.py` uses `svc`; #425 adds a recording `FilePicker` fake to `tests/fakes.py` (like `MemoryShare`) and a seam for the server, and settles what the function returns. Failures are caught there (D-71), said in place (D-210) and logged by class (D-72). The item, its place and its name are the same on both platforms (D-10, D-11). Only the words that report what each OS can do differ: Android's save SnackBar, and the Reminders interim, whose Android copy promises no alarm (ux.md §6.9). As with D-182's copy confirmation, which Android 13 and later make themselves, the visuals stay identical. | The share sheet would be two dead controls (D-60). The loopback route reaches iOS's own calendar sheet from Python with the standard library and what the pin has; on Android nothing at the pin opens Calendar, and a saved file is the honest step it can take ([spike](spikes/10-ics-share.md)). |
| D-274 | **Conference moments: the sign-ups are hand-written year data with their public source, the keynotes' and panels' are derived from the feed 15 min before each starts, and every moment opens its own page** (records NOTIF-05's data; amends D-58's source of the moments, D-59's id range, D-217's every-year session test, architecture §8.1, §8.2 and §14.1's `/now`, and section M's year modules; #381, P2-21).<br>• **Hand-written** (`content.toml`'s `[[event_reminders]]`, built as `Content.event_reminders` of `year_moments.py`'s `EventReminder`): `id` 8000-8049, `category` `lightning` or `open_space`, an aware `at`, `title`, `body`, `route`, `source` (`https://`) and `checked`. `check_moments()`, which `config.py` runs on every load, refuses a duplicate or out-of-range id, a naive time, a time outside the conference year in its zone, an unknown category and a hand `keynote` one, blank text, a route not from `/`, and a missing source or check date, naming the file and the entry (`2026/content.toml: event_reminders[0].id: 8050 is not in 8000-8049`). 2026 and 2025 have the same five: open-space sign-ups at 5 PM the day before each day of talks (Thursday to Saturday, "Open space sign-ups for Friday"), opening `/more/open-spaces`, and lightning-talk sign-ups at 10 AM on Friday and Saturday (the second "Last lightning talk sign-ups"), opening `/more/lightning-talks`. The public pages (each year's `/events/open-spaces/` and `/events/lightning-talks/`, checked 2026-10-20) say each day's open spaces take sign-ups from the day before, from Thursday, and speakers are picked at lunchtime for that evening or the next morning, but give no hours: `at` is our choice and no text names a clock time. Ionic's times came from the PSF's own data and put Saturday's and Sunday's open-space sign-ups on the day itself, against the public page; they are not used.<br>• **Derived** (`keynote_moments(data, keynotes, zone, *, use_24h)`): one per session the Keynotes page lists (`timeline.keynote_sessions()`: the `Keynote` track and the `[[keynotes]]` entries), in the order they happen, ids from 8050 (a 51st session would get none), `at` the feed's start minus 15 min, the title "Keynote at 9:45 AM" or, for a panel entry, "Panel at 9:00 AM" in the user's clock format, the body "Lin Qiao · Pacific Ballroom - Arena" (the feed's name and room), and the route `/schedule/session/<id>`, whose Back goes to the Schedule and then Now. The plan (P2-67) derives them from the feed it has, so a keynote the schedule moves takes its moment along, and no keynote time or copy is in the year data. 8999 stays the Developer test reminder's (DEV-06).<br>• **2025** gets its six keynote and panel entries (the people's names and roles only, from the site's 2025 keynote-speakers page): its feed files every keynote as a plenary, so the entries are what make them keynotes, for the Keynotes page and the moments, which are then tested in a second year and zone. 2025 bundles no snapshot, so the every-year session test checks its entries against its recorded feed (`tests/fixtures/feeds/`); `load_year(check_sessions=True)` still refuses keynotes without a snapshot. | Ionic hard-coded eleven moments, with keynote times and copy that drift when the schedule moves, and every tap opened Now. A public source and a check date let each year's hand moments be re-checked, as safety contacts are (D-45). For a keynote, session detail answers "where and when"; under the Schedule, Back shows what is on around it. |
| D-289 | **My schedule exports every saved session still in the schedule, ended ones too, as one `pycon-us-<year>-my-schedule.ics`, from an overflow shown only when there is something to export** (settles #426, P2-66; records ux.md §5.6's overflow for SESS-05; amends section M's `screens/` list).<br>• **What the file holds:** `Agenda.sessions`, the saved sessions the feed lists, day by day and slot by slot, each with its alarm (`core/ics.py`, D-268), not only what is still ahead: the export is the whole agenda, as the issue scopes it, and a second export adds no duplicate, since each event keeps its `UID` (iOS offers no Add All for events it has; importing the spike's file three times into Google Calendar left one copy of each event, P2-04). Saved ids the feed dropped are left out (D-172).<br>• **Its name:** `pycon-us-<year>-my-schedule.ics` (`export_slug()`), from the year, not `ics.file_slug()` of a title: it names what it holds, and two years' exports in the Files app stay apart.<br>• **Where it is:** `screens/my_schedule_extras.py`'s `ScheduleMenu`, `overflow_menu("my_schedule.overflow", ...)` around one `menu_item(EVENT_AVAILABLE_OUTLINED, "Export to calendar (.ics)", identifier="my_schedule.export")` (D-233; session detail's Add to calendar's icon), only in the branch that shows the agenda: nothing saved, only dropped ids, an unread list and no data have no overflow, so no item waits with nothing to do (D-60). Reminder settings (P2-89) joins the same menu later.<br>• **How it goes:** `services/calendar.py`'s `add_to_calendar(svc, export_slug(year), data)`, session detail's route (D-272): iOS's "N Events · Add All" sheet over the loopback, Android's save dialog and its SnackBar; a cancel says nothing, a failure "Couldn't open the calendar file" on My schedule (D-210). The item is disabled in place while the sheet or the dialog is up (as session detail's, so the button keeps its node and Flutter gives the focus back to it when the menu closes, D-225). Nothing is shared, so nothing needs an anchor (D-09, D-272).<br>• **The module:** `my_schedule.py` was 407 lines (D-66); the overflow and the all-over notice (`all_over()`, D-191) moved to `my_schedule_extras.py`, as session detail's extras live in `session_extras.py` (D-206).<br>• **Seen** (#426; the real clock, after the conference, so both saved tutorials were folded under "Earlier days" and still exported): on the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, its bundle matching `build/python-app`) two tutorials starred on the Schedule by the free Maestro CLI, the overflow's one item, and a tap brought up iOS's "2 Events · Add All" sheet; Add All put both in the simulator's calendar at 16:00Z with a −600 s alarm and the `UID`s `2026-2@…` and `2026-19@…`, and after the browser view closed the overflow was back. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK matching `build/python-app`) the same flow found `my_schedule.export` (a `MenuItem` named "Export to calendar (.ics)"), the save dialog offered `pycon-us-2026-my-schedule.ics` in Downloads, SAVE wrote a 994-byte file with both events and their `TRIGGER:-PT10M`, and "Saved pycon-us-2026-my-schedule.ics…" showed. Light and dark on both. | One file a person can recognise, with everything they saved, through the one calendar route both screens share; an overflow that appears only with something in it never offers a dead export. |
| D-293 | **E1 goes ahead: a `BarcodeScanner` layout control over `mobile_scanner` 7.4.2 whose Dart side owns the controller, the app lifecycle and its own blank builders** (spike S8, #362; [verdict](spikes/08-scanner.md); upstream write-up #520; completes D-65 and `docs/flet-gaps-and-extensions.md` §4.2).<br>• **API:** `formats` (default `[QR_CODE]`), `paused` (True stops the camera and releases it), `torch` (ignored where the camera has none), `on_detect` (`raw_value`, `format`; every detection, the app dedupes), `on_error` (`code`, the Dart `MobileScannerErrorCode` name, and `message`), `analyze_image(path) -> list[str]` (raises on the iOS simulator).<br>• **Dart duties:** a `MobileScannerController` passed in (`autoStart: !paused`, `torchEnabled: torch`, `DetectionSpeed.normal`), so the extension stops the camera on `inactive` and starts it on `resumed` unless paused or not permitted; `start()` and `stop()` serialised, with the wanted state applied when the call in flight ends, and their `MobileScannerException` reported through `on_error`, never left unhandled (S8 saw `controllerInitializing`); `placeholderBuilder` and `errorBuilder` return `SizedBox.expand` of a plain `ColoredBox`, so no SDK Material widget is built and the area keeps its size; only `widgets.dart` is imported; no `tapToFocus`.<br>• **App side:** the camera permission is primed and requested with `flet-permission-handler` before the preview mounts (D-61), since the plugin asks on its own at start; after a grant in Settings the scan screen re-checks on resume and sets `paused=False`, because nothing restarts by itself; "Open settings" follows `PermissionHandler`'s `PERMANENTLY_DENIED`, which `on_error` cannot tell apart. D-65's 4 s per-code cooldown stays: the plugin reports a held code about 3.6 times a second. Android keeps the bundled ML Kit model (+7.3 MB on the arm64 APK). | S8's release APK decoded the badge from the emulator's virtual scene camera on every cold start, turn, resume and after 50 pause/resume cycles, released the camera in the background and passed the `material_ui` gate on Android and on the iOS simulator; the three Dart duties are the failures it hit or would have hit without them. |

## H. Code style and project layout

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-66 | The Zen of Python is the code rule: one obvious way, explicit over implicit, flat over nested, and no speculative abstraction (no protocol with a single implementation, no registries, no code generation). Split a module at around 400 lines, not before. | Owner's direction. |
| D-67 | *(Store modules amended by D-160.)* Layout (naming rules in section M): `src/main.py`; `src/pycon_us/` with `app.py`, `routes.py`, `state.py`, a module per further store (`prefs.py`, D-160), `config.py`, `theme.py`, `capabilities.py`, `clock.py`, then `core/` (no Flet), `services/`, `ui/` (shared components, grouped by role into a few modules), `screens/` (one module per screen, flat) and `data/<year>/`; `src/assets/`; `tools/`; `tests/`. About 40 modules at the P1 exit, most of them screens. | Flat and readable. Most of v1's ~100 modules were seams for features we cut. |
| D-68 | `core/` imports nothing from Flet (a unit test scans its imports). It holds all business rules as pure functions over frozen dataclasses. | Testable in milliseconds without Flutter. |
| D-69 | *(The boot gate also waits for the slot layout's measurement: D-216.)* Declarative Flet: `page.render_views(App)`, `ft.Router(ROUTES, manage_views=True)`, `@ft.component` screens and `@ft.observable` stores. The stores are `Conference`, `Prefs` and `Favorites` until P3, which adds `Account` and `Staff`. Each store has its own context, with the providers written out in `App`. Services are built once with `ft.use_memo(lambda: Services.create(memory), [])` (an explicit empty dependency list; without one, `use_memo` recomputes on every render). `App` shows a boot view until `Prefs.loaded`. | Follows the verified subscription rules (F3); explicit and flat; no theme flash. |
| D-70 | *(Rule 3 amended by D-147 and D-387; rule 9 added by D-157 and amended by D-243.)* Flet rules, enforced in review:<br>1. Await coroutine APIs only in `async def` handlers or effects.<br>2. A `use_effect` setup is an `async def`, never a lambda that returns a coroutine.<br>3. Long-running work in an effect runs in its `async def` setup, which Flet cancels when the effect re-runs or its component unmounts, with or without a cleanup (F2). A cleanup undoes only what the setup started outside its task (a listener, a timer, a memory entry); a sync setup may return it or pass `cleanup=`, and it may be `async` (D-387). Long-running work also stops when its session is replaced (D-147).<br>4. Hooks are unconditional.<br>5. One context per observable.<br>6. Large values are `@dataclass(frozen=True, eq=False)` without object cycles.<br>7. No store writes from other threads.<br>8. Never `adaptive=True`.<br>9. A text field in a component is controlled: `value=` from the state its `on_change` sets (D-157), or, for a field that must keep up with fast typing, from what Flutter last reported (D-243). | Verified SDK behaviour (F1-F5, F33). |
| D-71 | Errors:<br>• Expected failures (network, parsing, storage, permissions, sharing or launching) are handled at explicit operation boundaries and become visible state.<br>• Unexpected exceptions in handlers and effects are caught by one small `@report_errors` decorator. It logs the traceback, shows "Something went wrong" (with the exception text in developer mode) and re-raises `CancelledError`. It returns an `async def` wrapper for coroutine functions and a plain wrapper otherwise, both with `functools.wraps`, so Flet's `iscoroutinefunction` and parameter-count checks still see the original; a unit test covers both cases.<br>• The decorator is deleted once Flet makes handler errors non-fatal (U15). | Otherwise a handler exception crashes the session (F4), and errors should never pass silently. |
| D-72 | Logging uses stdlib `logging`. Packaged apps capture it in `console.log` (F14), which can be shared from the Developer screen. Never log secrets, emails or badge codes. No Sentry and no analytics. | We use no crash-reporting service at first. |
| D-73 | One build, no flavours. A hidden Developer screen offers: fake clock, feed URL, mock API base URL, capability flags (read-only display, from `capabilities.py`), clear cached feed, show welcome again, test reminder, share log and reset app (confirmed). It is always available when not embedded. In packaged builds it unlocks after 7 taps on the version row in About this app; a minimal About this app ships with it in P1. | Environment variables don't reach packaged apps, and demos and off-season testing need a fake clock. |
| D-74 | *(Tests amended by D-253.)* Code is documented: a docstring on every module and on every public function and class (Google style), comments that say why, and type hints throughout. `ruff` (lint and format) and `pyright` (standard mode) must be clean. | Owner's direction: "sufficiently documented". |
| D-253 | **Tests are documented like the code: every test function says in its docstring the behaviour it pins, and every fixture, test helper, test class and method has a docstring too; `ruff`'s `D` rules apply to `tests/` with no exemption** (settles #350; amends D-74).<br>• **What a test's docstring says:** one sentence in the repo's Google style stating what the test checks, concretely (`test_open_spaces.py`: "A star tap re-renders one day body and one row (D-153)."), not a restatement of its name; a comment that explained the test moves into the docstring. Fixtures and helpers say what they provide or do. Module-level private helpers (`_x`) get one too, though `ruff` skips them; nested functions inside a test don't.<br>• **How it got there:** `pyproject.toml` exempted `tests/**` from `D`, and 912 of 1,489 tests had none; eight PRs (#351 to #358, over disjoint files) added 1,214 docstrings and reworded about 118, and changed no test logic: each file's syntax tree with docstrings removed is the same as before, but for one attribute docstring under a constant in `tests/row_sessions.py`, shortened to fit the line. Each PR was reviewed against its test bodies. This PR lifts the exemption, so CI enforces it. | The owner's rule (2026-10-19): a test should say in a sentence what it pins, as Flet's own tests do (`sdk/python/packages/flet/tests/test_version.py`). A lint keeps it from drifting again, which it did while tests were exempt. |
| D-75 | *(Every Flet package is pinned to one version from PyPI, D-481.)* *(`httpx` is declared and pinned too, D-470.)* *(The QR library is `segno`, D-391.)* Dependencies stay few and mobile-safe: `flet`, `tzdata`, `flet-permission-handler` (P2), `flet-secure-storage` (P3) and pinned extensions as they land. Dev only: the Flet host tooling from the D-81 pin (`flet-cli`, `flet-web` and `flet-desktop`, plus `flet-platform-assets` while the source pin lasts, so `flet run` never pip-installs a PyPI copy), `flet[test]` (for `flet test`; it brings `pytest-asyncio`), `pytest`, `hypothesis`, `ruff`, `pyright`, in P3 one QR library for the mock-badge sheet (e.g. `segno`), and the two calendar parsers of D-268's check (`icalendar`, `ical`; #823). No bs4, markdownify, pydantic or jsonschema in the app. | Mobile wheels, app size and licence hygiene. |
| D-470 | *(`.flet-ref` is gone and the Flet family is pinned exactly too, D-481.)* **`httpx` is declared and pinned exactly in `[project].dependencies`: `httpx==0.28.1`** (#914; notes D-75, D-53, F19; PR #888 taught why).<br>• **Why:** `feed_refresh.py`, `state.py`, `services/api.py` and `services/private_api.py` import it, but it came only through Flet's `httpx >=0.28.1`, and `flet build` never reads `uv.lock` (`docs/development.md` §2): a phone could ship a newer httpx than the tests ran.<br>• **The rule:** a third-party package imported under `src/` is listed there, and pinned exactly unless `.flet-ref` pins it (`[tool.flet.dev_packages]`). `tests/test_bundled_dependencies.py` checks both. Only what the app itself imports: what `httpx` pulls in (`certifi`, `anyio`, `httpcore`...) stays pip's choice, audited on builds (§7.6), so `certifi`'s root store keeps reaching phones without a bump (#888).<br>• **The cost:** one line in each Flet pin bump (§2.2, step 2). The pin stays while it is inside Flet's range; a range that leaves it out stops `uv lock` with "No solution found" (seen on a dry run with `httpx==0.27.0`), and then the pin moves in that bump. It also moves for an advisory (§7.6).<br>• **Checked on 2026-11-02 at `22a6dcb16`:** the release APK (`arm64-v8a`) and the unsigned iOS release archive (`iphoneos.arm64`) hold `httpx-0.28.1.dist-info`, among the same 16 packages. One refresh of the live feed worked on the release APK on an API 36 emulator, and on an iPhone 17 simulator with a simulator build, which runs in Flutter's debug mode. | Phones run the version the tests ran, without pinning everything the app never touches. |
| D-147 | **Page handlers re-render without `page.update()`, and a replaced session stops its own work** (amends D-70 rule 3; settles architecture §4.3; S4, F29, F30).<br>• `bootstrap()` assigns `page.on_platform_brightness_change` and `page.on_app_lifecycle_state_change` after the first render; they write stores only. The app relies on these sequences: going to the background gives `inactive`, `hide`, `pause`; returning gives `restart`, `show`, `resume`; Back on Now on Android adds `detach` and ends the session (F29). Dark mode, font scale and rotation never start a new session on Android (the template's `configChanges`).<br>• `bootstrap()` also sets `page.on_close` to `svc.close()`, which sets one `asyncio.Event` on `Services`; every long-running loop (the 60 s ticks of D-40, the P3 sync worker, a refresh in flight before it writes) checks it and returns. Only `page.on_close` sets it: an effect's own cleanup still stops just that effect (its own Event or task, rule 3), since a screen unmounts on every tab switch. Until U24 (D-149). | Seen on the Android 14 emulator (Home, relaunch, `cmd uimode night`) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (another app launched, relaunch, `simctl ui appearance`): every event reached the handler, and a component subscribed to the store showed it at once. Flet closes a replaced session (`page.on_close` fires) but does not unmount its components: in S4 an old session's ticking effect ran next to the new one's and died only at its next store write, with `RuntimeError: An attempt to fetch destroyed session.` that only asyncio saw (F4); with the stop event it ended cleanly. |
| D-387 | **Effects need no cleanup to stop, and a cleanup may be `async`** (amends D-70 rule 3, D-175's cleanup bullet and D-200's `stop_ticks()`; F2, F39, U27; #672).<br>• **What Flet does at the pin `08b89cce5`** (#673): an `async def` setup still running is cancelled when its effect re-runs or its component unmounts, whether or not the effect has a cleanup; a cleanup a sync setup returned survives the component's re-renders; cleanups run in order, the returned one first, and an `async` one finishes before the next setup runs (`flet-dev/flet#6983`, `flet-dev/flet#7005`; F2, F39).<br>• **The no-op cleanups go:** `stop_ticks()` (the status strip, `use_minute()`, the status line, the rows' and the reminder banner's ticks), the screens' `_cancel`, `_cancel_on_unmount`, `_stop_waiting` and `_nothing_to_undo`, and the location card's `lambda: None` existed only to make Flet cancel a task (D-175). The cleanups that undo something (forget a pin, close a listener) stay as they are: rewriting them to be returned is style, not a fix.<br>• **Pinned:** `tests/test_flet_effects.py` renders small components in a real Flet session and checks the three facts, so a pin bump (D-81) that changes one fails there first; `tests/test_session.py` and `tests/test_schedule_jump.py` see a screen's ticks and waiting jump cancelled with no cleanup. | Flet now does what the no-op cleanups were for, and their docstrings would describe a Flet that no longer exists. Sync-only cleanups guarded against an `async` cleanup cancelled before it started, which can no longer happen, so the rule only cost choices. Reversible: a cleanup is one argument to put back. |
| D-157 | *(Which text the field is built from amended by D-243.)* **Text fields in components are controlled** (adds rule 9 to D-70; S2, F33). A `TextField` (and any input whose value the user changes) that a component renders gets `value=` from the state its `on_change` handler sets. | Without it, the component's next re-render creates the field with its default value, the diff sends that to Flutter and the typed text disappears (F33). |
| D-243 | **A filter field is built from what Flutter last reported, and only a Clear sends it text; the count stays a live region on iOS** (settles #307 and #250; amends D-157 and D-70 rule 9 for these fields, and D-226's hook; F63, U43, #319).<br>• **The race (#307):** Flutter's reports of the typed text update the field the last render built as soon as they arrive, while change events and re-renders run later (F63). So on a busy host, a re-render for one keystroke could run after the next keystroke's report. It sent the older text back, and Flutter put it in the field. Once the state caught up with the last report, the diff had nothing to send, so the field kept the older text under a list filtered by the newer one.<br>• **The hook:** `use_field_query()` keeps one `Ref` for the screen's life, not a fresh one per render. At each render, the field that `Ref` still holds is the one Flutter's reports updated. `FieldQuery.value` is that field's text, so the diff sends nothing at a keystroke, and the screen filters by the same text it shows: the two cannot part. Only the initial text (first render) and a Clear are sent. `clear()` empties the state and bumps a Clear count, so each Clear re-renders and is sent exactly once, even when the state is already empty (a Clear tapped before the last key's event ran). `changed` still sets state, which re-renders the screen, and returns whether it took the report. Speakers and Sprints are unchanged; search writes `ViewMemory.search_query` only for a report `changed` took, so a View rebuilt later never brings back text a Clear dropped (D-193).<br>• **A Clear races too:** Flutter puts the Clear's empty text over any key typed between the tap and the moment the empty text arrives. Before this change, the field was left empty over a list still filtered by those keys (the owner's "mmumu" on Speakers). Now `clear()` starts a landing: from the tap on, `changed` drops reports. Once the render has sent the empty text, an effect on the Clear count asks the field for the focus. Flet runs effects after the render's patch, and Flutter handles messages in order (`flet_backend.dart`, `textfield.dart:113-121`), so Flutter answers only after it has the empty text, and every report before the answer was overwritten. If one came in, the empty text is sent again (the field here still holds that report's text) and waited for the same way. The same focus request is D-182's refocus, which `clear()` used to make directly.<br>• **What stays open (U43):** a key typed in the frame between Flutter's answer and the build that applies the empty text. Only a Flet change can close that frame (#319: Flutter would drop an echo of an older edit).<br>• **#250, the count's live region on iOS:** ruled out from the pin's Flutter source, not on a device. At Flutter 3.47.7, a live region whose label changes posts a queued announcement of its label (`UIAccessibilityAnnouncementNotification` with `UIAccessibilitySpeechAttributeQueueAnnouncement`; `accessibility_bridge.mm:139-151`, `SemanticsObject.mm:357-370`). VoiceOver's focus is not moved. When nodes come or go, the bridge refocuses the node that had the focus (`FindNextFocusableIfNecessary`, `accessibility_bridge.mm:218-226`, `:331-340`). Only a `focus` semantics event posts `UIAccessibilityLayoutChangedNotification` with a node (`:366-369`), and that is the sentence F13 had misread. So `found_count()` stays a polite live region on both platforms, with no `announce_message` path. Hearing it with VoiceOver on the iPhone is the owner's check (D-102, #320). | Fixing the shared hook once fixes Search, Speakers and Sprints. Not chosen: an `announce_message` path for the iOS count (#250's proposal), which would replace a polite, queued announcement with a less polite one and add a timer, for a problem the source shows does not exist. Not chosen either: pushing the state's text only while the field is unfocused, which still races with typing that continues after a Clear. In real Flet sessions (`tests/test_fields.py`), a burst whose reports land before the re-render that the previous event asked for now sends nothing to the field, and every render filters by a prefix of the typed word. Before this change, the same burst sent the older text back. Typing sends nothing, a Clear sends the empty text once, and a Clear before the last key's event still empties the field. Keys reported between a Clear's tap and Flutter's answer to its focus request are dropped, both before and after the Clear's render, and the empty text is sent again; without the landing, both tests fail. The devices ran a throwaway build that added a 300 ms sleep to the Speakers screen's render (never committed), so Python always fell behind the keys. On the shared iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, bundle checked against `build/python-app`), the simulator tool typed "chen" and "sweigart" in one burst. The field and the list stayed in step ("chen", 2 speakers). A build with the hook but without the Clear's landing reproduced the owner's case: a Clear tapped just before a burst left the field empty under a list filtered by "chensweigart". With the landing, the same steps ended empty over the whole list, and the next "mu" read "5 speakers". Search ("chen", "2 sessions, 2 speakers") and Sprints (two bursts of "python web", then the no-match "Clear filter" right before another burst) kept the field and the list together without the delay. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` after each `adb shell input text` burst read the same text in `speakers.filter` as in the count or no-match line, four runs out of four, Clear-then-burst included, and "chen" on Search read "2 sessions, 2 speakers". |
| D-160 | *(`ViewMemory`'s module amended by D-231; `reminders` is a `ReminderSettings`, D-275; `ChangeLog` is `change_log.py`'s, D-301.)* **A module per store, and the `Prefs` store** (amends D-67 and section M's `state.py`; records architecture §6.1's `Prefs`; #72).<br>• `state.py` keeps `Conference`, its context and hook~~, and `ViewMemory`~~ (`ViewMemory` is `memory.py`'s, D-231); `prefs.py` holds `Prefs`, `PrefsCtx` and `use_prefs()`; `Favorites` (P1), `Account` and `Staff` (P3) get `favorites.py`, `account.py` and `staff.py`. `Services`, `ServicesCtx` and `use_services()` live in `services/__init__.py`.<br>• `Prefs` holds the six app-wide keys of the storage table: `excluded_tracks` as a tuple (stored as `list[str]`, F12), ~~`reminders` and `dev` as dicts~~ `reminders` as a `ReminderSettings` (D-275) and `dev` as a dict. `theme` is an `Appearance` (`system`, `light`, `dark`) and `high_contrast` a `HighContrast` (`system`, `on`, `off`); a stored value outside its set reads as `system`, logged with the key only (as D-120 does for a wrong type), and `save()` raises `ValueError` for one before writing anything.<br>• `save(storage, *, theme=None, high_contrast=None, onboarded=None, excluded_tracks=None, reminders=None, dev=None) -> bool`: None leaves a setting as it is; it writes each given key, then assigns them all even when a write failed, and returns whether every write succeeded, so the caller shows "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). `load(storage)` assigns after all its reads and leaves `loaded` to `bootstrap()`. | With `Prefs`, `state.py` passed 500 lines (D-66), and P1 and P3 add three more stores; a top-level module per store keeps each one short and the imports flat (`from pycon_us.prefs import use_prefs`), with no package. Keyword arguments let pyright check every call, which `save(svc, **changes)` (architecture's sketch) would not. Checking the two fixed-value settings in the store keeps an unknown value from ever reaching `THEME_MODES`. |
| D-161 | **The boot gate and the start sequence** (records architecture §4 for SHELL-01 and DATA-35; #72).<br>• `BootView` is a full-bleed `#680579` View with a white `ProgressRing` after 1 s (`loop.call_later` from a sync effect whose cleanup cancels it), and no logo until O-4 gives the app an icon.<br>• `main.py` runs `ft.run(main, before_main=prepare_first_page)`, which gives the page's default View the boot purple (F35) and sets `page.theme_animation_style` to zero duration in that first page; `bootstrap()` applies the stored theme before it opens the gate and resets the animation after it, so every theme change before the gate, `App`'s mount-time one included, lands at once, and `App`'s theme effect follows later changes, which crossfade. The brightness handler reads `page.platform_brightness`, not the event (F36); U25 (amends D-98, with the small ones): deliver a real `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent`, and keep the boot screen until `main()`'s first patch (F35). An unexpected error while it loads the settings or the local feed is logged and the gate opens anyway, with whatever loaded. The refresh starts after the gate opens and is not awaited.<br>• In P0 the lifecycle handler only re-reads the accessibility flags on `resume`; the refresh on a stale resume is #82's (D-40).<br>• `bootstrap()` sets `page.on_close` before its first await; a session closed while it loads (F26, F29) stops there, without opening the gate, starting a refresh or logging the store write that fails (F30). `Conference.start_refresh()` and `refresh()` take `closed=svc.closed` (D-147): once it is set, the refresh writes nothing more, neither the store nor, after the fetch, the cache.<br>• Until #74 the Router has one stub route, `screens/stub.py`: the data source, fetch time and status, the first day's session count, Refresh, and an Appearance control that drives `Prefs.save()`; until #73 `main.py` calls `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)`. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build), an offline cold start showed the purple boot view, then the bundled schedule 0.3-0.55 s after the session started, with no network wait; online, the refresh replaced it 0.4-2.4 s later, and a new session after Back and a relaunch ran the gate again. With a stored Dark under a light OS, every recording (3 of 3 on each platform) went from the purple straight to the dark screen. Before the fixes, Android showed one white frame between Flet's boot screen and `BootView` (F35); iOS showed the first screen mid-crossfade from light to dark (mean grey 83 instead of 19), because the gate opened within Flutter's 200 ms theme animation; and a dark-mode switch crashed the iOS session (F36). Opening the gate after an unexpected error keeps a bug from leaving the user on a purple screen; the error is in the log. |
| D-163 | *(`setup_logging()`'s handler amended by D-196: it writes every email address as `<email>`.)* **The error net and logging** (records D-71 and D-72; #73). In `services/errors.py`:<br>• **`report_errors`** is D-71's decorator. Its wrapper logs `Unexpected error in <module>.<qualname>` with the traceback, returns None and shows a floating SnackBar, "Something went wrong", followed by `: <type>: <message>` when not embedded (the companion app; #88's Developer unlock adds packaged builds, D-73). Showing it is best effort: with no page in the context, or in a replaced session (F30), it logs a warning instead, so the net itself never raises. A generator or async generator function raises `TypeError` when wrapped: Flet iterates a generator handler only when the handler itself is one, so the wrapper would silently never run it.<br>• **What is wrapped:** every event handler, every effect setup and every effect cleanup that a component, a hook, `main()` or `bootstrap()` defines, sync or async, the page handlers included (this line named cleanups as unwrapped until #909 corrected it: `test_errors.py` holds every nested function to the net). `bootstrap()`'s loading steps keep their own `try`, since the gate opens anyway (D-161); a module's top-level function handed over as a cleanup (`partial(forget, ...)` in `screens/plan.py` and `screens/room.py`) and callables Flet does not call as handlers, effect setups or cleanups (`page.on_close = svc.close`) are not wrapped. The log holds an exception's message verbatim, so code that can raise keeps URLs, tokens and badge codes out of its messages (D-72): P3's API layer raises its own exceptions rather than letting httpx's `HTTPStatusError` (whose message is the full URL) escape.<br>• **`setup_logging()`** runs once, when `main.py` is imported (a new session reuses the process, F26), and a second call returns at once, without touching a level set since: one stderr handler on the root logger, lines `HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL logger: message`, at INFO, or at the level `FLET_LOG_LEVEL` names (`flet run -v` / `-vv`; Flet's own use of it does nothing once a handler exists). `httpx` and `httpcore` stay at WARNING. In a packaged app `sys.stderr` is the template's tee to logcat or the unified log and to `console.log`, so the one handler reaches both (F14).<br>• **No asyncio exception handler of our own** (amends the roadmap's P0-21 scope): asyncio's default handler already logs through `logging`, so the setup above sends what it reports to `console.log`, and no loop-level hook can report a failed effect sooner (F37). Background work is awaited, wrapped or given a done callback (`Conference.start_refresh`). | An exception that escapes a handler ends the session (F4), and one that escapes an async effect is reported late or never (F37), so the net has to be on the callables themselves. `console.log`, which testers share, has no time stamps of its own. httpx logs every request URL at INFO, query included, and P3's private API could put there what D-72 keeps out of logs. In packaged builds with temporary failures (never committed): on the Android 14 emulator, a button whose wrapped sync or async handler raised logged the traceback and showed the SnackBar, and the Appearance control still worked after it, while the same handler unwrapped ended the session on Flet's "Error running app" screen; a wrapped sync effect setup, which Flet calls inside its update loop rather than in an event task, raised at mount and again when the refresh changed the feed version: each time it logged the traceback and showed the SnackBar, the refresh finished and Appearance › Dark still worked; on the iOS 26.5 simulator (driven with `simctl`, so without taps), a wrapped async effect and the wrapped brightness handler raised, each logged its traceback to the unified log and `console.log` and showed the SnackBar, and the theme still followed the next brightness switch. |
| D-187 | *(The Flet row shows `flet.version.flet_version`, D-481.)* *(The build number on the Version row, by D-386.)* *(The `dev` keys, `with_dev_options()` and `parse_feed_url()` are `dev_options.py`'s, #737.)* *(Why its feedback is in place amended by D-210; who deletes the cache when a feed URL is left, and the race, by D-244; which feed URL changes delete it, with the change log, by D-255; the Developer fields and buttons by D-232.)* **The Developer screen, its options and the minimal About this app** (records D-73 and D-43's fake clock for DEV-01, and INFO-03's P1 part; amends section M's screen-module names for these two; #88).<br>• **Screens:** `screens/about_app.py` (`AboutApp`, `/more/app`) and `screens/developer.py` (`Developer`, `/more/developer`), named after their identifiers' screen part (`about_app.version`, `developer.*`; section M), since `app.py` is the root module. The fake clock's date and time pickers are `screens/developer_clock.py` (`clock_picker()`), so that the screen, which #107 grows, stays under about 400 lines (D-66).<br>• **Where the options live:** `pycon.dev` (`Prefs.dev`), with the keys `unlocked`, `fake_now` (the fake clock's start, ISO 8601 with its offset) and `feed_url`. `Prefs` reads them: `developer_unlocked`, `developer` (`DEVELOPER_ALWAYS`, i.e. `not is_embedded()`, or unlocked), `fake_now` and `feed_url`; a value of the wrong kind (a naive time, an empty URL) reads as its default. `prefs.with_dev_options()` sets or removes the clock and the URL and keeps the rest; `clock.fake_clock_text()` and `clock.parse_fake_clock()` are the clock field's text form and `prefs.parse_feed_url()` checks the URL field; `timefmt.utc_offset_label()` writes the device's offset.<br>• **The unlock persists:** in a packaged build, 7 taps on About this app's version row set `unlocked`, so it holds after a relaunch, because the fake clock and the feed URL do too and the screen that clears them must stay reachable; #107's Reset app locks it again. The 7th tap adds a **Developer options** row under the build's rows (`about_app.developer`, a live region), which opens the screen; once it is on, and always when not embedded, the row is there and taps do nothing more. A failed save keeps the unlock for the session and the row says "On until you close the app" (D-120). Opened at `/more/developer` before the unlock, the page says how to unlock.<br>• **The fake clock is stored as its start:** `bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()` runs `clock.start_fake_clock(prefs.fake_now)` right after the settings load, so every start of the app, and every new session in the process (F26, F29), begins at the same simulated moment and runs forward. Apply starts it only when the start changed (a feed-only change does not set it back); Reset returns to real time.<br>• **How screens learn of a change:** Apply and Reset set the clock and the feed URL **before** `prefs.save(dev=...)`. The save assigns `Prefs.dev`, `App` subscribes to `Prefs`, so the tree re-renders as on a theme change (D-150) and every screen reads `clock.now()` again; a tab root under the Developer stack mounts afresh on the switch (D-142), and the status strip's minute tick (D-175) keeps the age current. A memoised component that shows the time (P1's Schedule, D-153) takes the minute it shows as a prop. Now's stub shows today's date and the Schedule stub marks today, by `clock.now()` in conference time.<br>• **The feed URL** is `Conference.set_feed_url()` (a private field: it notifies nothing): every refresh started after it fetches from it, None returns to `conference.toml`'s. http is accepted (a test server on the laptop, testing-and-quality §5.2). A good feed from it is cached like any other, so when Apply or Reset leaves a feed URL for the year's feed, ~~`developer.leave_test_feed()`~~ `Conference.set_feed_url()` deletes the cache (D-244): an offline start would otherwise publish the test feed as the schedule, and starts from the bundled snapshot instead ~~(a refresh still running from the URL can cache it again)~~ (a refresh still running from the URL writes nothing, D-244).<br>• **Error details:** `errors.show_details(on)`; `bootstrap()` sets it from the stored unlock and About this app when it unlocks, and `_tell_user()` shows the exception text when it is on or the app is not embedded (D-71, D-163).<br>• **About this app (P1):** the app's name, "A companion for {conference}, built with Python and Flet." and "Not an official PSF app." (D-36; More's footnote goes), then Version, Python and Flet ("source build", `FLET_VERSION`, until `flet==1.1.0`). **No build number until U4:** `flet build` passes `--build-number` only to Flutter (Android `versionCode`, iOS `CFBundleVersion`), the template puts nothing about it in the app's environment, and `importlib.metadata` sees nothing (F15); a generated constant would be code generation (D-66).<br>• **The Developer screen (P1):** titled "Developer options" (the chip already says "Developer"), with a warning-tone chip (label and 1 dp outline in `Tokens.warning`; text only, as a badge: a fixed 18 dp icon looked lost beside the label at 200 % text) at the top of the page, not in the app bar, where it pushed the title out at 200 % text, drawn as a `Container`, as `TrackBadge` is: a mark, not a control, and a `Chip` without a handler is drawn faded, as disabled (seen on the iOS simulator); the fake-clock field (controlled, D-157; ISO 8601, conference time without an offset; its helper gives the format), filled by a Material `DatePicker` and then a `TimePicker`, which open on the first day at 09:00 while the field is empty (the date picker reports the device's local midnight in UTC, read back with `astimezone()`); both fields wrap to three lines at large text (`max_lines=3` without `multiline`, which would swap the date-time and URL keyboards for the multiline one, whose Return types a line break); the time zone (the conference's, and the device's UTC offset); the feed-URL field; Reset and Apply; errors under the fields, and after Apply or Reset a start-aligned line that says what is in effect (`developer.outcome`, a live region), after a check icon in `Tokens.success`, or a warning icon in `Tokens.warning` when the save failed (icon and word, ux.md §9); the capability flags in words. Identifiers: `developer.fake_clock`, `developer.feed_url`, `developer.reset` (real time and the year's feed, unconfirmed: nothing is lost that cannot be typed again), `developer.apply` and `developer.outcome`, with `about_app.version` and `about_app.developer`; Reset app (#107) gets one of its own.<br>• **Feedback in place, not in SnackBars,** on both pages (D-29's "results the user waits for appear in place")~~: at the pin a SnackBar shown from a pushed View is drawn on the tab root under it (#197)~~; a SnackBar would show on these pages too (D-210). | A persisted unlock keeps the way to the options that persist; an unlock per session would leave a fake clock running after a relaunch with its screen hidden. Starting the clock from its stored start at each start gives a demo the same moment every time; keeping its offset would let the simulated day drift with real time. Setting the clock before the save is what makes the re-render that the save causes read the new time (Flet renders a store change after the handler yields, `session.schedule_update`). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps): six taps on the version row did nothing and the seventh unlocked Developer, which then showed in More; typing `2026-05-15 10:30` and Apply made Now say "Today in Pacific Time: Friday, May 15" and the Schedule stub mark Fri, May 15 as today, with no restart; a feed URL on a closed port (`http://127.0.0.1:9/conference.json`) then made the Schedule stub's Refresh say "Offline · schedule from Fri, May 15, 10:30 AM" (the log: "Feed refreshes use a Developer feed URL", then "no answer"), and Reset cleared both. A screen recording at 2 frames/s showed Reset's SnackBar on More under the Developer page, never on it (#197), hence the in-place feedback (not seen again with this very commit at the same pin, D-210). On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), seven taps unlocked it, the date picker and then the time picker filled `2026-10-15 21:35` (the day read back right on a device at UTC+02:00), Apply said what it put into effect, and Now said "Thursday, October 15" and refreshed, the 15-min rule being due under the clock moved ahead. At 200 % text (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS) the chip in the app bar pushed the title out ("Develope…"), so it moved to the top of the page. |
| D-386 | **A packaged build shows its build number, from `page.get_package_info()`** (amends D-187's "no build number"; ends U4's interim, gaps doc §5.3; INFO-03; #452).<br>• **Where:** `services/build.py`. `read_build(page)` returns a `Build` (`version`, `number`); in a packaged build (`is_embedded()`) it asks `await page.get_package_info()` once and keeps the answer for the process, since it cannot change while the app runs; `known_build()` is that answer, or `UNPACKAGED` (`__version__` alone) before it. `use_build()` is the hook: `known_build()` at once, then the answer its effect reads, so only the first About this app of a run starts without the number.<br>• **Only when embedded:** outside a packaged build the call describes the Flet client (in the companion app, the companion's own version), so the app never asks there and says `__version__` alone (D-83, whose test stays). A failed call (`SERVICE_ERRORS`) says `__version__`, logs the class only (D-72) and is asked again next time; a blank version falls back to `__version__`, a blank number to none.<br>• **What it says:** About this app's Version row reads `0.4.0 (812)`, Android's "version (build)" form; the 7-tap unlock stays on that row (`about_app.version`). The diagnostics text gains a **Build** line under App (the number, or "none"), and App is the packaged build's own version: 19 lines (amends D-271).<br>• **Verified:** `async Page.get_package_info() -> PackageInfo` at the pin `08b89cce5` (`FLET/controls/page.py:1509`, `FLET/controls/package_info.py`): `version` and `build_number` are the `--build-version` and `--build-number` a `flet build` was given (Android `versionName`/`versionCode`, iOS `CFBundleShortVersionString`/`CFBundleVersion`). |
| D-198 | *(It also deletes other years' feed caches and change logs, D-466.)* *(It lands on Welcome, D-287.)* *(Share log's share, its words and `share_console_log()`'s module amended by D-297, and the rows' and outcome line's drawing moved to `ui/actions.py`; why its outcome is in place amended by D-210; its rows' disabled state on screen readers by D-214; Reset app deletes the change log and lists it among what is lost, D-255; Refresh now's line, `schedule_line()`, moved to `ui/status_strip.py` by D-292; the role of `developer_ui.py` amended by D-232.)* **The Developer screen's data actions: Refresh now, Clear cached feed, Share log, and a confirmed Reset app that resets every store without a restart** (records D-73's data actions for DEV-04, DEV-05 and STORE-13, and ux.md §5.13's P1 "Data" section; amends section M's screen modules and `ui/` (`semantics.py`) and DEV-05's route to `/`; #107).<br>• **Modules, by role:** `screens/developer_data.py` is what the actions do and say: `DataActions`, a component of its own under a **Data** `SectionHeader` between Schedule feed and Capabilities, `reset_app()`, `share_console_log()` and the words; `screens/developer_ui.py` is how the screen draws them: `action_row()`, `outcome_line()` and `padded()` (the last two moved from `developer.py`, which draws Apply's line with them too, so the screen never imports a layout piece from its Data section) and the two confirmations. So each module stays under D-66's 400 lines, as `developer_clock.py` does.<br>• **Rows:** two-line `ListTile`s, each one merged node with its identifier (`developer.refresh_now`, `developer.clear_cache`, `developer.share_log`, `developer.reset_app`), its icons and title 14 dp from the top (D-188's rule for rows with a subtitle), a disabled row saying why in its second line, which stays in `on_surface_variant` while the icon and title take the disabled colour (at the disabled 38 % the reason was too faint to read); identifiers through `ui/semantics.py`'s `named()`, D-190's merge, which Now's buttons use too; under them one outcome line (`developer.data_outcome`, a live region; a check in `success` or a warning in `warning` beside the words, the icon in a 24 dp slot 16 dp before them, so the words start in the tiles' text column, as Apply's line does), in place ~~(#197)~~ (D-29, as Apply's line; D-210). **Refresh now** is `Conference.refresh()`, a refresh the user asked for (D-181): disabled with "Updating the schedule…" and a small `ProgressRing` at its end while `status()` says `refreshing`, otherwise its second line says where the schedule on screen came from and its age ("From the cache, updated 3 hours ago"), kept current by `use_minute()` while the screen is open, as the strip's is (D-175); then "Schedule updated from the feed.", or by the failure (D-146) that the app keeps the schedule it has. **Clear cached feed** is confirmed, then `Storage.delete_feed()`, the one deletion path (a corrupt cache and ~~`leave_test_feed()`~~ `Conference.set_feed_url()` use it too; ~~#206 moves the feed URL's rule into the store~~ D-244), which now says whether the cache is gone and deletes under the write lock in a thread, so a write in progress (a cancelled refresh's, D-120) lands first instead of bringing the file back (D-244: every write asked for before the deletion, also one still queued for the lock). This session keeps the schedule it shows; the next start shows the bundled snapshot until a refresh saves a new copy. Disabled, "No cached schedule on this device", when there is none. **Share log** is `Share.share_files([ShareFile.from_path(await StoragePaths.get_console_log_filename())])` through `Services.share` and `Services.paths`, pointed from the window's centre for tablets (D-62), in a packaged build only, where the template writes `console.log` (F14); elsewhere disabled, "Not a packaged build: the log is in the terminal that runs it". No log or an empty one says "There's no log to share yet."; a failure "Couldn't open the share sheet.", logged by its class (D-72); `ShareResultStatus.SUCCESS` says "Log shared.", `DISMISSED` nothing, and `UNAVAILABLE` (the platform cannot tell, as with some Android share targets) "Log handed to the share sheet.", which is true either way. **Reset app**, its title and icon in `error`, is confirmed.<br>• **Confirmations (D-29):** an `AlertDialog(modal=True, scrollable=True)` with the row's icon, a question for a title, what is lost, then Cancel and the action in `error` (`<row>.cancel`, `<row>.confirm`), portalled with `ft.use_dialog` from the section's state; Back dismisses it as Cancel (`on_dismiss`; on Android it also pops the page, #235). "Clear the cached schedule?" says the session keeps showing it and the next start shows the bundled schedule, its capture day named (with non-breaking spaces, so it never wraps mid-date), until a refresh saves a new copy. Reset app's dialog icon is in `error`, as its row; Clear's keeps the dialog's default. "Reset the app?" lists exactly what is lost, one line each beside a decorative icon (`reset_losses()`): "Your settings: Appearance and the schedule's filters", "Your saved sessions in My schedule: 12" (the live count, or "none"), "The cached schedule: the app shows the bundled one until it refreshes" and "Developer options: the fake clock and the feed URL", with ", and Developer locks again" in a packaged build. Developer's own Reset (real time and the year's feed) stays unconfirmed (D-187): nothing it drops cannot be typed again.<br>• **What Reset app does, without a restart** (`reset_app()`): real time and no error details first, so the re-render the stores cause reads them (D-187's order); `Storage.clear_prefs()` removes every key under `storage.PREFIX` (`pycon.`): the settings, every year's favourites, the Developer options and their unlock, and no key that is not the app's; `Conference.reset()` cancels a running refresh and waits for it (one from a Developer feed URL would otherwise cache or publish the test feed afterwards; a caller awaiting `refresh()`, such as a Refresh now tapped before the reset, ends cancelled with it and says nothing), returns to the year's feed URL, deletes the cache through `delete_feed()` and publishes the bundled snapshot, so every screen shows it at once; `Prefs.reset()` puts the persisted settings back to their defaults (the device facts and `loaded` stay); `Favorites.reset()` empties the set without a write (the key is gone); `ViewMemory.reset()` puts every field back (the Schedule jumps to now again; a new session starts on Now). Then a quiet refresh starts, as at a start (D-181), and the app goes where a fresh start goes: `start_target()` over the reset `ViewMemory` (D-143), so Now, and Welcome once P2 adds it there (DEV-05's "routes to `/`"; a navigation to `/` itself left the app on the Developer View, now locked, on the iOS simulator), where a SnackBar says "App reset" or, when a key or the cache could not be deleted (logged by name, D-120), that the session is reset and some saved data returns when the app reopens. **What a restart would still differ in:** nothing a user sees. The Python process itself stays: its import-time constants (`capabilities.py`), the feed-keyed memos (which the new feed object makes miss), and `console.log`, which is diagnostics, not user data, and is kept so a tester can share what led up to the reset. From P2 and P3, the reminders plan, SecureStorage and the outbox join `reset_app()` with their features (DEV-05). | The issue asks for an app as after a fresh install with no restart where feasible, and every piece of state the app keeps is a store, `ViewMemory`, a `pycon.` key or the cache file, so resetting each explicitly in one function is enough, and a test checks every one of them on a used session. Clearing by prefix rather than `SharedPreferences.clear()` leaves keys the app does not own (Flutter's, a plugin's) alone, and needs no list kept in step with the key table. Cancelling the running refresh is what makes the reset hold: a refresh from a test feed URL that finished afterwards cached that feed again (`test_reset_cancels_a_refresh_from_a_developer_feed_before_it_caches_it` fails without the cancel). Confirming Clear cached feed too follows STORE-13 ("each confirmed with the list of what is lost"): offline, the next start falls back to an older schedule. Share log is gated on a packaged build because the companion app's Python runs on the laptop, so the device path `StoragePaths` returns is not a file it can check, and the template writes no `console.log` there. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps), light and dark: Refresh now said "Schedule updated from the feed." in place; Share log opened the share sheet with the 1 KB `console.log` ("Share the app log"), the row disabled while it was up, and the log held no address; Clear cached feed asked first, then deleted `feed-2026.json` and greyed its row ("No cached schedule on this device"); Reset app's dialog listed the four losses with the live count, Cancel kept everything, and Reset app left the app's preferences plist empty, locked Developer (About this app had no Developer row until seven new taps) and, after the fix above, landed on Now with "App reset"; the next refresh cached the year's feed again, as a fresh start's does. At the largest Larger Text the rows wrapped with nothing clipped, and the dialog's text scrolled under its fixed buttons. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` showed each row as one clickable `Button` node named by its two lines with its `resource-id` (`developer.refresh_now` … `developer.reset_app`) and the dialog's buttons as `developer.reset_app.cancel` and `.confirm`, 48 dp tall; Share log opened the system chooser with `console.log`; Reset app landed on Now with "App reset" and More lost its Developer row; dark at 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped. Back with a dialog open closes it, acting on nothing, but on Android also pops the page under it, as the fake clock's date picker does (#235). |
| D-214 | *(Its guards completed by D-230; the folds' identifiers moved onto their headers by D-354.)* **A semantics identifier is one node with its control, and that node says whether the control is disabled; around a control a merge would swallow, the identifier stays bare, and never on a tile's title** (settles #239 and #236; amends D-190's pattern, D-198's rows, section M's `ui/semantics.py`, ux.md §5.13 and §9; records F50 and U34).<br>• **The disabled state:** `named()` gives its `Semantics` the control's own `disabled` (`control.disabled`) instead of a keyword its callers pass: the Developer screen's rows passed none, so Clear cached feed without a cache dumped `enabled="true"` and TalkBack never said "disabled" (F42). `ui/links.py`'s `external()`, the other merged wrapper, does the same. Both read only the control they get: a `Semantics` passed in the button's place (open spaces' Propose, a sprint's More) needs the button's `disabled` too, as their docstrings say. `named()` is the one way to merge an identifier: the Schedule sheet's Reset, Show, chips and "What do these mean?", the Schedule's Show all, Search's scope chips, the When and Room tiles, a speaker row, More's appearance rows and About's Version, which built its shape by hand (Reset and Show passing `disabled` themselves, D-195), now call it, and a scan keeps it so.<br>• **Merged** (D-190, through `named()`): `developer.reset`, `developer.apply`, `developer.feed_url` (the field reads its label, helper and error as one node), the fake clock's picker (`developer.clock_picker`, new), session detail's `session.overflow` and a contact row's Copy (`<id>.copy`). Bare, each was a node of its own (F50): an empty stop beside the button, or, for More options, the tooltip on the identifier's node and the button under it unnamed.<br>• **Bare, around the control:** where a merge would take in another control: the fake clock field (its picker inside it, D-189) and the folds (`FoldTile`, which hold rows). A fold's identifier moves from its title to around the tile (`lightning_talks.doubts`, `my_schedule.earlier_days`, `my_schedule.earlier_today`, as `session.other_rooms` already was): on the title it split the title from the tile's button, which dumped as "Collapsed" alone. Each is an empty node until U34 (the field) or an identifier on the control itself (the folds). Live regions and containers (`developer.outcome`, `more.appearance`, the empty states) stay as they are.<br>• **Guards:** unit tests for the merged shape and the disabled state, and for the folds' identifier around the tile; an `ast` scan for a merge built by hand (testing-and-quality.md §3.5). Left to the phase's screen-reader pass (#109): VoiceOver's "dimmed" on a disabled row, and what TalkBack does with a fold's empty wrapper (focusable, not clickable, the header's bounds, where the title's node was before). | Before (main at `7e7840e`, release arm64 APK, Android 14 emulator `flet_probe_api34`, `uiautomator dump`): `developer.reset`, `developer.apply`, `developer.feed_url` and `developer.fake_clock` were empty focusable `View`s over the control; Clear cached feed, disabled, `enabled="true"`; `session.overflow` a `View` "More options" over an unnamed `Button`; the doubts fold's header read "Collapsed". After (this change rebased on `5a6a7b6`, the same emulator, the APK's `app.zip` checked against `build/python-app`): Reset and Apply one `Button` each with the identifier; the feed URL the `EditText`; the picker a `Button` "Pick a date and time" that opens the date picker; Clear cached feed, disabled, `enabled="false"`, with its reason; More options one `Button` that opens its menu; the folds' headers "Think you can't do one?, Collapsed" and "Earlier days (1 session), Collapsed". A Flutter widget test (3.47.7) of the same shapes settled what is Flet's and what is Flutter's (F50). Nothing on screen changed. |
| D-230 | *(`ON_PURPOSE`'s `track.add_all` entry moved to `menu_item()` by D-233; its four fold entries removed by D-354.)* **A scan keeps a bare identifier off a button, chip, field or tile; the ones that stay bare are listed, each with its reason** (completes D-214's guards; amends testing-and-quality.md §3.5; settles #279).<br>• **The rule:** an `ast` scan in `tests/test_semantics.py` flags a `Semantics` that passes `identifier` and nothing but `content` and `disabled`, not the content of a `MergeSemantics`, around a call of a `*Button` (`PopupMenuButton` too), `*Chip`, `TextField` or `*Tile` (`ListTile`, and the folds' `ExpansionTile` and `FoldTile`), or around a name its function itself assigns one to (a nested function's or class's names are its own), since screens build the control first (`fold = FoldTile(...)`). Each such identifier is a node apart from its control (F50); `named()` is the fix.<br>• **Bare on purpose** (`ON_PURPOSE`, by module and identifier as written, a comment giving each one's reason): D-214's fake clock field (`developer.fake_clock`) and folds (`lightning_talks.doubts`, `my_schedule.earlier_days` and `.earlier_today`, `session.other_rooms`); and `track.add_all`, inside the overflow's `PopupMenuItem`, whose own `MergeSemantics` (Flutter's `popup_menu.dart`) takes the identifier into the item's node, so `named()` there would merge what is merged already. The scan first checks that it finds every listed site, so a scan that matches nothing, or a stale entry, fails.<br>• **Outside the rule, so not listed:** `SearchField`'s field (merged, with a `label`), the nav icons (around an `Icon`: the destination's node takes the identifier, D-166, D-170), the day tabs' labels (D-166) and an in-app `LinkTile`'s tile (inside the `MergeSemantics` the function puts around it). | On `main` at `be97438` the scan finds those five sites and no other; a scratch `ft.Semantics(identifier="x", content=ft.TextButton("Go"))` in `screens/track.py` failed it (`track.py:215: 'x'`), reverted. `track.add_all` (main's release arm64 APK, its `app.zip` checked against `build/python-app`, Android 14 emulator `flet_probe_api34`, `uiautomator dump` with the Charlas overflow open): one `android.view.MenuItem` node, `resource-id="track.add_all"`, `content-desc="Add all to My schedule"`, clickable and enabled, with no empty node beside it. |
| D-354 | **The app's fold is its own: a `ListTile` header merged with the fold's identifier, its chevron saying "Collapsed" or "Expanded", in place of Flutter's `ExpansionTile`** (settles #332 under D-101, option C; amends D-191's and D-186's tiles, D-214's bare fold identifiers, D-230's `ON_PURPOSE`, section M's `fold.py`, ux.md §9 and testing-and-quality.md §3.5; records F172).<br>• **Why:** with TalkBack every fold took two swipes: a stop that said only "Collapsed" (or "Expanded") and took the list position, then the header. Removing the bare identifier wrappers (option A) left the stop in place, so Flet's forced `enabled` (F42, U34, the mechanism behind #564's split nodes) was not its cause: it is `ExpansionTile`'s Android header, a focus-blocked live region labelled with the state, whose label TalkBack reads as the stop of the nearest focusable node above it, a list item or the When and Where card (F172).<br>• **The fold** (`ui/fold.py`'s `FoldTile`, still controlled, D-157): a `ListTile` (`leading`, the title, a trailing `EXPAND_MORE` chevron that turns half a turn with `animate_rotation`) under `named(identifier, Semantics(live_region=True, ...))`, so the identifier, the header and its state are one node: TalkBack reads "Earlier days (1 session), Collapsed, Button" and VoiceOver the same words. The state is the chevron's `semantics_label`, after the title, because Android's bridge reports no expanded state (no `setExpandedState` at Flutter 3.47.7) and iOS's maps no `isExpanded`; no `expanded=` flag, so nothing says it twice. The live region says the new state when a double tap changes it. Icons in `primary` while open and `on_surface_variant` while folded, as `ExpansionTile`'s M3 defaults. What it holds is a `Column` that is `visible` only while open (out of the screen reader's tree while folded), in a `Container` whose `animate_size` is `expand_style()`, now an `ft.Animation` (`EXPAND_MS`, 0 under reduced motion, D-173). The open fill is the header `ListTile`'s own `bgcolor`, drawn as ink, so its ripple shows, and the body's `Container`'s; `lines` draws a 1 dp `outline` border above and below while open (D-306).<br>• **Identifiers:** `FoldTile(identifier=)` is required and puts it on the header: `my_schedule.earlier_days`, `my_schedule.earlier_today`, `lightning_talks.doubts`, `session.other_rooms`. No bare wrapper is left, so D-230's scan lists none of them and flags one put back around a `FoldTile`.<br>• **No `upstream` issue:** the gap is Flutter's `ExpansionTile`, not Flet's, and the app no longer uses it; F172 records it for the owner. | Before (#332's check, `ff8d43f` + option A, Android 14, TalkBack 14.2): My schedule "Collapsed. 2 of 4" then "Earlier days (1 session), Collapsed"; Lightning talks "Collapsed" then the header; session detail "Collapsed" on the When and Where card. After (this branch, release arm64 APK, own `pycon_pixel6a_api36` emulator, Android 16, TalkBack 16.0 with its verbose log, swipes and double taps written to the touch screen): My schedule (fake clock Saturday 14:10, three lightning talks saved) "More options" → "Earlier days (1 session), Collapsed, Button. 2 of 5. In list. 5 items" → "Saturday, May 16, Heading. 3 of 5" → "Earlier today (1 session), Collapsed, Button. 4 of 5" → "5:45 PM, Heading"; a double tap said "Earlier today (1 session), Expanded, Button" and the next swipe reached the folded talk. Lightning talks "Sign up…" → "Think you can't do one?, Collapsed, Button" → "More about lightning talks"; a double tap said "…, Expanded", the next swipe the first doubt, a second double tap "…, Collapsed". Session 163 "Lightning Talks, Heading" → "Saturday, May 16 …" → "Pacific Ballroom - Arena, Button" → "+5 more rooms, Collapsed, Button" → "Speakers, Heading". `tools/a11y_tree.py`: each header one `Button` with its identifier, and no focusable node around it. |
| D-234 | **A button's tooltip names it in its own node: each button whose tooltip is said is merged with it, through `merged()` or, with an identifier, `named()`, and a scan keeps it so** (settles #274; amends D-10's back arrow, D-27, ux.md §3.7, §5.2 and §9, section M's `ui/semantics.py` and testing-and-quality.md §3.5; records F55 and U37).<br>• **The cause is Flet's** (F55): it wraps a control in its `Tooltip` from outside, so a button's tooltip is a node of its own over the button, which has no name. The analysis and a suggested fix for Flet are #317 (`upstream`, D-212, D-213).<br>• **`merged(button)`** (`ui/semantics.py`): a `MergeSemantics` straight around the button, which then reads its tooltip as its name, as Flutter's own `IconButton(tooltip:)` does. No `Semantics` of its own, so no enabled state to pass (F42). Used by `AppScaffold`'s Back arrow, `search_button()` (now typed `ft.Control`), `SearchField`'s Clear (placed in its `Stack` by a `Container(right=4)`, since `MergeSemantics` has no position) and a contact row's Copy without an identifier (with one it was already `named()`). The buttons with an identifier were already merged through `named()` (Share, the overflows, the clock picker; D-214), and those named by a label keep their tooltip out of the semantics (the star, Filter sessions, Propose an open space; D-184).<br>• **The "Now" button:** a View takes only a `FloatingActionButton`, so no merge can go around it. Its tooltip still shows on a long press but is left out of the semantics (`exclude_from_semantics=True`), and what it does is said from its label's `semantics_label`, inside Flutter's own merge: "Now", then "Jump to the current time", as a link's "Opens in browser" is said (ux.md §4.7). Not a `Semantics`' `tooltip`, which Flet also shows as a second tooltip (F55), nor its `hint_text`, which never reaches Flutter (#187).<br>• **Not chosen:** an `icon_button()` builder for every icon button (the merge is the one thing to add, and the buttons differ in all else); a `Semantics(label=...)` copying each tooltip (two strings to keep in step, and iOS reads a node's tooltip after its label, D-184); an identifier on every button only to reach `named()`.<br>• **Guard:** `tests/test_tooltips.py` scans every module for a `*Button` call whose tooltip is said (not `None`, not a `Tooltip(..., exclude_from_semantics=True)`) and that reaches no merge (`MergeSemantics`, `named()`, `merged()`, `links.external()`): straight, through a `Semantics` around it, a conditional, a name its function assigns it to, or a function that returns it, each of whose calls must merge it (the clock picker). TalkBack said "Back. Button", "Search. Button" and "Clear search. Button". On the iOS 26.5 simulator the screens look as before. |
| D-196 | **The log handler writes every email address as `<email>`** (amends D-163's `setup_logging()`; records D-72 for Share log; #107). `services/errors.py`'s `RedactingFormatter` formats the line as before (`LOG_FORMAT`), then replaces each match of `EMAIL` (`name@host.tld`, or with the `@` percent-encoded as in a `mailto:` URL) with `<email>`, over the whole line, traceback included; `setup_logging()` gives it to its one handler, so it covers every line the app logs through `logging`, in `console.log`, logcat and the unified log. What reaches `sys.stderr` outside `logging` is not redacted: `sys.excepthook`'s report of an exception that escapes everything and a `warnings` message; the app writes nothing there itself, and `report_errors` (D-163) keeps handlers and effects inside `logging`. The rule stays where it was: our own lines never hold a secret, a badge code or an address (D-72, D-163), and this is a net under it, not a licence. An audit of the app's log calls (#107) found keys and file names, link kinds and error classes, counts, versions, routes, scroll keys and the fake clock's start, none of them personal; the lines that quote what the app does not control are `report_errors`' exception message and traceback, `Skipped a feed record: …` (which quotes a record's value), and asyncio's and Flet's own. | D-182 found platform errors that repeat a `mailto:` address (Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` names the intent), and the feed's records can carry contact addresses; once Share log sends `console.log`, an address in it leaves the device. Secrets and badge codes have no pattern to match, so for them the rule at the write site is the only guard; an address does, and a test writes one through the real handler, in a message and in a traceback, and finds `<email>` in both. `@ft.component`, `flet@1.1.0`, a Mastodon handle URL and `user@localhost` are kept. |
| D-271 | *(`FLET_VERSION` gave way to `flet.version.flet_version`, D-481.)* **The plain diagnostics text: facts that name nothing personal, written one per line by a pure function** (records INFO-04's text, ux.md §5.12's "plain diagnostics"; amends section M's `services/`, `__init__.py` and `capabilities.py`, and architecture's module table and §13; #386).<br>• **Modules:** `services/diagnostics.py` holds `Facts` (frozen), `diagnostics_text(facts)` (pure) and `gather_facts(page, *, info, conference, prefs, favorites)` (async, D-70); its helpers are private (D-227). `FLET_VERSION` ("source build" while the pin lasts) moves from `screens/about_app.py` to `pycon_us/__init__.py`, beside `__version__`, and the Developer screen's capability list to `capabilities.ALL`, so About this app, Developer and the text share one of each. `Conference.digest` exposes the published feed's SHA-256 (D-224).<br>• **What it says,** 18 `Name: value` lines (19 with D-386's Build under App): App, Python, Flet, Device (make and model, ", emulator" or ", simulator"), OS (`Android 15 (API 35)`, `iOS 26.5`; the platform when the device can't be read), Run (packaged or not embedded), Year, Schedule (source and the feed's first 12 hex digits), Schedule fetched, Conference zone, Device offset (`UTC-07:00`, an ASCII sign, so a search for `-07:00` finds it), Fake clock (off, or its start and the app's time), Written, Custom feed URL (yes or no), Reduced motion, Width (dp and Material's window size class: compact under 600, medium under 840, expanded), Capabilities and Saved sessions (a count; unknown when the list couldn't be read, D-172). Times are ISO 8601 in conference time with their offset.<br>• **What it never says:** the feed URL, saved ids or titles, a search query, an email, a device's user-given name (an iPhone's is often its owner's) or an identifier. The facts can't hold them (a flag, a count), and every value is written on one line, cut at 80 characters, with its emails and URLs replaced by `<email>` and `<url>` (D-196's pattern), so a value the app doesn't control can't add a line or carry an address. Sharing the text (About this app, Help & safety) is #443. | A bug report needs the build, the device, which schedule and which clock; Ionic's encrypted dump held every key, the API secret included (INFO-04). The schedule's "version" is the feed's digest, not `Conference.version`: a publish counter means nothing outside its session, while the digest names the same feed on any device. Facts typed so they can't hold a secret are a guard by construction; the line writer is the net under it, as D-196's formatter is for the log. |

Plain rows with no action: Refresh is the strip's and Developer's (D-198).<br>• **One wording:** `schedule_line()` and its `SOURCES` move from `screens/developer_data.py` to `ui/status_strip.py`, beside `strip_line()` and `use_minute()`, so Developer's Refresh now, this page and Settings' Data section (#422) say it alike. A component of its own keeps the minute's re-render to two rows. A constant, not a build flag, because a packaged app sees no environment (§5.6 of development.md), and one place to change at P4.
