On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release APK checked against `build/python-app`): online, "From the feed, updated just now"; with Wi-Fi and data off and the app's data cleared, the first start said "Bundled with the app, updated 8 days ago"; `uiautomator dump` read the headings "Conference data", "This build" and "Links", the row `about_app.schedule` as "Schedule, From the feed, updated just now" and `about_app.flet` as one node "Flet website, flet.dev, Opens in browser", whose tap opened Chrome (its first-run screen, as in D-182); dark at 200 % text wrapped the schedule line with nothing clipped. |

| D-480 | **Python comments and docstrings use Markdown's single backtick; pre-commit runs the hooks, `tools/check.sh` runs the same ones, and a `tokenize` checker keeps reST's double backtick out** (the owner's direction, 2026-11-04; #1028; extends D-74 and D-273).<br>• **The style.** Inline code in a docstring or comment is one backtick: `like this`. A span whose text holds a backtick is written with two (Markdown's own rule). A reST `::` literal-block introducer is a plain colon. #1029 converted the code.<br>• **The guard.** `tools/check_backticks.py` reads only comments and bare string statements (docstrings) of Python files, with `tokenize` and `ast`, and fails on a double-backtick span. It does not look at other strings, so test fixtures and Markdown that a converter must produce are never flagged. `tests/test_check_backticks.py` pins it and runs it on the repo.<br>• **One config, two stages.** `.pre-commit-config.yaml` pins its hooks by `rev`: `pre-commit-hooks` (trailing whitespace, end of file, YAML and TOML syntax, merge-conflict markers, files over 1 MB, no commit on `main`), `actionlint`, `gitleaks`, and local hooks for ruff and the guard. Hooks in stage `manual` are the ones `tools/check.sh` runs (a new step after `ruff format --check`, also in `ci.yml`), so the pre-push hook, the merge gate and, once the repo is public, CI run the same set. The commit-only ones are `ruff check`, `ruff format` (`check.sh` has its own ruff steps), `gitleaks` (it scans the staged diff) and `no-commit-to-branch` for `main` (D-88).<br>• **ruff is not pinned twice.** The ruff hooks are `language: system` through `uv run`, so the version is `uv.lock`'s (D-75), not a second `rev` to keep in step.<br>• **Commit-time hooks are opt-in.** `pre-commit install` refuses while `core.hooksPath` is set, and D-273 sets it for the pre-push hook. `.githooks/pre-commit` stands in: it does nothing unless `git config hooks.precommit true`, then runs `pre-commit run` on the staged files. Agents that commit often are not slowed down by it.<br>• **Lint families (#1028).** ruff 0.17's defaults plus: E (E501 at 120 columns, the length docstrings and comments already keep; code stays at 88), W, C4, PTH, PERF, T20, A, N, ARG, PT, ASYNC, S, PLC, PLW, RUF, PIE, RET, LOG, G, TID, ICN, ISC, RSE, FURB, FLY. Each has a comment in `pyproject.toml`. Left off: ERA (all hits are section-heading comments), TC (breaks Flet's runtime-evaluated annotations), PLR (magic values, argument counts: they fight `@ft.component` functions), and per rule N802 (CamelCase components), PT018, ASYNC109, PLC0415, PLW0108, PLW0603, RUF001-003, S101, SIM108. S and ARG apply to `src/` only, T20 to `src/` and `tests/` (tools print by design). Prefer a fix to a `noqa`; each `noqa` gives its reason.<br>• **Data files are excluded** from the hooks: `src/assets/`, `src/pycon_us/licence_texts/`, `tests/fixtures/`, `docs/icon-options/`, `docs/research/`. The gitleaks hook reads `.gitleaks.toml` (#847), which allows the signing fixture's dummy signatures and nothing else. | The owner asked for single backticks in Python and for pre-commit and ruff rules. A rule nobody enforces comes back, and about 9,000 double-backtick lines had accumulated. The hook-set is the one place where a hygiene or lint rule is added, and `check.sh` is the one place where the local gate, the pre-push hook and CI take their list from, so a hook cannot pass on one and fail on another. A regex would flag data strings; `tokenize` does not. The hook scans a commit's staged diff; the history sweep stays development.md §7.6's. |

## I. Testing

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-76 | Unit tests (pytest) cover everything in `core/`, plus the year-data loader, capabilities, storage keys and theme contrast. Fixtures are the recorded 2025 and 2026 feeds. | Protects correctness cheaply. |
| D-77 | Feed correctness is checked three ways: the named rule tests (D-41); about ten invariants (no session lost, stable ids, placeholders filtered, Job Fair grouping, collapsed rows, speakers linked, a start on every group); and a reviewed JSON snapshot of our processed model. Ionic's own processing output is captured once in P0 (`tools/capture_ionic_reference/`) to settle ambiguous rules; it does not run in CI, and contributors need no Node. In P3, hypothesis state-machine tests cover the outbox. | Ionic is a reference, not an oracle. This reconciles the three reviews. |
| D-78 | About eight `flet test` smoke flows run on a device or emulator: start offline, switch days, search, favourite with Undo, session → speaker → session → Back, map pin, and in P3 an offline scan that syncs. They run locally before merging UI-heavy PRs and at phase exits. `flet test` always runs in device mode, so flows run on an emulator and a simulator. Spike S9 tries `flet test linux` for CI; once it has run green for two weeks, it is proposed as a separate workflow through a `needs-decision` issue, never inside `ci.yml` (D-84). A handful of goldens arrive only in P4. | These are the journeys that matter. On-device testers cannot drag (F21), and goldens churn while the UI evolves. |
| D-79 | *(Devices amended by D-102.)* Each phase exit runs a device checklist ~~on our Android phone and iPhone (plus an emulator and a simulator)~~ on an Android emulator and an iOS simulator, with the iPhone for the items that need hardware or a release build (D-102): cold start, airplane-mode start, dark and high contrast, maximum font size, TalkBack and VoiceOver on three flows, 3-button and gesture navigation (with visible navigation-bar icons in light and dark, D-12), back gestures, reminders firing (P2 on) and queued scans (P3). Results go in the phase status issue. | An API that exists in the SDK is not proof it works on a device. |
| D-80 | *(Devices amended by D-102; emulator baseline recorded by D-112; interaction latency by D-159; how an emulator run is compared by D-453.)* Performance targets, measured by hand at phase exits ~~on a mid-range Android phone~~ on the devices D-102 names (release builds on the iPhone against the targets; the Android emulator as a relative regression signal; absolute Android numbers once a mid-range Android phone is borrowed) and recorded:<br>• cold start to the cached schedule: 2.5 s or less;<br>• feed processing: 400 ms or less;<br>• no visible jank scrolling the busiest day.<br>App size is recorded, not gated. S1 and S2 may revise the numbers. | Honest numbers without CI machinery, and budgets from evidence. |
| D-170 | *(The title's node amended by D-211: it is also the level-1 heading.)* **Tab roots name themselves to UI flows** (adds to section M's identifiers; #78). `AppScaffold` wraps a tab root's app-bar title in an unlabelled `Semantics(identifier="<tab>.title")`: `now.title`, `schedule.title`, `my_schedule.title`, `map.title`, `more.title` (`Tab.title_identifier`; `<tab>` is `Tab.key`, the bar's own `app.nav.<tab>` key). Pushed Views get none from the scaffold: a flow knows one by its back arrow and the bar's absence (D-144), and a screen that a flow must tell apart adds its own `<screen>.<element>` identifier. | A `Finder` only counts matches (testing-and-quality.md §13), and a root's title is also its tab's label, so the text alone cannot say which root shows. The title is the page's heading, one per root, and the screen modules stay untouched, so the More list (#77) can change freely. As with the bar (D-166), the identifier merges into the title's node: the shell flow found each one on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator, and `uiautomator dump` on the Now and More roots shows one leaf node with `resource-id` `now.title` (`more.title`) and the description `Now` (`More`), so the heading still reads once. Each pin bump re-checks it with the bar's (development.md §2.2). |
| D-171 | **How the UI flows run** (records D-78's harness; #78). In `tests/ui/`:<br>• `tests/ui/conftest.py` holds the fixtures and `tests/ui/helpers.py` the helpers; the inventory is testing-and-quality.md §5.2.<br>• Flows tap only by finder: no drag, no coordinates (F21). They find by identifier, exact text or tooltip, never by icon (`RemoteTester.find_by_icon` sends Flet's packed icon value).<br>• A step that follows a push or a pop waits until the leaving View is gone, and the back arrow is tapped only when exactly one shows: a tap on the View leaving is lost.<br>• Screenshots come from `adb exec-out screencap` and `xcrun simctl io screenshot` into `build/ui/<platform>/` (git-ignored); a timeout saves `timed-out.png` when the device still answers, and its own failure never replaces the step's. `RemoteTester.take_screenshot` fails from its second call on Android.<br>• Each flow runs on an emulator or a simulator of the agent's own: the test build uses the app's bundle id and `flutter test` uninstalls it afterwards. Commands: development.md §5.7. | The shell flow (`test_shell_starts`) passed with `flet test` on `flet_probe_api34` (Android 14) and an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5). Before these rules, it failed on Android when the second back-arrow tap landed on the page being popped, and when `take_screenshot` ran a second time. Host tools give every step's picture, on both platforms, without changing how Flutter draws. |
| D-223 | *(How `open_developer()` reaches About this app, and the section order on Developer, changed by D-478.)* **How the UI flows set the scene** (adds to D-171; #108, P1-30). In `tests/ui/`:<br>• `developer.py` drives Developer options as a person would: `open_developer()` goes More › About this app › the version row 7 times › Developer options, skipping the taps once unlocked, always through About; `apply_options()` types the fake clock, then the feed URL, and taps Apply; `reopen_developer()` leaves and reopens the screen; `refresh_now()` taps Refresh now and waits for its words; `fresh_start(now=, feed_url=)` does it all from the launch and returns to Now.<br>• `fresh_start()` first taps Refresh now only to let the launch's own refresh from the year's feed end: a Refresh now joins a running refresh, which keeps the URL it started with (`Conference.set_feed_url`). With a feed URL, it then reopens Developer (no outcome line, the keyboard gone) and waits for "Schedule updated from the feed."<br>• F2–F5 run on the recorded feed (`feed_url`) at 09:00 the day before the conference (`before_conference()`), where the Schedule opens on its first day at the top. F1 sets a feed that takes each request and hangs up unanswered (`silent_feed`) and checks the bundled snapshot's rows; it holds the strip's Refresh until the flow lets it hang up, so the flow sees that refresh run (its request counted, "Updating the schedule…") and end, not only the "Offline" line that was there before the tap; a networked device may already show the year's feed, whose sessions are the same. A cold start offline needs a relaunch and stays on the device checklist (D-79).<br>• What a flow looks for comes from `targets.py`, the feed processed by the app's own code, a day's rows filtered as a fresh install filters them (open spaces hidden, D-20); no id is written out.<br>• Waits are on identifiers and texts with deadlines. A tab switch waits for one bar and taps until the tab's title shows (`open_tab()`): both roots' bars show while one replaces the other, and the Android emulator lost a More tap while Now re-rendered. A tap that an animation can swallow, on a control that acts once (a SnackBar's Undo), is repeated until its effect shows (`tap_until()`), never after a fixed sleep. A timeout's picture is `timed-out-<flow>.png`.<br>• A flow cannot scroll or close the keyboard (`RemoteTester` has no drag and no keyboard action, U35, #288). So: the iOS flows run on an **iPhone 17 Pro Max** simulator (the iPhone 17 family, D-102), whose More root shows About this app, which the iPhone 17's hides under the bar; the Android AVD keeps its on-screen keyboard down (`show_ime_with_hard_keyboard 0`; its hardware keyboard types); the iOS software keyboard stays, so Developer's feed URL field keeps Apply and its outcome in sight above it (`APPLY_IN_SIGHT`, 304 dp of `scroll_padding`, a gain for people too: type, then Apply, with the keyboard up; sized for large text, since Flet does not give the app its text scale, after 168 dp left the outcome under the keyboard at 200 % on Android and Apply at iOS's largest size), and search's results are checked one scope at a time, near the top. Back to the iPhone 17 when the pin brings U35. | The five flows passed on `flet_probe_api34` (Android 14) and an iPhone 17 Pro Max simulator (iOS 26.5). Before these rules: on the iPhone 17 the run timed out on `more.app`, under the bar; the keyboard hid Apply on both platforms and search's speaker row on Android; a My schedule tap landed on the leaving root's bar on iOS. A Refresh now that may join the launch's refresh, or tell its own words from the last ones, would make a flow pass or fail by timing. |
| D-252 | *(The `adb` driver for row D: amended by D-467.)* **Maestro drives the device journeys and the regression matrices; D-223's `flet test` flows F1-F5 stay for what needs the host's side** (adds to D-78 and D-79; settles #348).<br>• **Maestro** (the free local CLI, 2.11.0; never Maestro Cloud or `maestro login`): YAML flows in `maestro/` run against the packaged app, a release APK on an Android emulator and an `ios-simulator` build on an iOS simulator, from one set of files. They own P1's exit journeys (PLAN §7, criteria 1-2), checks that need a gesture, a scroll, the keyboard, a relaunch that keeps the app's data or airplane mode (Android), and regression matrices that repeat a trial N times on two builds, such as #346's fling after the filter sheet (at Maestro's pace~~; row D's own window is the `adb` driver's, below~~). They find controls by identifier (`id:`) first, then by full-string text, copied from `maestro hierarchy`. Tags: `journeys`, `smoke` (two of them) and `regression`. New device journeys are Maestro flows, F6 included: `star_undo` checks that Now shows a saved session happening now under "Your next session"; F6's later session and ended session are still to add.<br>• **`flet test` (F1-F5, `RemoteTester`)** stays, and is not ported: it serves the feed from the laptop and controls it (`SilentFeed` holds a request until the flow lets it hang up, so F1 sees "Updating the schedule…"; Maestro can only point the app at a closed port), it takes its targets from the feed processed by the app's own code (`targets.py`), and a Flutter error such as a RenderFlex overflow fails the run (§5.1 of testing-and-quality.md), which Maestro cannot see. A new `flet test` flow is added only for one of those.<br>• **Neither runs in CI** today: `flet test` waits on S9, and Maestro on an emulator in Actions is a later issue, if it can run there free. Neither checks screen-reader speech (D-241) or performance (D-80).<br>• **What Maestro cannot do here:** it reads no Python state; on Android its reads of the screen make Flutter report a screen reader (F72), so the app runs its screen-reader paths there (the Schedule keeps its Now button, D-204, and by D-184 SnackBars stay until used), and a flow checks them on iOS only (`jump_to_now` checks the button there; no flow checks a SnackBar's timeout); it cannot cut an iOS simulator's network; and about 0.85 s passes between two of its commands on the emulator, so a fling follows a tap no sooner than that: #346's row D lost flings at 0.33 to 0.6 s, and at 0.8 to 1.0 s both `main` and #346's build ran 20 of 20 in full, so the flow guards the fling at Maestro's pace only. ~~Row D's window is reached by `tools/fling_after_sheet.py`, an `adb` driver (Android) that sends the tap and the fling in one shell command and measures the gap from the launcher's touch log; its first runs, at 0.37 and 0.52 s, did not yet tell #346's build from `main` (both lost flings only during load spikes; `maestro/README.md`).~~ ~~Both count~~ The flow counts a trial as full only when it ended within three rows of where one of three control flings (no sheet) ended, read from the first four rows of each, not merely in their slot, which is about 20 rows deep; ~~they check~~ it checks that the controls agree in the same way and that each trial starts on the now slot, and ~~fail~~ fails on a missed tap.<br>• **Identifiers:** the app bar's Search button carries `app.search`, in its icon, as the bottom bar's icons carry theirs (D-166), so the button stays one node named by its tooltip (D-234). The back arrow keeps none: Android flows press the system Back and iOS flows tap the button named "Back" (`maestro/subflows/back.yaml`).<br>• **When they run:** the journeys before merging a PR that changes one (one platform) and at phase exits and Flet pin bumps (both), with D-79's checklist; a regression matrix whenever a PR touches what it guards, on the PR's build and on `main`'s. | Both tools have something the other lacks. Maestro covers #288's gaps (drag, scroll until visible, the keyboard) and runs the packaged release app on both platforms; the hand-driven checks of #14 and #346 could not be re-run. On #348's branch the six journeys passed twice on `flet_probe_api34` and twice on an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5), with the flows as merged. |
| D-467 | **The `adb` driver for #346's row D is deleted; no tool reaches that window** (#906; amends D-252). `tools/fling_after_sheet.py` and `tools/fling_trials.py` go, with their tests and docs. `maestro/regression/fling_after_sheet.yaml` stays and guards the fling at Maestro's pace, 0.8 to 1.0 s after the tap. Git has the driver if U50 (#345) brings anchoring back. | It reproduced a bug in #346, which closed unmerged; D-371 removes the trigger, asking nothing of the list once the sheet has gone. `check.sh` and `gate.sh` did not run it, nothing but its tests imported it, and its recorded runs had not told #346's build from `main` (`maestro/README.md` before #906). |

## J. Build, CI and distribution

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-81 | *(How we get Flet: amended by D-481, PyPI's prereleases and no source checkout.)* *(Branch amended by D-104; the main checkout's setup by D-451.)* Flet version: until 1.1.0 is on PyPI, develop on Flet 1.1 from flet-dev/flet ~~`main`~~ `flet-1.1.0` (D-104) at a pinned commit; then pin the exact release. Bumps are their own PRs. One pin serves development, CI and `flet build`. The store companion app (`flet run --android/--ios`) is built from a released client and lacks 1.1's Dart changes, so until a 1.1 companion is in the stores the on-device dev loop uses `flet debug android` or `flet debug ios` from the source checkout (a packaged debug build, so embedded) or a companion client built from `client/` at the pinned commit (not embedded, like the store companion; D-63). S5 proves the mechanics, including that the companion client and the Python protocol match, and `docs/development.md` records them. | The owner's Xcode 27 cannot build iOS with Flet 1.0.4 (F20). We use 1.1 features (`ThemeSurfaces`, `material_ui` for new extensions), and flet-cli run from a source checkout builds with the matching template and Dart package (F18). |
| D-82 | Python 3.14 (`requires-python = ">=3.14,<3.15"`), managed with uv, with `uv.lock` committed. The project has a `[build-system]` (`uv_build`), so `uv sync` installs `pycon_us` editable from `src/` and the tests, tools and mock import it (D-52's shared signing); `flet build` still copies `src/` and bundles only `[project].dependencies` (F15). | Flet embeds Python 3.14 by default (F19), so development matches the device. |
| D-83 | Versioning: SemVer `0.y.z` until the showcase `1.0.0`. `pycon_us.__version__` must equal `[project].version` (unit test). The build number is the commit count on `main`, passed as `--build-number`. We tag `vX.Y.Z` when a build goes to testers. | `importlib.metadata` cannot see the app in packaged builds (F15). |
| D-84 | *(While the repo is private, every workflow runs only by hand and the weekly feed check is dropped: amended by D-273. The target of 2 minutes: amended by D-482.)* CI has three workflows:<br>• `ci.yml` on every PR and push to `main` (Linux): `uv sync --locked`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `pyright`, `pytest`; target under 2 min.<br>• `feed-check.yml` weekly: our parser and sanity checks on the public feed.<br>• `build-android.yml` on manual dispatch and `v*` tags: uploads a debug-signed APK.<br>iOS builds run locally. | Fast and enough for one or two people. macOS minutes cost 10× on private repos. |
| D-85 | **prov. O-5.** Distribution is internal only: Android by APK sideload and/or Play internal testing on the owner's account, iOS by TestFlight internal testing on the owner's team. No public store listing. | "PyCon" is a PSF trademark, so a public listing would need PSF permission. |
| D-86 | Signing keys, certificates and API keys never enter the repo or CI. `docs/development.md` says where they live and how to build signed artifacts locally. | The repo will become public. |
| D-87 | Licence: Apache-2.0, the same as the Ionic app. A `NOTICE` credits PyCon/pycon-us-mobile for ported logic and reused assets (e.g. floor plans). No CLA or DCO: GitHub's terms make contributions inbound = outbound. | Compatible with our source, and the simplest option. |
| D-176 | **A pub cache per worktree while other builds may run** (#176; supports D-102's parallel agents). serious_python 5.0.0 stages each build's Python app inside its own plugin folder in the pub cache, which every Flutter project on the Mac shares (iOS: `serious_python_darwin`'s `dist_ios/app` and its Swift package's `Resources/app`, bundled by Xcode minutes later; Android: `serious_python_android`'s `src/main/assets/app.zip`, merged later in the same Gradle run). So every `flet build`, `flet debug` and `flet test` that may overlap another build runs with `PUB_CACHE=$PWD/build/pub-cache`, an APFS clone of `~/.pub-cache` made once per worktree (`cp -cR`, only when `build/pub-cache` doesn't exist yet, since `cp -R` nests a second copy in an existing folder), and each build's Python is checked against `build/python-app` (the iOS bundle's `app`, the `app.zip` asset of an APK or AAB, the IPA's app bundle; development.md §5.8). It goes when U28 (#185, flet-dev/serious-python#255) is in the pin. | On 2026-10-12 three parallel iOS builds (#79, #80, #84) shipped another worktree's Python with no error or warning; one crashed on the other worktree's `conference.toml`. Each rebuild with a worktree-local `PUB_CACHE` bundled its own `src/`, on iOS and on Android (`assets/app.zip`). The clone took 6 s for the 1.0 GB cache and used no space beyond metadata, where an empty private cache cost a 9-10 minute first build; a check alone finds the problem only after the build. |
| D-219 | *(Ended by D-481: a wheel from PyPI builds nothing in a checkout. It applies again only on §2.2's temporary source route.)* *(The main checkout's setup and the clones' git rule amended by D-451.)* **A Flet checkout per worktree** (#208; supports D-102's parallel agents, alongside D-176). Every worktree of this repo other than the main checkout has its own `.flet-src`: an APFS clone (`cp -cR`) of the main checkout's `.flet-src`, without the clone's `sdk/python/packages/flet/build` folder, followed by `uv sync --locked`; never a link to it (development.md §5.8). The main checkout's `.flet-src` stays the read-only source of truth at the pin: nobody edits it or a clone, a clone takes no git command that writes (from the owner's `git worktree` setup its `.git` file names the same Flet worktree), a pin bump moves the main checkout and every worktree replaces its clone, and a clone's pin check also compares its files (`git --no-optional-locks diff --quiet HEAD`). It goes with the source pin, when 1.1.0 is on PyPI (D-81). | `flet build` pip-installs `flet` from `.flet-src/sdk/python/packages/flet` (`[tool.flet.dev_packages]`), and pip builds a local folder in place, so linked worktrees shared setuptools' `build/` there: overlapping builds failed with `[Errno 2]`, `[Errno 66]` and `[Errno 17]` (#94, #197, #245), and an interrupted build's leftover failed every later build. Four parallel `pip wheel` runs in one folder failed 12 of 20 times with those three errors; in four clones, 0 of 20. With clones, two worktrees' `flet build ios-simulator` started in the same second built their wheels in step and both passed, each bundle holding its own app; an APK beside an iOS build passed too. A leftover `build/lib` file also ships in the wheel. A link also pointed `uv sync`'s editable installs (so `uv run flet`) at the main checkout. The clone took 2 s for 178 MB and needs no prefix on each command; building out of tree would need a setuptools config through `DIST_EXTRA_CONFIG` on every command (pip has no out-of-tree option: 26.2 rejects `--use-deprecated=out-of-tree-build`), and a forgotten prefix races again. |
| D-472 | **Android's Auto Backup takes only the SharedPreferences file; the secure-storage files and the outbox stay out** (settles #818 under D-101, option 3 built as an allowlist; notes D-49, D-108, D-120 and D-414).<br>• **The rule:** `allowBackup="true"` is said outright, with `fullBackupContent` (Android 11 and older) and `dataExtractionRules` (12 and newer, for a cloud backup and a phone-to-phone transfer alike) pointing at two files that each include one path, `sharedpref` `FlutterSharedPreferences.xml`. Favourites, settings and the Developer options come back on a new phone; nothing else does.<br>• **An allowlist, not excludes:** `flutter_secure_storage` keeps three files (`FlutterSecureStorage.xml`, `FlutterSecureKeyStorage.xml` and `FlutterSecureStorageConfiguration:FlutterSecureStorage.xml`), the outbox is `outbox-<year>.db` with its `-wal` and `-shm`, and backup rules take no wildcards, so a list of excludes would need a new line every year and for every file a plugin adds. An allowlist also keeps `files/` (24 MB, mostly the Python Flet unpacks into `files/flet/`) out of a cloud backup, whose cap is 25 MB: the old build's whole backup was 24.3 MB, and one more file would have made Auto Backup fail for the app.<br>• **What a restore does:** the restored preferences hold `pycon.account` and the staff keys but no credentials, so `Account.restore()` takes its existing partial-sign-in path ("Clearing a partial sign-in: signed out", D-120) and removes the account; the staff keys left over are cleared by the next sign-in, which is always a switch from no one (`sign_in()`, D-414). No outbox comes back, so no scan is sent twice. Reminders are applied from `pycon.reminders` at every start, and the plugin's own preference files are not restored, since alarms are not. The notification permission is not restored either, though `pycon.reminders` keeps `primer="accepted"` (`ui/reminder_primer.py`): a user restored onto a new phone gets no first-star primer, and turns reminders on in Settings › Reminders, where the in-app Banner points meanwhile. Developer options come back too, a faked clock or feed URL included, which only affects someone who unlocked them.<br>• **Where:** `[tool.flet.android]` in `pyproject.toml` (`res_dir`, `manifest_application`, both in the pin's template and CLI) and `android_res/xml/`; no patch to Flet. `tests/test_android_backup_rules.py` pins the files and the manifest keys; `docs/development.md` §8.1 says what is backed up and how to run the restore. **A Flet pin bump re-runs that restore** (§2.2 step 4): a Flet that moved to `SharedPreferencesAsync` would keep the preferences in `files/datastore/`, and the allowlist would then back up nothing, which no unit test can see.<br>• **Rejected:** *Default* (a restore logged `InvalidKeyException: Failed to unwrap key`, and brought back two queued scans: rows are sent only for the signed-in owner (`services/sync.py`), so nothing is misattributed, but a backup can be a day old, so rows already sent come back pending, and a lead row has no idempotency key (`core/outbox.py`: only door rows have one), so the sponsor's next sign-in on the new phone would send it again; the cost of leaving the outbox out is that scans not yet sent when a phone is lost are lost, which a daily backup could never have kept safe either, D-56's promise holding only while the phone does); *`allowBackup="false"`* (a new phone would lose the favourites and settings; the 25 MB cap argued for it, but the allowlist meets the cap too). iOS is another question (its Keychain item is `FIRST_UNLOCK_THIS_DEVICE`, D-49).<br>• **Not run:** the Android 11 and older file (`fullBackupContent`): no such emulator image here, and Android 12 and newer ignores it when `dataExtractionRules` is present. It holds the same include as the other. | The issue's recommendation, built so it cannot rot. On an Android 16 emulator (`flet_probe_api36`, release arm64 APKs, the old one built at `a7bfe3a7`; local transport, `bmgr`): with the old build a backup was 24,303,104 bytes, held the three secure-storage files, the notification plugin's two preference files, `files/data/outbox-2026.db` with its `-wal` and `-shm`, the feed cache and `files/flet/`; after `uninstall`, `install` and `bmgr restore`, the app logged `InvalidKeyException: Failed to unwrap key` twice, "Clearing a partial sign-in: signed out", and held both queued scans (owner `sponsor@example.com`, `pending`). With this build the backup was 4,096 bytes holding `_manifest` and `sp/FlutterSharedPreferences.xml`; the restore logged no unwrap failure, only the partial sign-in; the app opened on Now, signed out, with the favourite in My schedule and no outbox file, and the mock saw no request. |

## K. Process and GitHub workflow

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-88 | One private GitHub repo, made public when the owner is satisfied (P4). Issues and PRs are the record of work. `main` stays green. Protect it with rulesets if the plan supports them for private repos; otherwise by convention until the repo is public. | GitHub Free does not enforce branch protection on private repos. |
| D-89 | Milestones are the phases: `P0 Foundations`, `P1 Attendee core`, `P2 Conference companion`, `P3 Accounts & staff tools`, `P4 Showcase`. Each milestone description carries the phase goal and exit criteria from PLAN.md. One pinned status issue per phase is updated weekly: done, next, blocked, decisions, numbers. | Progress is visible in one place. |
| D-90 | Labels form a fixed set:<br>• Type: `feature`, `bug`, `design`, `docs`, `test`, `chore`, `ci`, `refactor`, `epic`, `upstream` (a Flet gap written up here for the owner to take upstream, D-212).<br>• Area: `area:shell`, `area:theme`, `area:data`, `area:schedule`, `area:speakers`, `area:rooms-maps`, `area:sponsors`, `area:info`, `area:notifications`, `area:account`, `area:staff`, `area:build`, `area:quality`.<br>• Status: `blocked`, `needs-decision`, `good first issue`.<br>There are no phase or priority labels. | A small, stable vocabulary; milestones carry the phase. |
| D-91 | Every change starts from an issue, except trivial chores. An issue describes a user journey or a mechanism (not one feature id) and lists the feature ids, D-ids, acceptance criteria and the device evidence needed. An epic (`epic`) groups sub-issues. Spikes are issues titled `Spike: <question>`, labelled `test` and time-boxed; they close with a verdict in `docs/spikes/`. | Traceable without 263 tiny tickets. |
| D-92 | Pull requests:<br>• one issue per PR (`Closes #N`), on a short-lived branch `<type>/<issue>-<slug>`;<br>• open as a draft early, and aim for about 400 changed lines or fewer, excluding fixtures;<br>• the body says what and why; how it was tested (commands, devices, Flet pin); screenshots for UI changes (Android and iOS, light and dark); docs and decisions updated; follow-ups;<br>• dependency and Flet bumps are separate PRs.<br>~~The owner reviews and squash-merges; agents never merge their own PRs.~~ Merging: superseded by D-100. | Reviewable, traceable and easy to revert. |
| D-93 | Definition of done:<br>• acceptance criteria met;<br>• tests and linters green;<br>• UI checked on Android and iOS, with screenshots;<br>• accessibility checked (labels, contrast, 200 % text, touch targets);<br>• docs and this record updated in the same PR;<br>• follow-ups filed. | One bar for humans and agents. |
| D-94 | A GitHub Project, "PyCon US Flet", managed with `gh project`, gives a board view of issues and PRs (Todo, In progress, In review, Done). The issues stay the source of truth. | A glance view; optional. |
| D-95 | *(Trigger and process amended by D-100.)* For architecture changes and large PRs, a second opinion from another model (Claude Fable or Codex, run read-only) is summarised in a PR comment: what it flagged and what changed. | The owner asked for second opinions; summaries rather than transcripts. |
| D-96 | The documentation set:<br>• `PLAN.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`.<br>• `docs/`: `decisions.md`, `ux.md`, `architecture.md`, `feature-matrix.md`, `flet-gaps-and-extensions.md`, `development.md`, `testing-and-quality.md`, `roadmap.md` and `spikes/`. `development.md` covers the toolchain, dev loop, mock server, builds, CI, signing and releases, and replaces v1's build-and-release doc. `testing-and-quality.md` is the test strategy (D-76..D-80). `roadmap.md` breaks the phases into epics and issues and replaces v1's backlog; GitHub issues are the live task list.<br>• `docs/research/` stays as frozen input.<br>• The v1 docs are not kept in the tree: they stay in git history (the repository's first commit).<br>Docs change in the same PR as the behaviour they describe. | Fewer docs, kept current, with one place per topic. |
| D-100 | *(While the repo is private, condition 1's checks are the merge gate's: amended by D-273. A capped reviewer and who may review, in condition 3: amended by D-458.)* **Agents merge their own PRs** (supersedes the merging clause of D-92 and amends D-95; owner's direction, 2026-10-11). An agent squash-merges (`gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`) only when all hold:<br>1. checks pass on the PR's latest commit (the required checks are the `ci.yml` jobs listed in `docs/development.md`; missing, pending, skipped or cancelled checks don't count; before CI exists, the applicable local checks are run and listed in the PR);<br>2. the definition of done (D-93) is met and the agent has re-read the final diff;<br>3. a second opinion, when required, is done: required for PRs over 400 changed lines (excluding lockfiles, fixtures, generated files and data snapshots), architecture changes (module structure, state, routing, data core, signing, outbox, build or CI) and edits to this record. The reviewer is a different model from the author (~~Claude Fable~~ a Claude model, or Codex read-only) and reviews the final diff. Each finding is fixed or rejected with a recorded reason and evidence; a material disagreement (correctness, data loss, security) goes to the other reviewer model, a product question to `needs-decision`;<br>4. an owner request to stop or change something has been acted on, and every owner comment has a reply;<br>5. no unsettled `needs-decision` question affects the PR.<br>The owner watches, may revert at any time, and is never a blocker. Feedback after a merge becomes a new issue or a revert PR. | The project is driven by agents; waiting for a human would stall it, while these checks keep `main` green and every change traceable and reversible. |
| D-273 | *(The checks gain the pre-commit hooks: D-480.)* **While the repo is private, local checks replace GitHub Actions: a pre-push hook runs the fast checks, and the merge gate runs the full checks on the PRs merged onto the latest `main`** (owner's direction, 2026-10-20; amends D-84, D-100 condition 1, D-103 and the feed check's schedule).<br>• **The checks.** `tools/check.sh` runs `ci.yml`'s steps in their order: `uv sync --locked`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `pyright`, `pytest --hypothesis-profile=ci`; `--fast` runs every step but pytest.<br>• **Three layers.** (1) Before every push, the pre-push hook (`.githooks/pre-push`, turned on once per clone with `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) runs `tools/check.sh --fast`; a push that changes only Markdown skips it. (2) Before marking a PR ready, its author runs the full `tools/check.sh` and says so in the PR. (3) Before every merge, the gate `tools/gate.sh <pr> [<pr>...]` tests what will land: the latest `main` with the PR merged onto it, or a batch of PRs merged onto it in their merge order, in a scratch worktree. It runs the full checks there and, when they pass, posts on each PR the commits and the tree it checked. Its result holds for the PR's head and the `main` it names: after a new push to the PR, or once `main` has moved, it runs again. After a batch is merged, `main`'s tree must equal the gated tree; otherwise the full checks run again on `main`.<br>• **GitHub Actions run only by hand.** `ci.yml`, `feed-check.yml` and `build-android.yml` keep their jobs, and `workflow_dispatch` is their only trigger (`gh workflow run <file> --ref <branch>`). The weekly feed check is dropped: run `uv run python -m tools.check_feed` locally when needed. The quick-look APK is built locally (development.md §8.1) or by a manual run; a `v*` tag no longer builds it.<br>• **Copilot reviews are paused**: they use Actions minutes too, and their quota is used up.<br>• **No Linux VM until P4** (the owner's call). Until then the checks run only on macOS, and a failure that only Linux shows would surface when CI comes back. Spike S9 (#363, D-78), which needs GitHub's Linux runners, waits until then too.<br>• **When the repo goes public (P4)**, Actions are free for it: the PR that makes it public brings back `ci.yml`'s PR and `main` triggers (and with them D-100's `ci.yml` checks), the feed check's weekly schedule, `build-android.yml`'s `v*` tags and Copilot reviews. | By 2026-10-20 the month had used about 1,875 of the 2,000 Linux minutes that a private repo on GitHub Free includes, almost all of them `ci.yml` runs on every PR push and every push to `main`. On the owner's Mac the fast checks take about 5 s and the full ones about 1.5 minutes (3,061 tests). The gate checks the tree that lands, where a `pull_request` run checked the PR's merge with `main` as it was at the push, so `main` stays green (D-88) without a runner. |
| D-458 | **When the second opinion's reviewer is capped, the agent merges without it and owes the review** (#821; the owner's direction, 2026-10-20; amends D-100 condition 3).<br>• **The rule:** the agent re-reads the final diff, merges after the gate (D-273), says so in the PR, and runs the review later on what is current then, not on the old diff. D-100's other conditions still hold.<br>• **Where an owed review is tracked:** on the merged PR, in a signed comment that starts `Second opinion owed` and names the capped reviewer; `gh pr list --state merged --search 'in:comments "Second opinion owed"'` lists them. When the review has run, a signed comment there names the reviewer and the result, or the later PR that replaced the code. Each finding becomes an issue or is rejected with evidence.<br>• **Who may review:** on 2026-11-02 the owner welcomed Claude models or Codex as reviewers while their limit allows. The reviewer is still a different model from the author. | The owner, 2026-10-20: "If your PR looks good to you, feel free to go ahead and merge it... We should be able to iterate a little faster. I trust you. When Codex usage is back you could then ping it for the work you're on at that moment." The rule lived only in an agent's private memory, while D-100 read as if a second opinion always comes first; of the last 44 merged PRs, 12 of the 34 that needed one name no reviewer in their text (#821). |
| D-101 | *(Pre-authorised work narrowed to this repo by D-212.)* **`needs-decision` without blocking.** The issue states the options and a recommendation. **Reversible** choices (most code, UX and process details) are settled by the agent: the question is settled once the decision is posted on the issue; it is then recorded as a new D-number in a PR, noted in the phase status issue, and the label is removed; the owner can override later. **Owner-only** choices wait for the owner: app identity and bundle id (O-4); making anything public (the repo, a store listing, builds for people outside the team); accounts or anything that costs money; contacting people outside this work (the PSF, social media, blogs); anything that would lose data or need someone else's help to undo; and the open decisions marked owner-only. Routine work is pre-authorised: issues, PRs and comments in this repo, ~~and the flet-dev issues and PRs the upstream track (D-97) needs~~ and nowhere else (D-212). | Keeps work moving while the owner keeps control of what can't easily be undone. |

Packaged builds there are `flet build apk` or `flet debug android` on the emulator, and `flet build ios-simulator` or `flet debug ios` on the simulator (debug mode: Flutter has no release mode for simulators).<br>• The **iPhone** is used only for checks that need hardware or a release build: release-build performance numbers, camera scanning (S8, E1), notification delivery (S6, the P2 reminder criterion), TestFlight installs (S5, builds for testers), VoiceOver on the device, and at phase exits the release build's acceptance journeys that the exit criteria name on it (install over the previous build, offline start, the core journeys), since a simulator only runs debug builds.<br>• Android checks that would want hardware (a locked screen and a reboot for reminders, exact alarms denied, the camera through the emulator's virtual scene, TalkBack, low memory through a small-RAM AVD) run on the emulator, and their results say so; what an emulator cannot show (the torch, lighting, real low-RAM devices) waits for a borrowed phone.<br>• Android performance numbers from the emulator are a **relative regression signal** only (the same emulator image and host, compared build to build), never held against D-80's targets. Absolute Android numbers wait for a borrowed mid-range Android phone, a P4 risk (PLAN §11). The targets themselves do not change. | These are the devices we have. Emulators and simulators are reproducible, cover most behaviour and can be driven by agents; the iPhone is kept for what they cannot show. An emulator's timings depend on the host machine, so they reveal regressions, not real-device speed. |

| D-481 | **Flet comes from PyPI at one exact prerelease; no checkout of Flet is needed** (#1027; the owner's direction, 2026-11-04: go public on the latest Flet, and move to the prerelease if it holds; amends D-81, ends D-104, D-219 and D-451; supersedes `.flet-ref`). Every Flet package in `pyproject.toml` is pinned `==` to one version, `1.1.0.dev4` today, which is the commit we had pinned (`a9ef26fdb`, tag `v1.1.0.dev4`), and `tests/test_bundled_dependencies.py` fails if they disagree. development.md §2.2 has the table of what comes from where and the routes: to `dev5`, to `1.1.0`, and for a fix that isn't released (a Dart override by commit, or the old source pin as a temporary route).<br>• **The owner's question, Dart prereleases on pub.dev:** not a problem. The release's template names the Dart `flet` package by its exact commit (`git`, `ref: a9ef26fdb…`) under `dependencies` and `dependency_overrides`, and the override beats the `flet: path: ../../../../../../../packages/flet` in the wheels' `flutter/<extension>/pubspec.yaml`, as in a stable release: 1.0.5's wheels hold that path, and its template has `flet: 1.0.5` under both keys.<br>• **What we gained:** no clone per worktree (331 MB each, a step to keep right after every bump, and D-219's class of `[Errno 17]`, `[Errno 66]` and stale-`build/lib` failures, since a wheel builds nothing); CI loses its Flet checkout steps; `flet build` no longer passes `--no-cache-dir`, so a repeat APK took 21 s; `setup_worktree.sh` loses its clone logic; `flet --version` and About this app say `1.1.0.dev4`; `flet-web`'s wheel holds its web client, and the release holds the desktop client.<br>• **Every disadvantage we found:** (1) A build needs `git` and github.com for the Dart package, and each pub cache without them holds a mirror of Flet's repository (250 MB) and a checkout (180 MB), so the disk gain is smaller than the clone's size; an APFS clone of a pub cache that has them shares their blocks. (2) A build needs the release's template from GitHub, once per machine, and `flet test` pulls `flet_integration_test` by tag: deleting a prerelease or moving its tag would break or change us, which a SHA could not; both are the owner's own. (3) We can land only on what's released: a commit between prereleases needs a temporary route, where a bump used to be one SHA. (4) The two tests that mirror Flet's Dart source (`test_theme.py`, `test_high_contrast.py`) run only where a build has fetched it, so the gate skips them as CI always did; a bump's step 5 runs them. (5) U286: two first builds of a version at once can fail on the template's download, which a source checkout never reached; a second try works. (6) `.venv` grows by 94 MB, `flet-web`'s client, from uv's cache. (7) Hot reload's companion client still needs a Flet clone, optional now (§5.2), and the store's app still waits for 1.1. It renders from the wheels, and a reload strands it on the boot spinner just as it does from a source pin, so that isn't the prerelease's doing.<br>• **What this doesn't settle:** #1006 (a build quietly takes another Flutter patch from `PATH`) and #702 (the bundled CPython floats with `serious_python_*`) behave the same from a wheel: `flet.version.flutter_version` plays `.fvmrc`'s part, and the template pins the same `serious_python`.<br>• **Still needed:** a pub cache per worktree (D-176): at serious_python 6.1.3 the app is still staged in the plugin's folder (the build log's `SPM: staging ios via …/serious_python_darwin-6.1.3/darwin`). | A source pin was the only way to Flet 1.1 until a prerelease held everything we need; now it's a cost with no benefit. Proof on 2026-11-04: a release APK and an iOS simulator build from an empty pub cache with no `.flet-src`, in 184 s and 180 s, beside a source-pin build. The APKs have the same 604 entries and a byte-identical manifest; Flutter 3.47.9 in both; `pubspec.lock` differs only in `flet`'s source; the Dart package, the wheels' Python (but for `version.py`) and the template (but for `pubspec.yaml`'s `flet`) match the commit; bundled Python holds the same 16 packages at the same versions. Sizes: 61,146,527 against 61,150,827 bytes on Android, 296,436 against 296,332 KB on iOS. Nine Maestro journeys passed on both platforms (`drill_back`, `star_undo`, `reminder_primer`, `reminder_settings`, `reminder_banner`, `deep_links`, `about_app`, `coc_capture`, `lead_capture`). `flet test android` fetched `flet_integration_test` by git at the tag, resolved to `a9ef26fdb`; of F1-F5, four passed, and the first flow's first launch hit its 180 s wait on a busy host, then passed twice on rerun. `build-android.yml` passed on a clean runner in 14 min 36 s. |
| D-482 | **`ci.yml` runs the tests in parallel with `pytest -n auto`, and its target is under 5 minutes, not 2** (#1039; amends D-84's target and §12 of testing-and-quality.md).<br>• **Where the time goes.** On the private repo's 2-vCPU runner the test step is 90% of the job. No test or file is to blame: the durations of 9,170 tests sum to 232 s on the Mac, the largest file takes 17 s and the slowest test under 3 s, and Hypothesis is 14 small files. uv's install takes about 5 s and pyright about 30 s. So there's nothing to trim, and parallel workers are the lever.<br>• **The same flag in `tools/check.sh`,** so the gate and CI run alike; `tests/test_ci_matches_check.py` keeps the two lists equal. Not in `addopts`: a plain `uv run pytest` stays serial for a debugger.<br>• **The target** is a job under 5 minutes on 2 vCPUs. Two minutes was set for a suite of a few hundred tests. Locally pytest took 67 s against about 6 minutes, on a Mac under a load of 10, and three parallel runs there and two on the runner found no test that depends on another's process. Cutting tests or examples to reach 2 minutes would trade coverage for a number; 5 minutes per PR is free on a public repo. |

| D-112 | *(The baseline as a precondition replaced by D-453: its numbers stay as S1's reading.)* **Android regression baseline and how to measure it** (records for D-80 and D-102; S1). Method:<br>• `am start -W`'s `TotalTime` ends at Flutter's first frame, which is Flet's boot screen. So the app logs a line once the cached content has rendered: an `await` on a SharedPreferences call made after the render. That reply does not wait for a frame, so the line is a proxy; screen recordings in S1 showed it 0.2-0.3 s **after** the pixels, never before. Numbers held against D-80 come from screen recordings (`testing-and-quality.md` §8).<br>• Cold start = that line's timestamp minus logcat's `ActivityTaskManager: START u0` line, after `am force-stop`; ten runs on a quiet host, median and range.<br>Baseline on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (M4 host, Flet `15854a0ac`, release arm64 APK, `.pyc` on):<br>• cold start to cached content 760 ms (741-919);<br>• `TotalTime` 426 ms (400-507);<br>• Python start → first frame 220 ms (202-277);<br>• first launch after a fresh install: `TotalTime` 441 ms, first frame 770 ms.<br>The iPhone's release numbers (#121) are held against D-80's targets; the targets stay until then. | A busy host moved the medians by about 25 % (round a in the S1 verdict), more than the 20 % regression threshold in `testing-and-quality.md` §8, so the method demands a quiet host and repeated runs. The content line measures what users wait for; `TotalTime` misses about 330 ms of it. |
| D-159 | *(The emulator baseline read through D-453; the star tap's budget stays.)* **Interaction latency: how it is measured, and the emulator baseline** (records for D-80 and D-102; S2). Method:<br>• in the app, each handler logs its start, an effect logs when the re-render has been sent ("handler → patch", all of Python's work), then a SharedPreferences round trip (D-112's proxy);<br>• on screen, a recording: a keystroke from the frame that shows the typed character to the list's change; a day switch from the tab bar's first change; a star tap against its log line, the video's clock mapped onto the device clock by one keystroke at the start of the recording (an instant tap draws nothing before the star changes);<br>• frames from `dumpsys SurfaceFlinger --latency` for the app's `SurfaceView`: every interval within 1.5 s of the gesture's first frame, janky over 25 ms;<br>• `docs/spikes/02-day-list/drive_android.py` runs all of it.<br>Baseline on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (M4 host, Flet `15854a0ac`, release arm64 APK), the busiest day with D-153's memoised path: median handler → patch 3 ms for 12 star taps and for 12 keystrokes (single keystrokes that bring filtered rows back reach 17-33 ms), measured on lazy lists (`tree/lazy/all`; Python's share does not depend on the list, and `tree/cache/all` read the same), the star in the same frame as a local change; on a fully built list, as today's is (D-154), a filter keystroke's list change shows 0-83 ms after the key against 0-16 ms lazy (`tree/cache/all`), so on-screen numbers for today's list are compared with that variant; day switch 2 ms of Python; flings 0-4 of about 900 frames over 25 ms; cold start to the cached content 886 ms (856-958) with D-153..D-156 (`tree/today/deferred`, D-112's method; windows differ by about ±70 ms, D-156); `feed.process` 66 ms. A **median** handler → patch over one frame (16 ms) for the star taps or the keystrokes on the busiest day is a regression to file. The iPhone's release numbers (#121) are held against D-80's targets. | The emulator is a relative signal (D-102), and Python's own work is what the app controls; the method needs no Flutter changes. During S2 the emulator twice ran 3-4.4× slower while the host's user was away, its threads at background priority, so `testing-and-quality.md` §8 checks the processing time against the last run before measuring. |
| D-453 | *(The cold start's ratio to compare with named by D-474.)* **An Android emulator run is calibrated in its own window against S2's reproducer at the current pin, not against fixed numbers** (settles #339 under D-101; amends D-112, and how D-110 and D-159 read their baselines; `testing-and-quality.md` §8, §8.1).<br>• **The calibration:** on one boot of `pycon_pixel6a_api36`, 10 cold starts of the reproducer (`docs/spikes/02-day-list`, variant `tree/today/deferred`, a release arm64 APK built at the same pin in a throwaway copy, §8), then the app's run, then 5 more of the reproducer. The window counts when the reproducer's two medians agree within about 10 %. Its absolute number is no condition: the old check, `TotalTime` near 426 ms and processing near 66 ms, goes.<br>• **Nothing polls during a start:** logcat is read once, 6 s after `am start -W`, for both builds, since polling, as `drive_android.py` does, added about 70 ms to the reproducer and 30 ms to the app in the same window.<br>• **What is compared:** the app against the reproducer of its own window, as a ratio, with §8's 20 % threshold on the ratio. Each calibration also records the reproducer's own medians, because a ratio is blind to what both builds share, such as a Flet regression: a rise there across calibrations on one emulator is looked at on its own. D-159's budget stays absolute (a median star tap within 16.7 ms), and flings are read against the reproducer's.<br>• **First reading** (2026-11-02, `1f98738`, pin `22a6dcb16`, M4 host, emulator 37.2.12.0; medians): reproducer `TotalTime` 799 ms (765-820), cold start 1,522 ms (1,470-1,582), processing 110 ms, star tap 4 ms, flings 63.0 % of 557 frames over 25 ms; app 772 ms (737-802), 2,266 ms (2,123-2,351), 80 ms, 11 ms, 63.5 % of 584. So the ratios are 0.97 for `TotalTime` and 1.49 for the cold start, which is #860.<br>• **D-112's and D-159's numbers** stay as what S1 and S2 read at `15854a0ac`. D-110's trigger moves too: a growth over 100 ms in the reproducer's `py-start` → first `patched` between calibrations (583 ms here, on S2's reproducer, not S1's).<br>• **Not settled:** whether the pin or the emulator moved the baseline, which would take a reproducer built at `15854a0ac`; the calibration moves with both. The reproducer also takes today's `src/pycon_us`, so its numbers follow the feed, theme and config code as well. | Five tries on #339 failed D-112's precondition, the last on a host with no other work, and it could not tell a slow emulator from a grown app. Measured in one window, the reproducer's own `TotalTime` (799 ms) is at or above the app's (772 ms), and its flings match the app's, so neither gap to the old numbers is the app's and a quieter host would not close them. Whatever a reproducer in the same window also shows is not the app's; what it does not show is. |
| D-474 | **The app's cold start reads about 1.4× S2's reproducer on the content line and 1.15× on the screen; the gap is the shell's cost, and 1.4× is the ratio later runs compare with** (settles #860 under D-101; `testing-and-quality.md` §8, §8.1).<br>• **What is compared from now on:** D-453's measure stays, the content line over the reproducer's first `acked`, with 1.4 as its baseline: 1.40 in the calibrated window of 2026-11-03 (`b343fed8`, pin `22a6dcb16`; reproducer 1,574 and 1,562 ms, app 2,207 ms), 1.40-1.46 in two later pairs on the same boot. §8's 20 % applies on top, so a content-line ratio above about 1.7 gets a look, and a recording then says whether the screen moved: the line also moves with the second render alone.<br>• **What the user sees:** the first frame with rows, on a recording, is 1,937 ms (and 1,954 ms) for the app against 1,680 and 1,693 ms for the reproducer, so 1.15×, about +250 ms. That is net of the reproducer's slower launch, so it is not the shell's whole cost: from Python's first line to the first round trip back, the app takes 1,020 ms and the reproducer 595 ms, +425 ms.<br>• **Why the two differ:** the content line returns after the other days' render (D-156), which holds Python's event loop for about 340 ms; the reproducer's first `acked` comes before its own. The line stays where it is, since moving it would make no screen faster and would break the comparison with earlier runs.<br>• **No app change.** Lazy screens are dropped: all of `routes.py`'s imports beyond the Schedule are 92 ms, about 4 % at most, a normal start needs Now's among them, and a later tap would pay for the rest, so D-110 stands. The start's reads other than the feed are 76 ms, and the first render's 177 ms is mostly Flet's walk over new controls.<br>• **Flet:** that walk is U282 (#966), a modest general gain that did not move the first rows on this emulator (1,954 ms with and without it), so nothing here waits on it.<br>• **Not settled:** why the reproducer's launch is slower before any of its Python runs (`TotalTime` 825 against 664 ms, Python's first line 979 against 794 ms), and the iPhone's numbers, which D-80's targets are held against (#121). | #860 asked for a reduction or a recorded cost. A split on one boot found no single part large enough to fix simply: the app's imports are 451 ms against the reproducer's 225 ms, across every module the shell needs, and the rest is spread thinly. Pixels show about 250 ms on screen and the logs about 425 ms of Python and Flutter work, not the 630 ms the line suggests. Keeping the line and naming its ratio costs no code, and a later run can still see the app grow. |

The role resolves against the seeded, neutralised scheme (Flet parses the style before applying `color_scheme`), so a scheme that overrides `surface_container` (high contrast, P2) must check that the bar still matches. | Below Android 15 the app is not drawn under the navigation bar (Android enforces edge-to-edge only from API 35), so a transparent bar shows the window background, which a dark-mode switch while the app runs leaves in the old mode (the activity is not recreated, F26). | D-114 | **HTML → Markdown converter scope** (records D-44's details and corrects architecture §7.7; S3, 2026-10-12). `core/html_md.py` parses a value with `html.parser` into a small tree and renders it:<br>• **Tags** (the 2025 and 2026 census): `p`; `br` → hard break `"  \n"`; `hr` → `---`; `h1`..`h6` → `#`..`######`; `ul`, `ol`, `li` → `- ` / `N. `, with continuation lines indented by the marker's width (three spaces under `1. `, not two); `blockquote` → `> `; `strong`/`b`, `em`/`i`, `s`/`del`/`strike`, `code` → `**x**`, `*x*`, `~~x~~`, a code span; `a` → `[text](url)`. Edge whitespace stays outside emphasis and link markup. A `br` inside a heading becomes a space (Markdown headings are one line).<br>• **Emphasis that cannot flank:** when CommonMark's flanking rules would leave a marker literal (emphasis that starts or ends with punctuation and touches a letter outside, e.g. `a<em>(x)</em>b`), the markers are dropped and the text shows unemphasised. Literal punctuation flanks the same way as a character reference, so this is a Markdown limit, not an escaping one; the 2025 and 2026 feeds have no such case.<br>• **Any other tag is unwrapped** (its children take its place, so its text stays where it was); `script` and `style` are dropped with their text.<br>• **Whitespace** follows HTML: runs of whitespace, newlines included, collapse to one space; only `br` breaks a line.<br>• **A value with no tag is plain text** (15 of the 24 2026 sprint descriptions): entities are decoded, blank lines separate paragraphs, a single newline is a hard break.<br>• **Links:** the `href` is joined to the year's site URL (`urljoin`); only `http`, `https` and `mailto` become links (anything else stays text); the destination is percent-encoded (spaces, brackets, parentheses). A bare `http(s)` URL in text becomes an explicit link labelled with the URL, without trailing sentence punctuation (`.,;:!?` and closing quotes or brackets); inside a link's text, URLs stay text. `target`, `rel`, `class` and `style` are ignored.<br>• Slot names are not converted: the name rules (DATA-08) also strip tags.<br>P0-15's tests take the 22 feed samples of `docs/spikes/03-html-markdown/census.py` (selected from the feed fixtures by year, field and a unique substring) plus its synthetic line as inputs, and an invariant over both fixtures checks that no character is lost or added (whitespace aside). That check is static: a marker the renderer would show literally is caught only by the named tests and the device check. | S3 converted all 1,389 values of the 19 HTML fields without losing or adding a character and rendered 24 samples correctly on the Android 16 emulator. The census has 17 tags; only `span` (4 uses, a font-size style) is unwrapped. Collapsing newlines matches us.pycon.org and the Ionic app and avoids ragged hard-wrapped prose; plain-text paragraphs were lost in Ionic. |
| D-115 | **Converted text is escaped with numeric character references** (S3). In text (never in code spans), `\`, `` ` ``, `*`, `_`, `[`, `]`, `(`, `<`, `>`, `\|`, `~`, `$`, `&` and `:` become `&#NN;`, and so does a block marker at the start of a line (`#`, `>`, `-`, `+`, `=`, or the `.`/`)` after digits). Code spans are emitted raw, fenced with one backtick more than the longest run inside. Bare URLs become explicit links (D-114), so no text is left raw for GFM's autolinks. Backslash escapes are not used. | Flet's `Markdown` always adds LaTeX syntax (F27): on the emulator `5$ or 10$`, `( the notes )` and `[ the FAQ ]` rendered as math, `~y~` as strike-through and `:smile:` as an emoji; with references every one rendered literally. Backslash escapes collide with the `\(`/`\[` LaTeX openers. The 2026 job listings already carry `$` amounts. |
| D-116 | *(Sheet constant, fonts and decoration colours amended by D-152; the rule's style, the heading sizes (18/16/16/16) and the screen-reader check by D-197.)* **How converted text is rendered** (records for SESS-06, SPKR-03, DATA-23 and INFO-02; S3). Every feed text uses `ft.Markdown(value, selectable=True, extension_set=ft.MarkdownExtensionSet.GITHUB_WEB, on_tap_link=..., md_style_sheet=<the app's sheet>)`. `theme.py` builds ~~the one sheet for each colour scheme~~ one sheet, `MARKDOWN_STYLE`, for every scheme (D-152), and every text style in it names its colour (F28): links `PRIMARY` and underlined; body 16 sp at line height 1.5 in `ON_SURFACE`; headings 22/18/16/16 sp (h1..h4) at weight 500, so a description's heading never outranks the screen title; code spans on `SURFACE_CONTAINER_HIGHEST`; block quotes on the same with an `OUTLINE` bar. Markdown links stay inline (no `LinkTile` list): on Android each is its own focusable, clickable node named by its text; #91 checks iOS and #109 checks what TalkBack and VoiceOver call them, and a `LinkTile` list comes back only if either fails. | Flet's default sheet colours links `Colors.blue` with no underline (about 3.1:1 on the light surface, and colour as the only cue, against D-33) and starts headings at `headlineSmall`. A sheet without explicit colours rendered black body text on the dark surface. With the sheet above, light, dark and 200 % text read well on the emulator, and `on_tap_link` still fires with `selectable=True`. |
| D-117 | **E5 is cut; U21 and U22 are later candidates** (amends D-98; S3). The HTML control (E5) is not needed: the D-114 converter covers both feeds. U21: make `ft.Markdown`'s LaTeX syntax opt-in (an extension flag), so Markdown text and open-space descriptions need no LaTeX escaping. U22: let a partial `MarkdownStyleSheet` merge with the theme's defaults and derive link and block-quote colours from the colour scheme. Until they land, D-115 and D-116 are the interims; removing them is its own PR after a pin bump (D-64). | Two years of feeds use no tag the converter cannot express (no tables, images or preformatted blocks). Both upstream items are small changes to `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/markdown.dart` and `utils/markdown.dart` that help every Flet app. |
| D-152 | **The Markdown style sheet names fonts and decoration colours** (amends D-116; #71). `MARKDOWN_STYLE` in `theme.py` is one constant for every scheme: its colours are roles (`PRIMARY`, `ON_SURFACE`, `SURFACE_CONTAINER_HIGHEST`, `OUTLINE`), which Flet resolves against the theme on screen. Besides its colour (F28), every text style it gives names `font_family="packages/roboto_fonts/Roboto"`, except code spans, which use `"monospace"` with `font_family_fallback=["Menlo"]`; links and strike-through name `decoration_color` equal to their text colour. h5, h6 and the other styles keep Flet's theme-derived defaults. `tests/test_theme.py` checks all three rules. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator, the S3 sheet rendered Markdown text in SF Pro, because a style given in the sheet does not inherit the theme's font (Android hid this: its system font is Roboto), and `"monospace"` is no iOS family, so code spans were proportional; with the families named, both platforms showed Roboto and a monospace code font (Menlo on iOS). On the dark surface the link underline and the strike-through were drawn in `#1D1B20` (about 1.1:1) until their colour was named. |
| D-212 | **Agents act only in this repository** (the owner's direction, 2026-10-13; amends D-97 and D-101). Agents, and every agent they start, create and edit issues, PRs, comments, reviews, reactions or anything else only in `ndonkoHenri/pycon-us-flet`. They never open or comment on an issue, nor review or send a PR, nor make any other change, in `flet-dev/flet`, `flet-dev/serious-python`, an extension's repository or any other repository. Reading flet-dev (the source at the pin, its issues and PRs) stays allowed. An upstream gap is an `upstream` issue here with the full analysis (what happens, the Flet source at the pin with files and lines, a minimal repro, the suggested fix, the interim); the owner, who maintains Flet, decides whether and how to take it to Flet, and the issue links the flet-dev issue or PR once the owner has opened one. Every brief an agent gives another agent (a subagent, a reviewer, Codex) repeats this rule. | Before this rule, agents opened flet-dev/flet#6963, flet-dev/serious-python#255 and flet-dev/flet#6964 from the owner's account, relying on D-101's former "flet-dev issues the upstream track needs"; the owner had meant from the start that the project manages one repository. Those three stay as the owner decides. |
| D-213 | **What an `upstream` issue may hold** (the owner's decision on #267, 2026-10-13; completes D-212). By default the analysis: what happens, the Flet source at the pin with files and lines, a minimal repro, the suggested fix in words or a short snippet, and the interim. When a fix is small and its diff makes the analysis clearer, the issue may also hold a draft diff in a fenced block. Nothing for Flet lives in the app's source tree, nothing is applied to `.flet-src`, and nothing leaves this repository; any code for Flet beyond that is the owner's. | The owner takes upstream work to Flet; a short diff in the issue saves them time without putting Flet code in the app or anywhere outside this repo. |

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-97 | *(Filing amended by D-212.)* Upstream work is a parallel track. Each item is an `upstream` issue here ~~that links the flet-dev issue or PR~~ with the full analysis, linking the flet-dev issue or PR once the owner has opened one (D-212). App issues that wait on it are `blocked`, and the documented interim ships meanwhile. | The owner contributes the missing pieces to Flet; the app never forks Flet or its build template. |

E3, scheduled local notifications in the official flet-dev/flet#6948 extension (after S6).<br>2. U1, `RefreshIndicator`.<br>3. U15, non-fatal handler and effect errors with a Python error hook.<br>4. E1, a barcode-scanner extension (after S8, before P3).<br>5. U4, `page.get_app_info()`.<br>6. The small ones: U16 `PageMediaData.text_scale_factor`, U17 an accessibility-features change event, U10 `AppBar.bottom`, U2 sticky headers, U3 an absolute `InteractiveViewer` transform, U9 `use_route_search`, U18 Router Back inside a modal sub-chain (pop to the previous view entry unless the popped view is the modal itself; D-19), U25 a typed `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent` and the boot screen kept until `main()`'s first patch (F35, F36; D-161).<br>Later or low: E4 native calendar, ~~E5 HTML,~~ E2 Firebase; U19 pinning platform adaptations (a `Theme.platform` or scroll-behaviour option; D-10), U20 several weighted files per family in `page.fonts` (D-26), U21 LaTeX syntax opt-in in `ft.Markdown` (F27), U22 a Markdown style sheet that merges with the theme (F28); U6, U7, U12 and U13 only when a feature or a spike needs them. Cut: E5 HTML (D-117), E6 slidable, U5, U8, U11, U14. | Attendee value first. Small core items remove interims for every Flet app. |
