---

## M. Naming (binding)

- **Project, package, entry point.** The project is `pycon-us`, the import package `pycon_us`. The entry point is `src/main.py`, which renders `App` with `page.render_views`. `__version__` lives in `pycon_us/__init__.py`, with `APP_NAME` and `SOURCE_URL` (D-292).
- **Finding a module.** This section holds the rules, not the modules: every module starts with a docstring saying what it is for (AGENTS.md; `ruff`'s `D` rules check it has one), so `head -n1 src/pycon_us/*.py src/pycon_us/*/*.py` is the map, one line per module. A screen is found from its route, through `routes.py`'s imports and `ROUTES`, and a name with `grep -rn 'def use_query' src/pycon_us` (D-469).
- **Top-level modules** in `pycon_us/`: the shell (`app.py`, `routes.py`, `locations.py`), a module per store, `config.py` with the year data's `year_<part>.py`, and `theme.py` with its `theme_<part>.py`, with smaller modules beside them.
- **Packages** in `pycon_us/`:
  - `core/`: pure domain logic, with no Flet (D-67).
  - `services/`: I/O at explicit boundaries, a small module with one job each; one module per capability imports its extension (D-63).
  - `screens/`: one module per screen, flat, named after its route with a hyphen as an underscore (`now.py`, `my_schedule.py`, `sign_in.py`). `/more/app` is `about_app.py`, `tracks/<slug>` is `track.py`, the drill's `changes/<year>` is `schedule_changes.py`, and staff screens are `staff_<tool>.py`. A part split off a screen usually takes the screen's name first: `now_header.py`, `schedule_days.py`.
  - `ui/`: components that several screens share, grouped by role; split a module at about 400 lines (D-66).
  - `data/<year>/` and `licence_texts/`: data, not modules (Year data below; D-296).
- **Stores and hooks:** a store is a module of its own, with its context and its hook: `Conference` / `use_conference()`, `Prefs` / `use_prefs()`, `Favorites` / `use_favorites()`, `ChangeLog` / `use_change_log()` (P2, D-301), `Layout` / `use_layout()` (D-277), `Account` / `use_account()` (P3), `Staff` / `use_staff()` (P3), plus `use_services()` (`services/__init__.py`, with `Services` and `ServicesCtx`) and `use_tokens()`.
- **Routes.** S4 confirmed the shape (D-141), and `docs/architecture.md` §5 holds the full table; keep it and this list in sync.
  - Roots: `/now`, `/schedule`, `/my-schedule`, `/map`, `/more`. `/` goes to `/welcome` until its Get started (D-287) and to `/now` or the last location otherwise; `/welcome` is a root without a bar or the drill.
  - Schedule changes: ~~`/schedule/changes`~~ the drill pair `changes/<year>`, pushed on the View that opens it (D-255).
  - Pages under a root: Now's `/now/wifi`, `/now/help`, `/now/venue` (D-338) and `/now/staff/<tool>`, a staff tool again for the Scan badges card (D-421), and My schedule's `/my-schedule/settings`, Settings again (D-401).
  - More pages are `/more/<page>`: `speakers`, `tracks`, `tracks/<slug>` (and, ahead of it, the hub's pages of their own: `tracks/sprints`, `tracks/lightning-talks`, `tracks/open-spaces`, D-207, and `tracks/keynotes`, D-217), `keynotes`, `open-spaces`, `lightning-talks`, `sprints`, `sponsors`, `jobs`, `venue`, `wifi`, `help`, `about`, `psf`, `social`, `settings`, `account`, `sign-in`, `app` (About this app), `developer`, and `staff/<tool>`, where the tool is `lead`, `door`, `swag` or `coc`.
  - Search: `/search?scope=<scope>`, a global modal route. Back from a pushed result returns to search through our `page.on_view_pop` and `parent_of()` (D-19); Back from search returns to `ViewMemory.search_base`.
  - Drill: a recursive child of every root, More page and search, with the segment pairs `session/<id>`, `speaker/<id>`, `room/<slug>`, `sponsor/<slug>`, `plan/<plan>` (P2: a floor plan, `expo` or `job-fair` from `map.toml`) and `changes/<year>` (P2: Schedule changes, the year's change log; D-255); e.g. `/schedule/session/42/speaker/17/session/99`, `/more/sponsors/sponsor/acme/plan/expo?booth=613`.
  - Query parameters carry view options only (`?scope=`, `?pin=`, `?highlight=`, `?booth=`), read through `use_query()` until U9. The Router's Back navigates to pathnames only, so view options in the query are lost on Back: any option that must survive Back (map pin or booth, search query and scope) lives in `ViewMemory` (D-18), and the query only seeds it on entry.
- **Ids.**
  - In this plan: decisions `D-nn`, open decisions `O-n`, Flet facts `Fnn`, spikes `S1`..`S9`, phases `P0`..`P4`.
  - Upstream items: `E1`..`E6` and `U1`..`U15` keep their v1 meanings; later `U`-numbers are new (`flet-gaps-and-extensions.md`).
  - Features: catalog ids (`SCHED-05`) and our additions `NEW-01`..`NEW-09`.
- **Storage.**
  - Prefs keys are prefixed `pycon.`. App-wide: `theme`, `high_contrast`, `onboarded`, `excluded_tracks`, `reminders`, `dev`; in P3 `account` (JSON with email, nickname and role flags), `staff_scanner_consent` (bool, D-423) and `start_screen` (D-16). Per year: `pycon.staff_<year>_sponsor` (JSON), `pycon.staff_<year>_door_session` (JSON, D-433), `pycon.staff_<year>_swag_categories` (a string list of category ids, #768; all three D-466), `pycon.<year>.favorites`, `pycon.<year>.fetched` (P2; bool: a network feed for the year has been published on this install, so a refresh over the bundled snapshot is not diffed, D-255) and `pycon.<year>.favorites_sync` (P3; `merge`, `dirty` or `clean`, absent meaning `merge`; D-55, D-406).
  - Secure keys: `pycon.api_key`, `pycon.api_secret`.
  - Files in the data directory: `feed-<year>.json`, `changes-<year>.json`, `outbox-<year>.db` (D-466).
- **Year data:** `src/pycon_us/data/<year>/` holds `conference.toml`, `content.toml`, `map.toml` and `conference.json` (the snapshot). Assets live in `src/assets/` (~~`fonts/`,~~ `icons/`, `maps/<year>/`); the Roboto files live in the Flutter package `flutter_packages/roboto_fonts/` (D-105).
- **Tools:** `tools/mock_api/` (`handlers.py`, `server.py`, `transport.py`, `badges.py`, `README.md`), `tools/update_snapshot.py`, `tools/check_feed.py` with `tools/feed_invariants.py` (the invariants it shares with the tests, `INVARIANTS`), `tools/sponsor_invariants.py` (the sponsor and job-listing invariants, split out under D-66, #492) and `tools/feed_outline.py` (`outline()`, the reviewed snapshot's view, D-261), `tools/capture_ionic_reference/`, `tools/check_matrix.py`, `tools/shifted_feed.py` (the bundled feed shifted so a session starts soon, for real-time device checks; testing-and-quality.md §9.1, #383); `tools/feed_pair.py` (a change-log feed pair from `tests/feeds.py`, served at one URL by `tests/ui/feeds.py`'s `PairFeed`, old then new, for the change log's device check; testing-and-quality.md §9.2, #460); the local checks `tools/check.sh`, the merge gate `tools/gate.sh` and the hook `.githooks/pre-push` (D-273).
- **Flet version (D-81, D-481):** one exact version on every Flet line of `pyproject.toml`, from PyPI. `.flet-src` (git-ignored) is only an optional clone for reading.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit tests: `tests/test_<module>.py`; UI flows: `tests/ui/`; Maestro device flows: `maestro/` (`journeys/`, `regression/`, `subflows/`, `scripts/`; D-252).
  - Helpers and goldens: `tests/ui/helpers.py`, `tests/ui/golden/` (P4).
  - Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/feeds/conference-<year>.json`, ~~`tests/fixtures/feeds/pairs/<name>/` (change-log cases)~~ (change-log pairs are built in `tests/feeds.py` from the recorded feed by named edits, D-255), `tests/fixtures/snapshots/`, `tests/fixtures/ionic/<year>/`, `tests/fixtures/ionic/signing.json`, `tests/fixtures/mock/`.
  - Test names are descriptive; feature ids may appear in docstrings.
- **Semantics identifiers:** `<screen>.<element>[.<key>]`, e.g. `schedule.day_tab.2`, `session.favorite`, `now.next_session`; shell controls use `app.<element>[.<key>]`, e.g. `app.nav.schedule`, `app.nav.my_schedule` (the bar's; the rail's destinations have none yet, D-277), `app.search` (the app bar's Search, D-252); a tab root's app-bar title is `<tab>.title`, e.g. `now.title` (D-170).
- **GitHub:**
  - Branches: `<type>/<issue>-<slug>`.
  - Workflows: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `feed-check.yml`, `build-android.yml`.
  - Spike write-ups: `docs/spikes/NN-<slug>.md`.
  - Milestones, labels and the Project: D-89, D-90, D-94.

## N. Verified Flet SDK facts

Facts F25..F39 were added later and name the spike and commit that verified them. Each other fact below was re-verified on 2026-10-11 in the local monorepo (flet-dev/flet at `9fbe52a61`, unreleased 1.1.0 in `CHANGELOG.md`). PyPI's latest release is 1.0.4 (2026-10-08). Paths are relative to `sdk/python/packages/flet/src/flet/` unless stated. This is **API evidence**: behaviour on devices is confirmed by the spikes.

| # | Fact |
|---|---|
| F1 | These are coroutines, so they must be awaited from `async def`:<br>• `Page`: `show_drawer`, `close_drawer`, `push_route`, `pop_views_until`.<br>• Controls: `View.confirm_pop(should_pop)`, `ListView.scroll_to`, `InteractiveViewer.zoom`/`pan`/`reset`/`save_state`/`restore_state`, `Dismissible.confirm_dismiss`.<br>• Services, all the methods we use: `Clipboard`, `SharedPreferences`, `UrlLauncher`, `Share`, `SemanticsService`, `StoragePaths`, `HapticFeedback`, `Wakelock`, `Connectivity.get_connectivity`.<br>These are sync: `page.navigate`, `page.show_dialog`, `page.pop_dialog`, `page.run_task`. |
| F2 | `use_effect(setup, dependencies, cleanup)` (`components/hooks/use_effect.py`; runner in `messaging/session.py`, `__run_effect`), at the pin `08b89cce5`:<br>• a coroutine function runs as a task; any other callable is simply called, so a lambda that returns a coroutine never runs;<br>• a setup task still running is cancelled whenever the effect re-runs, and on unmount with or without a cleanup (`EffectHook.has_lifecycle_work()`, `_run_unmount_effects` in `components/component.py`);<br>• a sync setup may return a callable, its cleanup, which runs before the `cleanup` argument; an `async` setup's return value is not used;<br>• cleanups run once per setup run, and an `async` one is finished in a task before the effect's next setup runs (`flet-dev/flet#7005`). `tests/test_flet_effects.py` pins it (D-387). |
| F3 | `use_context` subscribes the component only when the provided value is an `Observable`, and nested observables do not propagate (`components/hooks/use_context.py`). |
| F4 | Error handling in `messaging/session.py`:<br>• an exception in an event handler is logged, then `session.error()` sends `SESSION_CRASHED` (`dispatch_event`); a sync effect setup does the same;<br>• exceptions in async effect tasks reach only asyncio;<br>• `page.on_error` is fed by Flutter-side errors (`packages/flet/lib/src/flet_backend.dart`). |
| F5 | Assigning an observable field notifies only when `value_equal(old, new)` is false: identity first, then `==` (`components/observable.py`, `components/utils.py`). |
| F6 | Router (`components/router.py`):<br>• Signatures: `Router(routes, not_found=None, manage_views=False)`; `Route` fields are `path`, `index`, `component`, `children`, `loader`, `outlet`, `modal` and `recursive`.<br>• With `manage_views=True`, each view level is its own `View`, and `not_found` is wrapped in `View(route=pathname, ...)`.<br>• A recursive route consumes one match per level, trying non-recursive children first. Each view level sees the params accumulated up to its own level, so same-named params hold that level's values.<br>• A top-level `modal=True` route is global: its base is the last non-modal location. A child modal is local.<br>• While a modal is anywhere in the chain, every pop (including from a child view pushed over the modal) navigates to the modal's base location. A non-modal pop navigates to the previous view entry's resolved pathname. Both drop the query string. An app's own `page.on_view_pop`, set before the Router mounts, receives every pop instead (`e.route` is the popped view's route).<br>• Hooks: `use_route_params`, `use_route_location` (pathname only), `use_view_path`, `use_route_outlet`, `use_route_loader_data`, and `is_route_active`, which is itself a hook. `LocationInfo.search` exists internally, but there is no `use_route_search`. |
| F7 | `ListView.scroll_to(offset, delta, scroll_key, duration, curve)` does nothing when the keyed control has not been built (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/scrollable_control.dart`). `ListView.build_controls_on_demand` defaults to `True`. |
| F8 | Absent from the SDK: `RefreshIndicator`; slivers, sticky headers and `SliverAppBar`; `AppBar.bottom`; an absolute `InteractiveViewer` transform (`zoom` and `pan` are relative); `Page.get_app_info`; `CarouselView`; an HTML control; `Theme.platform`; `TextStyle.font_features`; `PageMediaData.text_scale_factor`; an accessibility-features change event; a local-notifications package; a barcode-scanner package. |
| F9 | Theme:<br>• `Theme` has `color_scheme_seed`, `color_scheme`, `surfaces` (`ThemeSurfaces.NEUTRAL`/`TONAL`, new in 1.1; seeded themes default to neutral surfaces), `font_family` and `page_transitions`.<br>• The Dart side builds `ColorScheme.fromSeed`, neutralises surfaces unless `TONAL`, then applies the explicit `color_scheme` overrides (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/theme.dart`).<br>• `PageTransitionsTheme` has `android` and `ios`; `PageTransitionTheme` includes `CUPERTINO`, `PREDICTIVE`, `FADE_FORWARDS` and `ZOOM`.<br>• The build template sets `android:enableOnBackInvokedCallback="true"`. |
| F10 | Navigation controls:<br>• `NavigationBar.selected_index` is an `int` (default 0), `adaptive` defaults to `None`, and `label_behavior` includes `ALWAYS_SHOW`. `NavigationRail` exists.<br>• `View` has `can_pop`, `on_confirm_pop` and `fullscreen_dialog`, and renders either `navigation_bar` or `bottom_appbar`.<br>• `IconButton` has `selected`, `selected_icon` and `badge` (`str` or `Badge`). |
| F11 | Dialogs and overlays:<br>• `SnackBar` and `Banner` are `DialogControl`s: show them with `page.show_dialog` or `use_dialog`.<br>• `SnackBar` has no top position.<br>• `Banner` requires `content` and at least one visible action, and has no duration.<br>• `BottomSheet` has no detents; it offers `show_drag_handle`, `draggable`, `scrollable`, `use_safe_area` and `size_constraints`. |
| F12 | `SharedPreferences.set` accepts only `str`, `int`, `float`, `bool` and `list[str]` values. |
| F13 | *(Its iOS live-region sentence corrected by D-243.)* `SemanticsService.get_accessibility_features()` returns a snapshot, with no change event. Its fields: `accessible_navigation`, `bold_text`, `disable_animations`, `high_contrast` (iOS and Android 34+), `invert_colors`, `reduce_motion` (iOS only), `on_off_switch_labels` and `supports_announcements`. `announce_message(message, rtl, assertiveness)` exists, but Android discourages announcements; prefer `Semantics(live_region=True)` (how TalkBack takes one: F61). `supports_announcements` is never true on Android: the engine sets `NO_ANNOUNCE` on every version (`AccessibilityBridge.java`), though `announce` still calls `announceForAccessibility` (with a deprecation warning from API 36). On iOS a live region whose label changes posts a queued announcement of its label and leaves VoiceOver's focus where it is. Only a `focus` semantics event moves the focus to a node (`accessibility_bridge.mm:139-151`, `:366-369`, Flutter 3.47.7; corrected by D-243). |
| F14 | A packaged app's environment (build template `lib/main.dart`):<br>• `FLET_PLATFORM` is set, so `flet.utils.is_embedded()` is True.<br>• `FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA` is durable, app-private and under application support; it is also the working directory and survives updates.<br>• `FLET_APP_STORAGE_CACHE` may be purged by the OS; `FLET_APP_STORAGE_TEMP` is volatile.<br>• `FLET_APP_CONSOLE` is `<cache>/console.log` (also `StoragePaths.get_console_log_filename()`).<br>`flet run` mirrors the storage part: it sets the three `FLET_APP_STORAGE_*` variables to `<project>/.flet/storage/{data,cache,temp}` (git-ignored; the working directory is `data`), but not `FLET_PLATFORM` or `FLET_APP_CONSOLE` (flet-cli `commands/run.py`). |
| F15 | `flet build` pip-installs only the app's dependencies and copies the app directory (flet-cli `commands/build_base.py`, dependency step), so the app has no distribution metadata and `importlib.metadata.version("pycon_us")` fails in packaged apps. |
| F16 | *(Amended by D-269: the pin has the fallback. At `a9ef26fdb` the resolver is `get_deep_linking()`, `build_base.py:2801-2855`, #1005.)* For Android and iOS builds, flet-cli reads deep links ~~only from `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` and `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]`; the global table applies to other targets until `flet-dev/flet#6953` merges~~ from `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` and `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]`, key by key, else from the common `[tool.flet.deep_linking]`, which other targets read alone (`build_base.py`, deep-linking resolution: `get_deep_linking_setting()`, lines 2960-2982, from `flet-dev/flet#6953`, in the pin since `68a4b326c`). The template registers custom schemes; there is no App Links `autoVerify` and no universal links. |
| F17 | The iOS template's `Podfile` turns on permission_handler macros from the Info.plist usage keys the app declares. There is no `PERMISSION_NOTIFICATIONS` entry, so request iOS notification permission through the notifications plugin. The mapping also misspells `NSCalendarWriteOnlyAccessUsageDescription` (Apple's key is `NSCalendarsWriteOnlyAccessUsageDescription`), a small upstream fix candidate. |
| F245 | *(#448, `08b89cce5`, permission_handler_apple 9.6.4.)* So, in a `flet build` iOS app, `PermissionHandler().get_status(Permission.NOTIFICATION)` always returns `DENIED`, and `request()` returns `PERMANENTLY_DENIED` without a prompt: with no `PERMISSION_NOTIFICATIONS` from the Podfile (F17), `PermissionHandlerEnums.h` defines it 0 and the notification group falls to `UnknownPermissionStrategy`, whose `checkPermissionStatus` returns `PermissionStatusDenied` and whose `requestPermission` returns `PermissionStatusPermanentlyDenied`. E3 (`flet_notifications`) has no tri-state status: `are_notifications_enabled()` is a bool (provisional counts as enabled), and `request_permissions()` returns the OS's answer, False without a prompt after a refusal. |
| F18 | *(We no longer run from one: D-481.)* flet-cli run from a source checkout uses the local build template and overrides the Dart `flet` package with the monorepo path (`build_base.py`). `[tool.flet].dev_packages` maps the packaged app's Python dependencies to local paths or URLs, and `[tool.flet.flutter.pubspec]` merges extra pubspec entries. |
| F19 | `flet build` embeds Python 3.14 by default (3.14.7: python-build manifest `20260921`, the release flet-cli pins at `utils/python_versions.py:31`), and `flet` depends on `httpx` on non-Emscripten platforms. |
| F20 | Flet 1.0.4 builds iOS with a 13.0 deployment target and fails on Xcode 27. 1.1.0 raises the target to iOS 15 and fixes this (flet-dev/flet#6874; the fix, flet-dev/flet#6914, is on the `flet-1.1.0` branch, not `main`: S5, D-104). The owner's Mac runs Xcode 27.0. |
| F21 | `flet test` targets `android`, `ios`, `macos`, `linux` and `windows`, with `flet.testing.FletTestApp`. The host `Tester` has `tap`, `tap_at`, `drag`, `drag_from`, `long_press`, `enter_text` and `find_by_semantics_identifier`. The on-device `RemoteTester` has no `drag` or `tap_at`. |
| F22 | `flet run` watches only the entry script unless `-r`/`--recursive` is passed. A reload restarts the Python process, so app state resets. |
| F23 | Verified services and page fields:<br>• `UrlLauncher.launch_url(url, mode=LaunchMode.IN_APP_BROWSER_VIEW` or `EXTERNAL_APPLICATION, ...)`.<br>• `Share.share_text`, and `share_files` with `ShareFile.from_bytes` / `from_path`.<br>• `Clipboard.set`, the `HapticFeedback` methods, `Wakelock.enable`/`disable`, `Connectivity.on_change`.<br>• `page.media.always_use_24_hour_format` (`PageMediaData` also has `padding`, `view_padding`, `view_insets`, `device_pixel_ratio` and `orientation`), `page.on_app_lifecycle_state_change`, `page.platform`. |
| F24 | These controls exist with the fields the design uses:<br>• Tabs: `Tabs`, `TabBar` (`scrollable` defaults to `True`), `TabBarView`, `SegmentedButton`.<br>• Selection and status: `Chip` (`selected`, `show_checkmark`, `on_select`), `Badge`, `Shimmer`, `ProgressBar` (`semantics_label`, `semantics_value`).<br>• Layout and media: `SearchBar`, `ExpansionTile`, `InteractiveViewer`, `GestureDetector`, `PageView`, `Hero`, `SafeArea`, `Card` (`ELEVATED`/`FILLED`/`OUTLINED`), `CircleAvatar` (`foreground_image_src`, `content`), `Image` (`placeholder_src`, `error_content`, `semantics_label`), `ListTile` (`toggle_inputs`, `min_height`, `url`).<br>• Text and input: `Markdown` (`selectable`, `extension_set`, `on_tap_link`), `AutofillGroup` with `TextField.autofill_hints`.<br>• Accessibility: `Semantics` (`header`, `heading_level`, `live_region`, `identifier`, `link`, `button`), `MergeSemantics`.<br>• Also `use_dialog(dialog)`; `ft.Icons` has 8,825 names. |
| F25 | serious_python 5.0.0 starts the embedded Python with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` on every platform (`serious_python_android.dart:124`, `serious_python_darwin.dart:58`), so a build without `.pyc` compiles every imported module again on every start (S1, D-111). |
| F26 | On Android, when the activity is recreated (seen in S1 when the navigation mode changed), Flet reuses the running Python process and starts a **new session**: `main()` runs again and the component tree and its state start over, while module-level state survives. A dark-mode switch does not recreate the activity. |
| F27 | `ft.Markdown` always adds `LatexBlockSyntax` and `LatexInlineSyntax` (flutter_markdown_plus_latex 1.0.5) to the chosen extension set (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/markdown.dart`, Flet `15854a0ac`). Inline math opens on `$`, `$$`, `\(`, `\[`, `\pu{`, `\ce{`, `( ` and `[ `, whenever a matching closer comes later on the same line and is followed by whitespace, one of `?!.,:` (or their full-width forms) or the end of the text. These custom syntaxes are tried before Markdown's own at every position, so a backslash escape of `(` or `[` produces a LaTeX opener; numeric character references (`&#36;`) are decoded to text and never match (S3, D-115). |
| F28 | When `ft.Markdown.md_style_sheet` is set, each text style given replaces the theme-derived default instead of merging with it (`parseMarkdownStyleSheet` in `packages/flet/lib/src/utils/markdown.dart`): a style without `color` renders black in dark mode. The defaults themselves use `Colors.blue` for links (no underline) and `Colors.blue.shade100` for the block-quote box when a sheet is given; without a sheet, h1 is `headlineSmall` and h2 `titleLarge` (S3, D-116). |
| F29 | On Android, Back on a root View with nothing to pop finishes the activity (lifecycle `inactive`, `hide`, `pause`, `detach`); the Python process stays, and a relaunch starts a **new session** in it at `route=/`, the build template's "process reuse" path (`lib/main.dart`, `runPythonApp`). So F26's new session also follows an ordinary Back exit (S4, `15854a0ac`). |
| F30 | When a new session replaces an old one in the same process, `FletDartBridgeServer` closes the old `Session` (`page.on_close` fires) without unmounting its components: `use_effect` cleanups don't run, effect tasks keep running, and the first store write from one raises `RuntimeError: An attempt to fetch destroyed session.` A render after `detach` fails to send (`dart_bridge.send_bytes failed`, logged) (S4, `15854a0ac`; `messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py`, `app.py` `_DartBridgeServerHandle`). |
| F31 | *(When the openurl prompt shows amended by D-269.)* A packaged app's initial route comes from the platform: on Android the `route` intent extra (`am start --es route <path>`, what `flet debug --route` passes) or a deep-link intent; on the iOS simulator the engine's `--route=<path>` launch argument (`xcrun simctl launch <udid> <bundle> --route=<path>`). `simctl openurl` with a custom scheme stops at a system "Open in …?" prompt that needs a tap, the first time on a simulator; later links, even after a reinstall, open the app directly (D-269). Route strings round-trip percent-encoded (S4). |
| F32 | `ListView(build_controls_on_demand=False)` hands Flutter a `ListView(children: [...])` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/list_view.dart`), which creates every child widget but still builds (mounts) only those inside the viewport and its cache extent. A `ScrollKey`'s `GlobalKey` is registered in `ControlWidget.build` (`control_widget.dart`), so `scroll_to(scroll_key=...)` to a far item does nothing in either mode. It works in `Column(scroll=...)` and in a `ListView` whose `cache_extent` covers the list (S2, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 16 emulator and iOS 26.5 simulator). |
| F33 | A component that renders `TextField(on_change=...)` without `value=` loses the typed text on its next re-render: the new control's default `value` differs from the old control's (which the change event updated), so the diff sends the default to Flutter (`Component.update` in `components/component.py` diffs the new tree against the old; S2, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F34 | `ft.memo` skips only the memoised component's own body when its props are shallow-equal (`is`, then `==`; `components/utils.py`, `shallow_compare_args_and_kwargs`). The parent that renders it still runs and its own controls are rebuilt and diffed, so memoised rows under a parent that re-renders on every change keep the parent's cost; a frozen `eq=False` prop compares by identity, so it must be the same object across renders (S2). |
| F35 | Flet sends the page, with its default empty `View`, in the `REGISTER_CLIENT` response, before `main()` runs (`messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py`), and the build template removes its boot screen as soon as that page arrives (`FletBackend.isLoading`). So Flutter draws one frame of the default View (white in light mode, about 19 ms) before `main()`'s first patch. Changes made in `ft.run(main, before_main=...)` are part of that first page (#72, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 14 emulator). |
| F36 | `Page.on_platform_brightness_change` is typed `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent`, but `flet_backend.dart` sends the brightness name as a plain string, so the handler gets a `ControlEvent` (`data="dark"`) without `.brightness`, and reading it raises `AttributeError` (the session crashed on the iOS 26.5 simulator). `page.platform_brightness` is already updated when the handler runs (#72, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F37 | asyncio reports a task's unretrieved exception only when the task is released (`Task exception was never retrieved`, from `Task.__del__`), and never once `cancel()` has been called on it, even after it has finished. `use_effect` keeps an async setup's task on its hook (`hook._setup_task`) and cancels it only while it is pending (`components/hooks/use_effect.py`), so a failed async effect is reported only when its next run replaces the task or its hook is dropped: for an effect with `[]` dependencies, not while its component is mounted, and in a replaced session (F30), never (#73, Flet `15854a0ac`; a host script on Python 3.14: a held task logged nothing, a dropped one logged at once, a cancelled one never). |
| F38 | A re-rendered control takes over its predecessor's place: `_compare_dataclasses` (`controls/object_patch.py`) calls `dst._migrate_state(src)` (`_i`, `controls/base_control.py`), sets the new instance's `_parent` and re-indexes the session from the old instance to the new one, without unmount or mount. A control built with `ref=` sets `ref.current` to itself when it is created (`BaseControl.__post_init__`), and `Ref.current` is a weak reference (`controls/ref.py`). So a ref passed to the control on every render points at the live control after a re-render, and its methods (`scroll_to`) reach the mounted widget (#75, D-166, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F39 | *(Fixed at the pin `08b89cce5` by `flet-dev/flet#6983`; D-387.)* Before it, `use_effect` assigned its `cleanup` argument to the hook on every render (`hook.cleanup = cleanup`), so a cleanup that a **sync** setup returned (F2) was replaced by `None` at the component's next render and did not run on unmount. The hook now keeps it apart (`EffectHook._returned_cleanup`) and runs it on unmount after any number of re-renders (`tests/test_flet_effects.py`). Upstream: [flet-dev/flet#6963](https://github.com/flet-dev/flet/issues/6963), tracked in #182 (U27). |
| F40 | *(Sources at the pin's Flutter amended by F49.)* Screen-reader headings come from the heading **level**: on Android (API 28+) Flutter's `AccessibilityBridge.java` calls `setHeading(semanticsNode.headingLevel > 0)`, and the IS_HEADER flag alone does not make a heading; Flet's `Semantics.heading_level` docs say the same of VoiceOver. So `Semantics(header=True)` without `heading_level` is not a heading for TalkBack or VoiceOver (#205). A `Text(selectable=True)` (Flutter `SelectableText`) inside `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=1, identifier=...)` is one Android node of class `View` (not `EditText`) holding the text and the identifier, so the heading sits on the labelled node (`uiautomator dump`, Android 14, Flet `15854a0ac`, #90). |
| F41 | Flutter marks a tappable text span as a link (`isLink`, `paragraph.dart` and `editable.dart`, so in `ft.Markdown` with or without `selectable`), but on Android `AccessibilityBridge.java` treats a link without a URL as a button (`shouldBeTreatedAsButton`; only a `linkUrl` becomes a `URLSpan`), and no text span carries a URL; Flet's `Semantics` has `link` but no `link_url`. So TalkBack calls every link in the app a button (`android.widget.Button` in `uiautomator dump`), while iOS gets `UIAccessibilityTraitLink` (`SemanticsObject.mm`) (#91, Flutter 3.47.7, Flet `15854a0ac`; U31). |
| F42 | Flet's `Semantics` always sets Flutter's `enabled`, to `!control.disabled` (`controls/semantics.dart`), and `Control.disabled` is the control's own `disabled` or an ancestor's (`models/control.dart`), never a descendant's. So a `Semantics` around a disabled button says it is enabled, and wrapped in `MergeSemantics` the merged node read as enabled on Android (`enabled="true"` in `uiautomator dump`, Android 14). A wrapper around a control that can be disabled sets `disabled` itself too (#87, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-195); `named()` takes it from the control (D-214). Flutter's `Tristate.merge` lets `isTrue` win, which is why the merge read as enabled (`dart:ui` `semantics.dart:1136`, Flutter 3.47.7; #239, U34). |
| F43 | Given a `style`, `color`, `bgcolor` or an `elevation` other than 1, Flet's `Button` (so `FilledButton` and `FilledTonalButton`, not `TextButton` or `OutlinedButton`) sends `style.copy(color=..., bgcolor=..., elevation=self.elevation)` (`before_update`, `controls/material/button.py`). `Button.elevation` defaults to 1 and is never `None`, so it replaces the style's own `elevation`, and as a plain number it becomes every state's (`getWidgetStateProperty`, `utils/widget_state.dart`): a styled `FilledButton` has elevation 1 even when disabled, where M3 has 0. Such a button passes its elevation on the button (`elevation=0`, or a per-state value), not in the style (#87, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-195). |
| F44 | `ft.Image`'s `placeholder_src` takes the image's own size: `ImageControl` builds the placeholder with the `Image`'s `width` and `height`, and `ImageFadeConfig.wrapFrame` puts it in a `SizedBox(width: width, height: height)` inside an `AnimatedSwitcher`, whose default layout is a centred `Stack` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/image.dart`, `utils/images.dart`). So an image laid out without a size, scaled into a frame by its constraints, gets a placeholder at the placeholder's own intrinsic size, centred, which cannot fill the frame. `fade_in_animation` alone turns the same fade on, with an invisible copy of the image keeping the layout while it loads (#92, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-206). |
| F45 | A fading `ft.Image` is laid out loose: with `fade_in_animation` (or a placeholder) set, `ImageFadeConfig.wrapFrame` puts the image in an `AnimatedSwitcher` whose layout is a `Stack(alignment: center)` with the default loose fit (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/images.dart`), so the image gets loose constraints even where its parent's are tight. An image meant to fill a box at `COVER` (a `Stack` child at `left`…`bottom` 0) then keeps its own proportions, centred: seen on the iOS 26.5 simulator as a copy no wider than the image. Such an image takes no fade (#92, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-206). |
| F46 | Flet handles a system pop before Flutter's Navigator (the Android Back key or committed gesture, and VoiceOver's escape on iOS, the two-finger Z, which Flutter's iOS embedder sends as `popRoute` when the focused node has no dismiss action: `accessibilityPerformEscape` in `FlutterViewController.mm` and `SemanticsObject.mm`, Flutter 3.47.7): `SimpleRouterDelegate.popRoute()` calls `PageControl._handleSystemPopRoute()` first (`packages/flet/lib/src/routing/router_delegate.dart`, `controls/page.dart`, added by flet-dev/flet#5915). With two or more Views, when the top View has `can_pop` true and no `on_confirm_pop` handler, it marks that View popped and sends `view_pop` itself and returns true, so `Navigator.maybePop()` never runs. It does not look for pageless routes above the View's page: a dialog, a date or time picker, a bottom sheet or a popup menu, all pushed on the same Navigator (`useRootNavigator: false` in `alert_dialog.dart`, `date_picker.dart`, `time_picker.dart`); removing the View's page takes them along, so one Back closes the dialog and pops the View (#235). `_handleSystemPopRoute()` has no platform check, so the escape does the same on iOS (read from the source; VoiceOver does not run on the Simulator). A committed Back gesture ends there too: with a dialog on top the page route is not current, so its predictive-back detector declines (`predictive_back_page_transitions_builder.dart`, Flutter 3.47.7) and the commit falls back to `popRoute`. When the top View has `can_pop` false or an `on_confirm_pop` handler, or is the only View, the handler returns null and `Navigator.maybePop()` pops the topmost route; a View that can pop is then popped by the Navigator, which calls `onDidRemovePage` and so sends `view_pop`, as a committed gesture does, and its `on_confirm_pop` runs only for a pop that `can_pop` refuses (`PopScope` in `controls/view.dart`) (#235, `15854a0ac`; on flet-dev `main` too). |
| F47 | `PopupMenuItem.icon` and a text `content` are laid out as `Row([icon, SizedBox(width: 8), content])` with no `Expanded` (`buildPopupMenuEntries()`, `packages/flet/lib/src/utils/popup_menu.dart` at `15854a0ac`), inside Flutter's menu, which is at most 280 dp wide. So a label longer than the menu's width does not wrap: at 200 % text on the Android emulator "Add all to My schedule" ran off the menu's edge (#103). An item whose label may be long makes its `content` a `ListTile(leading=Icon(...), title=Text(label))` instead, which Flutter documents as a `PopupMenuItem` child (the item merges a zero `contentPadding` into its `ListTileTheme`): the title wraps, and the theme's `TITLE_HEIGHT` keeps the icon beside its first line (D-188), where a `Row` would centre it on the block; `min_height=48` keeps the menu's row height, which a tile would make 56 (D-209). Every overflow item is now `ui/menus.py`'s `menu_item()`, with that tile (D-233); the layout gap is U36 (#308). |
| F48 | A `SnackBar` shown with `page.show_dialog` is drawn on every View, the top one included. Flet builds the page's dialogs only into the last View's body (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/view.dart`, `control.id == pageViews.last.id`), where `SnackBarControl` calls `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar` after the frame (`controls/snack_bar.dart`). That messenger is the page's one, above the `Navigator` (`MaterialApp.router`'s own; `scaffoldMessengerBuilder` adds one to a `CupertinoApp`, `controls/page.dart`), and it hands the bar to every registered `Scaffold` with no `Scaffold` of the same messenger above it (`_isRoot`: `parent == null || !_scaffolds.contains(parent)`, and `_updateScaffolds`, in material_ui 1.6.0's `scaffold.dart`; a `Scaffold` under a nested `ScaffoldMessenger` leaves the ones below it roots of this one). Each View is a route of its own with its own `Scaffold`, so each is such a root: the top View shows the bar, a covered one draws it offstage, a `Scaffold` that mounts while it shows (a View pushed then) gets it when it registers (`_register`), and one timer, the messenger's, runs its duration (its `ModalRoute.of` is null above the `Navigator`) (#197, Flet `15854a0ac`; iOS 26.5 simulator, Android 14 emulator, a Flutter widget test of the same shape). |
| F49 | *(Amends F40's sources to the pin's Flutter.)* At Flutter 3.47.7 (the pin's `.fvmrc`) both bridges make a heading of the level alone: Android's `BaseRoleConfigurator.java:144` calls `result.setHeading(node.headingLevel > 0)` (the code F40 cites moved there from `AccessibilityBridge.java`), and iOS's `SemanticsObject.mm:840` adds `UIAccessibilityTraitHeader` only when `headingLevel > 0`; IS_HEADER is left to Android's collection item info and to iOS's scroll on focus. (Flutter 3.44.8's iOS bridge still took the trait from IS_HEADER.) The framework keeps the flag and the level apart (`header` never sets a level), and a merged node keeps a child's level when its own is 0 (`_mergeHeadingLevels`, `semantics.dart`). So Flutter's `AppBar` title, `Semantics(header: true)` without a level (`app_bar.dart`), is no heading for TalkBack or VoiceOver, and an app's `Semantics(heading_level=1)` around the title's text merges into that title's node with its identifier (on the Android 14 emulator, `isHeading()` true on "More" with `more.title`, #205). |
| F50 | A bare `Semantics(identifier=...)` around a button, a field or a tile, or on a tile's title, is not that control's node but one of its own. In `uiautomator dump` (Android 14, release APK): around a button an empty, focusable `View` over the `Button`; around an `IconButton` or a `PopupMenuButton` with a tooltip a non-clickable `View` that holds the tooltip over an unnamed `Button` (`NAF="true"`); around a `TextField` an empty `View` over the `EditText`; on an `ExpansionTile`'s title a node of the title's own, beside a header button read "Collapsed" alone. `MergeSemantics` around the wrapper makes one node that carries the identifier: the `Button` named by its label or tooltip, the `EditText` with its label, helper and error in its text (a Flutter widget test); a merge takes in every descendant, so it cannot go around a field with a button inside or a fold that holds rows. For the field the cause is Flet's: it always gives its `Semantics` an enabled state (F42), and Flutter keeps two configurations apart when both have one (`SemanticsConfiguration.isCompatibleWith`, `Tristate.hasConflict`), so a Flutter widget test with `enabled: null` (U34's fix) puts the bare identifier on the `TextField`'s node; around a button and on a tile's title it stays a node of its own even then, which is Flutter's (#239, #236, Flet `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7; D-214). |
| F51 | `on_size_change` (on any `LayoutControl`) reports the control's laid-out size at mount and after every change, sampled every `size_change_interval` ms (10 by default; `SizeChangeObserver` in `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/base_controls.dart`), so a text-size change or a rotation while the app runs sends the new width. A `Text` in a 0 dp high box keeps its own width: Flutter clamps only its height. It cannot be combined with absolute placement in a `Stack`: `LayoutControl` builds the `Positioned` (`_positionedControl`) and then wraps it in the size observer (`_sizeChangeObserver`), so the `Positioned` is no longer the stack's direct child, and a release build draws Flutter's grey error box over the stack. Seen at `15854a0ac`: the widths at mount and after a font-scale change on the Android 14 emulator, and at mount at each content size on the iOS 26.5 simulator; the grey box in a release APK on the Android 14 emulator (#224, D-216). |
| F52 | A Flet extension can bring native Android and iOS code with it. `flet build` copies the extension's whole `flutter/<name>/` folder from its wheel (`[tool.setuptools.package-data]` must include it) into `build/flutter-packages/` and adds it as a path dependency (`register_flutter_extensions()`, `sdk/python/packages/flet-cli/src/flet_cli/commands/build_base.py`). A `flutter: plugin: platforms:` entry in its pubspec then makes it a Flutter plugin: `android: package + pluginClass` builds its `android/` as a Gradle library module, whose `AndroidManifest.xml` (receivers, permissions) and `res/` Gradle merges into the app; `ios: pluginClass` with `ios/<name>/Package.swift` builds a Swift package (the pin's builds use Swift Package Manager), and `GeneratedPluginRegistrant` calls the class's `register(with:)`. A Java no-op class is enough on Android (no Kotlin plugin). Seen at `15854a0ac` in S6: the merged manifest of a release APK (`aapt2 dump xmltree`) held the extension's two receivers and `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, and the iOS registrant held `S6RemindersPlugin`. |
| F53 | At the pin's Flutter 3.47.7, `flet build ios-simulator` migrates the generated project to the UIScene life cycle ("Finished migration to UIScene lifecycle"): `AppDelegate` is a `FlutterAppDelegate, FlutterImplicitEngineDelegate` that registers plugins in `didInitializeImplicitFlutterEngine(_:)`, and nothing sets `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().delegate`. `flutter_local_notifications` 22.3.1 does not set it either (it only calls `addApplicationDelegate:`, `FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin.m:155`), so iOS reports no notification response: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a cold-start tap gave empty launch details and a tap while running no event. A plugin whose `register(with:)` sets `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().delegate = UIApplication.shared.delegate as? UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate` makes `FlutterAppDelegate` forward the responses, and it runs early enough: the cold-start tap then carried its payload (S6). |
| F54 | Flutter's `Navigator` gives the screen reader's focus back when a route closes (Flutter 3.47.7, the pin's): `NavigatorState` listens to `ServicesBinding.accessibilityFocus` (`widgets/navigator.dart:3851`), which the engine sets on its `didGainFocus` message each time TalkBack or VoiceOver focuses a node (`AccessibilityBridge.java`'s `ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_FOCUS`; `accessibility_bridge.mm`), and records the node on the topmost present route entry (`_recordLastFocus`, `:3856`). When a route above an entry pops, `handleDidPopNext` (`:3312`) waits for the popped route's disposal and sends a `FocusSemanticEvent` for that node: on Android 300 ms later (`_kAndroidRefocusingDelayDuration`), which the engine turns into `TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED` (`AccessibilityBridge.java:342`, `onFocus`), and TalkBack moves its focus there; on iOS at once, as `UIAccessibilityLayoutChangedNotification` with the node (`accessibility_bridge.mm`, `HandleEvent`). Only a pop runs it; a route taken away with `removeRoute`, which Flet's `closeModalRoute()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/misc.dart:249`) uses for a route that is not on top, does not. Flet's `AlertDialog` (`useRootNavigator: false`), `DatePicker`, `TimePicker`, `BottomSheet` and `PopupMenuButton` are routes on the Views' `Navigator`. Another activity on top (a browser, the share sheet) is no route; on the return `FocusManager._appLifecycleChange` (`widgets/focus_manager.dart:1868`) restores the input focus it suspended, not the screen reader's. Flet has `focus()` on `IconButton`, `Button`, `TextButton` and `OutlinedButton`, not on `ListTile` or `PopupMenuButton` (#262, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-225). |
| F55 | Flet applies a control's `tooltip` around the whole control: `BaseControl` and `LayoutControl` call `_tooltip()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/base_controls.dart:27`, `:46`, `:64`), which wraps it in Flutter's `Tooltip`, whose `RawTooltip` adds a `Semantics(tooltip: ...)` that is no boundary (`widgets/raw_tooltip.dart:870`). Flutter's own `IconButton(tooltip:)` puts that `Tooltip` inside the button's `Semantics(container: true, button: true)` (`ButtonStyleButton`, material_ui 1.6.0 `button_style_button.dart:581`, `:597`), and its `FloatingActionButton(tooltip:)` inside its `MergeSemantics` (`floating_action_button.dart:623`, `:630`), so the tooltip names the button. Flet's `IconButton`, `FloatingActionButton` and `Button` pass Flutter no tooltip and hand the built button to `LayoutControl` (`icon_button.dart:250`, `floating_action_button.dart:111`, `button.dart:225`), and its `PopupMenuButton` passes `tooltip: null` and hides Flutter's own (`popup_menu_button.dart:29`, `:57-60`). So the tooltip is a node of its own over the button: in `uiautomator dump` (Android 14, release APK) a non-focusable `View` with the tooltip as its `content-desc` over a `Button` with `NAF="true"`, which TalkBack reads as "Button" alone; over a FAB with a label, a `View` with the tooltip over the labelled `Button`. A `MergeSemantics` straight around the button makes one node named by the tooltip ("Back, Button"); a `Tooltip(exclude_from_semantics=True)` adds no node. A `Semantics`' own `tooltip` is shown as a tooltip too, since `Semantics` is a `LayoutControl` (`semantics.dart:47`, `:113`) (#274, Flet `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7; D-234, U37). |
| F60 | The packaged app's embedded Python reads the device's time zone, so `datetime.now().astimezone()` (which CPython computes with the C library's `localtime()`) gives the device's offset (checked 2026-10-14 on packaged builds at `15854a0ac`, #218). **Android** (API 34 emulator, release APK): no `TZ` in the app process's environment (`/proc/<pid>/environ` has only `ANDROID_TZDATA_ROOT`), so bionic's `localtime` follows the system zone; with the zone set through the system's time-zone state (`cmd time_zone_detector set_time_zone_state_for_tests`, which moves the status bar's clock too, as Settings does), Developer options read "This device: UTC+02:00" in Europe/Berlin and "UTC−07:00" in America/Los_Angeles after a relaunch, and a change made while the app ran reached the Schedule at its next minute tick, without a restart. Setting only the `persist.sys.timezone` property (`service call alarm 3 s16 <zone>`) moved `adb shell date` but neither the status bar nor the app, so it is no way to test this. **iOS** (26.5 simulator, `flet build ios-simulator`): the simulator follows the Mac's zone (Europe/Berlin here), and the app's Python saw it: Developer options read "This device: UTC+02:00" and the Schedule showed the note; launched with `SIMCTL_CHILD_TZ=America/Los_Angeles xcrun simctl launch ...`, its Python followed `TZ` and the note was gone. The bundled `tzdata` (D-43) serves `zoneinfo` only; `astimezone()` with no argument uses the C library's local time. |
| F61 | A Flutter live region on Android (Flutter 3.47.7, TalkBack 14.2, API 34): the engine sends `TYPE_WINDOW_CONTENT_CHANGED` (`CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_SUBTREE`) for a node with `IS_LIVE_REGION` whose label changed (`didChangeLabel()`, `AccessibilityBridge.java:1705`), only for a node that was in its previous update and is not hidden (`updateSemantics()`, from line 1555); TalkBack reads it as `nodeLiveRegion=1` and speaks the label without moving its focus. A node whose rect is empty is dropped from the tree (`SemanticsNode.isInvisible`, `semantics.dart:2912`; `shouldDrop`, `object.dart:5765`), so a live region needs a size. A node with no label, value, hint, actions or focusable flags is not focusable (`isFocusable()`, line 2698), so linear navigation skips it. TalkBack drops a second announced change of the same node within 30 s (`WindowContentChangeAnnouncementFilter`, `CONTENT_CHANGE_MINIMUM_CYCLE = 30000` while "limit text change rate" is on, unless the node sets `minDurationBetweenContentChanges`, which Flutter does not); a change with empty output is not counted, and the start of a touch interaction resets it (`invalidateRecordNode()`), so a change after a double tap is always spoken. Seen in D-241's checks. |
| F62 | A control whose `key` is an `ft.ScrollKey` gets a new Flutter `GlobalKey` at each build of its `ControlWidget` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/control_widget.dart:34-38`: `key = GlobalKey()`, registered in `FletBackend.globalKeys` for `scroll_to`), so Flutter unmounts it and mounts it again, with everything under it, whenever an ancestor rebuilds: new `State`s, render objects and semantics nodes with new ids. A push or a pop of a View, a bottom sheet and a re-render of one row all rebuild the covered list's items, so a row under a keyed box lost its node id each time, and the `Navigator`'s refocus after Back (F54) found no node. Python keeps every control's `_i` (#303, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 14 emulator, TalkBack 14.2; D-242, U42). |
| F63 | A controlled `TextField` can be rewritten by an older keystroke's re-render. Flutter reports each edit as a property update, then a `change` event (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/textfield.dart:356-360`). It applies any `value` from Python that differs from the last text it knows, and moves the cursor to the end (`:195-203`). Python applies a property update to the indexed control as soon as it arrives (`UPDATE_CONTROL_PROPS`, `messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py:204-206`, `Session.apply_patch`). It runs each event in a task of its own (`:198-202`), and the re-render that a state change asks for later still, from `Session.__updates_scheduler` (`messaging/session.py:702-720`). The re-render diffs against the indexed control (F38). So a keystroke's re-render that runs after the next keystroke's update sends the older text, and Flutter shows it. Once the later events' state matches the last report, the diff sends nothing, and the field and the state stay apart. Nothing tells an echo apart from a change the program made. Reproduced in a real session (`tests/test_fields.py`, the reports of "c", "ch", "che" and "chen" interleaved with their events) and seen on the iOS simulator and Android emulator under load (#307; U43, #319, D-243). Flet `15854a0ac`. |
| F69 | A `Chip`'s string `label` reaches Flutter as a plain `Text` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/chip.dart:55`, `buildTextOrWidget()` in `packages/flet/lib/src/extensions/control.dart:97-98`), and the `InputChip` it builds wraps its label in a `DefaultTextStyle` with `maxLines: 1`, `softWrap: false` and `TextOverflow.fade` (`material_ui` 1.6.0, `lib/src/chip.dart:1483-1489`), so a long name fades at the chip's end. A `Text` control label wraps when it sets `max_lines` (Flet's `Text` always passes `softWrap: !no_wrap`, `controls/text.dart:118-126`, but leaves `maxLines` to the `DefaultTextStyle` when unset), yet the chip still cuts it: `_RenderChip._computeSizes()` measures the label's dry layout at the chip's whole width (`chip.dart:1987`), then lays it out in that width less the check mark or avatar, `padding` and `label_padding` (`_labelConstraintsFrom()`, `:1893-1911`), with the measured height as its maximum. A label that fits one line at the whole width but not in the narrower one wraps into a second line that the chip clips (and fades). The chip also hit-tests only the label, at its centre (`hitTest()`, `:1942-1965`). Seen at `15854a0ac` on the iOS 26.5 simulator at the largest Larger Text: with `ft.Text(name, max_lines=3)`, "Sponsor presentation" (wider than the chip either way) wrapped whole, while "Lightning talks" showed "Lightning" over a cut-off "talks" (#330, D-249). With `show_checkmark=False` and no horizontal `padding` or `label_padding`, the two widths are the same and the label wraps in full. |
| F70 | *(#337, #345; `ListView` at the pin `22a6dcb`, Flutter 3.47.8.)* A `ListView` keeps its pixel offset when its `controls` change or its items' heights do, not the rows in view. With `build_controls_on_demand` (the default) it is a `ListView.builder` whose items are `ControlWidget(key: ValueKey(key ?? id))`, with no `findChildIndexCallback` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/list_view.dart:138-155`). Flutter's `SliverChildBuilderDelegate.findIndexByKey` then returns null (`widgets/scroll_delegate.dart:530-541`), so `SliverMultiBoxAdaptorElement.performRebuild` keeps each built child's `layoutOffset` by index, not by key (`widgets/sliver.dart:1010-1021`): a keyed item at a new index takes that index's old offset. `RenderSliverList.performLayout` corrects the scroll offset only to keep its first laid-out child consistent (`rendering/sliver_list.dart:148-191`). A list built in full (D-154) lays out from index 0 at 0, so a change above the top moves everything under a constant offset. A lazy list keeps the child at its first built index where it was, so a filter change keeps its slot in view unless a whole item leaves above the built band, and a text-size change still moves it: at Android's `font_scale` 1.3, Saturday's 12:00 PM slot gave way to 11:15 AM at the top (`15854a0ac`, Android 14 emulator). Python gets no first-visible index and no event when the content's extent changes (`scroll_notification_control.dart:55-65` forwards start, update, end, user and overscroll only). U50 asks for a scroll anchor; D-331 and D-371 are the interim. |
| F76 | `InteractiveViewer`'s Python methods change the transform only in relative steps, and two of them do not do what a reader expects (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/interactive_viewer.dart` at `15854a0ac`). `zoom(factor)` post-multiplies a clamped scale (`:150-156`, `_matrixScale()` `:370-410`), so it scales about the content's top-left corner, not the viewport's centre as a pinch does, and never re-clamps the translation: after a pan to the far corner a zoom-out left the viewport blank. `pan(dx, dy)` post-multiplies a translation (`:157-169`, `_matrixTranslate()` `:414-488`), so its deltas are content units, moving `scale × dx` on screen, though its docstring says logical pixels (`sdk/python/packages/flet/src/flet/controls/core/interactive_viewer.py:330-344`). An animated `reset()` returns when the animation starts: `get_transform()` straight after it reads the old transform. Nothing sets an absolute transform, so framing a rectangle takes `zoom()`, `get_transform()` and `pan()` in content units, instant and with a frame drawn in between. Seen on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release APK) and the iOS 26.5 simulator with a 360 × 360 viewer over a 1000 × 600 plan: `zoom(2)` left the translation at (0, 0), `pan(-100, 0)` at scale 2 moved it to (-200, 0), and `zoom(0.5)` after a clamped pan to (-1640, -840) kept that translation (#368, U3; #472, U56). |
| F81 | A `Semantics(exclude_semantics=True)` around a decoration still makes a node of its own: Flet's `Semantics` always sets `enabled` (F42), and that state alone makes Flutter's node focusable. Leaving each part out instead leaves no stop: an `Image` with `exclude_from_semantics=True` made no node, and a `Text` with `semantics_label=""` (Flutter wraps it in `Semantics(label: "")` over `ExcludeSemantics`, `controls/text.dart:118-126`) an empty `View` with `focusable="false"`, not a stop (#374, D-262; U34). Whether TalkBack stops on the focusable one was not checked by gesture. |
| F84 | On Android, `get_accessibility_features().high_contrast` is Flutter's `HIGH_CONTRAST` flag, which the engine sets from `UiModeManager.getContrast() > 0` (Settings › Accessibility › Color contrast, medium or high; `Settings.Secure.contrast_level`), on API 34 and later only, and updates through a `ContrastChangeListener` (`setHighContrastFlag()`, `AccessibilityBridge.java:737-755`, Flutter 3.47.7). High contrast text (`high_text_contrast_enabled`), which Android draws itself, does not set it. On `flet_probe_api34`: `high_text_contrast_enabled 1` left the standard scheme at a cold start, `contrast_level 1.0` gave the high-contrast one (#377, D-265). |
| F89 | *(#385, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* A splat path segment `:name*` matches zero or more segments: `repath.pattern("/:unknown*")` is `^(?:/(?P<unknown>[^/]+?(?:/[^/]+?)*))?(?:/(?=$))?$`, and the Router tries top-level routes in order (`_match_routes`, `components/router.py:163-178`), a parent whose children all fail returning `None` (`:259-299`, also for a recursive drill whose next pair fails, `:222-257`), so a last top-level `Route(path=":unknown*")` gets exactly the locations nothing else matched, as one View level. The Router's `not_found` is wrapped in `View(route=pathname, controls=[not_found()])` (`:731-741`), whose `can_pop` and `on_confirm_pop` stay at their defaults, so on a one-View stack the Back key finishes the activity (F29); a route component returns its own `View` and sets them. On iOS a one-View stack has no edge swipe: `ModalRoute.popGestureEnabled` is false for the first route, and for a route whose pop disposition is `doNotPop` (`can_pop=False`) (`flutter/lib/src/widgets/routes.dart:1910-1931`); a Maestro edge swipe on the iPhone 17 simulator did nothing there and popped a pushed session. |
| F83 | `ft.Image` keeps `exclude_from_semantics` for an SVG given as bytes or as an SVG string, but not for an SVG **asset file**: `buildImage()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/images.dart:195-205`) calls `getSvgPictureFromFile()`, which takes no such parameter (`utils/images_io.dart:36-52`), so `SvgPicture.file` keeps flutter_svg's default and vector_graphics 1.2.4 wraps the picture in `Semantics(container: semanticsLabel != null, image: true, label: '')` (`lib/src/vector_graphics.dart:539-545`), whose image flag merges into the enclosing node. In a packaged app every `src="<path>.svg"` asset is a file (`getAssetSrc()`, `images_io.dart`). Seen on the Android 14 emulator (release APK at `15854a0ac`): a merged `ListTile` row whose leading was such an image dumped as `android.widget.ImageView`, the same row with the SVG's bytes as `android.widget.Button` (#376; U63, #475; D-264). |
| F91 | What `UrlLauncher.launch_url` does with a calendar file (Flet `15854a0ac`, url_launcher_android 6.3.33, url_launcher_ios 6.4.2; #364): **iOS**, `IN_APP_BROWSER_VIEW` of an `http(s)` URL whose response is `text/calendar` presents SFSafariViewController's calendar sheet ("N Events", Add All) yet **raises** `RuntimeError: PlatformException(Error, Error while launching <url>)`, because SFSafariViewController reports the initial load as failed (`URLLaunchSession.swift` `didCompleteInitialLoad`, `url_launcher_ios.dart` `_failedSafariViewControllerLoadException`); after the sheet's Done the empty browser view stays until its close button. `EXTERNAL_APPLICATION` of the same URL opens Calendar's "Add Subscription Calendar".
