| F95 | *(#387, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7, material_ui 1.6.0.)* A `Semantics` with an `identifier` is a semantics node of its own: Flutter's `RenderSemanticsAnnotations` sets `isSemanticBoundary = container \|\| identifier != null` (`rendering/object.dart:4941`), so D-166's identifier on the bar's icons reaches the destination's node only because `NavigationBar` wraps each destination in a `MergeSemantics` (`navigation_bar.dart:322`), not because Flet's Dart control leaves `container` off. `NavigationRail`'s destination is a `Semantics(container: true, selected: …)` with no merge (`navigation_rail.dart:961-981`), so the same tagged icon there is a child node; Flet's `Semantics` always states `enabled` (F42), which is in the Android bridge's `FOCUSABLE_FLAGS` (`AccessibilityBridge.java:97-109`), so that child is focusable: an empty `View` with the identifier that TalkBack stops on. `NavigationRail` calls `onDestinationSelected` for every tap (`navigation_rail.dart`, `onTap`), and Flet's control sets `selected_index` and sends `change` each time (`controls/navigation_rail.dart` `_destinationChanged`), so a re-tap of the active destination reaches `on_change`, as the bar's does (S4). Its labels have no text-scale clamp (the bar clamps at 1.3x): at 200 % the rail widens with them. Seen on the Android 14 emulator (`uiautomator dump`, TalkBack's log) and on the iOS 26.5 simulator (#387, D-277). |
| F105 | A View without an `AppBar` takes its status- and navigation-bar style from the page's theme, with no way to set one of its own (Flet `15854a0ac`; #423): `view.dart:227-237` wraps the `Scaffold` in `AnnotatedRegion(value: <the theme's SystemUiOverlayStyleTheme>)` only when the View has no app bar, and `app_bar.dart:35-37` gives Flutter's `AppBar` the same theme extension; neither `View` nor `AppBar` has a `system_overlay_style` of its own (`controls/core/view.py`, `controls/material/app_bar.py`). Flutter takes the status bar's style from the frontmost `AnnotatedRegion` at the status bar's vertical middle, and the navigation bar's at its own (`rendering/view.dart` `_updateSystemChrome`, Flutter 3.44.8), so an `AppBar` under a `Container(theme=Theme(system_overlay_style=...))` sets the status bar's style for the View it is in (U85; D-287's interim, seen on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator). |
| F109 | *(#439, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* A route change leaves the modal routes open over the old top View open over the new one. On the Android 14 emulator a warm link showed Now under the Schedule's filter sheet (`/schedule` → `/now`, one shared View key) and a session pushed over the Schedule under it, and a session that replaced session detail under its open popup menu (`/schedule/session/105` → `/schedule/session/31`; from `/now/session/105` the menu went); Back closed the sheet or the menu. Flet draws a page's dialog controls in the last View only (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/view.dart` L156-180), and `AlertDialogControl` and `BottomSheetControl` keep the route they pushed in their widget's `State` and close only that route (`alert_dialog.dart` L31, L136-152; `bottom_sheet.dart` L26, L96-110): a new last View builds them again with no route, so `open = False` sent after the change closes nothing (read from the source). Sent before it, with the change held until Flet's dismissal wrapper removes the dialog from `page._dialogs` (`base_page.py` L456-491), it closes them (D-291). `DatePicker`, `TimePicker` and `DateRangePicker` only open (`date_picker.dart` L80-100): `open = False` is ignored, and on the emulator a date picker stayed until its View went. `PopupMenuButton` has no close in Python, and `page._dialogs` is the only list of a page's dialogs (U89, #516). SnackBars and Banners are `ScaffoldMessenger` messages: an Undo SnackBar stayed over the linked page and its Undo worked. |
| F111 | A release `flet build` app silences Dart's `debugPrint`: the build template's `prepareApp()` sets it to a no-op when `dart.vm.product` is true and the app was not started with `--debug` (`sdk/python/templates/build/{{cookiecutter.out_dir}}/lib/main.dart:314-316` at `15854a0ac`; the client app does the same, `client/lib/main.dart:45`), so an extension's `debugPrint` lines never reach logcat or the device log: diagnostics from a release build come from the Python side (`print`, logcat tag `flet.python`), events, or the platform's own logs (S8, #362). |
| F115 | *(Superseded at `08b89cce5` by F263: share_plus 13.3.1 opens the sheet from an empty origin.)* On an iPad, `ft.Share` cannot open its sheet when given a `share_position_origin` (Flet `15854a0ac`, share_plus 12.0.2; #443): `_share_args()` sends the `Offset` as `{"x", "y"}` only (`sdk/python/packages/flet/src/flet/controls/services/share.py:314-318`), the Dart side reads a `Rect` with `width` and `height` defaulting to 0 (`packages/flet/lib/src/services/share.dart:157-166`), and share_plus refuses an empty rectangle on an iPad (`FPPSharePlusPlugin.m:430-441`: "sharePositionOrigin: argument must be set, {{515.5, 687.5}, {0, 0}} must be non-zero …", seen on the iOS 26.5 iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) simulator). An `Offset` subclass with a size does not help: `_share_args()` copies `x` and `y` by name. Phones ignore the origin and are unaffected. Upstream: U95 (#525). |
| F118 | *(#428, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* What `ft.Banner` does as a page dialog: `BannerControl` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/banner.dart`) shows it once, through `ScaffoldMessenger.showMaterialBanner()`, when `open` turns true, and hides it with `hideCurrentMaterialBanner()` when it turns false; either close fires the Banner's `on_dismiss`.<br>• The messenger sits above the Router's Views, so a Banner stays on screen while the user navigates (seen on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator: Back from a session to the Schedule, Open to `/my-schedule/session/142`).<br>• `ft.use_dialog` given a new `Banner` of the same type while one is open patches it in place (a frozen diff), and the shown banner's text follows: the minutes counted down on both devices without the banner closing.<br>• Flutter's `MaterialBanner` wraps itself in `Semantics(liveRegion: true)` (`material/banner.dart`), so the app adds no live region of its own.<br>• Its 250 ms slide comes from the messenger's own `AnimationController`, which `Banner` cannot set: under Android's Remove animations it runs at 5 % (`disable_animations`, D-173); under iOS Reduce Motion it keeps its length, as Flutter's other implicit animations do (U26); with a screen reader on (`accessibleNavigation`) it does not animate. |
| F128 | *(#517, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* Flet's `Semantics` cannot name a route: its Dart control passes no `namesRoute` to Flutter's `Semantics` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/semantics.dart:20-70`) and the Python control has no `names_route` (U108). Each pushed View is a route scope, `Semantics(scopesRoute: true, explicitChildNodes: true)` (`packages/flet/lib/src/widgets/animated_transition_page.dart:57-61`), and Flutter names it from the first node in traversal order that has `namesRoute` and a non-empty label (`AccessibilityBridge.java:2729-2744`). Only Material's `AppBar` sets that flag, on its title, and only on Android, Fuchsia, Linux and Windows (`material/app_bar.dart:1070-1081`; `null` on iOS and macOS), so a View whose bar's title is empty has no name. On Android 9 and later the bridge then sets the pane title to a blank, meant to move TalkBack's focus to the route's first node without a word (`onWindowNameChange()`, `AccessibilityBridge.java:1851-1868`): TalkBack 14 said the window's title, "Conf Companion", then "Back. Button" (#439); on iOS the route's name, or none, goes with `UIAccessibilityScreenChangedNotification` and the focus moves to the first focusable node (`accessibility_bridge.mm:186-226`). `Semantics.focused` is input focus, not the screen reader's. Seen on the Android 14 emulator with TalkBack 14 (a release APK of `a1c67a8e`): a warm link to session detail said the window's title, a blank, then "Back, Button"; but a push to room detail, whose bar's title is "Grand Ballroom A", and a warm link to Settings said only "Back, Button", and `uiautomator events` showed no TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED from the app on the push, so in a Flet app a bar's title is not said as the route's name either; why is open (U108). |
| F130 | *(#361, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* Three traps for a zoomable plan.<br>• **`ft.Semantics(x)` puts `x` in `tooltip`, not `content`:** Semantics' first positional fields are `Control`'s `tooltip` and `badge` (`FLET/controls/core/semantics.py`; `dataclasses.fields` lists `tooltip`, `badge`, `content` as the positional ones), and the client draws a red `NoSuchMethodError` box in place of each such control. `Container`, `GestureDetector`, `InteractiveViewer` and `SafeArea` take `content` first. Always write `ft.Semantics(content=...)`.<br>• **`TabBarView` cannot stop swiping and keep its pages enabled:** it has `controls`, `clip_behavior` and `viewport_fraction` only (`FLET/controls/material/tabs.py:324-349`), its Dart side sets `NeverScrollableScrollPhysics` only when `disabled` (`DART/controls/tabs.dart:176`), and `disabled` is inherited by every descendant (`DART/models/control.dart:82-83`). Its `PageView` takes a horizontal drag after 18 dp, before an `InteractiveViewer`'s 36-dp pan slop (Flutter `gestures/scale.dart:746-752`), so a page steals pans at every zoom (U110, #560).<br>• **A pane that leaves loses its `InteractiveViewer` state:** the `TransformationController` and `save_state`'s matrix live in the Dart widget's `State` (`DART/controls/interactive_viewer.dart`, `_savedMatrix`), which a `TabBarView` page or a replaced pane disposes; on return the scale is 1.0 and `restore_state()` does nothing. A pane swapped into the same slot must carry its own `key`: two unkeyed calls of one `@ft.component` in one slot are one component to Flet, which migrates the old into the new, hook state and controls with it (Flet's `test_diff_migrates_same_component_fn_when_args_differ`; the spike's Concourse pane showed the Expo's selected booth until keyed). `reset()`, `zoom(s)`, `pan(x / s, y / s)` from remembered values restore it exactly. |
| F172 | *(#332, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7.)* On Android, Flutter's `ExpansionTile` wraps its header in `Semantics(label: "Collapsed" or "Expanded", liveRegion: true, accessibilityFocusBlockType: blockNode)` (`material/expansion_tile.dart`, `_buildHeader`); iOS gets the hint and an announcement instead. `blockNode` only makes that node unfocusable (`AccessibilityBridge.java` `isFocusable()`); it stays important for accessibility, since it has a label, and TalkBack reads that label as the words of the nearest focusable node above it with nothing actionable of its own: a list item of a `ListView` (which then takes the list position, "Collapsed. 2 of 4") or a `Card`. So inside any scrolling list or card an `ExpansionTile` costs a stop that says only its state, before its header, with or without a `Semantics` around it. Android's bridge sets no expanded state on a node (no `setExpandedState`; API 36 only sends `CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_EXPANDED`), and iOS's `SemanticsObject` maps no `isExpanded`, so a `Semantics(expanded=)` alone says nothing on either. The app's fold says its state in its label instead (D-354). | TalkBack 14.2 verbose log, Android 14 emulator, `ff8d43f` with the fold's identifier wrappers removed (#332's comment): the stop stayed, on `focusable=false` list items; the Flutter 3.47.7 sources at the pin. |
| F72 | A Flet `Semantics(identifier=...)` reaches the platform as the node's id that UI test tools read. Android: `AccessibilityBridge.createAccessibilityNodeInfo()` sets it as the node's `viewIdResourceName` (`shell/platform/android/io/flutter/view/AccessibilityBridge.java:856-858`, Flutter 3.47.7), which `uiautomator dump` and Maestro show as `resource-id`. iOS: `SemanticsObject`'s `accessibilityIdentifier` (`shell/platform/darwin/ios/framework/Source/SemanticsObject.mm:509-517`), which Maestro's XCTest driver shows as the element's id. An unlabelled `Semantics` with an identifier inside a merged node gives the merged node its id (seen for `app.nav.*` and `app.search`). **Side effect on Android:** the same `createAccessibilityNodeInfo()` first calls `setAccessibleNavigation(true)` (line 804), and only a change of the accessibility state or of touch exploration sets it back (lines 405, 565). So any accessibility client that reads the tree, Maestro's every hierarchy read and `uiautomator dump` included, makes Flutter report `accessible_navigation` as TalkBack would, with touch exploration off. An app that reads `get_accessibility_features()` afterwards (ours at start and on resume, D-23) takes it for a screen reader. Seen on #348's branch (release APK, Android 14 emulator): under Maestro the Schedule kept its Now button on the now slot, which it keeps only for a screen reader (D-204); launched and driven with `adb` alone, it hid it. iOS reads VoiceOver itself (`UIAccessibilityIsVoiceOverRunning()`, `FlutterViewController.mm:2038`), so XCTest's queries change nothing: on the iPhone 17 simulator the Now button hid on the now slot under Maestro (`jump_to_now.yaml`) (#348, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-252). |
| F149 | *(#337, `15854a0ac`, Android 16 emulator.)* After a text-size change, Flutter lays out a 0 dp probe in a `Column` above a `TabBarView` before the day list's items, so the probe's `on_size_change` reaches Python before theirs: a fully built list whose heights were all forgotten at the probe's report had every new height by the next scroll end, across 8 changes. A lazy list keeps the offset of its first built item by index (Flutter's sliver list), so a text-size change shifts the rows at the top, and the change back undoes the shift exactly. When the text shrinks, the item that was at the top can fall out of the built range, and a keyed `scroll_to` to it does nothing (F7). |
| F150 | *(#586, `15854a0ac`, iOS 26.5 simulator.)* **A component under a memoised component cannot re-render on its own once that memoised parent has been skipped, and the error stops every later re-render of the session.** A component finds a context by walking `_parent_component`, a weak reference to the component instance that rendered it (`components/hooks/use_context.py`, `use_context()`; `components/component.py`, `Renderer.render_component()`). Each render of a parent makes new child `Component` instances; the diff moves each old instance's state into the new one (`Component._migrate_state()`), and a memoised one that is skipped reuses its last output (`before_update()`, `self._b = self._state.last_b`), whose components still point at the instance that first rendered them. That instance has left the tree, and once Python's cycle collector frees it the weak reference is dead: a component inside that re-renders by itself (its own state, an effect, a store it subscribed to) finds no provider, so `use_context()` returns the default, and our `use_services()` raised `RuntimeError: use_services() needs a ServicesCtx provider above it`. Flet runs every scheduled update in one task, `Session.__updates_scheduler()` (`messaging/session.py:702-750`), which catches the errors of effects but not of `control.update()`, so the task ends; nothing logs it (the session holds the task), events still run and `page.update()` still sends, but no component re-renders again. Seen with the Schedule's status strip inside its memoised day lists (D-345): its minute tick, after a star tap or a minute's re-render had skipped the lists, stopped the page in 2 of 6 runs on the simulator (when the collector had run); in a test, `gc.collect()` makes it certain. **Rule:** under a memoised component, pass values; no component there may hold state, run an effect or subscribe to a store (D-153's rows already do not). (U111, #597.) |
| F173 | *(#342, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7, Android 16 emulator, TalkBack 16.0.)* A Flet control's semantic state changes only after the Python round trip, but Flutter's `InkResponse.handleTap()` calls `Feedback.forTap()` first, whose `TapSemanticEvent` the Android bridge sends at once as `TYPE_VIEW_CLICKED` (`AccessibilityBridge.java:330-332`); TalkBack speaks a clicked checkable node's state from the node as it is when it handles the event (`EventTypeViewClickedFeedbackRule: ttsOutputRule= checkedState`), so it can say the old state. `enable_feedback=False` on the `IconButton` sends no such event (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/icon_button.dart:96`). On API 36 the bridge also sends `CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_CHECKED` for a toggle change (lines 1708-1711), but TalkBack 16 got only `CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_SUBTREE` events and said nothing for it (D-355). |
| F182 | *(#600, `15854a0ac`, iOS 26.5 simulator.)* A scrollable's `scroll_interval` throttles every scroll notification type, the end included: `ScrollNotificationControl` keeps one timestamp per notification type and drops one that comes within `scroll_interval` ms of the last of its type (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/scroll_notification_control.dart:33-45`). So a list's second `end` within the interval of its first never reaches Python, though the list moved: two keyed `scroll_to`s 300 ms apart on a list with `scroll_interval=250` gave one end, and `jump_to_key()` said "did not move" (U162 #619, D-364). |
| F192 | *(#302, `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7; read from the sources.)* Flet reports every change of Flutter's lifecycle to `page.on_app_lifecycle_state_change`, `inactive` and `resume` included (`AppLifecycleListener` in `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/page.dart:132-139`). On a change away from `resumed`, Flutter's `FocusManager` takes the input focus away and keeps the node, and gives it back on `resumed` unless something else took the focus meanwhile (`widgets/focus_manager.dart:1868-1891`); a TalkBack double tap gives no input focus, so after a first trip there is nothing to give back. A button's `focus()` (`IconButton`, `Button`, `TextButton`, `OutlinedButton`: `_focusNode.requestFocus()`, e.g. `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/icon_button.dart:55-63`) sets the node's focused flag, and Android's `AccessibilityBridge` sends `TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED` for the input-focused node when it differs from the last one it reported (`engine/src/flutter/shell/platform/android/io/flutter/view/AccessibilityBridge.java:1726-1739` in the SDK), which TalkBack follows: #302's throwaway build saw TalkBack's focus on Share 2.5 s after the share call, and nothing 0.5 s after it. Once the button holds the input focus, the next trip's `resume` gives it back through `FocusManager` itself, so the app's own `focus()` then changes nothing and sends no event. `ListTile` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/list_tile.dart`, which passes no `focusNode`) and `PopupMenuButton` have no `focus()` (U182). In components mode the event's `control` is the instance of the render that was tapped: a later render of its component builds a new instance that takes over its id (`_migrate_state`, `controls/base_control.py:556-568`), and the old one, detached, refuses `focus()` ("Control must be added to the page first"); `page.get_control(id)` returns the mounted one (seen on Share, which a share re-renders, and in `tests/test_session_extras.py`). On the Android 16 emulator (TalkBack 16.0), back from the share sheet or Chrome, TalkBack first had its focus on no node or on the app bar's Back, for up to about a second; a focus sent 0.5 s after `resume` lost to it in 2 of 10 trips, 1 s after in none of 8. A row (`LinkTile`) there got TalkBack's focus back without the app (TalkBack 16's own restore, `FocusActorForScreen`), which #302 did not see on TalkBack 14.2. |
| F246 | *(#450, `08b89cce5`, flutter_local_notifications 22.3.3; read from the sources, then seen on the Android 16 emulator.)* How `flet_notifications` hands over the tap that launched the app.<br>• **The handler must be given to the constructor.** A `ft.Service` registers with the page inside `__post_init__`, with the fields it has then (`controls/services/service.py`, `Service.__post_init__`: fields assigned after it "require a later update to reach the client"), and the Dart side sends an event only when its `on_<name>` property is true (`flet_backend.dart`, `triggerControlEvent`). `Notifications()` followed by `notifications.on_tap = ...` lost the launch tap on the emulator (the app opened on Welcome); `Notifications(on_tap=...)` delivered it.<br>• **The launch tap comes before any call's answer.** The Dart service starts initializing in `init()`, and every method call awaits that initialization (`invokeMethod` → `_ensureInitialized()`), which ends with `_replayQueuedResponses()` then `_replayLaunchDetails()` (`lib/src/notifications.dart`), once per process. The event is sent before the call's result on the same channel, and Python schedules the event's dispatch task before it wakes the caller (`flet_socket_server.py`, `dispatch_event`; `session.py`, `handle_invoke_method_results`). On the emulator, with the app killed and after a reboot, the tap's "held for the start" log line came before the first answer each time.<br>• **Android groups two notifications of one app;** a tap on the collapsed group opens the app's launcher intent (`act=MAIN`), not a notification's, so no tap event comes; a tap on a child of the expanded group does (`act=SELECT_NOTIFICATION`). |
| F257 | *(#692, `08b89cce5`; read from the sources.)* A `Service` registers with the page in `__post_init__` (`controls/services/service.py`), so a field assigned after construction reaches Flutter only with a later update. `ConnectivityService` (`packages/flet/lib/src/services/connectivity.dart`) listens to `connectivity_plus` only while `hasEventHandler("change")`, checked at `init()` and `update()`; `ConnectivityChangeEvent.connectivity` is a list of `ConnectivityType` (`NONE` among them). So `Services.create()` passes `on_change` to `ft.Connectivity(...)` at construction, and the handler only sets an `asyncio.Event` (D-407). |
| F259 | *(#712, `08b89cce5`, permission_handler_android 13.0.1; read from the sources, then seen on the Android 16 emulator.)* On Android 13+, `PermissionHandler().get_status(Permission.NOTIFICATION)` reads `DENIED`, never `PERMANENTLY_DENIED`, while the app's notifications are off in system settings (`POST_NOTIFICATIONS` `USER_SET\|USER_FIXED`) and the app's prompt was never refused twice in this install: `PermissionUtils.determineDeniedVariant()` (`PermissionUtils.java:508-535`) says permanently denied only after it has recorded a denial, which it records only while `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale` is true, and a user-fixed permission has no rationale. Android then answers a request (E3's `request_permissions()`) with no dialog and False (`GrantPermissionsViewModel ... result=2`). A first refusal of the prompt reads `DENIED` too, with a rationale, and Android asks once more; the second refusal reads `PERMANENTLY_DENIED`. `flet-permission-handler` exposes no rationale (U239, #715), so the app can't tell them apart. Whether it had asked before (the primer `accepted`) doesn't settle it either: a request that failed or was cancelled before any prompt is recorded as asked too (D-395), so the next request may show Android's first real prompt. A refusal after an earlier request that still reads `DENIED` therefore reads `refused` for the session (#712, #716's review): Settings offers "Allow notifications" beside "Open settings", and only `PERMANENTLY_DENIED` or `RESTRICTED` read blocked. |
| F263 | *(#462, `08b89cce5`, share_plus 13.3.1; read from the sources, then seen on the iOS 26.5 iPad Pro 11-inch (M5) simulator.)* **An iPad opens the share sheet from a point with no size.** Flet still sends `share_position_origin` as `{"x", "y"}` only (`sdk/python/packages/flet/src/flet/controls/services/share.py:314-318`), read as a `Rect` of width and height 0 (`packages/flet/lib/src/services/share.dart:157-166`), but share_plus 13.3.1, which the pin resolves (12.0.2 at F115's `15854a0ac`), converts a non-empty origin to the view's coordinates and, when the rectangle is empty, points the popover from a 2 x 2 rect at the view's centre (`FPPSharePlusPlugin.m:435-442`), so it no longer refuses. About this app's Report a problem opened the sheet on the iPad, and `about_app` passed there without `SHARE_FAILS` (#462). Supersedes F115; U95 is no longer needed for the sheet to open. |
| F274 | *(#745, `08b89cce5`, Flutter 3.47.7; read from the sources, then seen in TalkBack's log on the Pixel 6a AVD, API 36. #830, `22a6dcb16`, Flutter 3.47.8; `text_input_action` seen on that AVD and on an iPhone 17 simulator, iOS 26.5.)* **A `TextField` loses the focus on every submit unless its `text_input_action` says otherwise, and only Android has a value that does.** Flet builds a `TextFormField` with `onFieldSubmitted` only (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/textfield.dart:288`), never `onEditingComplete`; for Done, Go, Search, Send and a single-line Newline, Flutter's `performAction()` (`editable_text.dart:3738-3763`) finalizes with `shouldUnfocus: true`, and `_finalizeEditing()` (`:3914-3949`) unfocuses the field before `onSubmitted` when no `onEditingComplete` is given. Since #808 `text_input_action` reaches Flutter (`textfield.dart:312`). **Android:** `UNSPECIFIED` finalizes with `shouldUnfocus: false`, a hardware Enter too (`adb shell input keyevent 66`): the soft keyboard stayed up (`mInputShown` true) with no `TYPE_WINDOWS_CHANGED` across repeated submits in TalkBack's verbose log; the saved log includes five consecutive "Not a badge code" utterances and a later "Not captured" result; `NONE` sends a newline, so it unfocuses (read from the sources). **iOS:** `UNSPECIFIED` is `UIReturnKeyDefault`, whose `"\n"` is a Newline, which unfocuses a single-line field (`FlutterTextInputPlugin.mm:172-173, :1514-1516`), against Flet's enum doc (`textfield.py:250-256`). Filmed: the field's border goes thin and the keyboard starts down, then the refocus (D-416) brings both back within about 0.4 s. The key reads a return arrow on both. So the scan screen sets `UNSPECIFIED` and keeps its refocus calls; U254 asks for a way to keep the focus on both. |
| F276 | *(#741, `08b89cce5`, Flutter 3.47.7; read from the sources, then seen on the iOS 26.5 iPhone 17 simulator under Maestro.)* **On iOS, `SemanticsService.announce_message()` never returns while the accessibility bridge runs (VoiceOver on, or an XCTest UI test).** Flet awaits the Dart call's result with no timeout (`_invoke_method(..., timeout=None)`, `controls/services/semantics_service.py:147`; `messaging/session.py:480-531`); its Dart side returns `SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/services/semantics_service.dart:27-36`), which awaits `SystemChannels.accessibility.send()` (`flutter/lib/src/semantics/semantics_service.dart:81-93`); the iOS engine's handler calls `HandleEvent()` and never `reply` (`engine/src/flutter/shell/platform/darwin/ios/framework/Source/accessibility_bridge.mm:59-61`), so the reply never comes. The announcement itself is made. Without the bridge the channel has no handler and answers at once. Seen as #724's scan screen holding its single flight after the first result: every later submit was dropped, its text kept. U256 asks Flet not to wait for that reply. |
| F288 | *(#775, `08b89cce5`, Flutter 3.47.7; read from the sources, then seen on the Pixel 6a AVD, API 36.)* **A `TextField.focus()` sent in the same update that turns `can_request_focus` back on is dropped.** Flet's `focus` method calls `_focusNode.requestFocus()` as soon as the call arrives (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/textfield.dart:116-117`), while the property reaches the node only when Flutter rebuilds the field at the next frame (`textfield.dart:333`; `_TextFieldState.didUpdateWidget`, `flutter/lib/src/material/text_field.dart:1318`); until then `canRequestFocus` is false and `_doRequestFocus` returns without a word (`flutter/lib/src/widgets/focus_manager.dart:1178-1183`). Seen as #770's effect, which asked for the focus as the scan screen came back on top and left `scan.entry` `focused="false"`. D-438 keeps the scan field focusable instead. |
| F295 | *(#794, `08b89cce5`; read from the sources, then seen on the iPhone 17 simulator, iOS 26.5, at the largest text, and the Pixel 6a AVD, API 36, at 200 %.)* **`scroll_to(scroll_key=...)` works on a `Row` with `scroll` set, and puts the keyed control's start at the row's start, clamped to the row's ends**: the handler calls `Scrollable.ensureVisible(ctx, duration:, curve:)` with Flutter's default alignment 0 (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/scrollable_control.dart:135-145`), and Flet passes no alignment, so a keyed scroll cannot centre an item or move only as far as it takes to show it. Search's scope row, opened on Places, put Places' gap at the left edge; picked, Sponsors, the last chip, stopped at the row's end with its 16 dp gutter. |
| F297 | *(#800, `08b89cce5`, Flutter 3.47.7, material_ui 1.8.0; read from the sources, then seen on the iPhone 17 simulator, iOS 26.5, at the largest text.)* **A scroll inside any box of a View's body colours its `AppBar`, and a scrollable merged into an identifier's node hides the identifier on iOS.** Flet's `AppBarControl` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/app_bar.dart`) passes no `notificationPredicate`, so Flutter's default (`depth == 0`) takes the first scrollable under the `Scaffold` for the page, a scrolling `Column` in a card included; with no `bgcolor`, the scrolled-under bar is `colorScheme.surfaceContainer` and its `elevation_on_scroll` (3 by default) adds the surface tint (`material_ui` `app_bar.dart`, `_resolveColor` and the `Material`'s `surfaceTintColor` fallback). A `bgcolor` that is not a state colour is used in both states, and `elevation_on_scroll=0` drops the tint. A `Column` with `scroll` is a `Scrollable` whose semantics set `hasImplicitScrolling`; merged into a `MergeSemantics` node, the node is built on iOS as a `FlutterScrollableSemanticsObject`, whose accessibility element is a `FlutterSemanticsScrollView` that forwards the label, value and hint but not `accessibilityIdentifier` (engine `SemanticsObject.mm`, `FlutterSemanticsScrollView.mm`). D-447. |
| F299 | *(#806 at `22a6dcb16`, read from the sources and seen in a real Flet session; fixed at `a9ef26fdb`, #1010, read at `FLET/controls/material/refresh_indicator.py:154-174` and seen on devices, #811.)* **`RefreshIndicator` ends its spinner through whichever instance holds its id when `on_refresh` returns.** In components mode a render replaces each control with a new instance under the same id, and the Dart widget, keyed by that id, stays mounted with its completer pending (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/refresh_indicator.dart`, completed only by `finish_refresh` or `dispose`). At `22a6dcb16` only the pulled instance could finish it (`session.index.get(self._i) is self`), so an `on_refresh` that re-rendered the indicator's component left the spinner turning for good: U279, #809. Since `a9ef26fdb` it goes through `session.index.get(self._i)`. A control removed during the refresh is finished by its unmount. **A control still in the index whose widget has been disposed, such as an indicator on a `TabBarView` page that has left, makes `finish_refresh` time out after 10 s with an exception no handler can catch, which ends in the full-screen error** (`FLET/controls/material/refresh_indicator.py:171-174`, `packages/flet/lib/src/models/control.dart:534-541`; seen on the iOS simulator, #811; U285, #1020). |
| F300 | *(#839, `22a6dcb16`, Flutter 3.47.8; read from the sources, not run on a device.)* **On Android, `RemoteTester.take_screenshot` works once per launch and draws only the Flutter view; `assert_screenshot` takes any PNG bytes.** `FlutterWidgetTester.takeScreenshot` calls `convertFlutterSurfaceToImage()` on every Android call (`packages/flet_integration_test/lib/src/flutter_tester.dart:85`). Flutter asserts it runs once, and only the test's teardown resets it (`integration_test/lib/src/_callback_io.dart:70`, `:74-78`); `flutter test` builds debug, so the assert is live, and the binding has no `revertFlutterImage` to call between shots. The capture is `captureView`, the `FlutterView` drawn to a bitmap, because `hasInstrumentation()` is always false (`FlutterDeviceScreenshot.java:74-80`, `:138`), so it has no status or navigation bar. On iOS nothing converts, and `IntegrationTestPlugin.m` only stores each capture under its name, with no guard, so repeated calls are fine. `FletTestApp.assert_screenshot(name, screenshot, similarity_threshold=0)` compares the bytes it is given (`testing/flet_test_app.py:632`), resizing the image when its size differs from the golden's (`_compare_images_rgb`). U280, #884. |

## Changes from v1

- **Purpose.** v1 aimed to ship as 27.0.0 of the PSF's store listings. v2 is a community showcase, so everything that existed only for the PSF is dropped: the PSF deliverables P1-P13, gates G1-G6 and the Track decision, Badge Days with PSF staff, Linear, outreach dates, PSF-held secrets and on-call.
- **Platforms.** Web and PWA are dropped (Pyodide transport, Firebase Hosting, offline service worker). Only Android and iOS remain.
- **Fidelity.** v1 kept Ionic's URLs, aria strings, exact colours, four-mode theme picker, Cupertino pickers and platform dark split. v2 redesigns in Material 3 (sections C and D).
- **Scope enforcement.** Gone: the 263 named tests, the `feature_trace` gate, the Node differential harness in CI, 168 goldens, the fallback-parity suite and contract tests against PSF staging. In their place: named rule tests, invariants and a one-off capture of Ionic's output (D-77).
- **Server-driven content.** `app-config.json`/`app-content.json`, kill switches, update gates and banner polling become bundled TOML year data (D-45). A content fix now needs a new build.
- **Push, analytics and Sentry** are cut. Local reminders are prioritised instead (D-58), with local logs (D-72).
- **Capabilities.** The observable `Caps`, CI manifest, API probes and remote veto become import-time constants (D-63).
- **Architecture.** 12 stores and 13 contexts become 3 stores (5 from P3). The service locator and provider families are gone. `@safe` plus a lint rule and a top-toast `Banner` become explicit error boundaries plus one small decorator until U15 lands (D-71).
- **Navigation.** The `/app/tabs` URLs, drawer, global floor-plan modal and short links become five fixed tabs, a flat More list and one recursive drill route (D-13..D-17).
- **Favourites.** Server-authoritative sync through the outbox becomes local-first sync with a dirty flag (D-55). The outbox goes from three retry caps to one (D-56).
- **Releases.** v1 had `27.x` versions, `YY*1000` build numbers, a `builds.txt` ledger, release/nightly/content workflows and a release train. v2 has SemVer `0.y`, commit-count build numbers, one CI workflow plus a weekly feed check and a manual APK build, and internal distribution (D-83..D-85).
- **Flet version.** v1 said "never ship on a prerelease; pin a released 1.1.x by 2027-03-05". v2 develops on a pinned 1.1 commit until 1.1.0 is released, because the owner's Xcode 27 needs it (D-81).
- **Planning.** The capacity model, dated milestones and cut order become phases with exit criteria and soft markers (PLAN §7).
- **DEMO build flavour** becomes the hidden Developer screen (D-73).
- **Docs.** v1's seven large documents become a smaller set (D-96), and v1 stays in git history.

## Open decisions

Each open decision has a recommendation. Where a provisional decision applies (marked **prov. O-n** above), it holds until it is settled. Under D-101, decisions marked **owner-only** wait for the owner; agents settle the others when the work needs them, recording a new D-number.

| # | Decision needed | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| O-1 | ~~Bottom-bar destinations and the start screen.~~ **Settled by D-454, with no dogfooding behind it.** | **Now · Schedule · My schedule · Map · More, opening on Now** (D-13, D-14). The week of dogfooding at the P1 exit didn't happen. The alternatives were a Speakers tab instead of My schedule, or opening on Schedule. |
| O-2 | ~~Per-platform page transitions, the one exception to "identical on both platforms".~~ **Settled by D-148 (S4).** | **Yes**: iOS swipe-back and Android predictive back, with identical visuals (D-11). |
| O-3 | ~~Font.~~ **Settled by D-455.** | **Bundle Roboto** (D-26). Consider a display face for headings only if a 2027 brand appears. |
| O-4 | ~~**Owner-only.** App identity: bundle id, display name, icon, URL scheme and deep-link host (D-21), and the calendar `UID` domain (`CALENDAR_DOMAIN`, D-268).~~ **Settled by the owner, D-452.** | Conf Companion, `io.github.ndonkohenri.confcompanion`, our own icon. |
| O-5 | **Owner-only.** Distribution. | **Internal only**: APK sideload plus Play internal testing, and TestFlight internal (D-85). Consider a public listing only with PSF permission. |
| O-6 | **Owner-only.** Submit a PyCon US 2027 CFP proposal? | Decide around **2026-11-20**, the expected mentorship deadline (UNCONFIRMED), based on P1 progress. If yes, submit one talk (optionally a poster with phones for a live demo) framed as a community showcase, never as the official app, by about **2026-12-18** (expected close, UNCONFIRMED). |
| O-7 | ~~How deep the staff tools go (P3).~~ **Settled by D-456.** | **All four** on the shared scan screen, in this order: lead → door → swag → CoC. All four are built; none moved to `later`. Staff-UX defaults from the UX study: show attendee names at the door, pre-check available swag items, hide attendee emails in CoC capture until revealed. |
| O-8 | ~~A backend for stage demos.~~ **Settled by D-457.** | **Keep the LAN mock from the laptop** (D-50). If the demo must run without a laptop, this reopens: ship the pure mock `handle()` behind the Developer screen as an in-app backend. |
